# DECISIONS — decision log (ADR-lite)

_Each entry records one decision, why we made it, and what would force us to revisit it. Entries marked **LOCKED** are settled: treat them as constraints and don't re-open them casually, because something downstream almost certainly depends on them._

**Format:** `ID · status · decision · rationale · what would force a change`

---

### D-001 · LOCKED · Stack is React + Next.js + Tailwind CSS
The app is built with React, Next.js, and Tailwind CSS.
**Why:** explicitly required by the build spec, and by the portfolio target.
**Changes if:** the stack changes.

### D-002 · LOCKED (scoped reopening, see D-038) · Itinerary data persists in browser localStorage; client-side, no server by default
All itinerary/planner data is saved and read from localStorage. There is no server or database in the default build.
**Why:** the spec calls for local browser persistence, and it keeps the app deployable as a static/client experience.
**Changes if:** server persistence is greenlit (then it becomes a backend slice).
**(Scoped reopening by D-038, greenlit for M9, 2026-06-27):** the itinerary, and only the itinerary, may now optionally sync to a managed BaaS (Firebase Cloud Firestore). localStorage remains the source-of-truth contract (D-018) and the only persistence when the BaaS is not configured; the remote layer is an additive mirror that is dormant by default. The base stance here (client-side, no server *we* run) still holds. We consume a managed service over HTTPS rather than operating a server, and D-010's static export is untouched. When the Firebase web-config env values are absent, this reopening has zero effect and the app behaves exactly as described above. The rest of the app (map, recommendations, photos, bookings, and so on) stays local/static, because the reopening is itinerary-scoped.

### D-003 · Superseded by D-079 · The map is a polished mock by default

### D-004 · LOCKED (scoped reopening, see D-038) · Scope is frontend-heavy; backend is idle unless a real server-side slice is greenlit
The client app is the primary implementation surface and is owned end to end by whoever is on the frontend. No server-side work happens unless a real server-side concern is greenlit: a real API integration needing secrets, server persistence, or auth.
**Why:** a client-side localStorage app has no backend; don't manufacture server work.
**Changes if:** a real server-side slice is greenlit.
**(Scoped reopening by D-038, greenlit for M9, 2026-06-27):** cross-friend itinerary sync via Firebase Firestore is greenlit. Note that this is not a server-side slice. There is no server we run, no route handler, and no secret-bearing proxy (the Firebase web config is public by design, D-040; Security Rules are the server-side gate, D-044). The integration is client code, the Firebase modular SDK called directly from the static bundle, so it stays on the frontend side, consistent with D-010's static export. The only non-code server-side artifact is `firestore.rules` (D-044), which is authored here and deployed by hand through the Firebase console/CLI rather than run by us. The "backend idle" stance therefore holds in substance; this annotation records that consuming a managed BaaS from the client is the exception that does not require standing up any server-side work.

### D-005 · LOCKED · Trip data — two destinations as structured, expandable data
Kathmandu, Nepal (**Dec 9 – Dec 18**) and Japan (**Dec 19 – Jan 9**), modeled as expandable structured data (destinations, days, itinerary items, recommendations, photo spots, etc.).
**Why:** the spec's trip; data structures must be easy to extend with more destinations later.
**Changes if:** the trip or data model is redesigned.

### D-006 · LOCKED · Countdown target = December 9, 2026 → January 9, 2027
The trip target is departure December 9, 2026 and trip end January 9, 2027. Confirmed 2026-06-20; it was previously an assumption, because the build spec gave Dec 9 → Jan 9 with no year. The target datetime is kept configurable in one place, implemented in slice S1. The existing `lib/trip-data.ts` already encodes `TRIP_START = 2026-12-09` / `TRIP_END = 2027-01-09`, so S1 consolidates the single source rather than changing the dates.
**Why:** the countdown cannot function without a concrete target, and the year is now confirmed.
**Changes if:** the trip year changes. That is a one-line config change in the single source.

### D-007 · LOCKED · Accessibility, responsiveness, and reduced-motion are acceptance-gating
Keyboard navigability, visible focus states, semantic HTML, sufficient contrast, alt text, mobile-first responsiveness, and `prefers-reduced-motion` support are part of the definition of done, not optional polish.
**Why:** the spec lists hard accessibility and performance requirements.
**Changes if:** the bar is relaxed (unlikely for a portfolio piece).

---

### D-008 · Superseded · Scaffolded server artifacts (Prisma / next-auth / seed / cloud) are unused and removed

### D-009 · LOCKED · Ship dark-only; remove the dead theme toggle and the forcedTheme contradiction
The app ships dark mode only. The scaffold contained a contradiction: `app/layout.tsx` sets `forcedTheme="dark"`, which hard-pins dark and makes any toggle a no-op, while `components/theme-toggle.tsx` offered a light/dark switch. The dead `ThemeToggle` is removed, and light mode is dropped from the Definition of Done.
**Keep the theme provider:** `next-themes`, `components/theme-provider.tsx`, and the `<ThemeProvider>` wrapper in `layout.tsx` all stay, because `components/ui/sonner.tsx` (the `<Toaster />`) calls `useTheme()` from `next-themes`; removing the provider would break the toaster at runtime. Keep `forcedTheme="dark"` (or equivalent) so the single theme is dark. Only the user-facing toggle is removed.
**Why:** a forced single theme with a visible toggle is a broken UX promise, and the spec's design direction (winter-neon / Himalayan-warmth glassmorphism) is authored for a dark surface (`bg-navy-900`).
**Changes if:** we want a real light theme. That reopens the DoD and reinstates a working toggle.

### D-010 · LOCKED · Deploy via GitHub Pages static export, project-page form
The app deploys as a static export to GitHub Pages in project-page form (`https://<user>.github.io/<repo>/`). `next.config.js` must set `output: 'export'`, `images: { unoptimized: true }`, `trailingSlash: true`, and derive `basePath` / `assetPrefix` from the repo name in one place (empty string for local dev, so `next dev` and local preview work at `/`). Add `public/.nojekyll` so GitHub Pages serves Next's `_next/` asset folder. No server features are permitted: no SSR, no API routes, no middleware, no Next image optimization, and no `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'`.
**Why:** this reinforces D-002 / D-004 (client-only). Static export is the simplest, free, durable host for a portfolio piece, and it forces the app to stay genuinely client-side. `force-dynamic` and image optimization are incompatible with `output: 'export'` and would fail the build.
**Changes if:** we move to a different host (e.g. Vercel) or greenlight server features. Then the basePath/output assumptions are revisited.

### D-011 · LOCKED · State management = local React hooks + localStorage; no global store library
State lives in component-local `useState` / `useReducer`, lifted only as far as needed, with persistence to localStorage for itinerary/planner data (per D-002). No global store library (no Redux, Zustand, Jotai, Recoil) and no data-fetching/cache library (no SWR, react-query): there is no server to fetch from. Verified by grep: no live feature component imports `jotai`, `zustand`, `swr`, or `@tanstack/react-query`. These four packages are unused and go on the prune list (D-008's spirit).
**Why:** the app's only cross-cutting state is the itinerary, which is small and owned by one section; a store library is unjustified complexity for a static client app.
**Changes if:** state genuinely becomes cross-cutting across many distant sections. Revisit with a lightweight context first, and reach for a library only if that proves insufficient. **(Revisited by D-025: the itinerary is now genuinely cross-cutting; the sanctioned answer is a Context + custom-event hook rather than a store library, so D-011 holds.)** **(Annotated by D-045, M9:** adding the firebase modular SDK does not violate D-011. firebase is a BaaS client, a transport dependency that talks to a remote datastore, not a client-side *state-management* library. The app's state mechanism stays exactly the Context + CustomEvent hook of D-026; firebase only fans that existing event out over the network. The "no store library" rule is untouched.**)**

### D-012 · LOCKED · Data model — canonical shapes are those already in `lib/*-data.ts`
The data shapes already authored in `lib/` are the canonical model. Extend them; do not redesign them. Summary (full source in the cited files):

- **`ItineraryCategory`** (`lib/trip-data.ts`): union of `'sightseeing' | 'food' | 'photography' | 'shopping' | 'nature' | 'cultural' | 'transportation' | 'hotel' | 'free' | 'nightlife'`. `nightlife` is part of the enum and stays. Paired with `CATEGORY_COLORS` and `CATEGORY_ICONS` records keyed by the same union.
- **`ItineraryItem`** (`lib/trip-data.ts`): `{ id: string; title: string; category: ItineraryCategory; time?: string; duration?: string; notes?: string; location?: string }`.
- **`DayPlan`** (`lib/trip-data.ts`): `{ date: string (YYYY-MM-DD); city: string; country: 'nepal' | 'japan'; items: ItineraryItem[] }`. This is the unit persisted to localStorage and the CRUD target for the calendar planner. Sample data: `lib/sample-itinerary.ts` (`SAMPLE_ITINERARY: DayPlan[]`).
- **`Recommendation`** (`lib/nepal-data.ts`, reused by `lib/japan-data.ts`): `{ id; name; category: string; description; bestTime; duration; photoRating: number; notes; location?; image? }`. Recommendation cards for Nepal/Japan attractions and food.
- **`PhotoSpot`** (`lib/photography-data.ts`): `{ id; name; country: 'Nepal' | 'Japan'; city; bestTime; style; gear; tip; category: string }`.
- **`NightlifeVenue`** (`lib/nightlife-data.ts`): `{ id; name; country: 'Nepal' | 'Japan'; location; vibe; musicType; priceRange; bestDays; description }`. This one stays.
- **Travel-tips shapes** (`lib/travel-tips-data.ts`): `FoodItem { name; country: 'Nepal' | 'Japan'; description; emoji }`, `EtiquetteTip { title; country: 'Nepal' | 'Japan' | 'Both'; description; icon }`, `PackingItem { item; category; essential: boolean }`, and `WEATHER_INFO` keyed by `nepal` / `japan`.
- **Marker-category** (map): the map mock reuses the same recommendation/category vocabulary (`ItineraryCategory` colors/icons and `Recommendation.category`). No separate marker type is introduced unless a map slice needs one, and if so it is recorded then.

Note that the country casing is intentionally inconsistent across files: `'nepal'|'japan'` lowercase in trip/itinerary data, `'Nepal'|'Japan'` capitalized in recommendation/photo/nightlife data. This is pre-existing and load-bearing for the current components, so don't "normalize" it as part of S0. If a future slice needs unification, record it as its own decision.
**Why:** these shapes are already wired into the rendered components and realistic sample data; re-deriving them risks churn and data loss.
**Changes if:** a new section needs a field. Extend the interface additively (optional field) and note it here. **(D-026 exercises this clause: two optional fields `sourceId?`/`sourceType?` are added to `ItineraryItem`, additive, with no existing field touched.)** **(D-034 exercises it again: a new module `lib/booking-data.ts` holds the flight/stay shapes. It does not modify any existing interface, so it is additive by construction.)** **(D-041 exercises it again, M9: three optional fields `createdBy?`/`updatedBy?`/`updatedAt?` are added to `ItineraryItem`, additive, and every existing item stays valid.)**

### D-013 · Superseded by D-070 · Routing — single page with anchored sections, smooth scroll, sticky nav

### D-014 · LOCKED · Component organization — feature components + `ui/` + `lib/` + `hooks/`
Directory layout:
- `components/`: feature components (one per section: `hero-section.tsx`, `trip-dashboard.tsx`, `calendar-planner.tsx`, `nepal-section.tsx`, `nightlife-section.tsx`, etc.), plus app-wide pieces (`navbar.tsx`, `footer.tsx`, `theme-provider.tsx`, `chunk-load-error-handler.tsx`).
- `components/ui/`: shadcn/Radix primitives (button, dialog, sonner, etc.).
- `lib/`: data modules (`*-data.ts`, `sample-itinerary.ts`), the trip-date single source (`trip-data.ts`), and `utils.ts` (`cn`).
- `hooks/`: shared React hooks (e.g. a `useLocalStorage` hook when introduced for persistence).
- `components/layouts/` is removed. Its five files (`container.tsx`, `section.tsx`, `page-header.tsx`, `app-shell.tsx`, `auth-layout.tsx`) are scaffold dashboard chrome with no live importer (they appear only in the style guide), and `auth-layout.tsx` additionally belongs to the removed auth stack (D-008).

**Why:** this is the de-facto structure already in the tree. Keeping feature components flat under `components/` with `ui/` for primitives is the conventional shadcn/Next layout and is easy to navigate cold.
**Changes if:** the component count grows enough to warrant feature subfolders. Group then; don't pre-optimize.

### D-015 · LOCKED · Image strategy — locally-hosted, freely-licensed photos (bundled); alt text required
Imagery must be locally hosted under a free license (Public Domain / CC0 / CC BY / CC BY-SA / Unsplash License / Pexels License), with the asset files bundled in the repo under `public/images/`. No remote hotlinking and no all-rights-reserved third-party images. Attribution is recorded in `public/images/CREDITS.md` for every asset whose license requires it (CC BY / CC BY-SA, and any source that asks for credit). Every image requires descriptive alt text (D-007). Because deploy is static export with `images: { unoptimized: true }` (D-010), images render via `next/image` in unoptimized mode, with no remote loader and no on-the-fly optimization. The image-rendering mechanics (path convention, basePath handling, mandatory `onError` fallback) are specified in D-024.
**Why:** the spec wants large imagery but provides none. Hotlinking is a licensing and reliability risk, and remote loaders are incompatible with static export. Bundling freely-licensed local files gives durable, legally-clean, offline-capable imagery.
**History:** slice S15 stripped all imagery, deleting every `image:` URL from data and replacing the hero with CSS/SVG art, to satisfy the prior "no hotlinked images" policy. Slice S20 supersedes that sweep: real photos return as locally-hosted, freely-licensed files across all four image areas (recommendation cards, hero, photography guide, map/inspiration), governed by this entry and D-024. The previously-flagged hotlinks (`footprintadventure.com`, `images.pexels.com`, `klook.com`, `cloudfront.net`, `googleusercontent.com`) were already removed in S15 and do not return. Only bundled local files are permitted.
**Changes if:** the licensing policy changes, or we pick a host with image optimization (then `images.unoptimized` and the rendering contract are revisited).

### D-016 · Superseded by D-306, revived in part by D-313 · Countdown decomposition vs. total-days
**Superseded by D-306 (issue #11, 2026-08-11, owner-ruled), revived in part by D-313 (issue #60, 2026-08-13, owner-ruled).** D-306 reversed both halves below: a month became fixed-length instead of calendar-accurate, and the fields reconciled with `totalDays` by construction. D-313 reverses the first half back — months are calendar-accurate again — but NOT the second: `totalDays` and the breakdown still do not reconcile, exactly as this entry originally said, and this entry's non-reconciliation text is correct again, not merely "the reasoning D-306 had to answer." What stayed binding through both reversals: `computeCountdown` stays pure with no internal clock, and the D-006 target is untouched. D-313 is not a byte-for-byte return to this entry's original implementation — it carries a correction (the `differenceInMonths`/`addMonths` overshoot guard) that this entry's own original code never had and never needed to defend against, because that defect was found only while reviving it.

The hero countdown's display fields (`months`/`weeks`/`days`/`hours`/`minutes`/`seconds`) are a calendar-accurate decomposition of the time-to-target, computed in the pure `lib/countdown.ts` `computeCountdown(target, now)` via `date-fns`. `totalDays` is a separate flat whole-day count (`differenceInDays(target, now)`) shown as the "total days" headline. By design these do not arithmetically reconcile (`totalDays ≠ months*30 + weeks*7 + days`), because `weeks` is a sub-month residue. Do not "fix" the apparent mismatch.
**Why:** calendar months vary in length, and a flat `floor(days/30)` (the old approach) was inaccurate. `computeCountdown` is pure, with no internal clock, so it is deterministically testable.
**Changes if:** the design wants a single reconciled unit scheme, or a fixed display timezone (currently local-time per D-006).

### D-017 · Superseded · Dashboard metrics derive from data; planned-days shares the calendar's localStorage contract

### D-018 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Itinerary persistence contract — key presence, never length
Itinerary persistence is centralized in `lib/itinerary-storage.ts` (`ITINERARY_STORAGE_KEY = 'nepal_japan_itinerary'`, `loadPlans()`, `savePlans()`).
**`loadPlans()` distinguishes by the key, never by array length:** key absent (or parse fails, or the value is not an array) → seed `SAMPLE_ITINERARY`; key present → return the stored `DayPlan[]` verbatim, including `[]`. `savePlans()` always writes, including `[]`. The calendar uses a `hydrated` flag so the first-render `plans=[]` cannot clobber storage before load. Both `calendar-planner.tsx` and `trip-dashboard.tsx` must route through this module.
**Why:** the old `length > 0` load/save gates resurrected sample data over a deliberately-emptied itinerary and dropped empty saves, breaking the client-side hard guarantee (create/edit/delete survives reload; delete-all stays empty).
**Changes if:** never reintroduce a `length > 0` gate in the load/save path. A real "reset to sample" must be an explicit user action, not an implicit empty→sample fallback. **(Preserved by D-039, M9:** the Firebase remote layer wraps `loadPlans`/`savePlans` and does not alter this contract. A remote snapshot writes through `savePlans()` (including `[]`), and `loadPlans()` stays the read path and offline cache. No length gate is introduced anywhere in the remote path. The one new subtlety is the first-snapshot reconciliation for the empty-itinerary case, specified in D-039 so that an empty remote doc and "never synced" stay distinguishable.**)**

### D-019 · LOCKED · Nightlife is an intentional bonus section (10th) — do not remove
The Nightlife & Bars section (`components/nightlife-section.tsx`, data `lib/nightlife-data.ts`, 11 Kathmandu/Tokyo venues) is a deliberate bonus beyond the nine spec sections, and it is kept on purpose. It is cleanly integrated: wired in `app/page.tsx` (`dynamic`, `ssr:false`), SSR-safe (`mounted` guard), on-theme (fuchsia/sakura/himalaya glass cards), and it has a self-contained show/hide toggle persisted to its own localStorage key `nightlife_section_visible`. The `nightlife` value in `ItineraryCategory` (D-012) stays. It is deliberately absent from the main navbar, since it's a toggleable extra reachable by scroll.
**Why:** it's built, working, and it enriches the portfolio piece; the toggle lets it be collapsed. Rendering and toggle persistence were verified in-browser (S8).
**Changes if:** we decide to remove it (then the section, its data, and the `nightlife` category come out together), or to surface it in the navbar. Don't delete it later as non-spec scope creep. This entry is the record that it is deliberate.

### D-020 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Tailwind classes must be static string literals
Never build Tailwind class names by interpolation (`text-${color}-400`, `bg-${x}/10`, `border-${y}`). Tailwind's JIT only emits classes it can see as complete literal strings in source, so interpolated classes are purged and render with no color or background. Use a conditional whole-class literal (`key === 'nepal' ? 'text-himalaya-400' : 'text-sakura-400'`) or a static record keyed to the variant, as `CATEGORY_COLORS` and `CATEGORY_STYLES` do. Also avoid non-generated opacity steps like `bg-white/3`; stick to standard steps (`/5`, `/10`, …).
**Why:** dynamic class concatenation silently broke colors in `travel-inspiration.tsx` (weather icon, etiquette card bg), caught in S14. Grep new components for `(text|bg|border)-\$\{` when reviewing.
**Changes if:** never. This is a framework constraint.

### D-021 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Editor-modal focus management lives in the parent, not the modal's effect cleanup
The itinerary editor modal's focus-return is owned by `CalendarPlanner`, the parent. It captures the trigger into `triggerRef` (`document.activeElement`) in `handleAddItem`/`handleEditItem` before `setShowEditor(true)`, and refocuses it in `<AnimatePresence onExitComplete={...}>`, i.e. only after the exit animation finishes. Esc is handled by a `document`-level `keydown` listener (via an `onCloseRef`, so the once-bound listener calls the latest `onClose`) rather than React `onKeyDown` alone. The X button is `type="button" onClick={onClose}`. Focus-on-open is the Title input's `autoFocus` plus a guarded 50ms backstop.
**Why (a real bug this fixed):** capturing the previously-focused element in a child `useEffect` grabs the autoFocused input, because autoFocus runs at commit, before passive effects. Calling `.focus()` in the effect cleanup then runs *during* the framer-motion exit and interrupts it, leaving the dialog stuck mounted. That only happened when closing from inside the panel (X/Esc); an overlay click, where focus sits on body, closed fine. Browser-verified (headless Chromium): X, Esc, and overlay all close, and focus returns.
**Changes if:** never move focus-return back into the modal's effect cleanup, and don't capture the trigger after the modal/autoFocus has mounted. **(D-028 inherits this pattern: the new AddToItinerary dialog reuses the identical parent-owned focus-return and document-level Esc contract.)**

### D-022 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Grid/flex children that must shrink need `min-w-0`
CSS grid/flex items default to `min-width: auto` (= min-content), which refuses to shrink below their content's intrinsic width. For `grid-cols-7` `aspect-square` day cells, and children of `grid-cols-[Npx_1fr]` wrappers, that floor forced a constant ~463px block that overflowed the page horizontally at narrow viewports. Add `min-w-0` to such children so the tracks can shrink. This is the first thing to check for any page-level horizontal-overflow regression.
**Why:** the S17 overflow at 360–414px traced entirely to this (the calendar month grid), and 5 `min-w-0` additions fixed it (headless-Chrome verified: `scrollWidth − clientWidth = 0` at 360/390/414).
**Changes if:** never. This is a CSS layout invariant.

### D-023 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Deploy automation + the `.nojekyll` guarantee
GitHub Pages deploy is automated by `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`. On push to `main` it runs `npm ci --legacy-peer-deps` and `npm run build` (static export) in `nextjs_space/`, with `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH=/<repo>` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL=https://<owner>.github.io/<repo>` derived from `${{ github.* }}` so no repo name is hardcoded. It then runs `upload-pages-artifact` (`nextjs_space/out`) → `deploy-pages`. Pages must be set to Source: GitHub Actions. The `.nojekyll` that lets Pages serve `_next/` assets comes from the git-tracked `nextjs_space/public/.nojekyll`, copied verbatim by `output:'export'`; it is not generated in CI.
**Why:** project-page basePath (D-010), and Jekyll strips `_`-prefixed paths without `.nojekyll`.
**Changes if:** never delete `nextjs_space/public/.nojekyll`, or Pages will silently 404 on every `_next/` chunk. A custom domain or user-page deploy would set `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` empty. **(Extended by D-040, M9:** the deploy workflow also passes the public `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` values from GitHub repo secrets/vars, so the *deployed* build is sync-enabled. Absent those, the deploy is dormant-safe and behaves exactly as today.**)**

### D-024 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Image-rendering contract — path convention, basePath handling, mandatory fallback
The concrete mechanics for rendering the locally-bundled imagery introduced by D-015 (slice S20):

- **(a) Data shape.** Data records carry an optional `image?: string` whose value is a root-relative path of the form `"/images/<area>/<id>.<ext>"`, where `area` ∈ `nepal | japan | photography | featured | map` and `<id>` matches the record's `id`. The field stays optional (additive per D-012), so a record without a local asset is valid.
- **(b) Rendering + basePath.** Images render via `next/image` in unoptimized mode (D-010) with a root-relative `src` string. Under static export with `images:{unoptimized:true}` + `output:'export'`, `next/image` does not prepend `basePath`/`assetPrefix` to a *string* `src`: `unoptimized` disables the optimizer URL, which is the only place next/image would have applied the prefix. So a bare string like `"/images/nepal/foo.jpg"` renders verbatim as `<img src="/images/nepal/foo.jpg">` and 404s on the project page. (Static *imports* are basePath-aware, but this project sources image paths as strings from data, not imports.) Every `<Image src>` string is therefore wrapped in `withBasePath()` (from `nextjs_space/lib/utils.ts`), which prepends the `BASE_PATH` constant when set and is a no-op when it is empty, as in local dev. This is the single, consistent rule for every string asset path: `<Image>`, plain `<img>`, CSS `background-image`, and `metadata` (favicon / OG image) alike. A string asset path under static export is wrapped in `withBasePath()`, no exceptions, and the helper is the sole source of basePath. **Corrected in S20:** the original clause (b) assumed `next/image` auto-prefixes, and reserved `withBasePath()` for the non-`next/image` cases. A real-browser test (headless, `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH=/trip_planner` build) disproved this for the unoptimized static-export string-src case: every photo rendered with a bare `/images/...` src and 404'd to the `onError` fallback. Verified fix: wrap all `<Image src>` strings in `withBasePath()`.
- **(c) Mandatory fallback.** Every image keeps an `onError` fallback to the pre-existing design (gradient / emoji / icon) it replaces. This makes image coverage safe to be partial and preserves the zero-broken-images acceptance bar: a missing or failed asset degrades gracefully to the prior CSS/SVG art rather than showing a broken-image glyph.
- **(d) Asset location.** Files live under `public/images/{nepal,japan,photography,featured,map}/`; license attribution is recorded in `public/images/CREDITS.md` (D-015).

**Why:** static export with `unoptimized` does not auto-prefix string `src` on any render path. `next/image`, raw `<img>`, CSS, and `metadata` all need the prefix applied explicitly. Centralizing basePath in one `withBasePath()` helper and applying it to every string asset path is the single rule that prevents the missing-prefix 404, and since the helper is a no-op when basePath is empty, it never double-prefixes in local dev. The `onError` requirement keeps the "no broken images" guarantee true even while imagery is rolled in incrementally.
**Changes if:** the deploy host changes such that basePath/optimization assumptions move (see D-010), or the data model adds a richer image type (multiple sizes, blur placeholders, static imports, which would be basePath-aware). Extend additively and record it.

### D-025 · LOCKED · M7 date-model correction — Dec 9 is the SYR→KMT travel day, Dec 10 is Kathmandu arrival; anchors unchanged
Correction of 2026-06-20 to the trip's first two days. This entry annotates LOCKED D-005/D-006 rather than reopening them.

**What changes (sample-itinerary *content* only):**
- **Dec 9 (`2026-12-09`)** becomes a travel day: depart Syracuse (SYR). Its items describe the outbound journey (e.g. `transportation`: "Depart Syracuse (SYR)", connections, long-haul legs). It is no longer "Arrive at Tribhuvan."
- **Dec 10 (`2026-12-10`)** becomes the Kathmandu arrival day: land at Tribhuvan International (KTM), visa-on-arrival, hotel check-in, first evening in Thamel. The "Arrive at Tribhuvan" item currently mis-placed on Dec 9 (`sample-itinerary.ts:9`, id `n1-1`) moves to Dec 10.
- Nepal sightseeing now spans Dec 10–18 (was Dec 9–18).
- This is a `lib/sample-itinerary.ts` edit (M7 goal 1) executed together with the full day-by-day fill (M7 goal 2). It is data and content, not a code-contract change.

**What stays (don't touch):**
- **`TRIP_START = 2026-12-09`**: the countdown anchor is the departure, the moment the journey starts, per D-006. Unchanged.
- **`NEPAL_START = 2026-12-09` / `NEPAL_END = 2026-12-18`** and `JAPAN_START`/`JAPAN_END`: the country *date ranges* are unchanged. Dec 9 still belongs to the Nepal leg, since it is the travel day *to* Nepal, and `getCountryForDate('2026-12-09')` correctly returns `'nepal'`.
- **`getCountryForDate`**, **`TRIP_DATES`**, **`TRIP_DATE_LABEL`**: all derived from the unchanged anchors, with no signature or value changes.
- `lib/trip-data.ts` constants are not edited by goal 1. The correction lives entirely in `sample-itinerary.ts` content.

**Why:** the real journey starts with a Dec 9 departure from Syracuse and a Dec 10 landing in Kathmandu, and the prior sample data compressed both into Dec 9 ("Arrive at Tribhuvan" on the departure day). The countdown must still target the moment the journey begins, the Dec 9 departure, so the anchor is deliberately not moved to the Dec 10 arrival.
**Changes if:** we move the countdown to the arrival moment (then `TRIP_START` → Dec 10 and D-006 reopens), or the trip dates change.
**(Annotated by D-036:** the M8 booking reconciliation replaces the Dec 9 / Dec 10 / Dec 18 / Dec 19 transportation and hotel *items* with the real confirmed legs and the Tulsi hotel. The day-model invariant here is unchanged, Dec 9 = travel/depart-SYR and Dec 10 = KTM arrival; only the item *content* is corrected to match the real bookings, and the Dec 19 Tokyo-arrival day is corrected from Narita-evening to Haneda-afternoon. Anchors still untouched.**)**

### D-026 · LOCKED · Shared reactive itinerary store — Context provider + same-tab CustomEvent hook, wrapping the D-018 storage module
The itinerary is now genuinely cross-cutting (calendar, dashboard, and every place card read and write it), so it is promoted from component-local state to a single shared reactive source of truth. This closes D-017's same-tab deferral and stays within D-011: a React Context plus a custom-event-over-localStorage hook is the sanctioned mechanism, explicitly blessed by D-011's "lightweight context first" clause.

**Mechanism (both layers, by design):**
1. **`hooks/use-itinerary.ts`**: a hook owning the live `plans: DayPlan[]` state. On mount it calls `loadPlans()` (D-018) and sets a `hydrated`/`mounted` flag. This is SSR-safe, because `loadPlans` returns `SAMPLE_ITINERARY` under no-`window`, matching first paint. Every mutator calls `savePlans()` (D-018, which always writes and never length-gates) and dispatches a same-tab `CustomEvent` (`ITINERARY_CHANGED_EVENT = 'itinerary:changed'`) on `window`. The hook also listens for that CustomEvent and for the cross-tab `storage` event, re-reading on either, so all instances stay in sync within and across tabs.
2. **`components/itinerary-provider.tsx`**: a React Context that instantiates the state once at the app root (wrapping the page in `app/page.tsx` / layout) and exposes it via `useItineraryContext()`. Consumers subscribe to one shared instance rather than each holding an independent copy. (Rationale for having both: Context gives a single in-memory instance and clean subscription for the common case, while the CustomEvent is the belt-and-suspenders path that also catches any non-Context reader and cross-tab writes, and is what physically closes D-017.)

**API surface (the contract every consumer uses):**
```ts
// hooks/use-itinerary.ts — the shape exposed by useItinerary() / useItineraryContext()
interface ItineraryStore {
  plans: DayPlan[];                 // live, reactive
  hydrated: boolean;                // false during SSR/first paint; gate writes on this (D-018)
  addItem(date: string, item: ItineraryItem): void;        // upsert day, append item
  updateItem(date: string, itemId: string, patch: Partial<ItineraryItem>): void;
  removeItem(date: string, itemId: string): void;
  moveItem(itemId: string, fromDate: string, toDate: string): void;  // calendar drag-between-days
  reorderItems(date: string, orderedIds: string[]): void;  // calendar within-day reorder
  // selectors (pure, derived from plans):
  getDayPlan(date: string): DayPlan;                       // existing-or-synthesized empty day (city/country via getCountryForDate)
  findPlacements(sourceId: string): Array<{ date: string; item: ItineraryItem }>; // every day+item a given place is scheduled on
}
```
- `addItem`/`updateItem` upsert the `DayPlan` for `date` when absent, synthesizing `{ date, city: getCountryForDate(date)==='nepal'?'Kathmandu':'Tokyo', country: getCountryForDate(date), items: [] }`. This is the same synthesis `calendar-planner.tsx` already does in `getDayPlan`/`updateDayPlan`, lifted verbatim with no behavior change.
- `findPlacements(sourceId)` is the "already added" selector: it returns every `{date,item}` whose `item.sourceId === sourceId` (D-027). An empty array means not yet added. A place may be on multiple days.
- All mutators write through `savePlans` and emit the CustomEvent, and none reintroduces a length gate (D-018).

**Migration (consumers, behavior-preserving):**
- **`calendar-planner.tsx`** drops its local `useState<DayPlan[]>` and its load/save effects, and consumes the store instead. Its `getDayPlan`/`updateDayPlan`/`handleSaveItem`/`handleDeleteItem` and drag handlers re-express in terms of `addItem`/`updateItem`/`removeItem`/`moveItem`/`reorderItems`. The D-021 focus-management and D-022 `min-w-0` invariants are unchanged, since this is a state-source swap rather than a UI rewrite. Calendar CRUD and drag must behave exactly as before, so re-run the S18 persistence/CRUD checks.
- **`trip-dashboard.tsx`** reads `plans` from the store instead of calling `loadPlans()` on mount. Its `countPlannedDays` now recomputes reactively, so a same-tab calendar or card edit updates the "Planned Days" card live. That is the visible proof D-017 is closed. Keep the `mounted` guard so the SSR/first-paint count matches.

**Why:** three or more distant consumers now mutate one dataset, and without a shared reactive source they desync. That is the exact D-017 bug, now multiplied across cards. Context plus CustomEvent is the smallest mechanism that satisfies D-011 and gives same-tab liveness, with zero new dependencies.
**Changes if:** cross-cutting state grows well beyond the itinerary, into multiple unrelated shared datasets. Only then revisit a library, per D-011. **(Extended by D-039, M9:** the Firebase remote layer plugs into this store's two seams. It feeds remote snapshots in via `savePlans()` plus the `itinerary:changed` CustomEvent, the existing re-read path, and pushes local mutations out from the `commit()` choke-point after the local `savePlans()`. The `ItineraryStore` public API and the Context provider are unchanged, and consumers (calendar, dashboard, cards) need no edits. D-026 is preserved, not replaced.**)**

### D-027 · LOCKED · Place → ItineraryItem adapter + additive source-linkage fields + category normalization
To detect "already added" and to prefill the add dialog, every place card converts its source record into a candidate `ItineraryItem` through one shared adapter, and items created that way carry a stable back-link to their source.

**(a) Additive `ItineraryItem` fields (per D-012, additive only, nothing existing touched):**
```ts
// added to ItineraryItem in lib/trip-data.ts:
sourceId?: string;     // the source record's id (e.g. 'na1', 'ps4', 'np-boudhanath') or a derived id for Featured
sourceType?: 'recommendation' | 'photo' | 'map' | 'featured';
```
Both are optional, so existing sample and calendar items (which have no `sourceId`) remain valid; only card-created items set them. "Already added" means any plan item whose `sourceId` equals the card's source id, matched via `findPlacements` (D-026). A place may be added to multiple days.

**(b) The adapter — `lib/itinerary-adapter.ts`:** one entry per source type, each returning a partial candidate `ItineraryItem`, since the user still picks date and time in the dialog (D-028):
```ts
toItineraryDraft(source, sourceType): {
  title;      // source.name (recommendation/photo/map) or FeaturedDestination.name
  location?;  // Recommendation.location ?? `${PhotoSpot.city}` ?? MapMarker.area ?? undefined
  notes?;     // Recommendation.notes ?? PhotoSpot.tip ?? MapMarker.description ?? FeaturedDestination.blurb
  category;   // normalized ItineraryCategory (see (c))
  duration?;  // Recommendation.duration ?? undefined (others have none)
  time?;      // PhotoSpot bestTime hint is free-text → leave time undefined; user sets it
  sourceId;   // source.id  (Featured has no id → derive: `featured-${slug(name)}`, see (d))
  sourceType;
}
```

**(c) Source-category → `ItineraryCategory` normalization (the load-bearing mapping).** Source `category` strings are free-form and must collapse to the canonical union. Normalize case-insensitively, and default to `sightseeing` for any unmapped value. The mapping table covers every value present across `NEPAL_CATEGORIES`, `JAPAN_CATEGORIES`, `PHOTO_CATEGORIES`, and `MARKER_CATEGORIES`:

| Source category (any source) | → ItineraryCategory |
|---|---|
| Food, Café, Restaurant, Ramen, Sushi, Street Food, Dessert | `food` |
| Temple, Cultural, Must-See, Must-Visit | `cultural` |
| Photography, Photo Spot, Sunrise, Sunset, Night, Architecture, Instagram, Street | `photography` |
| Nature, Scenic, Winter | `nature` |
| Shopping | `shopping` |
| Hotel | `hotel` |
| Attraction, Hidden Gem, Day Trip, Experience, Anime, Must-Visit*, (anything else) | `sightseeing` (default) |

(*"Must-Visit"/"Must-See" are mapped to `cultural` above, as they are predominantly temples and heritage sites; the table's last row is the catch-all default.) The mapping lives as a single `Record<string,ItineraryCategory>` with lowercased keys in `lib/itinerary-adapter.ts`. `transportation`, `free`, and `nightlife` are never produced by the adapter, since no source vocabulary maps to them, but they remain user-selectable in the dialog. This mapping is data, not behavior: extending it for a new source category is an additive edit recorded here.

**(d) Featured destinations have no `id` and no `category`.** Derive a stable `sourceId = 'featured-' + name.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g,'-')` (e.g. `featured-boudhanath-stupa`), and set `category = 'sightseeing'`, since there is no source category to map. `location`, `duration`, and `time` are undefined, and `notes = blurb`. This keeps Featured a first-class card type without touching its data shape.

**Why:** a single adapter plus one mapping table means "what category and notes does this place get" is decided in exactly one place, the four card families stay consistent, and "already added" is a pure `sourceId` equality with no fuzzy matching. Additive fields keep every existing item and the D-018 contract intact.
**Changes if:** a new source family or category string appears (extend the adapter switch and mapping table here), or we want per-source default times and durations.

### D-028 · LOCKED · Add-to-plan UX — a new shared `AddToItinerary` dialog (not the calendar editor), reused by all card families
Clicking a card's add action opens a new, lightweight shared dialog, `components/add-to-itinerary-dialog.tsx`, and not the calendar's `ItemEditor`. The two are deliberately separate. `ItemEditor` is a full free-form item editor owned by `CalendarPlanner`'s internal state; the card flow needs a *prefilled, source-linked* mini-form invokable from anywhere on the single page (D-013).

**Component contract:**
```ts
// components/add-to-itinerary-dialog.tsx
interface AddToItineraryDialogProps {
  open: boolean;
  draft: ItineraryDraft;          // from toItineraryDraft() (D-027): title/location/notes/category/sourceId/sourceType prefilled
  existingPlacements: Array<{ date: string; item: ItineraryItem }>;  // from findPlacements(sourceId) — drives "modify/remove" mode
  onClose(): void;
}
```
- The dialog reads and writes the itinerary through the store (`useItineraryContext`, D-026). It does not receive add/remove callbacks per call site: the store is the single write path.
- **Fields:** **date** (a select constrained to `TRIP_DATES`, each option labelled with `formatDate(date)` plus the city/country from `getCountryForDate` so the user sees "Dec 12 · Kathmandu, Nepal"), **time** (free text, same as `ItemEditor`), **category** (prefilled from the draft, editable via the same category-button grid pattern), optional **duration** / **notes** (prefilled). Title and location come from the draft, shown read-only or lightly editable at the implementer's choice, defaulting to a read-only title.
- **On confirm:** generate a fresh item id (D-029), call `store.addItem(selectedDate, { ...draft, id, time, category, duration, notes })`. The CustomEvent fan-out (D-026) makes the calendar, dashboard and card reflect it immediately.
- **A11y / focus (D-007, inherits D-021):** the dialog reuses the identical modal contract as `ItemEditor`: `role="dialog" aria-modal`, document-level Esc listener via `onCloseRef`, Tab-trap, autofocus the first field, and parent-owned focus-return (the invoking card captures `document.activeElement` before opening and refocuses it on `AnimatePresence onExitComplete`). Don't put focus-return in the dialog's effect cleanup; that was the D-021 bug. Reduced-motion respected (D-007). Tailwind classes stay static literals (D-020).

**Invocation from any card (the shared trigger):** a small presentational control, `components/add-to-plan-button.tsx` or an inline button within each card, takes the card's source record plus `sourceType`, builds the draft via the adapter (D-027), queries `findPlacements(sourceId)` for placements, owns the `open` state and `triggerRef`, and renders one `AddToItineraryDialog`. Card families adopt it by dropping this control into their markup (additive: see the D-029 affordance). Because the page is single-route (D-013) and the store is app-root Context (D-026), one dialog instance per card is fine. Only one is open at a time in practice, and each manages its own open state.

**Why:** reusing `ItemEditor` would force the calendar's internal state and selected-date coupling onto every card, and bloat that component. A purpose-built source-aware dialog with a date picker is the honest shape for "add this place to *some* day"; the calendar editor has no date picker, because it edits within an already-selected day. Sharing the proven D-021 focus contract avoids re-deriving modal a11y.
**Changes if:** the dialog and `ItemEditor` converge enough to share a single primitive (then extract a common `<Modal>` shell and record it), or we want add-to-plan inline with no dialog.

### D-029 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Card "already-added" affordance + runtime id strategy + cross-surface integrity
**(a) Card affordance (additive to every card family).** Each card gains, without disturbing its existing markup, a single shared control (`add-to-plan-button.tsx`, D-028) that is state-aware via `findPlacements(sourceId)`:
- **Not added** (`findPlacements` empty): show an "Add to plan" affordance, e.g. a `Plus` button or pill. Click opens `AddToItineraryDialog` in add mode.
- **Added** (≥1 placement): show an "added" badge plus a compact where-it's-planned summary ("On Dec 12", "On 2 days"), with modify (reopen the dialog to change date, time or category of an existing placement) and remove (call `store.removeItem(date, itemId)` for the placement) actions, all routed through the store (D-026). When added to multiple days, the summary lists or *counts* them and remove targets the specific placement's `{date,itemId}`.
- It has to be additive: the shared `RecommendationCard` (Nepal/Japan), the `PhotoSpot` cards (`components/photography-section.tsx`), the map popup (`components/map-section.tsx` `selected` panel), and the Featured cards (`components/travel-inspiration.tsx`) each render this one control. No source data shape changes; the control derives everything from the record, the adapter and the store. Honor D-024 (cards already wrap images in `withBasePath`) and D-020 (static classes).

**(b) Runtime id strategy for card-created items.** Card-created items get a fresh unique id at creation, independent of `sourceId`. Use `crypto.randomUUID()` when available, else the existing `calendar-planner` pattern `'item_' + Date.now().toString(36) + '_' + Math.random().toString(36).slice(2,7)`. Centralize as `generateItemId()`, lifting `calendar-planner.tsx`'s `generateId` into a shared `lib/` util so calendar and dialog share one generator. `id` is the per-placement identity; `sourceId` is the shared back-link. Adding the same place to two days creates two items with different `id`s and the same `sourceId`. Both surface in `findPlacements`, and each is independently editable and removable.

**(c) Cross-surface integrity.** The calendar and the card affordance are two views of one store (D-026). Therefore:
- Removing a placement from the card (`removeItem(date,itemId)`) immediately empties it from the calendar day (same store, same CustomEvent), and the card flips back to "Add to plan" once `findPlacements` is empty.
- Deleting the item in the calendar (existing delete) immediately flips the card back to "Add", because the card reads `findPlacements` reactively.
- Editing a card-created item's date in the calendar (drag-between-days / `moveItem`) keeps its `sourceId`, so the card's "where it's planned" summary follows it. There is no second source of truth and no id collision: `sourceId` matches places, `id` matches placements.

**Why:** one state-aware control across four card families keeps the added/where/modify/remove UX identical everywhere and impossible to desync (single store, `sourceId`/`id` separation). Fresh per-placement ids make multi-day adds and independent removal correct, and the shared generator avoids two id schemes.
**Changes if:** a card family needs a bespoke affordance (record the divergence), or `crypto.randomUUID` must be polyfilled for a target browser (it is available in all evergreen browsers, and the fallback is already specified).

### D-030 · LOCKED (implementation note) · `DayPlan.city` is the day's *base* city; day-trips are same-day round trips
In `lib/sample-itinerary.ts`, a `DayPlan.city` is the base city the traveler is lodged in for that day. A day-trip is modeled as a same-day round trip: an outbound `transportation` item and a return `transportation` item, both on that day, with the base `city` left as the lodging city (or set to the trip's destination town if they relocate). Consequently, a `city` differing between two consecutive days does not always require a `transportation` item on the *later* day: the return leg lives on the *earlier* (day-trip) day. Established by S23's Japan itinerary, where the Kawaguchiko, Nikko and Yokohama day-trips return to Tokyo within their own day, so the following Tokyo day correctly has no transport leg.
**Why:** itinerary realism. Travelers sleep in a base city and take round-trip excursions. A naive "city changed between days ⇒ must have a transport item" lint false-positives on this pattern, so read a city change against the *prior* day before flagging a missing transport leg.
**Changes if:** the data model adds an explicit lodging/base-city field separate from `city`, which would let day-trips set `city` to the excursion town without ambiguity. Record it then.

### D-031 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Itinerary-store mutators derive next state from a fresh `loadPlans()`, never the React `plans` closure
In `hooks/use-itinerary.ts` (D-026), every mutator (`addItem`/`updateItem`/`removeItem`/`moveItem`/`reorderItems`) computes its next state from a fresh `loadPlans()` read of the freshest persisted truth, then `savePlans(next)` + `setPlans(next)` + dispatches `itinerary:changed`. It must not derive next state from the React `plans` state closure.
**Why (a real bug this avoids):** `savePlans` is synchronous, but React batches `setPlans` within one event handler. The calendar's cross-day drag calls two mutators back-to-back in a single handler (`moveItem` then `reorderItems`). Reading from the `plans` closure would give the second mutator the stale pre-move snapshot, so its `id`-filter would silently drop the just-moved item: a lost update. Reading `loadPlans()` makes the second op see the first's already-persisted write, so chained mutations compose correctly. There is no divergence risk, because `setPlans(next)` and `savePlans(next)` always use the same object, so reactive state and storage never disagree; same-tab re-fires via the CustomEvent. Confirmed sound at S24 by a code trace and a data-integrity run: a cross-day move leaves the source, lands in the target, with no duplication or loss.
**Changes if:** never refactor `commit` to read the `plans` closure. (If the store ever moves to a reducer with synchronous queued dispatch that guarantees sequential application, revisit. The fresh-read contract is the simplest correct rule for chained mutations over a synchronous localStorage backing.) **(Reused by D-039, M9:** the remote push reads the same `commit()` choke-point. Because `commit` already computes `next` from a fresh `loadPlans()`, the remote diff (prev→next) is computed against the freshest persisted truth, so chained mutators (move+reorder) each push their own correct delta. The fresh-read rule is what makes the per-day diff push correct under chaining.**)**

### D-032 · Superseded by D-119 · Add-to-plan "modify-date" = remove-old + add-new-id (cross-day); update-in-place (same-day)

### D-033 · LOCKED (process note) · The de-AI mirror sync runs a mandatory governance-marker scan as a pre-push gate
When syncing source from this private repo into the public de-AI'd mirror (`Powan55/trip_planner`, app under `trip/`, pushed from the local mirror checkout), the sync has two obligations and a hard gate:
1. **Comment-scrub:** copy each changed or new file verbatim, then strip every governance marker from comments only, leaving code byte-identical: decision IDs `D-0\d\d`, build-slice refs `S\d{1,2}`, internal role and process words, and the internal planning-doc filenames and status markers (LOCKED and the like).
2. **Pre-push adversarial scan (the gate):** before committing or pushing, run a governance-marker `git grep` over all tracked mirror source (`*.ts *.tsx *.js *.json *.md`, excluding `node_modules`/`out`/`.next`/`package-lock.json`). A single hit is a no-push. Filter known-benign false positives by context: `Array.slice(`, SVG `xMidYMax slice`, the English "No length gate", travel content like "Thunder Gate" and "torii gate".
3. Commit as the human author (the identity already set in the mirror's git config) with a plain, feature-describing message. That is the mirror voice: no slice IDs, no internal process language.
**Why (a real leak this caught):** `public/images/CREDITS.md` is auto-generated attribution content. Its header re-introduced a `**D-015**` decision reference, plus a dangling `scripts/fetch-images.mjs` internal-tooling pointer, that survived prior syncs and sat in the public repo from the S20 photo sync until the M7 sync's adversarial scan flagged it. Scrubbing only the *changed code files* is insufficient: the scan must cover the whole tracked tree, including generated and content files that are not in the current delta. `public/images/CREDITS.md`s generator and template must never emit decision IDs or internal tooling paths.
**Changes if:** the mirror adds a CI-side secret- or marker-scan (then this becomes a belt-and-suspenders local check), or the public repo's identity or host changes. **(Extended by D-046, M9:** the M9 sync adds Firebase-specific scrub and scan obligations. The real `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` config values must never be committed to the mirror in source (they belong in env/secrets), `firestore.rules` is copied verbatim (it's a legitimate public artifact), and the scan vocabulary stays unchanged. See D-046.**)**

### D-034 · LOCKED · Real bookings live in a new `lib/booking-data.ts` module: flights/stays are presentation data, separate from the itinerary store
The confirmed flight and hotel bookings (M8) are modeled as their own static data module, `lib/booking-data.ts`. They are not folded into `ItineraryItem`/`DayPlan` and not persisted to localStorage. The booking data is read-only reference content: it describes fixed facts about the trip (flight numbers, terminals, seats, the hotel) and is rendered by a presentational section. It is categorically different from the user-editable, persisted itinerary (D-018/D-026). The two are linked only by *content agreement* (the itinerary's Dec 9/10/18/19 transport and hotel items, D-036, narrate the same journey), never by a shared type or a shared store.

**The exact shapes** (additive by construction: a new file touches no existing interface, satisfying D-012):
```ts
// lib/booking-data.ts
export type BookingStatus = 'booked' | 'to-book';
export type CabinClass = 'Economy' | 'Premium Economy' | 'Business' | 'First';

export interface FlightLeg {
  id: string;                 // stable, e.g. 'out-1', 'ret-2'
  flightNumber: string;       // 'Meridian Air 4471', 'Skyline Continental 512', 'Pacific Crown Air 8823'
  fromCode: string;           // 'SYR'
  fromName: string;           // 'Syracuse Hancock Intl'
  fromTerminal?: string;      // 'Terminal 4' (omit when not given)
  toCode: string;             // 'JFK'
  toName: string;             // 'New York JFK'
  toTerminal?: string;        // 'Terminal 4'
  departLabel: string;        // human label, exactly as the booking reads: '5:30am Wed Dec 9'
  arriveLabel: string;        // '7:02am Wed Dec 9'
  duration: string;           // '1h 32m'
  seats?: string[];           // ['11A','11B','11C']; omit on legs with no seats given
  cabin: CabinClass;          // 'Economy'
  cabinCode?: string;         // 'V','W','L' (fare/booking class letter from the booking)
}

export interface Layover {
  airportCode: string;        // 'JFK'
  airportName?: string;       // 'New York JFK'
  duration: string;           // '4h 53m'
}

export interface Journey {
  id: string;                 // 'outbound' | 'return-to-japan'
  label: string;              // 'Outbound — Syracuse to Kathmandu'
  status: BookingStatus;      // 'booked'
  fromSummary: string;        // 'Syracuse (SYR)'
  toSummary: string;          // 'Kathmandu (KTM)'
  totalDuration: string;      // '1d 0h 15m'  (render verbatim; do NOT recompute)
  legs: FlightLeg[];          // ordered
  layovers: Layover[];        // ordered, length === legs.length - 1; positionally between legs[i] and legs[i+1]
}

export interface Stay {
  id: string;                 // 'nepal-hotel'
  name: string;               // 'Tulsi Kathmandu Hotel'
  stars: number | null;       // 3.0  (null only if genuinely unrated; here 3)
  address?: string;           // full street address when known
  area?: string;              // 'Keshar Mahal Marga (beside Garden of Dreams / Thamel)'
  city: string;               // 'Kathmandu'
  country: 'nepal' | 'japan'; // lowercase, matching DayPlan.country (D-012)
  status: BookingStatus;      // 'booked'
  checkIn?: string;           // optional human label; omit if not a fixed booking fact
  checkOut?: string;
}

export interface ToBookPlaceholder {
  id: string;                 // 'japan-hotels', 'flight-home'
  kind: 'stay' | 'flight';
  label: string;              // 'Japan accommodation', 'Flight home'
  note: string;               // 'Not booked yet — dates Dec 19 – Jan 9'
}
```
The module exports concrete consts: `OUTBOUND_JOURNEY: Journey`, `RETURN_TO_JAPAN_JOURNEY: Journey`, `NEPAL_STAY: Stay`, and `JAPAN_TODO: ToBookPlaceholder[]` (the Japan hotels plus the Jan 9 flight home). Exact seed values are specified in the M8 build brief, transcribed verbatim from the bookings.

**Hard rules:**
- `totalDuration`, `departLabel`, `arriveLabel` and `duration` are rendered verbatim strings. The section does not parse, re-derive, or timezone-convert them. The booking is the source of truth for its own arithmetic: the outbound "1d 0h 15m" spans timezones and must not be "corrected." This keeps the section a pure presenter and immune to date-math and timezone bugs.
- No `Date` objects, no localStorage, no store in this module or its section. It is static, SSR-safe reference data. (The section still mounts under `ssr:false` per D-013 for consistency, but has no hydration concern.)
- `status: 'to-book'` (and `ToBookPlaceholder`) is the only sanctioned way to represent unbooked Japan hotels and the flight home. They are never faked with invented flight numbers or hotels; that is an explicit constraint. The presenter renders them in a visually-distinct "to be booked" treatment.
- Static-export-safe (D-010), additive (D-012), no new runtime dependency (D-011).

**Why:** flights and hotels are *fixed reference facts*, while the itinerary is a *user-editable persisted plan*. Conflating them would either pollute the localStorage shape with non-editable booking fields or force booking display through the CRUD path. A separate read-only module is the honest boundary: the itinerary store (D-026) stays exactly as-is, the booking section is a pure function of static data, and the only coupling is human-authored content agreement (D-036). Verbatim labels keep the presenter free of the timezone and date-math traps that a `Date`-based model would invite across a 14h55m trans-Pacific leg.
**Changes if:** we want the booking section to *drive* itinerary items (auto-inserting the legs into the calendar), which would add an adapter from `Journey`→`ItineraryItem` (like D-027) and is recorded then. Or the data must support live flight-status, at which point it stops being static and becomes a backend concern (D-004).

### D-035 · LOCKED · A dedicated "Flights & Stays" section (`components/flights-section.tsx`), placed after Calendar / before Nepal, anchor `#flights`
The bookings render as a dedicated section, not merely as enriched itinerary items. Component `components/flights-section.tsx`, loaded in `app/page.tsx` via `dynamic(() => import(...), { ssr: false })` (D-013), inserted between `<CalendarPlanner />` and `<NepalSection />`, because trip logistics sit with the planner, before the destination guides. The section wrapper carries `id="flights"`. A new navbar entry `{ label: 'Flights', href: '#flights', icon: Plane }` is added to `NAV_ITEMS` in `components/navbar.tsx`, placed after `Itinerary` and before `Nepal` to mirror the page order. (The `Plane` icon is already imported in `navbar.tsx`.)

**What the section renders:**
- Two `Journey` cards (Outbound, Return-to-Japan), each showing the journey label, a `status` chip, `fromSummary → toSummary`, `totalDuration`, then the ordered legs. Per leg: `flightNumber`, `fromCode`/`fromName`(+terminal) → `toCode`/`toName`(+terminal), `departLabel` → `arriveLabel`, `duration`, `seats` (when present), `cabin`(+`cabinCode`). Layovers render between legs as a visually-distinct connector row, "Layover 4h 53m · JFK". Seats absent ⇒ omit the seats line, rather than rendering "Seats: undefined"; the return journey legitimately has none.
- The Nepal `Stay` card (Tulsi Kathmandu Hotel): name, star rating, area and address, "Booked" status chip.
- The "Japan — to be booked" block rendering `JAPAN_TODO`, each `ToBookPlaceholder` in a clearly-distinct, dashed and muted "not yet booked" treatment, not styled like a confirmed booking. This is the honest unbooked state (D-034); it must read as deliberately pending, not broken or empty.

**Constraints:** the section is a pure presenter of `lib/booking-data.ts`, with no localStorage, no store and no `Date` math (D-034). It must satisfy D-007 (semantic headings, keyboard-reachable, sufficient contrast, reduced-motion), D-020 (Tailwind classes are static literals, so status and cabin styling go via a static record or whole-class conditionals, never interpolated), and the project's glassmorphism direction (himalaya/sakura/gold glass cards consistent with neighboring sections). No imagery is required, since airline and airport logos would reintroduce licensing risk under D-015; use lucide `Plane`/`Hotel`/`Building2` icons instead. If any decorative image is ever added it follows D-024.

**Why a dedicated section (the verdict):** this had to be settled outright rather than left open. The bookings carry rich structured detail — terminals, seat assignments, fare classes, layover durations, totals — that the `ItineraryItem` shape (title/time/duration/notes/location) cannot represent without cramming it all into a `notes` string, which would be unsearchable, unstyled and lossy. A dedicated section is the only place this detail renders as first-class, premium content, and the project mandate is a "premium travel-app feel," not a minimal demo. The itinerary still narrates the journey at a day level (D-036) for calendar, timeline and dashboard agreement, but the authoritative, detailed view lives in the Flights & Stays section. This is additive (one new component, one nav entry, one `page.tsx` line) and regression-light.
**Changes if:** we decide the bookings should be itinerary-only (drop the section, fold a summary into the Dec 9/10/18/19 notes). That loses the structured detail and the premium presentation, so it is not recommended.

### D-036 · Superseded · Itinerary reconciliation — Dec 9/10/18/19 transport+hotel items rewritten to the real bookings; Tulsi hotel sweep; Jan 9 flight-home stays a marked placeholder

### D-037 · Superseded · Local static-export builds may fail the next/font/google fetch behind a TLS-intercepting proxy: environmental, not a code defect

## M9 — Cross-friend itinerary sync (Firebase Firestore). Greenlit 2026-06-27.

> M9 reopens D-002/D-004 scoped to the itinerary only (see the annotations on those entries) and builds the cross-device sync that M8 researched look-only (`docs/cross-friend-sync-research.md`). The overriding mandate is dormant-safe (D-038): with no Firebase configured, the app builds and runs exactly as it does today (localStorage-only, D-018/D-026 intact). The research did the option analysis and chose Cloud Firestore (Spark free tier) plus silent Anonymous Auth, called directly from the static client. D-038–D-046 turn that into a buildable blueprint.

### D-038 · LOCKED · The dormant-safe gate: the entire remote layer is off unless Firebase web-config env is present; absent config ⇒ today's app, byte-for-byte behavior
The non-negotiable design invariant for all of M9. The remote (Firebase) layer is inert by default and activates only when the public Firebase web-config values are present at build time. With them absent, which is the state of the repo until the project is provisioned and the state of any fork or clone, the app is localStorage-only and behaves exactly as it does today: D-018 persistence, D-026 store, D-010 static export and the GitHub Pages deploy all unchanged. A misconfigured or unreachable Firebase must degrade to local-only, never hard-break (D-039).

**The single `isRemoteConfigured()` gate** (mirrors the D-024 `BASE_PATH` single-source pattern):
- A new single-source module, `lib/firebase-config.ts`, reads the public web config from `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` env (D-040) and exports a pure boolean `isRemoteConfigured(): boolean`, true iff the minimal required keys are all non-empty (at minimum `apiKey`, `projectId`, `appId`; absence of any ⇒ false). This is the one place the on/off decision is made. Every other module (`itinerary-remote.ts`, the provider wiring, the identity UI) asks this gate and no-ops when it is false. No `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` reads are scattered elsewhere.
- **Lazy SDK init:** the `firebase` SDK is only imported and initialized when `isRemoteConfigured()` is true. Use a dynamic `import()`, or an init function called behind the gate, so that when dormant no Firebase code path executes and tree-shaking or lazy-loading keeps it off the hot path. Static export (D-010) is unaffected: this is all client runtime, no SSR and no route handler.
- **Buildability:** the project builds green with the env absent (the gate is false, the SDK never inits). It also builds green with the env present, which is the deployed, sync-enabled artifact. Both are first-class.

**Why:** this is what lets the whole feature be built, reviewed, validated and committed before the Firebase project exists. The local-only path is fully testable now, and the live path is testable the moment the config is pasted in. It also protects the portfolio deploy (a fork with no config just works) and means a free-tier pause or limit later (D-045 risk) degrades the app to local-only instead of breaking it. Dormant-safe is the property that de-risks the entire milestone.
**Changes if:** never weaken the gate to "remote on by default." If we ever want remote *required*, with no local-only fallback, that is a deliberate reopening recorded then. For a portfolio piece on a free tier, dormant-safe stays. **(Scoped refinement by D-054, M10:** the dormant public build gains a new client-side landing (`components/token-gate.tsx`) plus a "Continue as guest" bypass. This is a deliberate *product* evolution of the "byte-for-byte today's app" wording: the dormant build is no longer literally byte-for-byte identical to the pre-M10 app, because it now shows the landing. But the core invariant D-038 exists to protect is fully preserved, because the landing imports no firebase, so the dormant bundle still contains no firebase code path (D-047 unaffected). The refinement is to the *cosmetic* "byte-for-byte" phrasing, not to the no-firebase-in-dormant-bundle guarantee. See D-054.**)**

### D-039 · LOCKED · The seam: `lib/itinerary-remote.ts` is the only new architectural surface; it wraps the D-018/D-026 store, never replaces it
Per research section 4.5, the existing store already has the exact seam a remote store needs: one write choke-point (`commit()`), one re-read path on the `itinerary:changed` CustomEvent, and persistence isolated behind `loadPlans`/`savePlans`. The remote layer slots in there with zero component changes and the `ItineraryStore` public API (D-026) unchanged.

**The module `lib/itinerary-remote.ts` (the single new surface):**
- **Subscribe (remote → local):** `subscribeRemote(onRemoteChange)` opens one Firestore `onSnapshot` listener on the trip's day-doc collection (D-042). On any remote change it maps the docs back to `DayPlan[]`, writes them through the existing `savePlans()` (D-018, including `[]`), and dispatches the existing `itinerary:changed` CustomEvent. Because that event is *already* what `use-itinerary.ts`'s `reread()` listens for, the entire reactive UI (calendar, dashboard, timeline, every card's `findPlacements`) updates with no component edits. Returns an unsubscribe fn.
- **Push (local → remote):** `pushPlans(prev, next)`, or `pushDayChange(date, dayPlan|null)`, is called from the store's `commit()` after the local `savePlans(next)`, so the offline cache and the instant same-tab echo are preserved. It diffs `prev`→`next` per day and writes only the changed day-docs to Firestore (per-day granularity, D-042/D-043 LWW), stamping `updatedBy` and `updatedAt` (D-041). A day that became empty writes `items: []` to its doc (D-018 parity); a day removed entirely deletes or empties its doc.
- **Identity:** `pushPlans` reads the current display name and `uid` from the identity module (D-043) to stamp writes.

**Provider wiring (`components/itinerary-provider.tsx`):** the provider already instantiates the store once at app root, so it is the natural home. It calls `subscribeRemote(...)` once on mount, inside an effect, only when `isRemoteConfigured()` is true (D-038), and unsubscribes on unmount. The store's `commit()` calls `pushPlans(...)` only when `isRemoteConfigured()`, and is a no-op import otherwise. There is no change to `useItineraryContext()`'s shape and no consumer edits.

**Where `commit()` changes (`hooks/use-itinerary.ts`):** the only edit to the store is that `commit()` captures `prev = loadPlans()` before computing `next` (it already reads `loadPlans()` per D-031, so `prev` is free), then after `savePlans(next)` + `setPlans(next)` + dispatch, it calls `pushPlans(prev, next)` behind the `isRemoteConfigured()` gate. Dormant, this line is a no-op and the store is byte-for-byte today's store.

**Echo-suppression (critical correctness point):** a remote snapshot calls `savePlans()` and dispatches `itinerary:changed`, which triggers `reread()`. That must not re-trigger a `pushPlans` back to Firestore, or we get an echo loop. The rule: `pushPlans` is called only from `commit()`, meaning genuine local mutations via the store mutators, and never from the snapshot-ingest path. The snapshot path calls `savePlans()` and dispatches the event directly, so it does not go through `commit()` and never pushes. This cleanly breaks the loop without needing origin tags. (Firestore's own local-write echo is separately handled by snapshot metadata if needed: see D-042.)

**First-snapshot reconciliation (preserves D-018's empty-vs-absent distinction):** on first subscribe, the local cache may hold real edits (offline-first) while the remote doc is empty or absent, or vice-versa. The rule for the first cut (LWW, D-043): the remote trip doc is the shared source of truth once it exists, so the first snapshot overwrites the local cache with remote state via `savePlans()`. Seeding works like this. If the remote collection is absent or empty and the local cache is the untouched `SAMPLE_ITINERARY` seed, the client may seed the remote once, writing the sample days so the group starts from the sample. If the remote is empty but the local cache holds user edits, prefer pushing the local edits up rather than silently wiping a user's offline work. The precise seeding handshake is a B-2 implementation detail to verify during validation; the invariant is to never silently destroy user data and never resurrect the sample over a deliberately-emptied shared plan (D-018's spirit, now across devices).

**Why:** the research's key insight is that the hard architectural work is already done (one store, one event), so remote sync is "fan the existing event out over the network," not a re-plumb. Confining all remote logic to one module, one gated line in `commit()` and one gated effect in the provider keeps the blast radius tiny, keeps D-026/D-018 intact, and makes the dormant path provably identical to today.
**Changes if:** the store moves off the synchronous-localStorage + CustomEvent model (D-026/D-031), or per-item rather than per-day write granularity proves necessary, in which case D-042's model and `pushDayChange` granularity are revisited. **(Scoped refinement by D-055, M10:** the gated subscribe now additionally requires an identified traveler (`isRemoteConfigured() && getActiveTraveler()`), so guests never open the Firestore listener. The seam itself (one module, one gated effect, the `commit()` push) is unchanged; only the *gate condition* on the subscribe tightens. See D-055.**)**

### D-040 · LOCKED · Firebase web config flows through one single-source module from `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` env (public-by-design; mirrors the BASE_PATH pattern)
The public Firebase web config (`apiKey`, `authDomain`, `projectId`, `storageBucket`, `messagingSenderId`, `appId`) is read from `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` env vars in exactly one module, `lib/firebase-config.ts`, mirroring how `BASE_PATH` is the sole reader of `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` (D-024). No other file reads `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*`.

- **Env var names:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_API_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_STORAGE_BUCKET`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_MESSAGING_SENDER_ID`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_APP_ID`. (Plus the gate `isRemoteConfigured()` of D-038.)
- **Public by design (the load-bearing security fact):** these values are not secrets. They only *name* the Firebase project and are visible in any client bundle by design (research sections 2.1 and 5). The real authorization gate is the server-side Firestore Security Rules (D-044), evaluated by Google, paired with Anonymous Auth (D-043). Shipping them in the static bundle is therefore correct, not a leak. By contrast, a bearer token or a repo PAT in client JS *would* be a leak, and those options were disqualified in the research.
- **Local dev:** an untracked `.env.local` (already git-ignored by Next convention) holds the values for a developer who wants to test sync locally. Absent it, `isRemoteConfigured()` is false and the app is dormant (D-038). A committed `.env.local.example` documents the six names with empty values, and no real values are committed.
- **Deploy (extends D-023):** the GitHub Actions workflow passes the six `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` values from GitHub repo secrets and variables into the build env, so the deployed artifact is sync-enabled. If the secrets are unset, the deploy is dormant-safe: it builds and runs local-only, with no failure.

**Why:** one single-source module is the same discipline that made `withBasePath` dependable (D-024). The on/off gate and the config shape live in exactly one place, so the dormant-safe property (D-038) is enforced structurally rather than by scattered `process.env` checks. Routing through env rather than hardcoded constants keeps the values out of source for the public mirror (D-046) and lets the same code be dormant in a fork and live in the deploy.
**Changes if:** Firebase changes its web-config shape, or we move to a host where these are injected differently. Update the single module.

### D-041 · LOCKED · Additive attribution fields on `ItineraryItem`: `createdBy?` / `updatedBy?` / `updatedAt?` (optional, every existing item stays valid)
Per D-012 (additive-only) and research section 4.2, `ItineraryItem` (in `lib/trip-data.ts`) gains three optional fields:
```ts
// added to ItineraryItem — additive, nothing existing touched (cf. how sourceId/sourceType were added in D-027a):
createdBy?: string;    // display name of whoever first added this item
updatedBy?: string;    // display name of whoever last edited it
updatedAt?: string;    // ISO 8601 timestamp of the last edit
```
- **Optional ⇒ backward-compatible:** every existing sample, calendar or card-created item, none of which set these, stays valid, exactly as `sourceId`/`sourceType` did. localStorage data written before M9 loads unchanged.
- **Stamping (who sets them):** the store's `commit()` and mutators stamp `updatedBy` and `updatedAt` (and `createdBy` on `addItem`) from the identity module (D-043), only when a name is set. With no name, or when dormant, they stay undefined and the item is still valid. Stamping happens at the store layer so both local-only and synced edits get attribution consistently: a local-only user still sees "by Me · time" if they set a name, it just isn't shared.
- **Per-item granularity is deliberate** (research section 4.2). The UI shows items, edits happen per item, and "Sushi dinner — last edited by Mei · 2h ago" is the exact affordance. A coarser per-day or whole-doc `updatedBy` loses "who changed *what*"; a finer per-field audit log is overkill. A full history or audit trail is explicitly out of scope for M9, being a separate heavier feature.

**Why:** additive optional fields satisfy D-012 with zero migration and zero risk to D-018; stamping at the store layer keeps attribution in one place and consistent across the local-only and synced paths.
**Changes if:** we want a full audit/history trail (a separate `changes` collection, out of scope now), or per-field attribution.

### D-042 · LOCKED · Firestore data model: per-day documents under one trip, so per-day writes are natural and LWW is per-day (research section 4.4)
The model is chosen so that a write touches one day, never the whole itinerary array. Research section 4.4 calls this the single most important correctness point, since it avoids lost updates across unrelated days.

**Shape:**
```
trips/{tripId}/days/{date}      // one document per DayPlan, doc id = the 'YYYY-MM-DD' date
   → { date, city, country, items: ItineraryItem[], updatedBy?, updatedAt? }
trips/{tripId}/members/{uid}    // optional: { name, lastSeen } — uid→name map (D-043), used for the member list
trips/{tripId}                  // the trip doc itself: { schemaVersion, createdAt } (metadata only)
```
- **One fixed `tripId`** (e.g. `'nepal-japan-2026'`, a constant in `lib/firebase-config.ts`): the group shares one trip. Multi-trip is out of scope, and a constant keeps it trivial.
- **`days/{date}`** maps 1:1 to a `DayPlan`, the doc id being the date string, which is already unique and stable. `pushDayChange(date, dayPlan)` writes or sets exactly that one doc; `pushPlans(prev,next)` diffs and writes only the days whose content changed. Two friends editing different days never touch the same doc, so there is no lost update. Two friends editing the same day get last-write-wins for that day's doc (D-043 LWW), and per-item `updatedBy`/`updatedAt` (D-041) make the winner visible.
- **`items` stays an array inside the day doc.** For 3 friends casually editing, same-day-same-second collisions are rare, and per-day LWW is the simplest correct grain; research section 4.4 explicitly chose per-item/day LWW over a CRDT. Going finer, with a doc per *item*, is possible but adds many tiny docs and read complexity for a benefit this scale doesn't need. Per-day is the sweet spot, and it matches the existing `DayPlan` unit (D-012), so the remote↔local mapping is near-identity.
- **Snapshot scope:** `onSnapshot` on the `days` collection delivers all day docs, and the module assembles them into `DayPlan[]`, sorted by date, for `savePlans()`. Use snapshot metadata (`hasPendingWrites`) if needed to ignore the local echo of the client's own optimistic write, as a belt to D-039's "snapshot path never pushes" suspenders.
- **Offline:** Firestore's built-in offline persistence and cache complement the localStorage cache. The app's own `loadPlans()` remains the first-paint source (D-018), and Firestore reconciles when online.

**Why:** the day is already the natural unit (`DayPlan`, the localStorage shape, the calendar's CRUD target, D-012/D-018), so a per-day doc makes the remote↔local mapping almost an identity function, makes per-day writes (the anti-lost-update rule of section 4.4) the default, and keeps the doc count tiny at 32 days. It's the model that makes the simplest-correct LWW fall out naturally.
