**Changes if:** real same-day concurrent editing becomes common enough that per-day LWW loses unrelated item edits (then move to a doc-per-item model and `pushDayChange`→`pushItemChange`, recorded then), or multi-trip support is wanted (then `tripId` stops being a constant). **(Extended by D-057, M10:** presence adds a sibling subcollection `trips/{tripId}/presence/{uid}`, a heartbeat/last-seen doc per traveler. It does not touch the `days` model or its LWW; it is an additive collection with its own write cadence and free-tier budget. See D-057.**)**

### D-043 · LOCKED · Identity: name-on-first-use (localStorage) for display, backed by silent Anonymous Auth uid for the security id; LWW per day
Per research sections 4.1 and 4.4. Two layers, minimal PII:
- **Display name (the "who"):** on first use, and only when sync is active (`isRemoteConfigured()` true, D-038), prompt once for a self-chosen first name or nickname and persist it to the localStorage key `tripPlannerUserName`. A new `lib/identity.ts` owns this, exposing `getUserName()`/`setUserName()`. It is stamped onto `createdBy`/`updatedBy` (D-041) on every write. The PII is a nickname only: no email, no surname (research section 4.1 privacy note), so the GDPR surface is essentially nil.
- **Security id (the unspoofable handle):** silent Anonymous Auth (`signInAnonymously`) mints a stable `uid` per device with no login UI. The `uid` is what Security Rules (D-044) check (`request.auth != null`, optionally `request.auth.uid in [...]`). The display name is not the security id: it is spoofable within the group, which is acceptable for a trusted friends group but not for a public link (research section 4.1).
- **Surfacing (UI):** a small, muted line on each itinerary item in the calendar and timeline, reading "last edited by {updatedBy} · {relative time}" (e.g. "by Mei · 2h ago"), reusing the existing `formatDate` and relative-time helpers and honoring D-007 (contrast/a11y) and D-020 (static classes). A per-friend color or initial chip is optional later polish. The name prompt is a minimal dialog reusing the D-021/D-028 modal and focus contract, or a lightweight inline prompt, and it is dormant when sync is off, so no prompt appears in the local-only portfolio build.
- **Conflict policy:** last-write-wins per day-doc (D-042), the simplest correct strategy for 3 casual editors (research section 4.4); `updatedBy`/`updatedAt` make the winner visible. No CRDT, which would be overkill (research section 4.4).

**Why:** name-only gives zero-friction attribution with negligible PII; the silent anon uid gives rules a real handle to gate on without a login flow; LWW-per-day is the honest, simplest-correct conflict story for this group size. All of it is dormant when sync is off, so the portfolio build is untouched.
**Changes if:** abuse or impersonation becomes a real concern (tighten rules to a known uid set, or require `updatedBy == auth-bound name`), or we want real login via Google. Both are upgrade paths, not the first cut. Or a public, non-friends share link is wanted, in which case name-only is insufficient and real auth is required. **(Scoped refinement by D-053, M10:** the M10 Trip Token gate adds a token→name front door. A valid token (`Powan`/`Sushil`/`Uttam`) calls the same `setUserName(name)`, so attribution is unchanged. The token is still soft identity (display-only, spoofable, exactly this entry's trust level), not an auth credential; hard token→uid binding stays the backend-greenlight upgrade path this entry already anticipates. See D-053. **(Also refined by D-055:** sync now requires an identified traveler — a token, or pre-M10 a typed name, via `getActiveTraveler()` — so a no-identity guest never opens the listener.**)**

### D-044 · LOCKED · Amended by D-314 · Security Rules are the real authorization gate; `firestore.rules` lives in the repo and we deploy it (it is not a server we run)
The public web config (D-040) is safe only because server-side Firestore Security Rules, evaluated by Google, decide who can read and write (research sections 2.1 and 5). The rules are therefore load-bearing and not optional.

- **Location:** a git-tracked `firestore.rules` at the repo root, plus a minimal `firebase.json` if the Firebase CLI is used to deploy. We deploy it via the Firebase console or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules`; we do not run a server, this file configures Google's managed service (D-004 annotation). **(M9 build note, 2026-06-28:** authored at the repo root as `firestore.rules` + `firebase.json` + a `.firebaserc` pinning `nepalxjapan` for CLI deploy. All three are public-safe and copy verbatim to the mirror per D-046. No `firestore.indexes.json` is needed, because the only query is an `onSnapshot` on the `days` collection with a client-side date sort, which needs no composite index.**)** **(Amended by D-314, issue #39, 2026-08-14:** the by-hand deploy clause above is RETIRED. The release publishes the rules — `deploy.yml`'s `publish-rules` job runs `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` between the build and the Pages deploy, unconditionally — so the Firebase console is now READ-ONLY for rules: an edit made there is reverted by the next release without warning, and a console hotfix must end in a commit. `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` by hand survives as break-glass, not as the route. This changes the deploy MECHANISM only; everything else in this entry, including that the rules are the authorization gate and live in the repo as reviewable code, stands unchanged.**)**
- **First-cut rule** (friends group, illustrative; verify syntax at build):
```
rules_version = '2';
service cloud.firestore {
  match /databases/{database}/documents {
    match /trips/{tripId}/{document=**} {
      // any signed-in (anonymous) user may read/write this trip.
      allow read, write: if request.auth != null;
      // OPTIONAL hardening (recommended once uids known): lock to the group —
      //   allow read, write: if request.auth != null
      //     && request.auth.uid in ['<uid1>','<uid2>','<uid3>'];
      // OPTIONAL attribution integrity on writes: require updatedBy present.
    }
  }
}
```
- **Hardening path:** start with "any authenticated (anonymous) user" for the active trip, then tighten to a known uid allow-list (collect the 3 friends' anon uids once) or a shared-passphrase claim before the data matters, or as soon as any abuse appears (research sections 5 and 6.5 risk). The B-5 slice (D-Slices) does this hardening.
- **The risk, stated plainly (research sections 5 and 6.5):** a wrong rule is the difference between "friends can edit" and "the public can wipe it." The rules have to be reviewed and the live behavior validated before the feature is considered done. Because of D-038/D-039, even a rules outage degrades to local-only rather than breaking the app.

**Why:** in a static-client world the only safe write model is "public key plus server-evaluated rules" (research section 2.1). The rules are the security, so they live in the repo as reviewable code and we deploy them. Starting permissive-but-authenticated and tightening to the group is the pragmatic path for 3 friends without over-engineering auth.
**Changes if:** the share model widens (a public link means the rules must get much stricter, plus real auth, D-043), or Firestore's rules language changes.

### D-045 · LOCKED · Dependency call: add the `firebase` modular SDK (v9+); it's a BaaS/transport dependency, not a state-management library, so D-011 holds
We add `firebase` (the modular, tree-shakeable v9+ SDK) to `package.json` `dependencies`. The research chose the SDK over a hand-rolled REST approach specifically for `onSnapshot` (true realtime push, with no polling to build) and for managed Anonymous Auth.

- **Why this doesn't violate D-011:** D-011 bans a client-side state-management library (Redux/Zustand/Jotai) and a data-fetch/cache library (SWR/react-query). `firebase` is neither. It is a BaaS client, a network transport to a remote datastore. The app's state mechanism remains exactly the D-026 Context + CustomEvent hook, and firebase only carries the existing `itinerary:changed` signal over the network (D-039). D-011's spirit (no unjustified state-lib complexity) is intact.
- **Modules used:** `firebase/app` (init), `firebase/firestore` (`onSnapshot`, `doc`/`collection`, `setDoc`/`deleteDoc`, offline persistence), `firebase/auth` (`signInAnonymously`). Import only these submodules, since the modular SDK is tree-shaken.
- **Bundle-size impact (stated plainly):** firestore + auth add roughly ~100–150 KB gzipped to the client bundle. For a greenlit realtime feature that is acceptable, and it is lazy-loaded behind `isRemoteConfigured()` (D-038), so the dormant build does not pay the full cost on the hot path (the SDK is dynamically imported only when sync is on). The static export (D-010) is unaffected: client runtime only, no SSR. Use `npm i firebase --legacy-peer-deps` to match the repo's existing install convention (D-023).
- **REST alternative (considered, rejected):** calling Firestore's REST API by hand avoids the dependency but loses `onSnapshot` (we would have to build polling) and re-implements auth token handling. More code, worse UX, for a smaller bundle. The research favored the SDK, and for a realtime feature the SDK is the right call.

**Why:** `onSnapshot` realtime is the feature's headline ("friends see edits live"). The modular SDK delivers it with managed auth and an offline cache for a bounded, lazy-loaded bundle cost, which beats hand-rolling REST plus polling. It's a transport dependency, so the no-store-library rule (D-011) is untouched.
**Changes if:** the bundle cost becomes unacceptable for the portfolio target (then reconsider the REST-without-realtime path), or we want zero third-party SDKs (then REST + polling, losing live updates).

### D-046 · Superseded · M9 mirror-sync obligations — never commit real Firebase config to the public mirror; firestore.rules is public-safe

### D-047 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Dormant-safe is enforced by dynamic-import code-splitting: firebase stays behind `isRemoteConfigured()`
The M9 dormant-safe property (D-038) is enforced concretely by code-splitting. Firebase is reached only via dynamic `import()` behind the `isRemoteConfigured()` gate, in two layers: `components/itinerary-provider.tsx` lazy-imports `lib/itinerary-remote.ts` inside a gated effect, and `itinerary-remote.ts` itself lazy-imports `firebase/app`, `firebase/auth`, `firebase/firestore`. Webpack therefore emits firebase as hashed async chunks referenced only in the lazy-chunk manifest, never preloaded by the page HTML; the env-absent build inlines no projectId and fetches no firebase chunk at runtime. Verified at S34 by bundle inspection and by a runtime check (0 firebase network calls with env absent); First Load JS for `/` stayed 209 kB with firebase present.
**Why:** this is the concrete mechanism that makes D-038 actually true rather than merely intended. A static top-level `import 'firebase/...'` anywhere (provider, hook, or any component) would pull the ~150 KB SDK onto the hot path and silently break dormant-safety.
**Changes if:** never introduce a static or eager firebase import. Every future remote slice (S35 push, S36 identity UI) must keep firebase behind the dynamic-import-behind-`isRemoteConfigured()` pattern. Reaffirmed by D-054/D-057, M10: the new `components/token-gate.tsx` imports no firebase (it's pure `lib/token-auth.ts` + `lib/identity.ts`), and `lib/presence.ts` must follow this same dynamic-import-behind-`isRemoteConfigured()` pattern, so M10 does not regress dormant-safety.

### D-048 · Superseded by D-049 · S34 read-path snapshot semantics are interim: full first-snapshot reconciliation/seeding is owned by S35

### D-049 · LOCKED · First-snapshot reconciliation handshake: trip-doc "ever-synced" marker + seed-by-key-presence (S35; supersedes D-048's interim rule)
The concrete handshake that makes M9 sync honor D-018's empty-vs-absent distinction across devices. On the first `onSnapshot` after subscribe, `lib/itinerary-remote.ts` reads `getDoc(trips/{TRIP_ID})` once:
- **Trip doc exists (the group has synced before):** the remote is authoritative, so apply the days snapshot including empty (`savePlans(remoteDays)` even `[]`, plus dispatch `itinerary:changed`). This is also the steady state for every later snapshot. A deliberately-emptied shared plan therefore stays empty on all devices and after reload, with no `SAMPLE_ITINERARY` resurrection.
- **Trip doc does not exist (never synced), so this client seeds the group:** create the trip doc `{ schemaVersion:1, createdAt: serverTimestamp(), seededFrom }` and push every local day-doc up (`pushPlans([], localPlans)`). Never call the remote-apply path on the seed branch, so an empty remote can never wipe local. Seed source is recorded in `seededFrom` (audit only): `hasStoredPlans()` true means `'local-edits'` (the user's own offline work is pushed up), false means `'sample'` (the untouched `SAMPLE_ITINERARY` seeds the group).
- **Echo guard (two layers):** `pushPlans` is called only from the store's `commit()` (genuine local mutations) and the one-time seed branch, never from the snapshot-ingest path. The snapshot handler also drops the client's own optimistic write via `if (snapshot.metadata.hasPendingWrites) return;`. No write→read→write loop.
- **`hasStoredPlans()`** (additive in `lib/itinerary-storage.ts`) is pure localStorage key-presence. It does not alter the D-018 load/save contract (no length gate) and feeds only the `seededFrom` marker: the data-loss safety depends on the `tripExists` branch plus never-apply-remote-on-seed, not on this signal.

**Why:** the trip doc is the unambiguous "has this group ever synced" marker — the only way to distinguish "never synced" (must seed) from "deliberately emptied" (must stay empty) when the `days` collection is empty in both cases. Verified at S35 by a source trace of all four invariants and by a live run with two isolated clients: A-deletes-all leaves B empty and it stays empty after reload, with no resurrection; a never-synced client seeds without losing local edits; the `seededFrom` markers confirm both branches fire.
**Known accepted edge (not a defect):** the seed path is not transactional. Two never-synced clients cold-starting simultaneously could both `getDoc` (absent) before either `setDoc`, and both would seed. Under D-042 per-day LWW this causes no data loss (union-by-day, last writer wins per doc, and the trip doc is set idempotently); it is only a benign double-write in a narrow concurrent-cold-start window. Acceptable for M9's friends-group scale.
**Changes if:** a single-writer or transactional seed is ever needed (use a Firestore transaction on the trip doc), or the conflict model moves off per-day LWW (D-042).

### D-050 · Superseded · Attribution stamping lives in lib/attribution.ts; relative-time is a pure hand-rolled helper

### D-051 · Superseded · Offline/edge hardening — server-truth reconciliation + offline→online one-shot retry (S37)

### D-052 · Superseded · The Pages deploy workflow must reference the 6 NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_* in the build-step env:

## M10 — UI/UX redesign + Trip Token gate. Greenlit 2026-06-29.

> M10 adds a Trip Token front door (a token identifies the traveler so edits are attributed to Powna/Sushil/Uttam) and a UI/UX glow-up (LazyMotion-based motion system, scroll-driven accents, ⌘K palette, identity-aware features including presence). The headline architectural insight: an identity system already exists (`lib/identity.ts` + `lib/attribution.ts` + `components/name-prompt.tsx`), so the token gate is a swap-in on top of it. It reuses rather than rebuilds: attribution, the calendar, the timeline, and the sync layer need zero changes to start attributing edits. D-053–D-057 lock the seams (token→identity contract, the always-on landing's reconciliation with D-038, the guest = local-only subscribe-gate, the reduced-motion contract for the new motion types, and the presence data model + free-tier budget) before any M10 code is written. Hard rule carried in: Firebase stays free Spark, never Blaze.

### D-053 · LOCKED · Trip Token → identity contract: `lib/token-auth.ts` (pure, firebase-free) calls the existing `setUserName`; the token is soft identity
The Trip Token front door is built as a thin, additive layer on top of the existing identity system (`lib/identity.ts`), not as new identity infrastructure. A new module `lib/token-auth.ts` is pure and SSR-safe with no firebase import; it mirrors `lib/identity.ts`'s discipline exactly (every `localStorage` access guarded by `typeof window`).

**The module contract:**
- **`TRAVELERS`** is the fixed roster: `[{ name: 'Powna', token: 'Powan' }, { name: 'Sushil', token: 'Sushil' }, { name: 'Uttam', token: 'Uttam' }]`. (Per-traveler accent color is added in S42; the name↔token pairing is the load-bearing part here.) The spelling is preserved verbatim as given: display name "Powna" vs token "Powan". These are deliberately different, and the name-vs-handle spelling is pending confirmation (the GitHub account handle uses the "Powan" spelling), so it must not be silently "corrected." Per-trip/per-user unique tokens are a *future* feature; today's tokens are simple shared strings.
- **`resolveToken(raw): string | null`** does a trim plus case-insensitive match of `raw` against each traveler's token; it returns the canonical `name` on a hit and `null` otherwise.
- **`signIn(raw): boolean`** (or returns the name|null): on a valid token it calls the existing `setUserName(name)` from `lib/identity.ts`, so that `getUserName()` returns the traveler's name and the entire attribution pipeline (`lib/attribution.ts`, the store mutators stamping `createdBy`/`updatedBy`/`updatedAt` per D-041/D-050) is unchanged. It just starts returning a name earlier in the session. It also persists a separate localStorage key `tripPlannerToken` recording that this name came from a token rather than a free-typed nickname, so sign-out can cleanly distinguish and clear the token-auth path.
- **`getActiveTraveler()`** returns the current traveler (resolved from the persisted name/token), or null. This is the predicate D-055 gates the remote subscribe on.
- **`signOut()`** clears `tripPlannerToken` and the name (`tripPlannerUserName`), returning the app to the gate. Per the plan's first assumption, already-stamped `createdBy`/`updatedBy` on existing items are historical data and left intact: sign-out is a session/identity reset, not a data scrub.

**Files that must stay untouched (the proof the integration is clean; if any needs edits, the design has leaked):** `lib/identity.ts`, `lib/attribution.ts`, `hooks/use-itinerary.ts`, `lib/itinerary-remote.ts`, `lib/trip-data.ts`.

**Soft identity (stated explicitly, refines D-043):** client-side tokens are display-only and spoofable. They are readable in the shipped bundle and decide only the *display* name. This is the same trust level as today's free-typed nickname (D-043): a token is not an auth credential. Real enforcement (binding a token to an anon `uid` via `firestore.rules` membership or a `members/{uid}` collection, D-044) needs a backend greenlight (plan E9). Flag it, don't build it. The unspoofable security id remains the silent Anonymous Auth `uid` (D-043), unchanged.

**Why:** the win here is real. `setUserName` is already the single entry point the whole attribution/sync stack reads from (D-041/D-043/D-050), so the token gate only needs to *call it* with a roster-resolved name. A pure firebase-free `token-auth.ts` keeps the front door dormant-safe (D-038/D-047) and unit-testable, and the separate `tripPlannerToken` key keeps "authed via token" and "typed a nickname" cleanly distinguishable for sign-out without overloading the existing name key.
**Changes if:** the Powna/Powan spelling is confirmed or changed (update `TRAVELERS`), we want per-trip unique tokens, or hard token→uid enforcement is greenlit (E9). Then the soft-identity stance reopens and `firestore.rules` binds the roster to real uids (D-044).

### D-054 · LOCKED (scoped refinement of D-038) · The token gate is an always-on client landing; D-038's "byte-for-byte" wording is refined, its no-firebase-in-dormant-bundle core is preserved
A new component `components/token-gate.tsx` is an always-on landing wall shown in all builds (dormant and synced), mounted in `components/itinerary-provider.tsx` at `z-[70]`, above `name-prompt.tsx`'s `z-[60]` (verified: `name-prompt.tsx` overlay is `fixed inset-0 z-[60]`). It clones the D-021 modal/focus/a11y contract verbatim from `name-prompt.tsx` (role="dialog" `aria-modal`, document-level Esc via an `onCloseRef`, lightweight Tab-trap, autofocus the token field, `AnimatePresence` + parent-owned focus-return on `onExitComplete`), with these deliberate divergences (flag them at review):
- It is a wall in the signed-out state: no overlay-click, no X, no Esc dismiss, unlike name-prompt, which is skippable. The only ways past are a valid token or the explicit "Continue as guest" escape.
- An invalid token shows an inline `aria-live` error rather than a silent no-op.
- A "Continue as guest" control provides the bypass (D-055).

Content stays mounted behind the wall (the wall is an overlay, not a replacement of `children`), so localStorage hydration and first paint happen normally. D-018 is intact: the store hydrates underneath regardless of gate state. `name-prompt.tsx` self-suppresses with zero edits: once a token sets the name, its precondition `isRemoteConfigured() && !getUserName()` (verified at `name-prompt.tsx:45`) is false, so it renders nothing, and the gate and the name-prompt never both ask for a name.

**The scoped refinement of D-038** (called out so nobody later reads it as a violation): D-038 says the dormant build behaves "exactly as today, byte-for-byte." With this landing, the dormant/public build is no longer literally byte-for-byte the pre-M10 app, since it now renders the token wall and guest-bypass. That is a deliberate product evolution we approved for M10, not a regression. The invariant D-038 actually exists to protect is fully preserved: `token-gate.tsx` imports only `lib/token-auth.ts` and `lib/identity.ts` (both pure, firebase-free), so it pulls in no firebase, the dormant bundle still contains zero firebase code path, and D-047's dynamic-import code-splitting is unaffected. The refinement touches only the *cosmetic* "byte-for-byte" phrasing of D-038; the no-firebase-in-the-dormant-bundle guarantee, the substantive core, stands unchanged.

**Why:** an always-on landing is the requested "front door," and putting it above the name-prompt's z-index with content mounted behind it reuses the proven D-021 focus machinery and keeps hydration untouched. Recording this as an explicit *scoped refinement* of D-038, rather than quietly diverging, is what keeps the decision log honest: the headline property (dormant = no firebase) is intact, and only the literal "byte-for-byte" claim is relaxed, deliberately.
**Changes if:** we want the landing only in the synced build (gate it on `isRemoteConfigured()` like the name-prompt), or want it to fully replace `children` rather than overlay them (re-verify D-018 first-paint/hydration first). Record it then. Never let `token-gate.tsx` acquire a firebase import; that would break D-038's core (D-047).

### D-055 · LOCKED (scoped refinement of D-039/D-043) · Guest mode = local-only sandbox: the remote subscribe now requires an identified traveler
"Continue as guest" (D-054) drops the user into a local-only sandbox: they can browse and plan locally, but their edits are unattributed, never pushed to the shared trip, and they never see the friends' live data. The mechanism is a one-line tightening of the gate on the remote subscription in `components/itinerary-provider.tsx`:

- **Today (verified):** the gated effect is `if (!isRemoteConfigured()) return;`, then it dynamically imports `lib/itinerary-remote` and calls `subscribeRemote()`.
- **M10:** the gate becomes `if (!isRemoteConfigured() || !getActiveTraveler()) return;` (equivalently, subscribe only when `isRemoteConfigured() && getActiveTraveler()`). A guest has no active traveler, so the effect returns early and no Firestore `onSnapshot` is ever opened, which means a guest can neither read nor pollute the shared trip. The effect must re-run when identity changes (it depends on the active-traveler/name state) so that signing in *after* landing as guest opens the subscription, and signing out closes it.

This refines D-039 and D-043: sync now requires both `isRemoteConfigured()` (D-038, unchanged) and an identified traveler. A guest's unattributed local edits remain fully valid, since `lib/attribution.ts` already no-ops when no name is set (verified: `stampCreated`/`stampUpdated` return the item unchanged when `getName()` is falsy), and D-018's localStorage hard-guarantees are untouched. The push path is implicitly covered too: with no listener and no name, nothing attributes or fans out. The binding gate is the subscribe.

**Hard enforcement is deliberately not built here.** Preventing a *determined* guest from writing to Firestore, as opposed to simply not opening a client subscription, requires `firestore.rules` membership binding writes to a known-uid set or a `members/{uid}` collection (D-044), and that needs a backend greenlight (plan E9). This entry's gate is the client-side "guests don't sync" behavior, consistent with the soft-identity stance (D-053). Flag the hard-enforcement gap; do not implement it without a greenlight.

**Why:** gating the subscribe on `getActiveTraveler()` is the minimal, honest way to make "guest = local-only" true on the client. It reuses the existing single subscribe choke-point (D-039) and the existing attribution no-op, adding exactly one predicate. It keeps the public portfolio viewable (guests browse freely) while ensuring only identified travelers join the shared trip.
**Changes if:** hard token enforcement is greenlit (E9, after which `firestore.rules` binds writes to the roster's uids and this client gate becomes defense-in-depth rather than the only gate), or we want guests to have read-only visibility of the shared trip (then the subscribe gate splits read vs write). Record it then.

### D-056 · LOCKED (extends D-007) · Reduced-motion contract for the M10 motion system + LazyMotion is the standard motion path
The M10 redesign adds motion types that the existing global guard does not automatically neutralize, so this entry makes the reduced-motion obligations explicit, extending D-007's acceptance-gating reduced-motion rule.

**(a) What is auto-neutralized (no per-component work):** declarative framer-motion (`initial`/`animate`/`exit`/`whileHover`/`whileTap` and so on) is auto-gated by the existing `<MotionConfig reducedMotion="user">` in `components/theme-provider.tsx` (verified present). Plain CSS transitions and `@keyframes` already in `app/globals.css` are covered by its `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)` block.

**(b) What is not auto-neutralized and requires an explicit `useReducedMotion()` guard** (or a `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)` addition in `app/globals.css`):
- **Scroll-linked `useTransform` / `useScroll` parallax** (hero parallax, the scroll progress bar, the scroll-driven `--accent` engine). `MotionConfig` does not neutralize transform values derived from scroll position. Under reduce-motion these must render static: no parallax translate, and the accent shifts instantly rather than animating.
- **Hand-rolled `requestAnimationFrame` count-ups** (the eased count-up on the countdown/dashboard numbers) must render the final value instantly under reduce-motion. The computed values and the live tick stay exact regardless: easing is presentational only, and the countdown still targets Dec 9 2026 (D-006). This is a hard correctness fence, not a motion preference.
- **Any new CSS `@keyframes`** added for M10 must be suppressed in the `prefers-reduced-motion` block.

**(c) LazyMotion plus the lightweight `m` component is the standard motion import path (locked).** M10 introduces `<LazyMotion features={domAnimation} strict>` nested inside the existing `<MotionConfig reducedMotion="user">` in `theme-provider.tsx`. Note that `LazyMotion` does not exist there yet: S39 adds it, `MotionConfig` already does, and the nesting order MotionConfig → LazyMotion must be preserved so reduced-motion still wraps everything. All motion is then authored via the lightweight `m.*` component rather than the heavy `motion.*` barrel, routed through `components/ui/animate.tsx` as the shared home for the motion primitives (FadeIn/SlideIn/Stagger/PressScale/HoverLift and friends). `strict` makes any leftover `motion.*` throw in dev, a built-in completeness check for the S39 migration (16 files).

**Why:** D-007 makes reduced-motion acceptance-gating, but the new scroll-linked and rAF-driven motion types fall *outside* what `MotionConfig` can catch. Without this entry someone would reasonably assume the global config covers everything and ship un-guarded parallax and count-ups. Locking LazyMotion, `m` and `animate.tsx` as the one motion path keeps the shared bundle light (D-045's bundle-discipline spirit) and gives a `strict` completeness check; nesting it inside MotionConfig preserves the reduced-motion guard. The count-up "values stay exact" fence protects D-006.
**Changes if:** framer-motion changes whether `MotionConfig` neutralizes scroll-linked transforms, or the reduced-motion bar is dropped (unlikely, see D-007). Never let count-up easing alter the computed or ticked countdown values (D-006).

### D-057 · LOCKED · Presence/activity data model + free-tier write budget: heartbeat doc per traveler, gated exactly like `itinerary-remote.ts`; activity feed derived for free
The two identity-aware realtime features (active-traveler presence, and the recent-changes feed) are modeled to stay firmly within Firebase's free Spark tier. That is a hard rule: never Blaze.

**(a) Presence: a `trips/{TRIP_ID}/presence/{uid}` heartbeat (additive to the D-042 model).** A new module `lib/presence.ts` writes a last-seen/heartbeat doc per traveler at `trips/{TRIP_ID}/presence/{uid}` (`uid` = the silent anon uid, D-043), shape roughly `{ name, lastSeen: serverTimestamp() }`, refreshed every ~30–60s while the tab is open (and on visibility/online changes). "Active" means `lastSeen` within N minutes, N being a small constant, e.g. 2–5. A presence-bar UI subscribes to the `presence` collection and shows who's currently on the trip. `lib/presence.ts` is gated exactly like `lib/itinerary-remote.ts`: a dynamic firebase `import()` behind `isRemoteConfigured()`, and, for writing presence, behind an identified traveler, consistent with D-055. So it renders nothing and loads no firebase when dormant (D-038/D-047 preserved, reaffirmed). This is the only new collection M10 adds, and it does not touch the `days` model or its per-day LWW (D-042).

**(b) Activity / recent-changes feed: derived for free, no new collection by default.** The "recent edits" feed is computed from the existing `updatedBy`/`updatedAt` fields already on every item (D-041), rendered with `lib/relative-time.ts` (D-050). It reads the data the app already syncs, so it adds zero extra reads or writes. A dedicated append-only `changes` log is optional and only for history beyond current state (e.g. "what was deleted"); if it is ever added it must watch quota. It is not built by default, consistent with D-041's "full audit trail out of scope".

**(c) Free-tier write budget (documented so it's auditable, not rediscovered).** Presence is the only sustained write source M10 adds. Worst case at the chosen cadence: about one write per 60s per traveler across 3 travelers, roughly 4,320 writes/day (3 × 1440). Spark's free quota is about 20,000 document writes/day, so presence consumes ~22% and leaves comfortable headroom for itinerary edits. Reads: one `onSnapshot` on `presence` (3 docs) is negligible against Spark's ~50k reads/day. Conclusion: presence at a 30–60s cadence stays free, and it must never require Blaze. To stay safe, keep the cadence at 30s or more, pause the heartbeat when the tab is hidden (`visibilitychange`), and never drop the interval into the single-digit-seconds range. At 5s it would be ~52k/day, over quota. If a future feature pressures the cap, throttle presence first.

**Why:** a single self-overwriting heartbeat doc per traveler is the standard, cheapest presence primitive (each tick overwrites one doc rather than appending), and deriving the activity feed from already-synced attribution fields means the headline "who's here and what changed" features cost essentially one small extra collection. Writing the budget math into the log makes the free-tier hard rule checkable at review: the D-054/S54 validation must show the dormant build loads no firebase and cite this math.
**Changes if:** the group grows, the cadence drops below ~30s, or a real append-only history log is greenlit. Then re-run the budget and confirm it still fits Spark, or make it a deliberate decision. Never an accidental Blaze trip.

### D-058 · LOCKED · `--accent-scroll` is the scroll-driven accent hook; shadcn `--accent` (sakura) stays the interactive-fill token. **Hues stale (S366, 2026-08-02): every hue named in this entry is dead. `--accent-scroll`, `--accent`, `--primary` and `--ring` are all cyan `189 90% 60%` (`--accent-scroll-rgb: 61, 217, 245`); there is no gold default and no sakura `--accent`. Authority: D-269 plus the locked wave scope call #3. The structural rule below, that `--accent-scroll` is a separate additive hook and shadcn `--accent` must not be repurposed by the engine, is unchanged and still binding. Do not restore any hue from this entry on the strength of its LOCKED status.**
S43 introduced the scroll warm/cool accent as a new additive CSS var `--accent-scroll` (default gold `44 80% 61%`, mirror `--accent-scroll-rgb`, glow via `--shadow-glow`), rather than by repurposing shadcn's `--accent`. Rationale: `--accent` (`340 60% 65%`, sakura) is the shadcn/Radix interactive-fill token consumed by ~10 `ui/*` primitives (`hover:bg-accent`, `aria-selected:bg-accent`) with near-white `--accent-foreground`; repurposing it would recolor all interactive chrome and put light text on gold (an AA failure). So the scroll engine uses `--accent-scroll` exclusively. Already-wired consumers (S43): the `h2[id$="-heading"]::after` section underline, `--shadow-glow`/`pulse-glow`, and the global `:focus-visible` fallback all read `--accent-scroll`. S44 animates only `--accent-scroll` (himalaya↔gold↔sakura by active section) and must not touch shadcn `--accent`.

Also recorded for accuracy: S43 was not "tokens-only." Beyond token refinement it added two app-wide runtime CSS rules. The first is the `h2[id$="-heading"]::after` gold underline on section `<h2>`s (the hero `<h1>` and `japan-todo` `<h3>` are deliberately bare, an intentional-looking asymmetry that was surfaced for a call). The second is the global `:focus-visible` outline fallback (shadcn `focus-visible:outline-none` components still win; verified 257/257 own their ring, 0 double-rings). Both passed review (D-009/D-007/D-021/D-022 honored).
**Why:** it keeps shadcn theming intact and AA-safe while giving the scroll engine one clean hook. Proposed at the S43 review. Refines the S43 scope framing; extends D-009.

### D-053 addendum (S40) · confirmed traveler names + the `signOut` key-coupling
**Names confirmed (2026-06-29):** name == token for all three: **Powan**/`Powan` (accent `#f0c760` gold), **Sushil**/`Sushil` (`#f7a0b3` sakura), **Uttam**/`Uttam` (`#ff8c42` himalaya). The earlier "Powna" was a typo; it is "Powan", matching the GitHub account handle. These live in the single `TRAVELERS` array in `lib/token-auth.ts` (S40, committed `ce20779`).
**Key coupling:** `token-auth.signOut()` clears `lib/identity.ts`'s `tripPlannerUserName` key via a locally-declared `USER_NAME_KEY = 'tripPlannerUserName'` constant, because identity.ts does not export its key and the D-053 clean-integration tripwire forbids editing it. The two string literals must stay in sync (renaming identity's key requires updating `token-auth.ts:20`); this is documented in code comments at both sites. Verified at S40 (`signIn`→`getUserName()`=='Powan'; `signOut` clears both keys; SSR-inert; no firebase; 5 tripwire files unchanged).

### D-054 addendum (S41) · name-prompt removed (not "self-suppressed")
D-054 assumed `name-prompt` would self-suppress once a token set the name. At runtime (synced build) it did not: `name-prompt` decides visibility at mount, before any token is entered, so it opened behind the z-[70] gate and was revealed when the gate dismissed, producing a redundant "What should we call you?" after sign-in. Resolution (S41, committed `b00723f`): the `<NamePrompt/>` import and mount were removed from `components/itinerary-provider.tsx`. The Trip Token gate is now the sole identity mechanism (name-on-first-use is superseded, and a guest is intentionally unattributed). `components/name-prompt.tsx` remains on disk, unused: a housekeeping prune candidate, but do not delete it without a greenlight. Caught during validation via the preview tools.

### D-059 · LOCKED · Guest mode persists across reload
"Explore as guest" sets `localStorage['tripPlannerGuest']='1'`, and the wall does not re-appear on subsequent loads (only sign-out or clearing the flag re-shows it). Chosen so the local-only portfolio/demo experience isn't gated on every visit. Guests are unattributed (D-018) and never open the Firestore subscription (D-055). Proposed at the S41 review.

### D-060 · LOCKED · `identity:changed` is the same-tab reactive identity signal
A single `CustomEvent('identity:changed')` on `window` (constant `IDENTITY_CHANGED_EVENT` in `lib/token-auth.ts`) is the reactive identity signal, mirroring D-026's `itinerary:changed`. It is dispatched by `signIn` and `signOut` plus the guest opt-in/exit, and consumed by the navbar "You are {name}" chip (`hooks/use-active-traveler.ts`, `useSyncExternalStore`), the token-gate's live re-show on sign-out, and the gated remote-subscribe in `itinerary-provider.tsx`. It is firebase-free (it lives in pure `token-auth.ts`) and SSR-guarded at every call site. It resolves the S41 seam: the gated remote subscribe now activates live on sign-in, and tears down on sign-out, instead of only on the next reload. The subscribe gate stays `isRemoteConfigured() && getActiveTraveler()` (D-055), so dormant and guest remain firebase-free, proven at S42 by a `.next` first-load chunk probe (no firebase) and a network check (0 fb requests signed-out, 8 after a live sign-in).

### D-061 · Superseded · Layout-sibling client islands that consume useActiveSection defer binding until the page subtree mounts

### D-062 · LOCKED · The scroll-accent engine writes the **authored RGB literal** at every settled endpoint (derivation only mid-tween). **Subject deleted (S366, 2026-08-02): this entry has no live code. The component it governs is gone: neither `components/scroll-accent-engine.tsx` nor its D-072 rename `route-accent-engine.tsx` exists (deleted at S354), and nothing writes `--accent-scroll` at runtime any more. The authored literals it names (gold `240, 199, 96`, himalaya, sakura) are retired, and the single accent is cyan (D-269). The durable lesson survives and is worth keeping: HSL→RGB round-tripping does not reproduce a pinned brand literal, so any future engine must write the authored value verbatim at settled endpoints. Do not resurrect the engine or its hues from this entry.**
The scroll-accent engine (`components/scroll-accent-engine.tsx`, S44) stores each accent as `{ hsl, rgb }` where `rgb` is the authored brand literal (gold `240, 199, 96`, himalaya `255, 140, 66`, sakura `247, 160, 179`) and writes that literal verbatim at every settled endpoint: the reduced-motion target, the "already-there" no-op, and the final tween frame, all via `applySettled()` (it never recomputes RGB from HSL). RGB derivation (`Math.round(lerp(...))` per channel) is permitted only for intermediate in-flight tween frames (`0 < t < 1`), which always terminate on the authored literal at `t === 1`.
**Why:** standard HSL→RGB does not reproduce the pinned brand values. Gold rounds to `235, 193, 76` against the authored `240, 199, 96` — a visible ~20-pt blue gap — so deriving RGB at rest reintroduced the exact D-058 violation. This entry is the disposition of the S44 review failure: the engine derived `--accent-scroll-rgb` from HSL and was wrong at-rest for gold. Re-check evidence (real headless): at rest, at `#nepal` and at `#japan`, the settled `--accent-scroll-rgb` is byte-exact to the three authored literals; reduced-motion lands instantly in a single `applySettled` write (D-056). Extends D-058.

### Governance note (2026-06-30) · Archived · Code review skipped for the S47–S54 build wave

### D-063 · Superseded · Hero scroll-parallax engine (S48): single useScroll, per-layer rates, reduced-motion collapses to rest

### D-064 · Superseded · Card micro-interaction recipe (S49): framer lift gated by useReducedMotion, CSS glow/border, wrapper-driven image zoom

### D-065 · Superseded · Presence module shape (S54): implements D-057

### D-066 · LOCKED · Image delivery via `<OptimizedImage>` + build-time `sharp` (S51), extends D-024
A build-time pipeline (`scripts/gen-images.mjs`, with `sharp` as a devDependency only, run via `npm run gen:images` and never in `build`/`prebuild`/CI per D-010/D-023) pre-generates AVIF and WebP derivatives plus an LQIP `blurDataURL` into `lib/image-manifest.json`; the generated assets are committed (bundled, D-015). `components/optimized-image.tsx` renders a `<picture>` (AVIF `<source>` → WebP `<source>` → fallback `<img>`) that preserves `withBasePath` internally and the `onError` fallback (D-024), and sets explicit dims (or `fill` plus an aspect wrapper) so CLS≈0. The hero is `priority`/eager (LCP), the rest lazy. Cost accepted: about +9 kB `/` First Load JS (the component plus the manifest's LQIP strings) against roughly 56% image-byte savings (measured: 10.4 MB AVIF vs 23.7 MB originals), a worthwhile tradeoff (S52 audit). `sharp` ships 0 bytes to the client. Extends D-024.

### D-067 · Superseded · useCountUp hardening: resume-on-remount + live-value fallback (S56 bug-fix)

### D-068 · LOCKED · Add-to-plan dialog is a flex-column with a pinned action footer (S56 bug-fix)
The `components/add-to-itinerary-dialog.tsx` panel is `flex flex-col max-h-[90vh] overflow-hidden`: a non-scrolling header (`shrink-0`), a scrollable body (`flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-y-auto`, native scrollbar), and a pinned footer (`shrink-0`, top divider) holding the confirm ("Add to plan"/"Update plan") button, so the action is always visible and clickable at any viewport height. Root cause of the S56 bug: the old single-scroll panel (`max-h-[90vh] overflow-y-auto scrollbar-hide`) put the confirm button below the fold on short viewports with a hidden scrollbar, leaving it unreachable. The D-021 contract (autofocus, document-Esc, Tab-trap, parent focus-return) is preserved; static Tailwind, dark-only, reduced-motion; 0-overflow @360/390/414. Extends D-028 / D-021.

### D-069 · LOCKED · Add-to-plan dialog portals to `document.body`: it escapes transformed and `overflow-hidden` card ancestors (S57 bug-fix)
`components/add-to-itinerary-dialog.tsx` renders its `fixed inset-0` overlay through `createPortal(overlay, document.body)`, mount-guarded (`const [mounted,setMounted]=useState(false); useEffect(()=>setMounted(true),[]); if(!mounted) return null;`) so it is SSR-safe under `output:'export'` (no `document` at prerender). Root cause of the S57 bug: the dialog is rendered inline inside a place card whose root is a framer `m.div` with an active `whileHover={{ y:-6 }}` transform and `overflow-hidden` (D-064). Per the CSS containing-block rule, a `position:fixed` element with a transformed ancestor resolves against that ancestor rather than the viewport, so the backdrop covered only the card (neighbours stayed bright) and the `max-h-[90vh]` panel overflowed the card and was clipped by its `overflow-hidden`, hiding the S56 pinned footer (D-068). The portal moves only the DOM node; the React tree and the parent `AnimatePresence` are unchanged, so D-021 (Esc, Tab-trap, autofocus, parent-owned focus-return via `onExitComplete`) is fully preserved (focus-return was verified by element reference). One fix covers every trigger surface (recs, photography, map, featured) since they all route through `AddToPlanButton`. Why the S56 verification missed it: a headless `.click()` never sets CSS `:hover`/`whileHover`, so the transform was absent at the time; the S57 pass forced the transform to reproduce it. Rule going forward: any new viewport-fixed overlay rendered from within a card or animated subtree must portal to `document.body`, since `whileHover` and transforms are pervasive on the cards (D-064). The calendar `ItemEditor` is unaffected only because it isn't inside a transformed or `overflow-hidden` ancestor; if it ever moves into one it needs the same treatment. Extends D-068 / D-021; relates to D-064.
**Addendum (S75, 2026-07-02):** `backdrop-filter` on an ancestor also establishes a containing block for `position:fixed`. It is the same failure class, and it bit the map fullscreen shell inside `.glass-card`. And for portal children that must not be re-created by React reconciliation (a live MapLibre canvas), use the persistent-host variant: render one host node, relocate it imperatively via `appendChild` between an inline slot and a body-portaled slot, and call `map.resize()` after each move. Do not "simplify" this back to declaratively re-parenting the canvas through `createPortal`; that re-creates the GL context. (`components/map-section.tsx`, S75 fix.)

### Note (S56) · Archived · Bug 3 "no Dec 9 plan" was stale localStorage, not a code defect (D-018 upheld)

## M14 — v2 multi-page rebuild ("laptop to plan, phone during the trip"). Approved 2026-07-02.

> v2 splits the single page into 5 routes with a mobile bottom tab bar, a full visual redesign, PWA/offline, a Today travel mode, a custom add flow, and map/guide upgrades, built in parallel worktree lanes. D-013 (single-page) is superseded by D-070, and approval of the v2 plan is the sign-off. Everything else carries forward unchanged: D-010 (static export, GitHub Pages), D-018 (key-presence persistence), D-026 (shared reactive store), D-038/D-047 (dormant-safe Firebase), D-062 (authored RGB literals), D-069 (portal overlays). Hard rules carried in: Firebase stays free Spark; free-only APIs, no paid map/places.

### D-070 · LOCKED · v2 route tree: five static pages; providers stay in the root layout; legacy hashes redirect (supersedes D-013)
The app becomes five Next.js App Router routes, all `output:'export'`-compatible (every route must show `○ Static` in the build output, so no dynamic or SSR routes, D-010 untouched; `trailingSlash:true` makes `/plan/` and friends the canonical forms):

```
app/layout.tsx        — root layout: providers (Theme/Itinerary), TokenGate, PresenceBar stay HERE;
                        Navbar + Footer move here; exports `viewport` with `viewportFit:'cover'` (D-071)
app/page.tsx          → /        HOME: hero · trip-dashboard · flights · trip-timeline · travel-essentials · <LegacyHashRedirect/>
app/plan/page.tsx     → /plan/   PLAN: calendar-planner · author filter · activity feed
app/nepal/page.tsx    → /nepal/  NEPAL: recommendations · photography (country) · nightlife (country) · foods/etiquette/featured (Nepal)
app/japan/page.tsx    → /japan/  JAPAN: mirror of /nepal/ with country="Japan"
app/map/page.tsx      → /map/    MAP: map-section, full-height
```

- **Providers, TokenGate and PresenceBar remain in the root layout:** the store is instantiated once (D-026), the token gate walls every route (D-054), and route navigation must not remount providers. Client-side route transitions keep layout state, and that is the property that lets the split leave D-018/D-026/D-038 completely untouched. Sections stay `dynamic({ ssr:false })` client islands composed in server-component pages (the pattern D-077 protects).
- **Legacy-hash redirects (v1 deep links keep working):** a new `components/legacy-hash-redirect.tsx`, mounted on Home only, maps on load: `#itinerary→/plan/`, `#nepal→/nepal/`, `#japan→/japan/`, `#map→/map/`, `#photography→/nepal/#photography`, `#nightlife→/nepal/#nightlife`. Hashes that remain Home-local (e.g. `#hero`, `#flights`, `#dashboard`) scroll in place, and an unknown hash no-ops harmlessly. All existing section `id`s are kept so cross-route hash targets resolve; cross-route hash scroll defers via the double-rAF pattern already proven in `navbar.tsx:170-174`.
- **Per-page `metadata`** (title/description/OG per route). The S31 metadataBase/OG rule carries verbatim (`app/layout.tsx:16-33`): `metadataBase` comes from `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` in the root layout, and metadata image paths stay bare root-relative, never wrapped in `withBasePath()` (metadataBase already carries the basePath segment, so wrapping would double-prefix). This is the one sanctioned exception to D-024's wrap-everything rule, and it now applies to five pages instead of one.

**Why:** real usage is two-device — laptop to plan now, phone during the trip — and 13 stacked sections made scrolling the primary pain. Five routes give per-route code-splitting (measure per-route First Load JS), honest URLs, and a `/`-rooted tree that keeps the future multi-trip token landing free (D-078). Static export supports multiple static routes by design (D-013 anticipated exactly this reopening).
**Changes if:** a new top-level page is added (extend the tree, but 5 tabs is the mobile ceiling, so a 6th route forces a nav redesign, D-071), or the deploy host changes (D-010).

### D-071 · LOCKED · Navigation contract: desktop top navbar + mobile bottom tab bar; pathname-driven active state; the z-ladder; FAB placement
- **Desktop:** the existing top navbar (`components/navbar.tsx`) converts `NAV_ITEMS` from anchors to routes; active state via `usePathname()` (basePath-agnostic, since `usePathname` excludes basePath).
- **Mobile:** a new `components/bottom-tab-bar.tsx` with 5 tabs (Home / Plan / Nepal / Japan / Map), `md:hidden`, fixed bottom, navy/90 blur, icon + label, tap targets ≥44px, `aria-current="page"` on the active tab, active tint `hsl(var(--accent-scroll))` (D-058/D-072). Bottom padding uses `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, which requires the root layout to export `viewport` with `viewportFit:'cover'` (D-070). `components/presence-bar.tsx` gains a bottom offset on mobile so the tab bar never covers it.
- **The z-ladder (normative for all fixed and overlay chrome):** scrim 30 < presence bar / side panels 40 < navbar / tab bar / dialogs 50 < token gate 70. Any new fixed element declares its rung against this ladder at review; portaled dialogs (D-069) sit at 50 with their scrim at 30.
- **Quick-add FAB** (new `components/quick-add-fab.tsx`): 56px, fixed above the tab bar (offset = tab-bar height + safe-area), opens the custom-add dialog (D-074) preset to today or the selected day (D-075). It hides while any dialog is open, since the FAB must never float over an open dialog's scrim.

**Why:** thumb-reach bottom navigation is the v2 mobile headline. Writing the z-ladder down once is the lesson of the S57 stacking bug (D-069) applied proactively: v2 adds three fixed bars, a FAB, portaled dialogs and the gate, and an unrecorded stacking order would guarantee regressions.
**Changes if:** a 6th destination page (tab-bar redesign), or we want a compact icon-only bar. Never add a fixed or overlay element without placing it on this ladder.

### D-072 · LOCKED · Route-driven accent engine: `usePathname()` replaces the scroll spy; D-062's authored-literal rule unchanged; D-061's rAF gate retired. **Retired in full (S366, 2026-08-02): the problem is its rule contradicting the live one, not only its hues. `components/route-accent-engine.tsx` no longer exists (deleted at S354), and route-driven accent switching was already retired by D-235, whose one-accent rule is route-independent and remains binding. There is no per-route accent: every route uses the single cyan chrome accent `189 90% 60%` (D-269). Country tints survive only as content-wayfinding (D-235), never as chrome. Do not reintroduce a per-route accent from this entry.**
`components/scroll-accent-engine.tsx` becomes `components/route-accent-engine.tsx`. The input changes from section-scroll (`useActiveSection`) to `usePathname()`: `/nepal/` → himalaya `255, 140, 66`; `/japan/` → sakura `247, 160, 179`; every other route → gold `240, 199, 96`.
- **D-062 carries unchanged:** the engine stores `{ hsl, rgb }` with `rgb` the authored brand literal, written byte-exact at every settled endpoint (reduced-motion target, already-there no-op, final tween frame), with channel derivation only mid-tween. The existing rAF HSL tween and `applySettled()` machinery is reused as-is, and reduced-motion lands instantly in a single write (D-056).
- **D-061 is retired:** the outer rAF poll for `document.getElementById('nepal')` (`scroll-accent-engine.tsx:254-271`) existed only because a layout-root island consumed the DOM-anchored `useActiveSection` before the page subtree mounted. `usePathname()` has no DOM dependency and is valid immediately, so the outer/inner split and the poll are deleted. D-061's *general* rule (a layout-root island consuming a DOM-anchored hook must defer binding) remains true as a pattern but has no live consumer after this change.
- **The token name `--accent-scroll` stays** (along with `--accent-scroll-rgb` and `--shadow-glow`, D-058): consumers (section-heading underline, glow, global `:focus-visible` fallback, and the new tab-bar tint, D-071) are untouched. Do not rename it, and do not touch shadcn `--accent`.

**Why:** with the sections split across routes, section-scroll cannot drive a cross-page accent. The route is the honest signal, and it is simpler: no IO spy, no mount-timing gate. Keeping the CSS token stable makes this a drop-in input swap rather than a consumer migration.
**Changes if:** per-section accents *within* a page are wanted again (layer a scroll-spy on top of the route base, and D-061's deferral rule immediately re-applies to any layout-root consumer).

### D-073 · LOCKED · PWA: hand-rolled service worker generated post-build from `out/`; images runtime-cached; cross-origin never intercepted; next-pwa and @serwist/next rejected
The PWA layer is hand-rolled, with zero new runtime dependencies:
- **Generation:** a new `scripts/gen-sw.mjs` runs after `next build` and walks `out/` to emit `out/sw.js` with a precache manifest that exactly matches the shipped artifact, including basePath builds, since it prefixes from the same single source. `package.json` becomes `"build": "next build && node scripts/gen-sw.mjs"` (currently `"next build"`), so `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, which runs `npm run build` (D-023), is untouched. Contrast D-066: `gen:images` stays a manual script, but the SW must regenerate every build because its manifest is build-specific.
- **Precache:** route HTMLs, `_next/static/**`, and the manifest and icons. Not `public/images/**`, where ~10 MB would blow the cache and install time, so images are runtime cache-first with an LRU cap (~80 entries).
- **Strategies:** navigations are cache-first, since the app is a static shell and freshness comes from the update flow rather than network-first latency. Cross-origin requests (Firebase/Firestore, Google Fonts) are never intercepted: the SW passes them through untouched, so the sync layer's degrade semantics (D-038/D-051) are preserved and the SW can never cache or block Firestore traffic.
- **Update flow:** `updatefound` → sonner toast ("update available") → on accept, `postMessage SKIP_WAITING` → reload on `controllerchange`. The registrar (new `components/service-worker-registrar.tsx`) is production-only and registers with `updateViaCache:'none'` so the SW script itself is always revalidated.
- **Manifest + icons:** a new `scripts/gen-icons.mjs` (using the existing `sharp` devDep, D-066) emits 192/512/maskable/apple-touch from `favicon.svg`. The web manifest is generated with basePath-prefixed `start_url`/`scope`/icon paths from one prefix source, which is the D-024 lesson: a bare root-relative path in the manifest would 404 on the project page.
- **Rejections (recorded so they aren't re-litigated):** `next-pwa` is unmaintained, pinned to Workbox 6, with known App-Router breakage. `@serwist/next` injects via a webpack plugin that collides with our custom `output.filename`/`chunkFilename` overrides (`nextjs_space/next.config.js:25-31`, the de-AI chunk naming), and we will not risk the chunk-name contract for a convenience wrapper.

**Why:** a static-export site is the easy case for a hand-rolled SW. Walking `out/` post-build gives a byte-accurate precache list with no build-pipeline coupling, no plugin risk against the customized webpack output, and full control over the one genuinely dangerous rule: never touch cross-origin/Firestore.
**Changes if:** Next ships first-party SW support, or the chunk-filename override is removed (serwist could be reconsidered), or the precache budget grows past sane install size (revisit the manifest split).

### D-074 · LOCKED · Custom itinerary items are plain `ItineraryItem`s with no `sourceId`/`sourceType`; the Google Maps link-out is a URL scheme, not an API
The custom add flow ("add your own dinner spot") produces plain `ItineraryItem`s with `sourceId`/`sourceType` unset:
- The D-027 union (`'recommendation' | 'photo' | 'map' | 'featured'`) is untouched, and no `'custom'` member is added. `findPlacements` matches on `sourceId`, so custom items can never produce a false "Added" badge on any card family (D-029). This keeps the D-027 invariant clean: `sourceId` ≡ "back-link to a card", absent ≡ user-authored, exactly like hand-created calendar items today.
- ids come from the shared `generateItemId()` (D-029b); writes go through `store.addItem` (D-026); attribution stamps arrive free through the store mutators (D-050). No new persistence shape, no D-018 impact.
- **Maps link-out scheme (free-only rule):** `https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=<encodeURIComponent(title [+ ' ' + location])>`, rendered `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`. This is a URL, not an API: no key, no quota, no billing (the real Places API was explicitly declined). The link is disabled until the title is non-empty, and confirm is blocked on an empty title. The same Maps row is added to the calendar `ItemEditor` (done in FU-5, commit `e4ddb8c`). Implementation note (FU-5): `buildMapsSearchUrl` now lives in the pure, React-free `lib/maps-link.ts`. Eager consumers (e.g. the calendar on `/plan` first-load) must import it from there, not from `add-to-itinerary-dialog.tsx`. The dialog re-exports it for back-compat (`place-detail-sheet`), but importing from the *component* module drags the whole dialog plus framer-motion into first-load: that mistake cost +12 kB on `/plan` in an FU-5 v1 and was reverted.
- The dialog's custom mode inherits the full modal contract: D-021 (parent-owned focus-return, document-level Esc), D-068 (flex-column, pinned action footer), D-069 (portal to `document.body`).

**Why:** a user's own place needs zero source machinery. The D-012 item shape already is the right container, and refusing a `'custom'` sourceType means the entire card/badge/adapter stack (D-027/D-028/D-029) needs zero edits and cannot regress.
**Changes if:** custom items ever need dedup or card-style back-links (then a `'custom'` sourceType is recorded as a D-027 extension), or a real Places integration is greenlit (that is the backend-seam conversation, D-004).

### D-075 · LOCKED · Today engine: `lib/trip-now.ts` is the single clock source; `?today=` is a sessionStorage-scoped simulation override; `computeCountdown` stays pure
A new `lib/trip-now.ts` is the only module that answers "what time or day is it":
- **`getNow(): Date`** is the app-wide clock read. **`getTodayInTrip()`** returns the trip-day for "now" (`{ date: 'YYYY-MM-DD', dayNumber, city, country }`), or `null` outside the trip window. Day-N derives from `TRIP_DATES.indexOf(date) + 1`, reusing the existing single source (`lib/trip-data.ts`, D-006); there is no second date table.
- **`?today=YYYY-MM-DD` override:** read from the URL and persisted to sessionStorage (one named key constant inside `trip-now.ts`); `?today=off` clears it. It ships in all builds, invisible unless used, which is how we simulate any trip day against the real static artifact. It is session-scoped by design: it can never leak into localStorage or shared state, the D-018 key namespace is untouched, and a synced client simulating a day cannot pollute peers.
- **Consumers:** the hero (countdown ⇄ "Day N — {city}" swap once in-trip), Plan's initial selected date, the dashboard trip-status, and the FAB preset (D-071). All of them call `trip-now`, and none reads `new Date()` directly for trip-day logic.
- **`computeCountdown(target, now)` stays pure** (D-016): `now` is passed at call sites from `getNow()`. The countdown target (D-006) is unchanged.
- **Timezone stance (accepted):** trip-day comparison uses the device's local date. During the trip the device travels with the user, so the local date is the honest "today" (NPT/JST as lived). A remote viewer without `?today=` sees Day-N flip at *their* local midnight, which is accepted for a personal trip app. Don't add timezone math speculatively.

**Why:** one injectable clock makes every travel-mode feature testable now (a headless run can use `?today=2026-12-11` against the real build) and keeps D-016's purity intact. sessionStorage is the honest scope for a simulation flag: persistent enough to survive route navigation, gone with the tab.
**Changes if:** we want pinned trip-timezone day boundaries (record the tz rule here), or want the override stripped from production builds (then it gates on `NODE_ENV`, losing live-site simulation).

### D-076 · LOCKED · Plan page mobile layout: single-day agenda + snapping day strip below `lg`; a render-layer-only change (store/DnD/persistence contracts untouched)
Below `lg`, the Plan page renders a single-day agenda instead of the month grid:
- A new `components/day-strip.tsx` is a horizontally scroll-snapping strip of 32 day chips (`TRIP_DATES`), each with a country dot and item-count badge. It auto-centers the selected chip and shows a "Today" chip when in the trip window (D-075). A new `lib/selected-day.ts` holds the selected date as in-memory state plus a CustomEvent (mirroring D-026/D-060's signal pattern), deliberately with no storage key (D-078's storage rule; selection is ephemeral UI state).
- The month grid collapses on mobile (expandable, not deleted). Cross-day moves on mobile go through the edit dialog's date change, the D-032 remove+add rule that is already proven, since drag-between-cells has no honest agenda-view gesture. Desktop `lg+` keeps the existing two-pane layout unchanged.
- **Render layer only:** the `ItineraryStore` API (D-026), fresh-read mutators (D-031), keyboard reorder/DnD, the D-021 focus contract, and D-018 persistence are all untouched. This slice swaps what the calendar *renders* below `lg`, not how it reads or writes. Validation re-runs the D-018 hard-guarantee matrix from `/plan/` to prove it.

**Why:** a 32-day month grid is desktop-shaped, but phone-in-hand usage is "what's happening today or tomorrow": one day at a time with a thumb-swipe strip. Constraining the change to the render layer is what keeps the persistence/CRUD acceptance evidence valid.
**Changes if:** a gesture-based cross-day drag is wanted on mobile (then the D-032 dialog path gains a sibling, recorded then), or the trip length changes (chip count derives from `TRIP_DATES`).

### D-077 · Superseded · Next version policy: stay on 14.2.x; Next 15 rejected for now

### D-078 · LOCKED · v2 design language ships at token level (brand accents unchanged) + the parallel-lane protocol + the storage-literal rule (multi-trip seam)
**(a) Token-level design delivery.** The v2 visual upgrade is delivered as tokens and utilities in `app/globals.css` + `tailwind.config.ts` (spec artifact: new `docs/design-spec-v2.md`), so components owned by other lanes upgrade without cross-lane file edits. That is the property that makes five parallel lanes merge conflict-free by construction. Brand accents are unchanged: the navy base plus the gold/himalaya/sakura authored RGB literals are load-bearing (D-058/D-062, and D-072 re-pins them). Surfaces, type scale, depth, spacing and motion get the upgrade. Changing accent hues requires explicit sign-off; no lane may do it on its own.
**(b) Parallel-lane protocol (M14 process).** Phase-1 work runs as five file-scope-fenced lanes, one per lane document (LANE-V, LANE-M, LANE-G, LANE-X, LANE-P). Each lane session works in an isolated git worktree and commits only to its `lane/<x>` branch, never to `main`. The one shared hotspot is `app/layout.tsx` + `app/*/page.tsx` mount-points, and it belongs to whoever does the S73 integration: lanes deliver components plus a one-line mount instruction, and do not edit layout or pages themselves. Merge order is V → M → G → X → P, because V's tokens change what every later lane's output looks like and P's SW precache walks the final `out/`. Only the integrator commits to `main`. A lane that needs a file outside its fence stops and escalates rather than editing across the fence.
**(c) Storage-literal rule (the multi-trip seam, design-for only).** Trip identity already funnels through one env-overridable constant (`TRIP_ID`, `lib/firebase-config.ts:46`). The real localStorage seam is the six existing unnamespaced keys: `nepal_japan_itinerary` (`lib/itinerary-storage.ts:18`), `tripPlannerUserName` (`lib/identity.ts:12`), `tripPlannerToken` (`lib/token-auth.ts:19`), `tripPlannerGuest` (`components/token-gate.tsx:44`, `hooks/use-active-traveler.ts:28`, raw literal in `components/navbar.tsx:31`), `nightlife_section_visible` (`components/nightlife-section.tsx:8`), and `packing_checklist` (`components/travel-inspiration.tsx:102/110`), a sixth key the v2 plan's list of five missed, sitting as raw literals in a component. New rule, effective M14: no new raw `localStorage` string literals in components. Any new persisted key lives as a named constant in a `lib/` module, as itinerary-storage, identity and token-auth already do. New v2 state deliberately avoids localStorage entirely (`trip-now` = sessionStorage, D-075; `selected-day` = in-memory + CustomEvent, D-076). Existing keys are grandfathered. When S61 splits `travel-inspiration.tsx`, the `packing_checklist` read/write moves to `travel-essentials.tsx` keeping the same key, because users' checklists have to survive v2. Future multi-trip migration is then one storage-key helper plus a one-time migration in `itinerary-storage.ts`/`token-auth.ts`/`identity.ts`, and is not built now. Deepest coupling flagged but not addressed — the `TRIP_DATES`/`TRIP_START` module constants.

**Why:** (a) tokens-not-components is the only design-delivery shape compatible with parallel lanes. (b) The fences plus the single-committer rule are what make five concurrent sessions safe; this project has proven worktree parallelism at S46/S51/S54, and this entry makes the protocol binding rather than folklore. (c) The storage rule costs nothing today and converts "multi-trip someday" from a grep-hunt into a one-module change.
**Changes if:** new accent hues are signed off (reopens D-058/D-062/D-072 together); a lane genuinely needs a cross-fence edit (the fence is redrawn explicitly, never silently); multi-trip is greenlit (then the key-helper migration is its own slice, recorded here).

---

> **S73 integration note (2026-07-02):** all five M14 Phase-1 lanes merged onto `main` in the D-078b order (V → M → G → X → P), fences disjoint, zero merge conflicts. Lanes may not write this file (D-078b), so the contracts they delivered are recorded here at integration as **D-079–D-087**. D-003 is stamped Superseded by D-079.

### D-079 · LOCKED · The map is a real MapLibre GL map: free keyless CARTO tiles + demotiles glyphs, no key, no backend (supersedes D-003; S72, that workstream)
`components/map-section.tsx` renders a real **`maplibre-gl@4.7.1`** GL map, and the D-003 CSS/SVG mock is retired. It adds zero new dependencies: maplibre-gl was already a declared dependency with 0 imports. The SDK loads via dynamic **`await import('maplibre-gl')`** inside the page's `ssr:false` island, so it ships as a separate async chunk and never lands on first-load (`/map` First Load 155→158 kB in-lane, 160 kB post-S73, under budget). The contract:
- **Free and keyless only (hard rule):** basemap = CARTO **dark-matter** raster XYZ `https://{a-d}.basemaps.cartocdn.com/dark_all/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` (OSM + CARTO attribution rendered); glyphs = **`https://demotiles.maplibre.org/font/{fontstack}/{range}.pbf`**. Never `fonts.openmaptiles.org`: it returns HTML for these font stacks, which MapLibre parses as protobuf and throws `Unimplemented type: 4`, silently breaking every symbol layer (found and fixed in-lane, headless-verified). Stadia, MapTiler and Mapbox are rejected because they need keys. Style JSON builder: new `lib/map-style.ts`.
- **Real coordinates:** `MapMarker` (`lib/map-data.ts`) now carries **`lng`/`lat` (WGS84)**, authored for all **27** curated markers (13 Nepal, 14 Japan). The legacy `x`/`y` panel-percent fields are retained as harmless unused metadata and can be dropped later, since nothing consumes them.
- **Clustering:** built-in GeoJSON `cluster:true`, `clusterRadius:50`, `clusterMaxZoom:8`, so no `supercluster` dependency. Cluster click → `getClusterExpansionZoom` → ease/jump.
- **Reduced-motion (D-007):** every camera move reads `prefers-reduced-motion` at call time and branches `flyTo`/`easeTo`/`fitBounds(animate:true)` to `jumpTo`/`fitBounds(animate:false)`. Verified in both modes headless.
- **Container sizing (the trap):** the map container is `h-full w-full` inside a definite-height parent, not `absolute inset-0`. MapLibre stamps `position:relative` on the container node, which defeats `absolute inset-0` and collapses it to 0px, rendering nothing.
- **Popups:** the MapLibre `Popup` receives an empty DOM node and the React content renders into it via `createPortal`, so the popup stays inside `MapSection`'s React tree. Context flows, so `AddToPlanButton` is consumed as-is (D-069's portal spirit). The "My itinerary" overlay reads the shared store (D-026) and live-updates without reload.
- **D-004 stays intact:** no key, no server we run, no paid provider. This entry supersedes only D-003's mock stance.
- **Two new render-time externals** (flag for the mirror/deploy CSP review at the next mirror sync, D-033/D-046): the CARTO tile CDN and the demotiles glyph server, both free and keyless. Self-hosting tiles and glyphs is a possible future slice, not built. **[Superseded for GLYPHS by D-319 (issue #8, 2026-08-14): the glyph server is gone and the PBFs are served from `public/font/` and precached. Tiles are unchanged and remain cross-origin.]**

**Why:** the approved M14 plan greenlit the "Better map". With the dependency already present and keyless tiles available, the real map costs no new dependency, no key and no backend, so D-003's original "changes if" condition fired.
**Changes if:** CARTO or demotiles change terms or availability (swap another keyless source, or vendor the assets, and record it), or a keyed provider is greenlit (that reopens the D-004 seam conversation).

### D-085 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Place-detail sheet: one responsive component, bottom sheet below `sm`, 440px side panel at `sm`+; the standard for future detail surfaces (that workstream, S68)
`components/place-detail-sheet.tsx` is a single component rendering a **bottom sheet on mobile and a right-side panel from `sm` (640px) up**: one element, two transforms via breakpoint classes (overlay `items-end justify-center sm:items-stretch sm:justify-end`; panel `w-full rounded-t-2xl max-h-[88vh]` → `sm:w-[440px] sm:h-full sm:rounded-l-2xl`), rather than separate mobile and desktop components. It **portals to `document.body`** (D-069) at the dialog rung of the z-ladder (`z-50`, D-071) and carries the full modal contract: **D-021** (document-level Esc, Tab-trap both directions, parent-owned focus-return by element reference), **D-068** pinned footer (Maps link-out and add-to-plan always visible and clickable, verified at 740×360), and the `body[data-dialog-open]` flag (D-083). Add-to-plan branches by source: recs and photos use the state-aware `AddToPlanButton` (sourceId + "Added" badge), nightlife uses the custom flow (D-084). This is the standard pattern for any future place-detail or preview surface.
**Why:** one responsive component cannot drift into two divergent implementations, and it inherits the proven modal contracts wholesale instead of re-deriving them.
**Changes if:** a detail surface genuinely needs different mobile and desktop content rather than only a different layout. Split deliberately then, and record it here.

### D-086 · LOCKED (extends D-073) · PWA contract details: precache-hash cache name; `trip-images-v1` LRU-80; origin check first; theme color navy-900 (that workstream, S70/S71)
The concrete values locked by the shipped SW (`scripts/gen-sw.mjs` emits `out/sw.js` + `out/manifest.webmanifest` after every `next build`, D-073):
- **(a) Precache cache name = `trip-precache-<sha256(precacheUrls.join('\n')).digest('hex').slice(0,12)>`**, content-hashed from the precache URL list, so it changes whenever the shell changes (route HTMLs, hashed chunks, manifest/icons; buildId drift counts). This hash is the update-flow driver: the D-073 toast fires when a new SW installs. Stale `trip-precache-*` caches are deleted on `activate`, while the images cache survives on a different prefix, deliberately.
- **(b) Runtime image cache = `trip-images-v1`**, cache-first, with an LRU cap of 80 entries (`IMAGE_CACHE_LIMIT`), evicting oldest by insertion order on overflow. Images are never precached (D-073).
- **(c) The same-origin guard is structurally the first executable statement of the SW `fetch` handler** — `if (url.origin !== self.location.origin) return;` before any `respondWith`. This is the hard structural D-038 guarantee: Firestore, identitytoolkit, gstatic and fonts traffic can never be intercepted, cached, or blocked by the SW (proof: 0 cross-origin cache keys). Any future SW edit has to keep this the first statement.
- **(d) PWA `theme_color`/`background_color` = navy-900 `#0a0e27`**, in the emitted manifest and in `viewport.themeColor` in `app/layout.tsx` (Next 14 wants it on `viewport`, not `metadata`). This is the app's visible painted surface (`<body className="… bg-navy-900">`), deliberately not the shadcn `--background` token, because OS chrome should match what users actually see.

**Why:** these four are precisely the values a well-meaning future SW edit would silently get wrong: rename the cache and break updates, grow the image cache unbounded, move the origin check below a handler, or "unify" the theme color with the token.
**Changes if:** the image corpus outgrows cap 80 (raise it deliberately here), the shell precache outgrows sane install size (D-073's revisit clause), or any cross-origin request ever needs interception. That last one is a D-038 conversation, not an SW tweak.

### D-087 · Superseded · v2 surface-language mechanics: hairline glass edges, body pseudo-layer ambience, template.tsx route fade, PageHero <h1> rule, SectionSkeleton

### D-088 · LOCKED · Free-tier-only is the standing, top-priority requirement for all v3+ work (generalizes D-038/D-074/D-079 beyond Firebase/Maps/the map)
Staying on genuinely free software, tools and services is the biggest requirement, ahead of feature scope, for v3 and everything after (stated directly 2026-07-04). This elevates and generalizes what were previously feature-scoped free-only rules: D-038 (Firebase stays on Spark, never Blaze), D-074 (Maps link-out is a URL, not a paid Places API call), and D-079 (the map uses free keyless tiles, no key or provider). Together they become one standing constraint that governs every future engineering choice, not only the Firebase, Maps and map cases. Any library, API, cloud service or infrastructure choice that requires a paid tier, a credit card on file, or ongoing cost is disqualified by default, full stop. That includes "free trial", "free tier that still wants a card", and "effectively free at our usage volume" options, since those still put a card on file. If a paid option is ever genuinely the best answer to a real need, it has to be surfaced explicitly as a named exception request, never assumed, defaulted into, or silently adopted mid-build.

**Why:** a direct instruction, stated as the top priority ahead of which features get built. That is a stronger and broader claim than the existing per-feature free rules already on record.
**Changes if:** spending on a specific named tool or service is explicitly authorized.

### D-091 · Superseded · Quarantine-on-corrupt: loadPlans() preserves a corrupt payload before the sample fallback

### D-092 · Superseded · data-testid convention + docs/test-ids.md is the E2E selector contract
