### D-094 · Superseded · Calendar ItemEditor is portaled to document.body (FU-11)

### D-095 · LOCKED (blueprint note) · Trip Vault envelope: versioned `{schemaVersion, updatedAt, payload}`, Zod-validated, ordered-migration runner (S90; blueprint `docs/data-core-blueprint.md`)
Local persisted domain data is wrapped in a minimal versioned envelope `{ schemaVersion: number; updatedAt: string(ISO); payload: T }`, validated with the **already-present** `zod` dependency (no new deps, D-088). A **migration runner** holds an ordered `migrations[]` of pure `from→to` steps. The current itinerary target is **schemaVersion 3**, and **migration #1 is v2→v3**, where v2 is today's bare `DayPlan[]` array on `nepal_japan_itinerary` and the v3 payload is the same array: the step is a payload identity, lossless by construction, adding only the wrapper. Read resolves four inputs: **absent** → seed sample; **legacy un-enveloped array (v2)** → run the migration runner → v3; **valid v3 envelope** → payload verbatim (including `[]`); **corrupt, parse-fail, lenient-Zod-fail or migrate-throw** → quarantine, then sample. Reads use **lenient** schemas (`category` as `z.string()`, `.passthrough()` unknown fields) so real deployed data with unknown categories or fields is never destroyed (D-018). A future or unknown `schemaVersion` above the current one is read leniently, never down-converted and never quarantined, so rollback stays safe. Writes always emit the current envelope (including `payload: []`).
**Why:** the itinerary is a bare array with no way to know which schema wrote it. An on-disk version header turns every future format change into a deterministic, testable, ordered migration instead of a defensive guess, at the cost of one wrapper object. The site is live and sync-enabled, so lossless-with-quarantine is mandatory, and an identity v2→v3 is the safest possible first migration.
**Bundle note (S90, ACCEPTED):** because `loadPlans()` is a **synchronous** hot-path read (D-031) and D-095 mandates Zod-validated reads, the Vault and Zod are statically pulled into `/plan`'s first-load chunk. Zod was a declared dependency but imported nowhere before S90 (0 prior bundle cost), so this is a real **`/plan` First-Load JS 144 kB → 158 kB (+14 kB)** regression, isolated to `/plan` (all other routes byte-identical). It is intrinsic to "Zod-validate the sync read", avoidable only by making the read async (which breaks the byte-identical sync `loadPlans` contract) or by hand-rolling a validator (a blueprint deviation). **Accepted** because validated, versioned reads are the deliverable and Zod is now load-bearing across Phase B (S92 import, S96 merge); the 144 kB acceptance figure for `/plan` is superseded by **158 kB**. **Revisit in Phase D (JS diet, S107–S109):** dynamic-import islands or a lighter validator on the read boundary can reclaim it as part of the whole-bundle pass. Flagged for post-run review.
**Changes if:** a real v3→v4 shape change lands (append a step; never reorder or renumber a shipped step), or the envelope genuinely needs `id`/`checksum`/history (add only when a slice needs it, and record it then).

### D-096 · LOCKED (blueprint note) · Vault quarantine-on-failure generalizes D-091 to migrate + validate failures (S90)
D-091's corrupt-payload convention is folded into the Vault read path and now fires on **any** failure: a JSON parse error, an unrecognized shape, failed *lenient* Zod validation, or a throwing migration step. The handler preserves the **raw original string verbatim** to `nepal_japan_itinerary_corrupt`, but only if that key is absent (don't-clobber-first-capture), then `console.warn`s and never throws (the preserve attempt is itself try/caught), then falls back to `SAMPLE_ITINERARY`. This preserves the D-031 non-destructiveness: because `commit()` derives its next state from the Vault read, quarantining the raw bytes *before* the sample fallback is what stops the next edit from overwriting the user's real (corrupt but recoverable) trip.
**Why:** the migration and validation path introduces new failure modes. Without folding quarantine into them, a failed migration would fall back to sample and the next `commit()` would destroy the original bytes — the exact pre-S78 destructive loop.
**Changes if:** a recovery UI is built that consumes the quarantine key (record its contract then). The preserve-before-fallback ordering is never removed.

### D-097 · LOCKED (blueprint note) · Typed storage gateway: one module fronts all 8 persisted keys; D-078c becomes structural (S91)
All persisted web-storage access funnels through one typed gateway (`core/storage/gateway.ts`, re-exported through `lib/`). It owns the single `STORAGE_KEYS` map and is the only place raw `window.localStorage`/`window.sessionStorage` is touched in app code (tests excepted). The **verified full inventory is 8 keys**, not the backlog's "~10+": `nepal_japan_itinerary` (plus `…_corrupt`), `tripPlannerUserName`, `tripPlannerToken`, `tripPlannerGuest`, `packing_checklist`, `nightlife_section_visible` (all localStorage), and `tripPlannerTodayOverride` (**sessionStorage**, D-075). On-disk **key names and value shapes are unchanged**: the gateway is a typed wrapper over identical bytes. The three duplicated or raw literals collapse to single references (`tripPlannerGuest` across navbar, token-gate and use-active-traveler, including a raw literal in `navbar.tsx`; `tripPlannerUserName` duplicated in `identity.ts` and `token-auth.ts`; `packing_checklist` raw in `travel-essentials.tsx`). Every accessor is SSR-safe (`typeof window` guard → typed fallback or no-op) and never throws, so quota limits and disabled storage degrade quietly. The gateway is **store-aware** (per-key `local` or `session`) and does not unify or migrate across backends: D-075's session override stays session. `clearIdentity` clears both name and token, and the nightlife pref stays `String(boolean)` with a lenient parse rather than `JSON.parse`.
**Why:** makes D-078c's storage-literal rule structural rather than a convention (grep-clean of raw `localStorage.` outside the gateway), pays down the concrete duplicated and raw-literal debt, and is the one-module seam a future multi-trip migration would extend, without building multi-trip now.
**Changes if:** multi-trip is greenlit (the gateway grows a trip-prefix helper as its own slice, reopening the D-078c "future migration" note), or a new persisted key is added (it lives in `STORAGE_KEYS`, never as a raw literal).

### D-098 · LOCKED (blueprint note) · Whole-trip export/import validates through the Vault schemas and fails safe (S92)
User-facing whole-trip **export** serializes the itinerary Vault envelope to a downloadable JSON. **Import** parses, validates through the **same** Zod schemas, and replaces via the Vault write path on success. A bad or hostile import **never destroys current data**: validation failure rejects (or quarantines the imported blob, the D-096 pattern) and leaves the live itinerary untouched. Import runs the migration runner, so a v2-era export imported into a v3 build migrates. It is client-side only (Blob/File, D-004 intact). **v1 scope is itinerary-only**, since identity and token are device-soft rather than portable trip data, unless we widen it later.
**Why:** import is the one place untrusted data enters the Vault. The schema is the trust boundary, and D-091's never-destroy discipline extends to it. Reusing the same schemas guarantees an export/import round-trip is lossless.
**Changes if:** we decide export should include prefs or identity (widen the serialized set and record it), or a recovery UI consumes import-quarantine.

### D-099 · LOCKED (blueprint note) · Headless Core `core/` boundary + Storage/Sync/Clock ports; extraction order S93 (clock) before S94 (itinerary) (S93/S94)
A framework-free `core/` package (plain TS, no React, Next or `window` except via an injected port) holds the Vault, the domain logic (dates and countdown; itinerary CRUD), and three narrow **ports** it defines and the framework layer implements: **StoragePort** (implemented by the Vault gateway, wired at S94), **ClockPort** (implemented by the `lib/trip-now.ts` adapter, resolving the `?today=` override, D-075), and **SyncPort** (implemented by `lib/itinerary-remote.ts`, per-day-shaped to stay Firestore/Spark-compatible, D-042/D-088; left compatible rather than redesigned, since Sync v2 is S95). Arrows point inward: UI→core, never core→UI. **Extraction order is S93 (dates, clock, countdown — the low-risk pure math) before S94 (itinerary CRUD and persistence — the high-blast-radius live-data core)**, and the gateway becomes the StoragePort implementation at S94. Both extractions are **refactors behind frozen nets**: the S77 unit suites and the S80/S82 E2E waves (persistence, countdown, calendar CRUD, date-boundary) stay green with **zero assertion changes**. A test that needs editing means behavior changed and the extraction is wrong. D-018, D-031, D-091, D-026, D-039 and D-041 are all preserved, and the dynamic-import dormant-safe firebase path (D-038/D-047) survives the SyncPort indirection because the adapter stays lazy and gated. The cross-tab `storage`-event key literal and the `itinerary:changed` event route through the gateway key constant, not hardcoded strings.
**Why:** ports-and-adapters makes the domain pure and unit-testable in isolation and decouples it from Next and React. Extracting the pure clock first proves the boundary and the frozen-net method on data that cannot corrupt, before moving the dangerous itinerary core. The clock is also a dependency of itinerary day-math, so it has to exist as a port first.
**Changes if:** IndexedDB or a non-localStorage backend is wanted (StoragePort's implementation changes; localStorage-as-source-of-truth is D-002 LOCKED, so that needs explicit sign-off), or Sync v2 (S95) needs a different granularity (reopens D-042/D-088, also a sign-off). The SyncPort *interface* is the seam it designs against, but changing per-day granularity is not S89–S94's call to make.

### D-100 · LOCKED (implementation note) · Muted-caption WCAG AA contrast policy + axe-deterministic reveals (S85 / FU-12)
The a11y net (`e2e/a11y.spec.ts`, S85; `@axe-core/playwright`, **MPL-2.0** devDependency, free, OSI-approved, dev-only, so D-088 is satisfied) asserts **0 serious/critical axe violations per route** and is a permanent gate. FU-12 fixed the pre-existing violations it surfaced.

(a) **Muted-text contrast.** Failing low-opacity captions were raised to the minimum opacity that clears WCAG AA (normal ≥4.5:1, large ≥3:1) on the dark background `#0a0e27`: `text-white/30`→`/50` (2.63→5.32), `text-white/40`→`/55` (3.77→6.22, or `/60` where it must also pass over an active tint), and brand-tinted muted text (`text-himalaya-400/70`, `text-sakura-400/70`) raised to `/90` **by opacity only, hue preserved (D-062)**. The muted hierarchy is kept: a label stays brighter than its count badge.

(b) **List semantics.** `flights-section.tsx`'s journey `<ol>`s had `role="separator"` on an `<li>`, which overrides its implicit `listitem` role, so the `<ol>` had a non-listitem direct child. The role and `aria-label` moved to an inner `<span>`, so the `<ol>`'s direct children are valid listitems and assistive tech still announces the layover.

(c) **Axe-deterministic reveals.** Axe scans without reduced-motion, so framer-motion fade-in reveals were caught mid-fade at sub-AA opacity, an intermittent false-serious in 2 of 8 runs. Section mastheads are now **slide-only** (`opacity` starts at 1, the `y` slide is kept) and the nightlife panel uses `<AnimatePresence initial={false}>`, so scanned text is never below full opacity while the reveal motion is preserved (0 flakes in 10+ runs after).

**Rule going forward:** new muted captions meet AA at rest, and text reveals must not fade text through sub-AA opacity (slide, scale, or reveal at full opacity). **Known-remaining (moderate, non-gating):** `heading-order` on `/nepal` and `/japan`, deferred to a future slice.
**Why:** accessibility (sufficient contrast, semantic structure) is an explicit v3 acceptance criterion, and a strict *deterministic* axe gate only holds if the app meets AA at rest and never animates text through failing states.
**Changes if:** we prefer the darker muted glassmorphism aesthetic over AA (revert the contrast bumps and relax the axe contrast rule — a deliberate call, flagged), the design system adds a dedicated AA-compliant muted-foreground token (route captions through it), or `heading-order` is fixed (drop the known-remaining note).

### D-101 · Superseded · CI harness: report-only ci.yml + enforcing deploy gate (activation held for a manual go-ahead) + flake policy

### D-102 · LOCKED · Visible semantic app version (MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH) in the footer on every page; bumped by hand every commit; CI is a backstop gate, never an auto-bumper (confirmed 2026-07-05)
The app carries a visible release identifier, independent of **D-095's `schemaVersion`** (the Trip Vault's *data envelope* version, a different concept entirely). Never conflate the two, and never let one field serve both purposes.

**Single source of truth:** `nextjs_space/package.json`'s new `"version"` field, seeded at **`3.0.0`** to align with the in-flight "v3"/M15 milestone naming already used throughout the planning docs. `next.config.js` reads it at build time via Next's `env` config key (`env: { NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_VERSION: require('./package.json').version }`). That is deliberately not the externally-set-shell-var pattern D-010/D-023 use for `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH`, which would require CI to separately compute and pass the value; sourcing it straight from `package.json` inside `next.config.js` means it can never drift and needs zero extra CI plumbing. Client code reads `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_VERSION`, and the string is never hardcoded a second place anywhere in the tree.

**Display:** `components/footer.tsx`, mounted exactly once in `app/layout.tsx` so every route inherits it with no per-page work, renders it as `v{version}` (all three segments always, e.g. `v3.0.0`, never truncated to two) beside the existing copyright line, in the same muted small treatment (D-100's AA-contrast rule applies to it like any other footer text).

**Bump discipline:** `package.json`'s version is bumped by hand as part of the commit for every slice that lands on `main` — patch for fixes, minor for features and slices, major only on an explicit call. This is a **manual, semantic, human-in-the-loop bump, not an auto-incrementing CI step.** CI is a **backstop only** (tracked as follow-up **FU-14**, deferred until the in-flight S87/S88 `deploy.yml` work, D-101, lands and commits, to avoid entangling two unrelated diffs in one file): the deploy workflow fails if `package.json`'s version is byte-identical to the previous commit on `main`, catching the case where someone forgets. The backstop **only blocks**; it never itself chooses or auto-bumps a number.
**Why:** we want a visible, at-a-glance build identifier ("so when I view it I know what version of the page I'm viewing") and an enforced guarantee that every deployment carries a version change, strong enough that it can't silently lapse, but without surrendering the semantic major/minor/patch judgment call to a machine. Manual-bump-plus-backstop beat fully-automatic CI bumping because the equivalent manual ritual already happens every commit, and a fully automatic bump would both remove that human signal and introduce CI-authored commits back into the repo.
**Changes if:** we want full-auto CI bumping instead (reopens this entry), or the starting number or format changes.

### D-103 · LOCKED (blueprint note; S95, 2026-07-05) · Sync v2 = item-level merge inside the per-day doc; per-day Firestore shape unchanged (D-042/D-088 held)
Same-day concurrent edits by two friends no longer clobber each other. The merge is **item-level**, keyed on `ItineraryItem.id`, and lives **entirely inside** the existing `trips/{TRIP_ID}/days/{date}` doc's `items` array: no per-item docs, no new collection (D-042/D-088 LOCKED, Spark-free). It replaces per-day LWW with per-item resolution plus a deterministic tie-break, and adds a merge-aware (transactional) remote write so even simultaneous same-day pushes don't lose updates. Blueprint: the sync design note. Build slices: S96 (pure merge core) → S97 (wire-in + two-client E2E proof).
**Why:** the day is still the doc unit (D-042 near-identity mapping), but the conflict grain has to be the item: the UI edits items, and "different items, same day" is the exact clobber to fix.
**Changes if:** same-field concurrent editing becomes common enough to need a field-level CRDT (out of scope now), or per-day granularity is genuinely reopened (D-088).

### D-104 · LOCKED (blueprint note; S95, 2026-07-05) · Additive per-item sync fields `rev?` / `hlc?` / `deleted?`; lossless Vault migration v3→v4
`ItineraryItem` gains three **optional** fields: `rev?: number` (monotonic per-item revision), `hlc?: string` (Hybrid Logical Clock stamp, the primary merge order key), and `deleted?: boolean` (tombstone). This is additive per D-012, so every existing item stays valid. It lands via a **routine Vault migration v3→v4** (`CURRENT_ITINERARY_VERSION 3→4`, append-only per D-095), a pure backfill that defaults each item (`rev:1`, `hlc` derived from `updatedAt` via a clock-free `seedHlcFromLegacy`, `deleted:false`). Schemas stay **lenient + `.passthrough()`** (D-095), and forward-reads of a v4 blob by an old build are lenient, never quarantined, so rollback is safe.
**Why:** the merge needs per-item version, identity and delete-intent. Additive plus defaulted is lossless and satisfies D-012/D-095 with zero risk to D-018.
**Changes if:** a real v4→v5 shape change lands (append a step), or a full audit trail is wanted (D-041 clause).

### D-105 · LOCKED (blueprint note; S95, 2026-07-05) · Hybrid Logical Clock (pure `core/sync/hlc.ts`) orders edits under skew; built on `updatedAt` (D-041), tie-break by anon-auth uid
Cross-client edit ordering uses a standard **HLC** = `{pt: physicalMs, ct: logicalCounter, actor: uid}`, serialized to a fixed-width lexicographically-sortable string. Physical time is **injected via ClockPort** (D-099) so the HLC ops are pure and testable, with no internal clock. It maps to the existing `updatedAt` (D-041): a local edit stamps both consistently, `updatedAt` stays display and `hlc` is ordering, so nothing is duplicated. **The tie-break is `pt` → `ct` → `actor` (uid string compare)**, giving a deterministic total order and therefore a convergent merge. The `actor` is the unspoofable anon-auth uid (D-043), not the display name.
**Why:** wall-clock `updatedAt` alone loses updates under phone-clock skew. An HLC gives bounded-drift, causal, deterministic ordering — the correctness core of the merge.
**Changes if:** a coarser ordering proves sufficient (unlikely), or Firestore server timestamps are adopted as the order source (which would reopen the pure-core stamping).
**S97 implementation reality (2026-07-05; flagged):** the `actor` is sourced synchronously and firebase-free from the active traveler token (`getActiveTraveler().name` — Powan, Sushil, Uttam, distinct per friend), not the anon-auth uid this entry specified. The uid is only available **async** inside the remote handle, but the store stamps **synchronously** in the mutators and has to stay **dormant-safe** (D-038: no firebase on the hot path). The per-friend token is a stable, distinct-per-client id, sufficient for the deterministic HLC tie-break. The consequence is bounded: `actor` is only the final tie-break for a genuinely simultaneous same-item edit (equal `pt` and `ct`, astronomically rare), and in the trusted 3-friend group (D-043) a spoofed name only changes which of two simultaneous edits wins, never causes data loss. The uid (unspoofable, stricter) remains available if we want it; it would require an async actor-cache warmed by the remote handle. Fold into the eventual D-105 wording at FU-1.

### D-106 · LOCKED (blueprint note; S95, 2026-07-05) · The per-day merge is a pure `core/` function behind `SyncPort`; tombstone-wins-by-HLC (configurable); commutative and idempotent, therefore convergent
`mergeDay(local, remote): DayPlan` (and `mergeDays`) is a **pure, framework-free** function in `core/sync/merge-day.ts`: the function S96 builds and exhaustively unit-tests, called by the `lib/itinerary-remote.ts` adapter behind the finalized **`SyncPort`** (D-099). It resolves per `id`: different items survive independently; same `id` means higher HLC wins; **delete-vs-edit defaults to tombstone-wins-by-HLC**, so a strictly later edit can resurrect. The policy is a single `deleteWins: 'hlc'|'always'` flag. Tombstones live **inside the per-day doc**, are filtered out of the UI-exposed `plans` (zero consumer edits, D-026), and are GC'd by a separate pure time-plus-supersession pass (conservative, and correct even if retained forever). The merge is **commutative and idempotent**, so all clients converge (property-tested). Echo-suppression (D-039) is preserved and strengthened by idempotence: the snapshot path merges and `savePlans`s directly, never pushes.
**Why:** pure-core keeps the mergeable logic fully testable off firebase (D-099). HLC-ordered tombstones keep deletes deterministic without an unconditional "delete always wins" that breaks under skew, and convergence is the formal safety property.
**Changes if:** field-level merge is needed (CRDT, out of scope), or the delete policy default is changed.
**Note for S97 (D-038 byte-identity):** making `removeItem` write a tombstone rather than physically remove changes local-storage bytes even in the dormant build. Gate the tombstone and expose-filter behavior on `isRemoteConfigured()` alongside `hlc` stamping so the dormant portfolio build stays byte-for-byte; the blueprint's section 4.2 "(or gated)" hedge should be made explicit at S97.

### D-107 · LOCKED (blueprint note; S95, 2026-07-05) · Lazy, lossless dual-read rollout for the live shared trip; no destructive DB migration; mixed-fleet clobber is a controlled-reload risk
The deployed v1 (no-`rev`) `days/{date}` docs are upgraded **lazily**: new clients **default v1 fields on read** (`docToDayPlan`) so they merge immediately with **no remote write required**, and a day-doc is rewritten in v2 shape only when a new client next edits that day. There is no batch DB rewrite and no flag-day, and the trip-doc `schemaVersion` bump is diagnostic rather than a gate. Old and new clients coexist: old plus old is today's per-day LWW, new plus new is item-merge, and **old plus new can still clobber or drop tombstones until the old client reloads the new (static-export) build**. That is an irreducible mixed-fleet property, mitigated by a **coordinated reload** of the 3 friends rather than by engineering old-client compatibility. No path destroys data; a resurrected item is the worst mixed-window surprise. Local v3→v4 is the same lazy on-read Vault upgrade (D-095), rollback-safe, since forward blobs read leniently and are never quarantined.
**Why:** the trip is live with real friend data. A lossless lazy upgrade (the D-095 philosophy) avoids any risky migration while letting the fix roll out by simply reloading the page.
**Changes if:** we want zero mixed-window surprises (then gate v2 writes on a fleet-ready marker, or coordinate a hard cutover — escalate), or a public (non-friends) share widens the trust model (D-043).

### D-108 · LOCKED (implementation note; S99, 2026-07-05) · Weather via keyless Open-Meteo, client-side, offline-cached through the gateway (D-088 single-external-touchpoint check passed)
Weather and golden hour (S99) use **Open-Meteo** (`api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast`), which is genuinely **keyless: no card, no signup, free**. It is the one external-service touchpoint in M15, and the D-088 free-tools check is **satisfied with no escalation** (a unit test asserts the request URL carries no `api_key`, `apikey`, `appid` or `token`). The fetch is a **client-side browser `fetch` straight to Open-Meteo**, with no route handler and no server: D-004 stays intact, since keyless means no secret and therefore no backend is warranted. It adds **no npm dependency** (native `fetch`). The required **CC-BY 4.0 attribution** ("Weather data by Open-Meteo.com") is rendered. `fetchWeather` is **total and never throws**: on any failure it returns the cached last-good value (tagged stale) or a quiet `unavailable` state, so offline shows "last updated …" rather than an error. The offline cache is a new typed-gateway slot, **key 9 `weatherCache` = `nepal_japan_weather_cache`** (localStorage, D-078/D-002), holding a `Record<city, WeatherNow>` map (merge-on-set so cities don't evict each other); the gateway stays byte-transport-only (generic `<T>`, shape owned by `lib/weather.ts`). `goldenHour` and `weatherCodeToLabel` are pure (D-016). City→coords is a 2-entry const (Kathmandu, Tokyo), which S100 generalizes.
**Why:** the backlog flagged S99 as the sole slice needing an explicit D-088 provider check with an escalate-don't-adopt fallback. Open-Meteo qualifies cleanly, so it is recorded as the locked provider along with the new storage key, so future weather work doesn't re-litigate it or reach for a keyed provider.
**Changes if:** Open-Meteo adds a key or card requirement (escalate, re-evaluate a keyless alternative, and never adopt a card-on-file "free tier"), or weather needs data a keyless provider can't give (which may pressure D-004).

### D-109 · LOCKED (implementation note; S100, 2026-07-05) · Per-day trip-city map is trip config in `core/dates`; anti-drift vs the sample enforced by a unit test
The authoritative per-day city (`TRIP_CITIES: Record<'YYYY-MM-DD', string>` plus the pure, total `getCityForDate(date)`) lives in **`core/dates/trip-cities.ts`**, alongside `TRIP_DATES` and `getCountryForDate` (D-006 one-source-of-truth). It is not imported from `lib/sample-itinerary.ts`: that would invert the **D-099** layering, since `core/` must not depend on `lib/`, so the map's data is defined independently in core. The two former hardcode sites, `dayInTripFor` (`core/dates/day-in-trip.ts`) and `synthesizeDay` (`core/itinerary/crud.ts`), both call `getCityForDate`. The hero travel-mode label, the Today header, the synthesized day plan, and (via `city`) the weather card therefore share one per-day city source, and now surface the real day-trip city (Nagarkot, Bhaktapur, Lalitpur, Kyoto, Osaka, Hakone, Kawaguchiko, Yuzawa, Nikko, Yokohama) instead of collapsing to the base city. `getCityForDate` is **total**: an unmapped date falls back to the pre-S100 country default (`nepal ? 'Kathmandu' : 'Tokyo'`), preserving old behavior off the trip window. The **anti-drift invariant** — the map equals `SAMPLE_ITINERARY`'s per-day `city` for all 32 `TRIP_DATES` — is enforced by a unit test in `lib/__tests__/` (which may import both surfaces), so the two can never silently diverge. `lib/weather.ts`'s `CITY_COORDS` was extended from 2 to all 12 trip cities so every trip day is weather-queryable (a coverage test asserts `isKnownWeatherCity` for all 12). This is **S82-safe by construction:** the five S82-frozen boundary dates (Dec 9, 12, 18 → Kathmandu; Dec 19 and Jan 9 → Tokyo) are base-city days in the sample, so the map returns the exact frozen cities and the S82 E2E net stays green byte-for-byte. Only non-asserted day-trip dates change.
**Why:** S100 generalized the trip city beyond the Kathmandu/Tokyo hardcoding. Recording where the map lives (core, not lib) plus the anti-drift test contract keeps the layering discipline and the map-to-sample coupling from rotting in future slices (the budget work, the post-trip work) that also key off per-day city.
**Changes if:** the trip gains user-editable per-day cities (then the map becomes a default and the stored `DayPlan.city` wins, so revisit the anti-drift test), or a multi-trip refactor (D-006, proposal #13 Trip Packs) makes the single-trip city map one of many.

### D-110 · LOCKED (implementation note; S101, 2026-07-05) · Trip-budget domain: dual-currency, USD-anchored overridable rates, home-currency view, gateway key 10, offline-safe
The trip budget (proposal #12) is an **independent persisted domain, not the itinerary Vault.** It lives behind typed storage **gateway key 10**, `budget: 'nepal_japan_budget'` (localStorage, D-002), plus a byte-transport `budgetStore` accessor mirroring `weatherCache` (key 9). It is **additive, with no migration**, since the itinerary Vault envelope and migrations are itinerary-only. The pure model and math live in **`core/budget/model.ts`** (framework-free per D-099; `import type` of `ItineraryCategory` only, following the crud.ts precedent), and every function is **pure and total** (D-016): bad, NaN or negative input yields a safe 0 and never throws, so the panel can never render `NaN` and a corrupt slot never crashes. `normalizeModel` turns any parsed value into a valid model.

**Currency model:** two legs by country (D-012), with Nepal's local currency **NPR** and Japan's **JPY**. Each leg's budget and optional per-category budgets are entered and stored in that leg's **local** currency, with no per-amount currency tag. A single `homeCurrency` (default **USD**) is the display currency for roll-ups. **Rates are units of local currency per 1 USD** (USD is the fixed internal anchor, `rate(USD)=1`), so conversion routes local→USD→home and **switching the home currency is presentation-only and non-destructive**: stored amounts and rates never change. Rates are **build-seeded** (`{NPR:138, JPY:155}`, approximate, clearly labelled defaults with a Reset control) and **user-overridable by hand**. There are zero rate APIs and no runtime fetch (D-088/D-004, the free-tools-only rule).

Per-category budgets reuse the 10 canonical `ItineraryCategory` values, so S103's timeline and calendar cost overlays map with no remapping. The `rollUp(model, spent?)` shape carries an optional `spent` input (by-leg or by-category, in the same local-currency shape) and returns `budget`, `spent` and `remaining` per line plus the home-currency grand total. In S101 `spent` is absent, so `remaining === budget`; **S102 (expense logging) feeds the same shape with no reshape.** The panel mounts on `/plan` as a `dynamic(ssr:false)` island between CalendarPlanner and BackupRestore.
**Why:** proposal #12 spans three slices (S101 core → S102 expense logging → S103 burn-rate and overlays) that all share this data model. Recording the domain (gateway, not Vault), the USD-anchored reversible currency model, the seeded-overridable-no-API rate rule, and the `spent` seam keeps the three slices coherent and stops a future slice re-litigating currency handling or reaching for a rate API.
**Changes if:** we want a different home or entry currency model (cheap, since display is presentation-only over local-currency amounts), a real (still free and keyless) rate source is desired (a D-088 provider check like D-108, escalate rather than adopt), or a multi-trip refactor (#13) makes the two fixed legs one of many currency pairs.

### D-111 · LOCKED (implementation note; S102, 2026-07-05) · Expense-logging domain: gateway key 11, reactive hook, parallel `expense:open` fast-log flow (itinerary FAB untouched), `expensesToSpent` feeds the S101 rollup seam
Expense logging (proposal #12, S102) is an **independent persisted domain**: typed storage **gateway key 11**, `expenses: 'nepal_japan_expenses'` (localStorage D-002, additive `expensesStore`, no migration, not the itinerary Vault), storing an **`Expense[]`**. Each entry is `{ id, leg, category, amount, date?, note?, createdAt }` with `amount` in the leg's **local** currency (D-110), so the aggregator needs no conversion. The pure domain (`core/budget/expenses.ts`, framework-free per D-016/D-099: `import type` only from lib/model, plus a core→core runtime import of `model.ts`'s `safeAmount` and `BUDGET_CATEGORIES`) is **total**: `sanitizeExpense`/`sanitizeExpenses` drop unsalvageable entries and a bad amount becomes 0. `id` and `createdAt` are **caller-injected** so the CRUD transforms stay deterministic (the D-041/D-099 injection pattern).

**`expensesToSpent(expenses): SpentInput`** is the sole consumer of S101's `rollUp(model, spent?)` seam. The budget panel now calls `rollUp(model, expensesToSpent(expenses))` and shows spent and remaining per leg, per (leg, category), and as a grand total with an over-budget cue, with **no reshape of the rollup**.

Reactivity: **`hooks/use-expenses.ts`** mirrors `use-itinerary.ts`'s CustomEvent (`expenses:changed`) plus cross-tab `storage` reactivity, minus sync, attribution and tombstones, since expenses are private and single-user. A log from the global dialog updates the panel live, and the id is generated at the adapter boundary (browser `Date.now` plus random).

**The fast-log flow is parallel to the itinerary quick-add FAB, which is left single-purpose (S63/D-071):** a new `expense:open` event, `components/expense-log-host.tsx` (mounted in `app/layout.tsx` beside `QuickAddHost`, `dynamic ssr:false`), and `components/expense-dialog.tsx` (the full modal contract D-021/D-068/D-069). **Sub-5s** logging comes from amount autofocus (`inputMode="decimal"`, Enter saves), one-tap category chips, a preset leg (`getTodayInTrip()?.country` → selected-day → first-trip-day), and Save. Amount and category are the only required fields.
**Why:** #12's middle slice. Recording the key-11 domain, the `expensesToSpent`→`rollUp` seam (so S103 burn-rate and overlays consume the same computed spent/remaining plus the raw dated `Expense[]` with no reshape), and the deliberate choice to keep the itinerary FAB single-purpose (a parallel expense trigger rather than an overload) stops S103 or a fast-follow re-litigating any of it.
**Changes if:** true one-tap in-trip logging from Home is wanted (add a Today-panel "Log expense" affordance, or a second mobile FAB emitting the same `expense:open` event — a fast-follow, deliberately not in S102), or expenses need attribution or sync for shared trips (which would pressure D-002/D-004, so escalate).

### D-112 · Superseded · Burn-rate + calendar cost overlay derive over the S101/S102 seams; the calendar overlay is a read-only additive D-018-respecting display

### D-113 · LOCKED (implementation note; S104, 2026-07-06) · In-trip journal domain: gateway key 12, localStorage-only per-day text journal (at most 1 entry per date); photos and IndexedDB are out
The in-trip journal (proposal #11, S104, the L-core) is the **fourth additive persisted data domain**, built on the identical pattern to weather (key 9, S99), budget (key 10, S101) and expenses (key 11, S102): a pure framework-free core (`core/journal/model.ts`, D-016/D-099, every function total, caller-injected timestamps), a thin sanitize-on-read/write adapter (`core/journal/storage.ts`) over typed storage **gateway key 12**, `journal: 'nepal_japan_journal'` (`journalStore`, byte-transport only, D-097), and a reactive hook (`hooks/use-journal.ts`, the D-026 idiom: `journal:changed` CustomEvent plus cross-tab `storage`, freshest-state commit, timestamp injected at the boundary).

**Shape:** `JournalEntry { date: 'YYYY-MM-DD', text, mood?: 'great'|'good'|'okay'|'rough', highlight?, createdAt, updatedAt }` — **one entry per trip day, keyed by date** (upsert; `sanitizeEntries` dedupes last-write-wins so a corrupt slot can never surface two entries for one day). Content is non-empty if and only if there is trimmed text, a valid mood, or a trimmed highlight. **Clearing all content removes the entry**, so the "delete everything and save" path lands on a clean empty state with no phantom re-seed (D-018). Patch semantics: `mood`/`highlight` = `null` clears the field, `undefined` leaves it unchanged.

The capture surface (`components/journal-card.tsx`: mood chips with `aria-pressed`, a highlight input, a multiline body, and Save/Cancel/Edit, a11y AA per D-100, reduced-motion-safe CSS-only transitions, glass-card language) mounts inside the in-trip Today panel below the agenda, the only change to `today-panel.tsx`. It is therefore intrinsically in-trip-gated, rendering only inside the Dec 9 – Jan 9 window, and demoable via `?today=` (D-075). It is **localStorage-only (D-002 intact), with no backend (D-004) and no new dependency (D-088)**; additive, not part of the itinerary Vault, no migration, no `CURRENT_ITINERARY_VERSION` bump.

**Photos, IndexedDB and rich stories are explicitly out.** The XL photo/story phase of #11 is a declared future milestone requiring a D-002 amendment (localStorage-as-source-of-truth → local-first including IndexedDB), and keeping the journal localStorage-only is precisely why that phase is held out. S105 (plan-vs-actual day recap) consumes this via the pure per-day `getEntry` seam.
**Why:** completes proposal #11's L-core on the proven additive-domain pattern. Recording the key-12 domain, the at-most-one-entry-per-date and clear-removes invariants, and the photos-out boundary keeps the persistence guarantee and the D-097 storage-literal invariant structural, and stops S105 or a future photo phase re-litigating any of it.
**Changes if:** the photo/story phase is greenlit (that introduces IndexedDB or blob storage — a new decision plus a D-002 amendment, not a mutation of this one), or the journal moves to a Home/off-trip entry point or to server persistence (a backend greenlight, D-004, so escalate).

### D-114 · Superseded · Read-only plan-vs-actual day-recap island: derives over S98 done-state + S104 journal getEntry; in-trip + post-trip

### D-115 · Superseded · Min supported browsers = modern evergreen; IE11 dropped. FU-17 finding: Next injects the ~112 KB polyfills chunk unconditionally, not browserslist-driven, so a polyfill strip needs a separate gated slice

### D-116 · LOCKED (implementation note; S107, 2026-07-06) · Below-the-fold lazy-island pattern for Home: cuts Home First Load JS 205 → 91 kB with no CLS
Home's four below-the-fold sections (`TripDashboard`, `FlightsSection`, `TripTimeline`, `TravelEssentials`) are deferred via `components/lazy-visible.tsx`, a client wrapper that renders a sized `SectionSkeleton` placeholder on first paint and mounts the real section only on a trigger: near-viewport visibility (`react-intersection-observer`'s `useInView`, `rootMargin: 600px`, `triggerOnce` — an already-present dependency per D-088, so no new dependency), or a post-hydration `requestIdleCallback` fallback (`setTimeout` on Safari).

**Two structural requirements make the chunks genuinely split**, and this is the non-obvious part: a `dynamic(ssr:false)` at page-module scope in the App Router is inlined into the page chunk and does not drop from First Load JS on its own. (a) The section is passed to `LazyVisible` as a **component reference, not JSX children**, because JSX children evaluate in the parent render and re-preload the chunk. (b) `app/page.tsx` is a **client component** (`'use client'`), because a server component cannot pass a component reference across the server→client boundary. Both are behaviorally inert here, since every Home section was already `dynamic(ssr:false)` (client-only) so the page emitted almost no server HTML; the route stays `○ Static` (SSG intact, verified by the green static export and the `/ 3.05 kB` route line).

**Kept eager, not gated:** `HeroSection` (above the fold, LCP), `TodayPanel` and `TripRecap` (near-top, null pre-trip), and `LegacyHashRedirect`. The last one is never gated on visibility because it is a behavioral hash→route redirect effect with no visible box that has to run regardless of scroll. The idle fallback is intentional and does not cost First Load JS (the metric is the initial preload tree, which the deferred chunks never enter); it guarantees the sections mount a beat after hydration even without scroll, which is what keeps the no-scroll frozen E2E assertions (`persistence` packing toggle, `countdown`, `today`, `recap`) green with **zero frozen-spec edits**. Result: Home First Load JS **205 → 91.2 kB** (page chunk 104 → 8.2 kB), other routes roughly unchanged (±1–2 kB shared-hash drift). 18 visual baselines pass (Hero at scroll=0), and reserved `min-height`s plus the 600px lead prevent CLS.
**Why:** with S106's polyfill win proven unachievable via browserslist (D-115), this is the slice that actually moves Home's number: it delivers proposal #14's "halve Home's shipped JS" target on its own. Recording the reference-passing, client-page and trigger mechanism stops a future slice re-discovering (the hard way) that naive page-scope `dynamic` doesn't split in the App Router, and records why `LegacyHashRedirect` stays eager.
**Changes if:** the pattern is extended to `/plan` or the destination pages (a new slice; `LazyVisible` is generic and reusable, and was left out there to keep the fence tight), or a Next version changes how page-scope dynamic imports are chunked.
**Floor update (S115, `be7e8b3`):** Home First Load JS is now **≈90.5 kB** (was 91.2). `react-intersection-observer` was genuinely in Home's initial chunk, because `LazyVisible` renders its placeholder on first paint so the library's bytes counted toward First Load JS. S115 replaced `useInView` with a ~20-line inline native `IntersectionObserver` hook (no new deps, D-088; behavior identical) which is smaller than the library, shaving ~0.7 kB. The D-116 lever is intact: shared chunks unchanged (88.2 kB), and no section leaked back into the preload manifest. **Track the floor as ≈90.5 kB** so a future build doesn't misread it as a regression.
**Floor update 2 (S116, `33306e8`, Next 15):** Home First Load JS is now **≈106 kB** (from 90.5). This is the **Next 15 framework-runtime baseline**: the shared First-Load chunk grew 88.2 → 104 kB (framework chunk ~32 → ~46 kB, react-dom unchanged), uniformly across all 7 routes, inherent to the Next major and not reclaimable without leaving Next 15. **The D-116 lever is not regressed:** Home's page-specific JS is still tiny (2.48 kB) and the below-fold sections are still deferred via `LazyVisible`. Sub-pages actually got *lighter* (japan, nepal, plan, map went from 177–192 kB to 106 kB) because the D-131 island extraction deferred chunks Next 14 had been preloading. Accepted as within the G3/D-130-approved Next 15 envelope (D-129). **Track the floor as ≈106 kB.** Flagged for visibility: this is a framework-cost baseline shift, not a mechanism regression.

### D-117 · Superseded · One form stack = zod validation + hand-rolled forms; formik/yup/react-hook-form/@hookform/resolvers removed as unused

### D-118 · Superseded · One date stack = date-fns sole date library; dayjs/react-datepicker/react-day-picker removed

### D-119 · LOCKED (implementation note; S110-FIX, 2026-07-06) · Cross-day move under sync = tombstone-source + fresh-id-target (D-032 generalized to drag-move)
With sync on, `moveItem` (`hooks/use-itinerary.ts`) performs, in one commit, a tombstone-stamp of the source-day copy (exactly like `removeItem`) plus an add of a **fresh-id** copy on the target day. A physical cross-day remove has no tombstone, so the Sync v2 union merge resurrects the source copy and the item duplicates onto both days for every friend (S110 TL-A P1-1, repro-proven). The fresh target id is load-bearing: reusing the id would collide with its own tombstone on a later move-back. The dormant path stays the plain physical move (byte-identical, D-038). Regression net: `lib/__tests__/use-itinerary-move-sync.test.ts`, mutation-proven non-vacuous.
**Why:** the dialog date-change already followed this rule (D-032), but the drag path predated Sync v2 and was never generalized. Any future path that relocates an item across days has to tombstone the source under sync.
**Changes if:** Sync v2's merge stops being a union (a new decision), or true id-preserving moves land in the merge protocol.

### D-120 · LOCKED (S110-FIX, 2026-07-06) · The remote push gate requires an identified traveler; D-055 made structural
`pushPlans` (`lib/itinerary-remote.ts`) gates on `isRemoteConfigured() && getActiveTraveler()`. Config alone is not sufficient: without the traveler check, any guest on the public deployment could push, and their first edit would union-merge sample-day content into the friends' live day-doc (S110 TL-A P1-2, a LOCKED D-055 violation). `getActiveTraveler` comes from the firebase-free `lib/token-auth`, so dormant-safety (D-038/D-047) is unaffected. Regression net: `lib/__tests__/itinerary-remote-guest-gate.test.ts`, mutation-proven, since dropping the check fails the suite. Note that this is the client-side guarantee only. The server-side rules exposure is a separate, separately-owned decision: `firestore.rules` currently allows any anonymous user, and the uid-allowlist drafted at `firestore.rules:36-43` awaits a manual deploy per D-044.
**Why:** D-055's "guest edits are never pushed" was convention, not structure, and one missing check made it false.
**Changes if:** the auth model changes (real sign-in), or rules-level enforcement lands and supersedes the client gate as the primary barrier.

### D-121 · LOCKED (S110-FIX, 2026-07-06) · Backup Restore is local-mode-only while sync is active, until a tombstone-replace slice lands
On a synced and identified build (`isRemoteConfigured() && getActiveTraveler()`), the Restore/import action in `components/backup-restore.tsx` is disabled with explanatory copy. The reason (S110 TL-A P1-3): `importItinerary` is an ingest-style path that correctly never pushes, per D-039. On a synced build a "successful" restore therefore propagated to no one, was union-merge-resurrected by the next snapshot, and was wholesale reverted by the first-snapshot-authoritative apply on reload, all while the UI claimed it had replaced the trip for everyone. The false module comment in `core/vault/export-import.ts` is corrected, and the import logic itself is unchanged, so dormant and guest builds keep full Restore. A true synced replace requires tombstoning every dropped id before push: a flagged follow-up slice, not an improvisation.
**Why:** an enabled control that silently unwinds is worse than a disabled one that says why.
**Changes if:** the tombstone-replace slice lands, at which point Restore re-enables under sync and this entry is superseded.

### D-122 · LOCKED (implementation notes bundle; S110-FIX, 2026-07-06) · S110 UI/infra conventions: heading-id styling hook · muted-text floor · sonner mobile override · gateway key 13 · in-trip axe net
(a) **The `-heading` id suffix is a styling hook** (`app/globals.css`'s `h2[id$="-heading"]::after` paints the centered gold dash) reserved for centered section titles; left-aligned card headings use a `-title` suffix (today, recap, budget-panel and backup-restore were renamed at S110).

(b) **Muted-content floor on the navy/glass surface = `text-white/55`** (≈5.3–5.9:1), with interactive labels at `/50` or above plus a hover raise. This extends D-100: `white/25`–`/45` computed 2.2–4.2:1, below AA.

(c) **Sonner 1.5.0 mobile-bottom override:** sonner's own ≤600px layout hard-sets `bottom:20px` and its `--mobile-offset` is left/right-only, so the tab-bar clearance is enforced by a scoped `!important` rule in `globals.css` pinned to sonner 1.5.0's emitted attributes. Retire that override in any future sonner-upgrade slice, since later majors have a real `mobileOffset` prop.

(d) **Typed-gateway key 13 `chunk_reload_once`** (sessionStorage): the chunk-load error handler auto-reloads at most once per session as a loop guard, routed through the gateway per D-097.

(e) **The in-trip axe net is permanent:** `e2e/a11y-intrip.spec.ts` scans `?today=` in-trip surfaces with seeded journal and expense data. It was added because the route-level axe pack structurally cannot see in-trip islands (they render null without `?today=`) nor below-fold opacity-0 reveals; M15 reveal variants were aligned to the D-100 full-opacity-slide convention at S110 for the same reason.
**Why:** each of these was a silent convention someone violated or a blind spot the S110 sweep proved; recording them stops recurrence.
**Changes if:** per item — the CSS hook is redesigned; the AA bar is relaxed (unlikely, D-007); sonner is upgraded; the SW or chunk strategy changes; the a11y harness is redesigned.

### D-123 · LOCKED (implementation note; S111, 2026-07-06) · Return-home flight (Delta 274 + Delta 1689, Tokyo HND → Syracuse SYR, Sat Jan 9 2027) is now booked; `flight-home` graduates from `JAPAN_TODO` to a real `Journey`
`lib/booking-data.ts` gains `FLIGHT_HOME_JOURNEY` (id `flight-home`, reusing the placeholder's id per the D-036/D-034 convention), appended to `JOURNEYS` after `RETURN_TO_JAPAN_JOURNEY`. The matching `ToBookPlaceholder` entry is removed from `JAPAN_TODO`, leaving only `japan-hotels`, since Japan lodging is still unbooked. `components/flights-section.tsx` needed zero changes: it already maps both arrays generically by content, not by count. Naming precedent for any future journey: a `<NAME>_JOURNEY` const, with `home-N` or leg-style short ids following the existing `out-N`/`ret-N` pattern.
**Why:** D-034's booking-data module is read-only presentation data reflecting real trip facts. Once a real confirmation exists, the placeholder graduates to a real entry rather than staying flagged "to be booked".
**Changes if:** never. This is a fixed reference fact once Delta's booking is confirmed, short of a schedule change from the airline, which would just update the same record.

### D-124 · LOCKED (S112, 2026-07-07) · AMENDED by D-126 (2026-07-10) · Japan leg rerouted: Osaka (5N, Dec 19–24) → Kyoto (4N, Dec 24–28) → Tokyo (12N, Dec 28–Jan 9), replacing the Tokyo-based day-trip structure
Per the new trip brief (the original trip spec, a 3-guys nightlife-first trip), the whole Japan portion of `core/dates/trip-cities.ts` and `lib/sample-itinerary.ts` was replaced. Osaka and Kyoto are now genuine multi-night base-city legs rather than day trips, and the old Tokyo-based day trips to Hakone, Kawaguchiko, Yuzawa, Nikko and Yokohama are gone entirely. `JAPAN_START`, `JAPAN_END`, `TRIP_START`, `TRIP_END` (D-006) and `TRIP_DATES` (32 entries) are unchanged: only which city and activities are assigned to each already-existing date changed. Transfer days use the arrival city per D-030 (Dec 24 → Kyoto, Dec 28 → Tokyo).

Two inconsistencies in the source brief were reconciled (2026-07-07). (1) **Kyoto is 4 nights**, not the spec summary table's "3": its own day-by-day content runs Dec 24 (arrival) through Dec 27 ("pack and prepare for Tokyo transfer"), unambiguously 4 nights. (2) **Tokyo is 12 nights** (Dec 28–Jan 9), not the spec’s "11 nights (Dec 28–Jan 8)", extended one night by an explicit choice to match the already-booked real return flight (D-123: departs Haneda 5:35pm Sat Jan 9). That added one new, brief-unscripted day: **Jan 8 = Tokyo DisneySea plus one more night out**, since the brief only scripted through "Jan 7: final big night". Departure logistics moved to Jan 9 and are now rendered from the real `FLIGHT_HOME_JOURNEY` data, replacing stale "to be booked" placeholder items.

Other content judgment calls made during S112, recorded here for the trail: Jan 6 flex day → TeamLab; Jan 4 daytime → Tokyo Tower/Roppongi Hills, to avoid duplicating TeamLab; Dec 27 Kyoto flex day → Kyoto International Manga Museum (new guide card `ja31`); Dec 23 Osaka flex day → Shinsekai + Spa World, chosen over a Kobe day trip to avoid extra travel logistics before the Kyoto transfer. New guide cards added: `ja28`–`ja31` (USJ/Super Nintendo World, Den Den Town, Osaka Castle, Kyoto Manga Museum). New nightlife venues added: `nl11`–`nl16` (Osaka: Namba/Dotonbori, Shinsaibashi, Amerikamura; Kyoto: Kiyamachi-dori, Pontocho, Gion), all legal venues and districts only, with no Tobita Shinchi card, per the spec's own legal-only boundary. `JAPAN_TODO` in `lib/booking-data.ts` was restructured into 3 honest placeholders: Osaka and Kyoto read "already booked, details pending" with no invented hotel name or address per D-034, and Tokyo reads "not booked yet", carrying the spec’s ranked hotel shortlist as reference notes.

**Open item flagged, not resolved by this decision:** the already-booked `RETURN_TO_JAPAN_JOURNEY` (Kathmandu → Tokyo Haneda) lands in Tokyo, but the new itinerary now starts in Osaka on Dec 19. Rather than invent a fake domestic flight or train, Dec 19's itinerary item honestly reads as an unbooked "Haneda → Osaka domestic transfer" (~3h, mode to be confirmed). This is a real logistics question, not a code defect: either accept the domestic-transfer day, or look into whether that connecting flight could still be rebooked into Kansai/Osaka directly. It is a travel-booking call, not an engineering one.
**Why:** honors the explicit re-scoping of the Japan leg while keeping the trip's overall length and every already-booked flight fact untouched.
**Changes if:** the return-to-Japan flight is rebooked into Kansai/Osaka (then Dec 19's itinerary drops the domestic-transfer item), or a different Jan 6, Jan 8, Dec 23 or Dec 27 flex-day option is picked than the ones listed above.

### D-125 · LOCKED (S113, 2026-07-07) · Nightlife guide section is now visibility-gated on a real Trip Token; a guest sees no trace of it
`components/nightlife-section.tsx` (mounted on `/nepal` and `/japan` only, **not** `/`, correcting a wrong premise in the S113 brief and verified against `app/page.tsx`'s actual section list) now reads `const { traveler } = useActiveTraveler();`, and its existing SSR/hydration guard changed from `if (!mounted) return null;` to `if (!mounted || !traveler) return null;`. A guest who bypassed the Trip Token wall without a real token gets zero DOM trace of the section: no heading, no toggle, nothing. A signed-in traveler (one of the 3 named `Traveler`s) sees it exactly as before. No other line in the file changed, and no existing auth infrastructure (`lib/token-auth.ts`, `hooks/use-active-traveler.ts`, `core/storage/gateway.ts`, `components/token-gate.tsx`) was touched: this reuses the exact D-055/D-120 "add one predicate to an existing choke point" shape, applied to a render boundary instead of a sync effect. A new E2E spec, `e2e/nightlife-gate.spec.ts`, proves both branches on real rendered DOM (guest → count 0 on both routes; signed-in → visible, with toggle and venue cards still working) and confirms zero collateral hiding, since recommendations, photography and essentials sections render identically for both identities. The 18 `visual.spec.ts` baselines needed no regeneration, verified rather than assumed: that spec screenshots isolated element locators (hero, header, footer) and never full pages, and nightlife was never one of them.
**Why:** this is a **soft, UI-level visibility gate per D-053, not real access control.** The section's code and venue data still ship in the static bundle for every visitor, guest included, and the gate is a client-side conditional render, spoofable like every other Trip Token surface: clearing `tripPlannerGuest` and setting `tripPlannerToken` in devtools bypasses it, same as any other soft gate in this app. The request (2026-07-07) was explicitly about visibility, not security.
**Changes if:** a real backend-greenlit auth layer is ever added (D-053's own "flag it, do not build it" backend-enforcement note), at which point this could move server-side. It is not needed for the current ask.

### D-126 · LOCKED (S113C, 2026-07-10) · AMENDS D-124 · Kyoto is really 3 nights (Dec 24–27), not 4; Tokyo grows to 13 nights (Dec 27–Jan 9)
S113B rendered the real Hotel Forza Kyoto Shijo Kawaramachi booking verbatim (`KYOTO_STAY.note: '3 nights · 3 adults · 3 rooms'`) without resolving the conflict against D-124's locked 4-night Kyoto span; it was flagged rather than silently corrected. The real booking is now confirmed: check-in Thu Dec 24 2:00pm, **check-out Sun Dec 27 11:00am**, unambiguously 3 nights. `core/dates/trip-cities.ts`'s `TRIP_CITIES['2026-12-27']` changes from `'Kyoto'` to `'Tokyo'`, moving the Kyoto→Tokyo transfer day from Dec 28 to Dec 27, arrival city per D-030, the same convention as Dec 24's Osaka→Kyoto transfer. In `lib/sample-itinerary.ts`, Dec 27 becomes the real checkout (11:00) plus Shinkansen plus Tokyo hotel check-in plus arrival-evening day, which is the content that used to live on Dec 28, retimed to start after the real checkout rather than the old assumed 09:30. Dec 28 gets a brand-new "classic Tokyo" day (Senso-ji/Nakamise, Ueno Park/Ameyoko, an easy Shinjuku evening) since the old arrival-day content moved off it, and that theme is not duplicated anywhere else in the file. The anti-drift test (`lib/__tests__/trip-cities.test.ts`) and the day-trip-city E2E spec (`e2e/today-next.spec.ts`, whose Kyoto-day fixture moved from Dec 27, now Tokyo, to Dec 26, still genuinely Kyoto) were updated in lockstep. `JAPAN_START`, `JAPAN_END`, `TRIP_START`, `TRIP_END` (D-006) and `TRIP_DATES` (32 entries) are unchanged; Osaka's dates and content (Dec 19–24) and everything from Dec 29 onward are byte-unchanged. Only which city owns Dec 27 and Dec 28 changed. `lib/booking-data.ts` needed no change, already correct from S113B.
**Why:** the itinerary has to agree with the real, confirmed hotel booking. A locked planning assumption made before the real confirmation existed is exactly the kind of thing that should yield to the real fact once we have it, per the same discipline that governs every other real-world data flag in this project.
**Changes if:** never. This is a fixed reference fact once the hotel stay is confirmed, short of actually rebooking a different span.

### D-129 · LOCKED (2026-07-10) · All commit gates skipped; the work runs autonomously and commits on the implementer's own self-verify evidence
The direct instruction of 2026-07-10, in its verbatim intent: we are skipping all the review gates, the work should run autonomously without per-slice input, and it has permission to make changes to this project as it sees fit. This supersedes both the original two-review flow and D-089's standing single-review rule. The flow is now: brief → build and self-verify → commit directly. The house net (tsc, build, Vitest, Playwright) with pasted evidence is still required for every piece of work; the evidence habit survives even though the independent reviewers are stood down. Project decisions are made without waiting for sign-off too, including the v4 G1–G5 gate picks (see D-130), escalating only genuinely irreversible, paid, or external-account actions. The free-tools-only standing rule (D-088) is unchanged and still binds everything.

The `docs/V4-DEVPLAN.md` section 7 gates, resolved under the D-129 autonomy grant:
