It requires creating external accounts (Cloudflare) and standing up a public endpoint, an outward-facing, irreversible-class action under D-129's own escalation carve-out. The plan's own fallback (a client-side rule-based suggester) stands available but is not scheduled; Phase 4's G1 track is dropped from M17. It can be reopened with one word.
- **G2 (D-002 amendment — local-first storage now includes IndexedDB for photo blobs via `BlobStorePort`): APPROVED,** with the plan's narrowed scope confirmed (the text-only post-trip recap S156 is ungated and ships regardless). Client-side only, free by construction, D-038-style privacy: photos never leave the device. S159–S160 should land before Dec 9, since photos are captured during the trip. D-002 is hereby amended accordingly.
- **G3 (D-077 revisit — platform majors): APPROVED.** S116 (Next 14→15) → S117 (React 18.2→19) → S118 (framer-motion 10→12 plus maplibre 4→5), one major family per slice, full net between, FU-17 byte-hash proof required. **Stop at Next 15** for v4 (the plan's default; no 15→16 follow-on this milestone). The S116 SW-coupling stop-and-escalate rule stands, since an SW strategy change is an architecture decision rather than in-slice improvisation.
- **G4 (Trip Packs multi-trip engine): DEFERRED to v5,** endorsing the plan: the S121–S122 content layer covers the real editability pain without touching LOCKED D-006.
- **G5 (firestore.rules uid-allowlist, FU-18/D-044): stays manual, re-escalated rather than absorbed.** It needs the 3 friends' live anonymous uids and a manual deploy per D-044, neither of which can be obtained here. It remains the one open P1, outranking all of v4. Warning: any anonymous internet user can currently read, write or delete the shared trip data.

Ancillary section 9 picks under the same authority: Next 16 = no (stop at 15) · packing-list sync = **moot**, since S113D deletes the packing feature and gateway key 6 entirely, so the S139 blueprint must note the key's retirement rather than price its sync · Concierge no-card fallback = moot while G1 is declined · the FU-1 courtesy flag is satisfied by the D-129 autonomy, so S120 proceeds · FU-6 (Home PageHero parity) stays unscheduled.
**Why:** unblocks the entire M17 build per the "start v4" go-ahead, while keeping the two genuinely externally-owned items (backend activation, Firebase rules deploy) explicitly on the desk instead of silently decided.
**Changes if:** any pick is overridden (a one-line say-so suffices; G1 especially is reversible into Phase 4 as planned).

### D-131 · LOCKED (implementation note; S116 `33306e8`, Next 15 migration) · `dynamic({ssr:false})` islands used by a Server Component live in a colocated `'use client'` module
Next 15 disallows `next/dynamic` with `ssr:false` inside a Server Component (this was D-077's #2 rejection reason). The app's page/layout files are Server Components (they export `metadata`/`viewport`, so `'use client'` on the file itself is impossible) that compose `ssr:false` client islands (D-070). **Pattern:** extract each such island's `dynamic(() => import(...), {ssr:false, loading:…})` declaration into a colocated `'use client'` module (`app/<route>/sections.tsx`, and `app/chrome-islands.tsx` for the layout), then have the server file `import { X } from './sections'` and render it in the same tree position with the same props and the same `loading:` skeleton. Behavior is preserved: the island is still `ssr:false`, still chunk-split, still dormant-safe (D-047). Applied to all 6 server pages/layout in S116 (`nepal/japan/map/plan/flights/page.tsx` + `layout.tsx`). `app/page.tsx` (Home) was already `'use client'`, so its D-116 critical path was untouched. Verified: 7 routes `○ Static`, dormant Playwright 136/5 with pixel-identical visual snapshots, SW precache coupling (D-073) intact.
**Why:** it is the minimal, behavior-preserving way to keep the D-070 server-page + `ssr:false`-island composition under Next 15's Server Component rules. Recording the convention means every new page added in v4 feature work (S121+) follows it instead of re-hitting the build error.
**Changes if:** a page legitimately becomes a client component for another reason (then its islands can inline again), or a future Next relaxes the Server-Component `ssr:false` rule.

### D-132 · LOCKED (implementation note; S117 `20f150d`, React 19 major) · React 18.2 → 19.2.7 adopted; framer-motion 12 pulled forward into S117; S118 rescoped to maplibre-only
Under G3/D-130, S117 landed `react`+`react-dom` 18.2.0 → 19.2.7 (`@types/react` 19.2.17, `@types/react-dom` 19.2.3) on the Next-15.5.20 tree. framer-motion 10.18.0 → 12.42.2 was pulled forward from S118 into S117 (the plan's pre-authorized escape hatch), because framer 10 is genuinely non-functional under `@types/react@19` rather than merely peer-warning: framer 10's `HTMLMotionProps<T>` indexes `React.ReactHTML`, the DOM factory-map interface removed in `@types/react@19`, which collapses every `motion.*` element's HTML props to `unknown` (64 `tsc` errors, 61 from this). No shim keeps framer 10 cleanly; downgrading the types isn't possible (the React 19 runtime needs 19 types); the DoD requires `tsc` exit 0. framer 12 (pre-approved in D-130) cleared it with zero app API-break edits. The full API surface used here (`m`/`LazyMotion`/`domAnimation` per D-116, `MotionConfig`, `AnimatePresence`, `useReducedMotion`, `useInView`, `useScroll`/`useSpring`/`useTransform`, `Variants`) is unchanged in v12; reduced-motion (D-100) re-verified 6/6, 18 visual baselines 0-drift (no regen). Consequence: S118 is now `maplibre-gl` 4.7.1 → 5 only (framer is already done) and must be re-scoped accordingly. The only app-code change in S117: `components/journal-card.tsx` ref prop `RefObject<HTMLInputElement>` → `<… | null>`, the canonical React 19 `useRef(null)` migration. The 43 Radix `ui/` wrappers, the hand-rolled dialogs, the `LazyVisible` callback-ref hook and the SW registrar were tsc-clean and untouched. Install mode stays `--legacy-peer-deps` (pre-existing since Next 15 / a `@typescript-eslint` conflict, not React-caused); no `.npmrc` added, no new deps. Home First Load JS held at the ≈106 kB post-S116 floor.
**Why:** completes G3 major #2. It records that framer 12 is already landed (so S118 doesn't re-attempt it) and preserves the exact type-system reason for the forced ride-along, so nobody later puzzles over why framer moved in the "React" slice.
**Changes if:** settled adoption; the escape hatch is spent. S118 proceeds as maplibre-only.

### D-133 · LOCKED (implementation note; S118 `73610b0`, maplibre major) · maplibre-gl 4.7.1 → 5.24.0 adopted; the G3 platform-majors track is closed
S118 landed `maplibre-gl` 4.7.1 → 5.24.0 (latest stable v5; `6.0.0-20` is pre-release and was not taken), maplibre only, since framer 12 was pulled into S117 per D-132. The v5 major is fully API-compatible for our map surface with zero source changes: every API `components/map-section.tsx` uses (`new Map({style,bounds,fitBoundsOptions,attributionControl,maxZoom,minZoom})`, `AttributionControl`/`NavigationControl`/`GeolocateControl`, `addControl`, `addSource` geojson+cluster, `addLayer` circle/symbol/line, `Popup`, `GeoJSONSource.getClusterExpansionZoom` promise form, `setData`, the `on(...)` events) is present in the v5 `.d.ts`. Style-spec `version:8` is still valid (the two `as never` casts still absorb the boundary typing, so `lib/map-style.ts` was untouched), and flat mercator stays the default (globe is opt-in and unused). The CSS import `maplibre-gl/dist/maplibre-gl.css` still resolves. The D-047 dormant-lazy pattern is intact: runtime stays behind `await import`, and `/map` First Load JS is identical at 106 kB before and after, proof that the ~200 kB runtime never joined the initial bundle. Verified: tsc clean, Vitest 400/400, build 7 routes Static, dormant Playwright 136/5 with the `/map` visual baseline 0-drift at all 3 breakpoints plus a map smoke run (no console errors). Two-file change (package.json + lockfile).
This closes gate G3 (D-130): the full platform floor is now Next 15.5.20 · React 19.2.7 · framer-motion 12.42.2 · maplibre-gl 5.24.0, each landed one-major-per-slice with the full net green between (S116→S117→S118). FU-17 (polyfill chunk) remains the one open platform follow-up.
**Why:** completes G3 major #3/3 and records that the map upgrade needed no code, so it is clear later that the v4 map subsystem is already on v5 and API-clean.
**Changes if:** maplibre v6 is later wanted (a new slice; v6 was pre-release at S118 and explicitly not taken).

### D-134 · LOCKED (implementation note; S119 `3aecb83`) · FU-14 version-bump backstop gate: success-only git-tag marker in `deploy.yml`
The deploy workflow (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`) has a `version-gate` job that fails the pipeline iff a git tag `v<version>` already exists for the version in `nextjs_space/package.json` (`git rev-parse -q --verify refs/tags/v<version>`). The marker tag is written by the deploy job only after `actions/deploy-pages@v4` succeeds, so it records a genuinely-live version and never a failed attempt. Wired via `build: needs: [test, version-gate]`; the deploy job gains job-level `permissions: contents: write` (restating `pages`/`id-token`) solely to push the marker tag. Semantics: it compares the deploying version to the last successfully-deployed version, not to HEAD~1. Bumps are per-milestone and the version is stable across many commits, so a HEAD~1 gate would false-fail nearly every deploy. It also handles the documented failed-deploy re-trigger (`d89a6bc`): a failed attempt writes no tag, so a same-version re-run passes. It asserts inequality only, and never picks, writes or increments a version (D-102: bumps are done by hand). Backstops FU-13's "every deploy bumps" ritual. Extends D-101. Runner-less, the honest limit from D-101: the workflow has never executed (no remote, no runner), and the `git rev-parse` predicate was verified locally on 3 cases (tag-exists→FAIL, bumped→PASS, failed-retrigger→PASS), twice and independently. The source is dormant until the mirror sync runs (D-033/D-046): authoring the gate does not activate it, and the real deploy runs from `Powan55/trip_planner`.
**Why:** makes the "never ship the same version twice" ritual a mechanical gate instead of discipline, without ever auto-bumping (which D-102 forbids). The success-only tag is the simplest reliable marker: zero extra tracked files, idempotent, intrinsically success-gated.
**Changes if:** the mirror-sync/deploy topology changes (the marker basis may move to the GH deployment API), or `contents: write` on the deploy job is judged too broad (swap to a `DEPLOYED_VERSION`-file or deployment-record marker; that is a call for us to make, and the widening is flagged in the S119 notes).

### D-135 · LOCKED (proposed S121, recorded 2026-07-10) · Trip-content layer — `core/content/` authoring root + strict schemas + `validate:content`; seed swaps never rewrite live data
The trip's editable content is unified into a schema-validated content layer. (1) The day-by-day plan content moves verbatim to `core/content/itinerary.ts` (`TRIP_ITINERARY: DayPlan[]`, framework-free, with a type-only `@/lib/trip-data` import). That follows the shipped 9-file core precedent, and it records the accepted reading of D-099: runtime `core→lib` imports are forbidden, erased type-only imports are allowed. `lib/sample-itinerary.ts` becomes a one-line delegate re-export (`SAMPLE_ITINERARY` ≡ the same object, the S90/S93 pattern), so the Vault fallback wiring (D-095, `VaultConfig.fallback`) and every caller are untouched. (2) Guide/nightlife/photography/tips/booking content deliberately stays in `lib/*-data.ts`: nothing in core consumes it, D-034 names `lib/booking-data.ts` as the bookings home, and each domain is already one-file-per-edit. The layer unifies them through `core/content/schema.ts` (strict authoring schemas: `.strict()` objects, `z.enum` categories, ISO-date regex) and `npm run validate:content`. Those authoring schemas are deliberately the opposite tolerance of the Vault's lenient read schemas, which validate user data and must never be merged with these. `validate:content` is a Vitest suite (`lib/__tests__/content-validation.test.ts`; vitest is the installed TS runner, and a node script would need tsx, a new dep, which D-088 rules out) checking every domain's schema plus the cross-content invariants: exact 32-`TRIP_DATES` coverage, `country` agrees with `getCountryForDate`, globally-unique item ids, weather-known cities, guide categories ∈ filter lists. A deliberately-broken fixture proves the validator rejects (red-proof inside a green suite). Validation is authoring/CI-time only, with zero runtime schema work: the seed remains a synchronous pure module-scope const at the Vault read boundary (D-031/D-095). Hard invariant: a content or seed swap never rewrites live saved or synced data. The seed is consumed only as the Vault fallback (key-absent / post-quarantine, D-018); no code path writes it to localStorage or Firestore; a returning user's stored plan (including a deliberate `[]`) is untouched by any content deploy. The produced `SAMPLE_ITINERARY` is proven deep-equal before and after consolidation by a golden file snapshot captured on the pre-refactor tree (`toMatchFileSnapshot`, regen `-u`). Post-S122 the golden is the seed's deliberate-edit baseline (one-regen discipline, like the visual baselines), not a frozen net blocking content edits. Bookings and itinerary remain linked by human-authored agreement only (D-034/D-036: deriving either direction is forbidden); D-125's nightlife gate is untouched. Runbook: `docs/trip-content.md` (edit map, invariants, workflow, Dec-26 worked example, danger zone). New static-content domains (e.g. S158) join as one data module + one strict schema + one validator case + one runbook row.
**Why:** the real S112 pain was one trip's content spread across hand-coupled files pinned by an anti-drift test, so a content swap needed archaeology. This makes every content change a one-file, schema-validated edit with a runbook: the light answer to "easy to update the trip plan" without touching D-006 or building G4 multi-trip machinery (deferred to v5, which would build on this layer).
**Changes if:** G4 Trip Packs is greenlit (the layer becomes per-trip data, which reinterprets D-006 and needs a deliberate call); a domain gains a core consumer (then relocate that domain into `core/content/` with a delegate, same pattern); D-034 is ever reopened to fuse bookings with the itinerary (not this layer's call to make).

### D-136 · LOCKED (proposed S121, recorded 2026-07-10) · Amends D-109: `TRIP_CITIES` is now derived from the content root; the anti-drift test becomes a derivation-identity test
D-109's direction reverses. `core/dates/trip-cities.ts` no longer hand-authors the 32-entry per-day city map: it computes `TRIP_CITIES = deriveTripCities(TRIP_ITINERARY)` (a pure, exported `days → Object.fromEntries(days.map(d => [d.date, d.city]))`) from `core/content/itinerary.ts`. That is a core→core import, so D-109's original rationale ("defining the data independently in core because core can't import lib") is dissolved rather than violated: the source now lives inside core, D-099 intact. The content root imports nothing from `core/dates`, which keeps the arrow acyclic, and `country`-vs-date agreement is a validator check rather than a derivation. `getCityForDate` is byte-identical (pure, total, pre-S100 country default off the map). `lib/__tests__/trip-cities.test.ts` is reworked: the headline assertion becomes derivation identity (`TRIP_CITIES ≡ deriveTripCities(SAMPLE_ITINERARY)`), which guards against future decoupling or re-hand-authoring. The S82-frozen boundary-date assertions (Dec-9/12/18 → Kathmandu, Dec-19 → Osaka, Jan-9 → Tokyo), the 32-date coverage, day-trip/transfer-city, totality and weather-coverage assertions are retained verbatim. Their duty shifts from policing map↔sample drift, now impossible, to policing the content itself: an edit to a frozen boundary city goes loudly red and requires the deliberate S112/D-124-style lockstep with the frozen E2E specs.
**Why:** the map existed as duplicated data solely because the content source lived in `lib/` where core couldn't reach it. With the content root in `core/content`, deriving is strictly safer than duplicating-plus-testing: the drift class is eliminated by construction and the test net's remaining protections are kept whole.
**Changes if:** user-editable per-day cities land (D-109's original escape hatch: the derived map becomes a default and a stored `DayPlan.city` wins, revisit then), or G4 makes the single-trip map one of many.

### D-137 · LOCKED (proposed S123, recorded 2026-07-10) · Item times are wall-clock-at-place: `startMinutes` 0–1439 local to the day's place, never TZ-converted for display
**Decision:** `startMinutes` (int 0–1439) = minutes-from-midnight local wall-clock at the item's day's place (country via `getCountryForDate`, city via `getCityForDate`); `durationMinutes` (int > 0) = elapsed minutes, and may spill past 1439 un-wrapped. The NPT/JST badge is derived from the day's country only. Offsets are explicit constants: NPT +345, JST +540. Mid-day border crossings are out of scope (no such day exists in this trip; a travel day's items use that day's offset).
**Why:** a trip itinerary is authored and read in place-local time. UTC instants would force conversion everywhere and reintroduce the B-01 bug class. One dated day = one place = zero ambiguity.
**Changes if:** a trip ever has a same-day timezone change, which forces a per-item place override and a new decision.

### D-138 · LOCKED (proposed S123, recorded 2026-07-10) · Additive time fields: `startMinutes?`/`durationMinutes?` on `ItineraryItem`; `time?`/`duration?` retained; the lenient Vault schema learns them, the strict content schema deliberately does not; user edits dual-write
**Decision:** both fields are optional-additive (D-012). The Vault read schema adds them as plain `z.number().optional()` (range enforced only at `effectiveStartMinutes`, where out-of-range degrades to untimed and never quarantines), plus a v5 payload/envelope schema pair. The S122 strict content schema stays `time`-only (`.strict()` keeps rejecting the new fields in seed content): one authored source, with the runtime fallback parser covering the seed. User edits via the picker write both `startMinutes` and canonical 24h `time`, and clearing clears both. That is a user write, not a migration rewrite (D-012 intact), and it keeps old clients' display current in the mixed fleet.
**Why:** additive-only protects live data; lenient-read protects against buggy writers; a single-source seed prevents dual-value drift; dual-write prevents mixed-fleet display skew and zombie legacy text after a clear.
**Note (2026-07-10):** the picker rewriting `time` on a user edit was flagged as sitting close to D-012 and the tech-doc's "never drop/rewrite the original `time`" wording. Confirmed: that invariant is scoped to the *migration* (a migration must be lossless and never mutate authored strings). A deliberate user edit updating both fields is expected, and is required for mixed-fleet display and for a real "clear". D-012 stands.
**Changes if:** the fleet is confirmed single-version post-trip (dual-write could be dropped), or seed content ever needs durations for clash demos (the content schema would then learn the fields).

### D-139 · LOCKED (proposed S123, recorded 2026-07-10) · Vault migration #3 (v4→v5): best-effort `time`→`startMinutes`, three formats, lossless, idempotent, never-clobber, no clock, duration not parsed
**Decision:** append `{from:4,to:5}`; `CURRENT_ITINERARY_VERSION = 5`. Pure item map: keep an existing `startMinutes` verbatim, else `startMinutes = parseTimeString(time)`. `parseTimeString` is one shared core parser, also the runtime fallback, and it accepts exactly: 24h `H:MM`/`HH:MM`, 24h dot `H.MM`/`HH.MM`, and 12h `h(:mm|.mm)? am/pm` (optional space/periods; 12am→0, 12pm→720). Everything else (bare numbers, ranges, words, out-of-range) returns `undefined` and the legacy text is shown verbatim. The migration never touches `time`/`duration`/any other field and never sets `durationMinutes`: legacy duration text is not parsed, a flagged gap, decided as clash warnings starting editor-set-only. Quarantine-on-throw is unchanged (D-096); export and import reuse the runner (v4 exports import cleanly).
**Why:** lossless + idempotent + never-clobber is what makes a migration over live synced data safe, including the D-073 SW-lag loop where an old build re-saves a v4 envelope over v5 fields. One parser is mandatory because the sync ingest bypasses migrations forever in a mixed fleet.
**Changes if:** quarantine-free real data shows a common missed format (widen the parser additively, with tests), never by reordering or renumbering shipped steps (D-095).

### D-140 · LOCKED (proposed S123, recorded 2026-07-10) · One offset-injected pure comparison: `core/dates/item-time.ts` `isPastAtPlace` via `placeWallClockToUtcMs` (Date.UTC arithmetic, no `Date` TZ tricks); clock seam `getNowUtcMsForPlace` in `lib/trip-now.ts`
**Decision:** all time helpers (parser, `effectiveStartMinutes`, `formatTimeAmPm`, the NPT/JST constants, `placeWallClockToUtcMs`, `isPastAtPlace`) live in one framework-free core module (D-099). `isPastAtPlace(dateStr, startMinutes, offsetMin, nowUtcMs)` is an instant compare, `itemUtc < nowUtc`, so at-now counts as upcoming and today's strictness is preserved. ISO dates are split, never parsed with `new Date(string)`; only `Date.UTC` field arithmetic (B-01-safe). "Now" comes from one adapter read, `lib/trip-now.ts getNowUtcMsForPlace(dayDate, offsetMin)`: with the override active, the synthetic clock's wall-clock face is re-interpreted at the place offset (demo "now" = place noon, byte-parity with today's `"12:00"` behavior and TZ-deterministic); with no override it is `getNow().getTime()` (in-zone, an exact no-op). `nextUp(items, {dayDate, placeOffsetMin, nowUtcMs})` consumes it, and the sole caller `today-panel.tsx` is rewired. D-075 storage and precedence are untouched.
**Why:** exactly one place does cross-TZ math, with injected offsets and a pure instant compare that stays correct across day boundaries; the override rule keeps the frozen `?today=` E2E net green.
**Changes if:** a second comparison site appears (route it through this module, never a second implementation).

### D-141 · LOCKED (proposed S123, recorded 2026-07-10) · Hand-rolled AM/PM picker: three columns (hour 1–12 / minute 00–59 / AM-PM), 44px targets, D-021 focus, reduced-motion, Clear-time; no native `input[type=time]`, no new dependency
**Decision:** per section 5 of the time-model blueprint (`docs/time-model-blueprint.md`). A full 0–59 minute column, no 5-min grid, so migrated values like `07:02` stay representable with zero special cases. Display everywhere is `formatTimeAmPm(effectiveStartMinutes)` plus the NPT/JST day-country badge; legacy-only items show their `time` text verbatim, unbadged. Saves dual-write per D-138.
**Why:** the native control's inconsistent 12/24h rendering is the complaint being fixed, and the column pattern is the boring, keyboard-friendly shape that meets the S110 a11y floor without a picker library (D-088/D-118).
**Changes if:** usability testing on-device shows the 60-row minute column too heavy (then a 5-min grid plus an inject-current-value variant, as an amendment).

### D-142 · LOCKED (proposed S123, recorded 2026-07-10) · Sort/timeline/clash: view-level stable sort (untimed sink), warn-only half-open overlap requiring both start AND duration
**Decision:** `sortItemsByTime` is pure, view-level and non-destructive: stable by `effectiveStartMinutes`, with untimed items sinking and preserving their relative order. Stored manual order stays the persisted truth (zero writes, D-018). The timeline renders the same projection. Overlap warns iff both items have `effectiveStartMinutes` and `durationMinutes > 0` and `(a.start < b.start + b.dur) && (b.start < a.start + a.dur)`: half-open (touching never warns), raw minutes (spill un-wrapped), a warn-only badge, never blocking.
**Why:** sorting must never mutate user order (undo-free, sync-safe), and fuzzy free-text reality makes blocking or duration-less warnings noise.
**Changes if:** durations become near-universal on items (a "gap view" could then be more than warn-only, a new decision).

### D-143 · LOCKED (process note; proposed S123, recorded 2026-07-10) · D-107 mixed-fleet coordination for the S124 deploy: friends reload right after; old-client `time` edits can leave stale `startMinutes` until re-touched
**Decision:** after S124 deploys, coordinate the travelers' reload (the D-073 update flow). Known bounded skew: old clients preserve but ignore `startMinutes`, and an old-client free-text `time` edit does not update it, so new clients may briefly show a stale structured time. Old-build v4-envelope re-saves over v5 data are safe by migration idempotence (D-139).
**Why:** additive fields ride the per-item rev/hlc merge unchanged; the only exposure is display skew in the reload window, so coordination rather than code is the right fix.
**Changes if:** the skew window proves painful in practice (a `timeRev` tiebreak would be the code fix, not now, YAGNI).

### D-144 · Superseded · Add-to-plan flying chip uses native WAAPI (`lib/fly-chip.ts`), not framer

### D-145 · Superseded · Expense count-up is a one-time scroll-into-view reveal, not a per-log re-trigger

### D-146 · Superseded · Nightlife venues get a namespaced `nightlife-<id>` sourceId so they show planned-state; amends D-074

### D-147 · implementation note (proposed S135, recorded 2026-07-11) · `<TripMap>` is the reusable map engine; `lib/itinerary-map.ts` is the shared plan→coord join
**Decision:** the MapLibre engine (lazy `import('maplibre-gl')` init, style/controls, marker + itinerary-route sources/layers, popups, reduced-motion, camera-fit) lives in `components/trip-map.tsx` as a `forwardRef` component. The pure plan→coordinate join (`DayStop`, `MARKER_BY_ID`, `buildItineraryStops`) lives in `lib/itinerary-map.ts`. Consumers build `routeStops` and pass them in; TripMap only renders given data. The props contract is the S136 seam: `markers` (day-subset for /plan), `routeStops` (re-passing triggers a cheap `setData`, so no `ITINERARY_CHANGED` wiring is needed), `onMarkerClick`/`highlightId` (marker↔stop highlight), `fitBounds={false}` (hold camera on reorder), `onGeoNote`, and a ref `resize()`. `MapSection` keeps only the /map chrome (filter, fullscreen slot-swap, legend, masthead).
**Why:** the tech-doc's #22 component split, done as the enabling refactor for S136's split map/list `/plan` view. It was a pure move (the engine was lifted verbatim); D-047 held (maplibre stays a lazy chunk, /map First Load 106 kB); behavior is identical on /map (0 visual drift). Speculative `activeFilter`/`interactive` props were dropped (YAGNI: popup-close by marker-set membership is equivalent and needs no filter to cross the boundary).
**Changes if:** S136 needs a seam not on the current props (add it then, not now).
**Amended S136 (`1a7480f`, 2026-07-11):** `highlightId` is now live, consumed in `trip-map.tsx` via a `setPaintProperty` case-expression keyed on feature `id` (it fattens and gilds the matching browse marker and the numbered route stop; route Point features gained an `id`). Two seams were added: `onViewChange` (a camera-settle reporter, fires on `moveend` and load) and `onReady` (GL-canvas-ready). All three are strict no-ops when their prop is `undefined`, so `/map`, which passes none, stays byte-identical. The `/plan` fit rule ("fit on marker-set change, hold on reorder") lives in `plan-day-map.tsx`, gated on `onReady` because maplibre load is async.

<!-- Phase 2 (Sync Everywhere) blueprint decisions, proposed in the S139 blueprint (docs/sync-everywhere-blueprint.md), recorded 2026-07-11. These are the contract the build slices S140–S145 execute; mechanics may refine during build (they are recorded design decisions, not locked handshakes), except D-152/D-154 which are permanent. -->

### D-148 · (proposed S139, recorded 2026-07-11) · `createReactiveStore`, the one reactive-store skeleton; four domains, frozen contract; migration order journal → expenses → budget → itinerary last
**Decision:** a `hooks/create-reactive-store.ts` factory (blueprint section 1.2): config = frozen event name + storage-key match set + `StoragePort<T>` + optional `SyncPort<T>`, returning a hook that exposes `{value, hydrated, commit(compute)}`. It reproduces the four proven behavior lines: hydration gating, dual-layer D-026 reactivity, D-031 fresh-base commit, D-039 push-from-commit-only. Domain mutators, stamping and selectors stay in the domain hooks. Migrate journal → expenses → budget (which gains its missing hook plus `budget-panel` adoption) → itinerary last, under its frozen nets. "Zero consumer edits" means the diff is confined to the hooks and the factory, with byte-identical interfaces, event strings and export paths (`ITINERARY_CHANGED_EVENT` stays exported from `@/hooks/use-itinerary`), zero assertion edits in existing suites, and full nets green after each domain. Note: the tech-doc's sketched `coreOps` param is dropped, since generic mutator-wrapping is exactly the speculative framework this work is meant to avoid.
**Why:** the skeleton is triplicated near-verbatim today and budget bypasses hooks entirely; this extracts what works instead of building a plugin framework.
**Changes if:** a domain needs a fundamentally different reactivity model (it opts out of the factory rather than bending it).

### D-149 · (proposed S139, recorded 2026-07-11) · Per-domain sync is a four-ingredient recipe over the unchanged `SyncPort<T>`; `mergeItems` id-keyed generalization of `mergeDay`; budget LWW-per-field, no tombstones
**Decision:** `SyncPort<T>` keeps its S97 shape. Expenses: additive `rev?/hlc?/deleted?` on `Expense`; a new pure `core/sync/merge-items.ts` (`resolvePair` extracted, `merge-day.ts` delegates, and the S96 suite passes with zero assertion edits); dormant physical-delete, sync-on tombstone plus filter; undo-under-sync mints a fresh id (D-032/D-119, one rule everywhere, confirmed over same-id-restamped). Budget: a leaf-path HLC map (`flattenBudget` closed path set ≤~25 leaves), a stamped `null` meaning a cleared field, `mergeBudget` as per-path higher-HLC-wins with a canonical-JSON tie-break, and additive `sync?.fieldHlc` on the local model (`normalizeModel` must preserve it, an S143 DoD line). The per-domain first-snapshot marker is chunk/singleton doc presence, not the itinerary trip-doc marker D-049: two markers coexist and locked D-049 is untouched. All gates carry over verbatim (D-038/D-047/D-055/D-120/D-039).
**Why:** the itinerary machinery generalizes to id-keyed rows, while a bounded struct needs field-LWW rather than row machinery.
**Changes if:** a synced domain arrives that is neither id-keyed rows nor a bounded struct.

### D-150 · (proposed S139, recorded 2026-07-11) · Offline outbox is state-based (dirty-chunk sets), a `SyncPort` decorator; push-before-subscribe plus a dirty-chunk merge exception; exactly-once = at-least-once × idempotent merges
**Decision:** `core/sync/outbox.ts` (blueprint section 3): gateway key 14 `nepal_japan_sync_outbox` = `{version, dirty:{domain: chunkKey[]}}`, with a write-ahead enqueue in the decorated push, ack-on-resolve, and rejection meaning the chunk stays dirty. `pushChunk` implementations have to reject honestly, because the decorator is the swallower. Flush on `online`, on visible, and at app-start; app-start flushes and then subscribes. First-snapshot apply is authoritative only for chunks that are not dirty, and dirty chunks steady-state-merge (the FU-19 reload fix). Enqueue is gated on `isConfigured() && getActiveTraveler()`, so dormant and guest sessions never write the slot and D-038/D-055 hold. D-091/D-018 hold too, because a dirty chunk exists only via real commits, so the sample-resurrection path has an empty outbox and stays byte-identical. Undo versus outbox is resolved by construction: it records state, not ops, so the flush pushes netted local state once and add-then-undo offline needs no coalescing. Exactly-once = at-least-once transport × idempotent merged writes: the merge algebra is the dedup, with no tokens and no sequence numbers. The itinerary adopts it, which closes S110 TL-A P2-1 / FU-19. Accepted edge: a flush of a locally-absent day is skipped rather than issuing a blind `deleteDoc` (whole-day removal isn't user-reachable). The key-14 collision with the tech-doc favorites sketch is resolved: the outbox takes 14, because it builds first, and favorites takes the next free number.
**Why:** recording chunks instead of ops eliminates ordering, coalescing and replay machinery entirely; convergence comes from the merge algebra that already exists.
**Changes if:** a non-merge-idempotent write path ever ships, which it must not, since that breaks the exactly-once argument at its root.

### D-151 · (proposed S139, recorded 2026-07-11) · Spark layout: expenses chunked by leg, budget as one doc; quota math ≥19× margin (free-tier only, D-088)
**Decision:** `trips/{tripId}/expenses/{nepal|japan}` (2 chunk docs, keyed by the existing required `expense.leg`) plus `trips/{tripId}/budget/model` (1 singleton). ~1k rows gives 75/165 KB chunks (≤16% of the 1 MiB doc limit); peak-day totals are ≈ 825 writes / 2,600 reads against Spark's 20k/50k (~4%/~5%, a ≥19× margin, presence load included). The write pattern is the `pushDayMerged` transactional read→merge→set analog per chunk. Escape hatch, recorded but not built: re-chunk by month if a chunk approaches the doc limit. Never Blaze (D-088 LOCKED).
**Why:** leg is already a row field; 2 docs is the fewest that avoid whole-collection contention; the math holds with 10× error on every assumption.
**Changes if:** row volume grows an order of magnitude beyond the 3-friend/32-day reality (then month-chunking).

### D-152 · LOCKED (proposed S139, recorded 2026-07-11) · The journal never syncs — privacy by design, permanent
**Decision:** gateway key 12 (journal) stays device-local: no `SyncPort` wired, no Firestore path assigned, ever. Export/import, an explicit user act, remains the only egress. The absence is the design, recorded so no future slice "helpfully" syncs it.
**Why:** a private diary shared by silent default betrays the surface's premise; syncing it later, if ever wanted, costs one config field, so locking local-by-default costs nothing.
**Changes if:** we explicitly want shared entries, and then it is an opt-in per-entry share design, never a default sync.

### D-153 · (proposed S139, recorded 2026-07-11) · `gcTombstones` runs as a post-merge pass at the two merge boundaries only; 30-day horizon; zero dedicated writes
**Decision:** invoked on merged results inside `push*Merged` (before `tx.set`) and on the steady-state snapshot apply (before persist), never in the hot merge path and never as its own Firestore write. Expenses get the id-keyed analog in `merge-items.ts`; budget is N/A. Cross-client `nowPt` skew is accepted: it is conservative, and it converges as docs rewrite. S145 implements it, and the S110 TL-A P3 riders ride the same slice: `getEntry` memoization (version-stamped ref), the provider re-routed through `itinerarySyncPort.subscribe` (which kills the dead surface, since `itinerary-provider.tsx:65` imports `subscribeRemote` directly today), and hydration min-height reservation.
**Why:** GC is tidiness, not correctness, so the design keeps it structurally unable to lose a live item or cost a write.
**Changes if:** doc sizes ever make tombstone growth a real cost (shorten the horizon or add an explicit compaction write, a new decision).

### D-154 · LOCKED (proposed S139, recorded 2026-07-11) · Gateway key 6 (`packing_checklist`) is retired — the D-130 packing-sync question is moot
**Decision:** number 6 and the string are never reused; no sync design or quota line is owed; residual on-disk values in deployed browsers are harmless orphaned bytes, deliberately not cleaned.
**Why:** S113D deleted the packing feature, and pricing sync for a deleted feature is work against nothing.
**Changes if:** never (a future packing feature is a new slot with a new key).

### D-155 · Superseded · Expense split: additive `paidBy?`/`split?`, per-leg/per-currency even-split settlement, read-only

### D-156 · (S145, recorded 2026-07-12) · Backup Restore works under sync as a tombstone-replace merge, superseding the D-121 local-only disable (FU-20)
**Decision:** the D-121 containment ("Restore is disabled when sync is configured and a traveler is signed in") is gone. Restore now runs in every mode. Dormant/guest (sync off, D-038) is unchanged: a plain local overwrite (`commit(() => backup)`, byte-identical to `importItinerary`'s `savePlans`, with nothing to unwind). Synced and signed-in: the picked file is trust-validated by the extracted `parseBackup()` (the same schema and quarantine as import), then applied by the new `restorePlans(backup)` itinerary mutator as a tombstone-replace in one `commit()`. (a) Tombstone every currently-live item across every day (`stampSyncDeleted`) so removals propagate and win over a peer's still-live copy. (b) Re-add every live backup item as a `freshCopyOf` + `stampSyncCreated` copy (D-032/D-119, fresh-id-beats-tombstone) so the restore can't lose an HLC tie and a strictly-later concurrent peer edit still survives instead of being blindly clobbered. Backup tombstones are skipped. One commit means the normal `pushPlans`/outbox per-day fan-out, so the restore syncs and survives the next snapshot. Restore-to-empty stays empty (D-018/D-091: an empty backup day means items are tombstoned and none re-added). Export and the Vault schema (D-098) are unchanged, because Restore is a merge over existing fields with no new persisted shape. Note: there is no expenses or budget Restore surface (the Vault is itinerary-only, D-098), so FU-20's "expenses Restore via `mergeItems`" clause had nothing to re-enable. The real expenses sync-debt work in S145 is the id-keyed `gcTombstoneRows` GC analog (D-153). An actual expenses Backup/Restore UI would be a new slice (see FU-37).
**Why:** a Restore expressed as a merge with winning fresh stamps propagates instead of being silently unwound by the next server snapshot, which is the exact failure D-121 was chartered to contain until this slice. Reusing `freshCopyOf`/`stampSync*`/`commit`/outbox means no new restore engine.
**Changes if:** a Restore ever needs to preserve peer-concurrent edits by id rather than mint fresh ids (a different merge policy, a new decision), or an expenses/budget backup surface is ever built (FU-37, a new slice).

### D-157 · (FU-34, recorded 2026-07-12 at merge) · `/map` favorites & offline: shared flat key-14 store (no namespacing), `enablePopupFavorite` prop-gate, Saved-chip ANDs category
**Decision:** the `/map` popup favorite heart and the guide-card favorites share one flat gateway key-14 `favorites: string[]` store with no id namespacing. Marker ids (`np-*`/`jp-*` kebab) and guide ids (`na#`/`ja#` codes) are provably disjoint id-spaces, guarded by a Vitest static disjointness assertion and an E2E cross-contamination proof (favoriting a map pin never lights a guide card, and vice-versa). The heart is prop-gated behind `enablePopupFavorite` so it renders on `/map` only, never on `/plan`'s day-map. The "Saved" filter chip mirrors the guide idiom (absent at 0 favorites, appears at ≥1) and ANDs the category filter (Saved ∧ category, not either-or).
**Why:** disjoint id-spaces make a shared store safe without namespacing ceremony (fewer keys, one source of truth for "saved"); the prop-gate keeps the day-map read-only; ANDing matches how the guide Saved chip already composes.
**Changes if:** map and guide id-spaces ever risk collision (then namespace the key or split the stores), and the static assertion is the tripwire.

### D-158 · Amended by D-320 · (S151, recorded 2026-07-12 at merge) · `/map` trip-mode: destination-only directions URL, search-select resets filter to All, `focusMarker` moveend-then-popup
**Decision:** (1) `buildMapsDirectionsUrl(lat,lng)` is destination-only: no origin is read, injected or persisted. This extends D-074's URL-not-API stance, a plain maps deep-link with no geolocation egress, and the popup Directions link carries `target`/`rel`. (2) Selecting a search result resets the category filter to `'All'` before fly+popup, because the target marker must be present in the map source to focus it. (3) `focusMarker` reuses the existing `openPopup` via `map.once('moveend', …)` after `flyTo` (synchronous after `jumpTo` under reduced motion), the established imperative camera-to-marker pattern. The location toggle is the built-in maplibre `GeolocateControl` (permission-gated, nothing persisted), verify-only with no new code. D-047 held (maplibre lazy, `/map` First Load 106 kB). **(Amended by D-320, issue #30, 2026-08-15:** the app-wide reading of "nothing persisted" is RETIRED and replaced by "nothing COORDINATE-shaped persisted". A one-shot location check on trip-day change may now resolve a device fix in memory against `lib/city-coords.ts` and store the resulting PLACE NAME plus the confirmation instant (gateway key 34); the fix itself is still never persisted, transmitted or logged. **This clause of D-158 — the `GeolocateControl` itself — is unchanged: that toggle still writes nothing at all.** Clauses (1)–(3) above are untouched. Read D-320 for the five boundaries and for what would reopen it.**)**
**Why:** destination-only keeps the zero-egress privacy stance; the filter reset avoids a "selected a hidden marker" dead-end; moveend-then-popup is the camera pattern already used elsewhere.
**Changes if:** a real routing provider is ever wired (then origin plus a route API, a new greenlit server-side decision rather than this URL deep-link).

<!-- Phase 4 (photo track, G2) decisions, proposed in the S159 blueprint (docs/photo-storage-blueprint.md), recorded 2026-07-12. S160/S161 execute these. -->

### D-159 · LOCKED (proposed S159, recorded 2026-07-12) · Photos are device-local IndexedDB blobs with zero egress — the D-130 G2 charter made permanent
**Decision:** user photos (journal day-photos and expense receipts) are stored as Blobs in IndexedDB (`nepal_japan_photos` db, `blobs` store) behind a `BlobStorePort`. Their metadata (`PhotoMeta[]`: id, owner ref, altText, caption?, w/h, bytes, createdAt) lives in localStorage gateway key 16 = `nepal_japan_photos`. Zero egress, permanent (D-038-strict): photo bytes and photo references never reach Firestore (no sync port, no dormant remote layer) and are never in the Vault JSON export. Key 16 and the IDB store sit outside the Vault, and not even metadata is exported, because a metadata export invites bytes-creep. There is no photo field on any synced or Vault schema (`Expense`/`JournalEntry` gain nothing): the entry↔photo link lives only in the local metadata index (`owner: {kind:'journal',date} | {kind:'expense',expenseId}`). Journal photos are keyed by date, so S161's per-day recap is a pure filter. A fresh device reads `[]` and renders empty states; photos deliberately do not migrate between devices.
**Why:** the only way to make "photos never leave the device" a structural guarantee rather than a discipline is to keep both the bytes and the linking refs out of every schema that syncs or exports, and a separate local index does that. Putting a `photoIds?` on the expense row would drag refs through `sanitizeExpense`'s allowlist, the merge payload, and every device. Free by construction (browser IDB + Canvas, D-088).
**Changes if:** never for the egress stance; it is the privacy premise, as D-152 is for the journal. A future device-to-device photo transfer, if ever wanted, would be an explicit user-initiated export of a separate photo bundle, never the default JSON export and never sync.

### D-160 · (proposed S159, recorded 2026-07-12) · Photo pipeline policy: `BlobStorePort` + result-typed `put`, Canvas downscale 1600px/q0.8, quota/eviction graceful, `restoreExpense` void→string
**Decision:** the `BlobStorePort` is total and never throws, except that `put` returns `{ok:true,id} | {ok:false,reason:'quota'|'unavailable'}`, because quota failure has to be user-visible. The default implementation is native IndexedDB (out-of-line `ph-<ts36>-<rand6>` keys), and the port exists so unit tests can use a ~15-line in-memory Map fake, with no `fake-indexeddb`/`idb` dependency. Capture downscales before store: long edge ≤ 1600 px, JPEG q0.8, via `createImageBitmap(file,{imageOrientation:'from-image'})` → canvas → `toBlob` (~150–450 KB per photo; never upscale; a decode failure gives a non-destructive message). Quota: a rough budget of ≈ 40 MB at 100 photos, orders under browser floors, so no hard cap. `QuotaExceededError` is caught inside `put` and becomes a "storage full" state, while the entry or expense still saves. `navigator.storage.persist()` is best-effort once. An evicted blob makes `get` return null, which renders a placeholder tile that keeps altText and caption; metadata is never auto-deleted. `use-photos` is a `createReactiveStore` (D-148) with no sync. S160 rider: `restoreExpense` (D-032/D-119 fresh-id) must change its return from `void` to `string` (the minted id) so the local photo metadata can be re-pointed after a synced restore strands the receipt's owner ref, a purely local, additive signature change.
**Why:** a result-typed `put` plus graceful quota is the difference between "storage full" and a crashed capture; downscaling keeps a 32-day trip's photos an order under quota; the test fake keeps the free-tools/no-dep rule; the `restoreExpense` tweak is the one place the local index couples to the synced-expense lifecycle.
**Changes if:** IDB quota proves tighter than measured (lower the max dimension, or add an explicit cap plus prune-oldest), or a browser drops `createImageBitmap` orientation support (fall back to an `<img>`/EXIF path).

<!-- Phase-3 slice decisions (S147/S148) from the parallel session; D-numbers assigned at the 2026-07-12 merge, since none were written at the time. -->

### D-161 · (S147, recorded 2026-07-12 at merge) · Search-within-plan: `?focus=` URL channel, on-demand `loadPlans()` palette bridge, title>notes>category matcher, `highlightId` dual id-space
**Decision:** client-side search over itinerary item title/notes/category from `/plan` and the command palette. (1) The palette→planner hand-off is a `?focus=<itemId>` URL param consumed via `useSearchParams`, which re-highlights in place with no remount. It is distinct from `?today=`'s raw `window.location.search` parse, which must not reactively remount. (2) The command palette is mounted outside `ItineraryProvider`, so it reads an on-demand `loadPlans()` snapshot on open (a read-only bridge) rather than adding a second live store or editing `layout.tsx`. (3) Matcher rank is title > notes > category, plain substring (pure `lib/search-plan.ts`). (4) `highlightId` is now a dual id-space: the `calendar-planner` row-highlight condition generalizes to `item.id === highlightId || (showMap && markerId === highlightId)`, which amends the S136/D-147 marker-id-only invariant (marker-click is regression-guarded by `plan-map-split.spec` 6/6). (5) cmdk `forceMount` on the dynamically-computed palette group avoids a stale-filter trap. Read-only (D-018), with no store, schema or core change.
**Why:** a URL channel keeps palette and planner decoupled and shareable; the out-of-provider snapshot avoids a second store; the dual id-space is the minimal change that lets search-highlight and marker-highlight share one prop.
**Changes if:** the palette ever moves inside the provider (then drop the `loadPlans()` snapshot for the live context), or highlight needs to distinguish marker-vs-item origin (then split the prop again).

### D-162 · Superseded · Multi-day items: additive `endDate?` (absolute ISO), render-expansion-only / never multi-homed, clash-v1 excludes spans

### D-163 · Superseded · First-run tour: gateway key 17 exactly-once flag, centered stepper, entry condition = TokenGate's own resolved condition

### D-164 · (v5 plan lock, approved 2026-07-16; recorded S176) · Travel Mode shape: `/travel` route, `?date=`, gateway `travelMode` flag, guests blocked, persistent nav-chrome entry plus a hard exit affordance
**Decision:** Travel Mode is a chrome-free route `/travel` with `?date=YYYY-MM-DD` (default `getTodayInTrip()`; pre-trip resolves to Day 1). It is decoupled from the `?today=` clock sim (D-075): the two compose, they never merge. A `travelMode` gateway flag makes a PWA relaunch re-enter the mode. Guests are blocked (`/travel` inherits the token-gate stance, and a spec asserts it). Entry (directive 2026-07-16, binding): a persistent Travel Mode button in the top-right of the app-wide nav chrome (`chrome-islands.tsx` surfaces), on every page, not gated by the trip-date window, because pre-trip preview is a feature. Exit (a hard DoD line, not polish): a ≥44px X that restores the prior route with no history trap. Chrome suppression uses pathname conditionals in the six `chrome-islands` client components, not `layout.tsx` restructuring (fact 10).
**Why:** a route, rather than a modal or a theme, keeps Travel Mode addressable, precacheable (D-170) and testable; the nav-chrome button makes it field-testable before Dec 9, which the calendar depends on.
**Changes if:** the entry/exit UX is redirected, or the trip-date default changes (the D-006 amendment path).

### D-165 · (v5 plan lock, approved 2026-07-16; recorded S176) · No light mode; Travel Mode gets an outdoor high-legibility toggle instead
**Decision:** v5 ships no app-wide light mode (an explicit scope fence, section 0). Outdoor readability is solved inside Travel Mode only: a TM-local high-legibility toggle (token-level contrast and size overrides on the S177 token system, persisted via a gateway flag, axe-clean in both states, S189).
**Why:** a full light theme re-opens all 21 visual baselines and the whole palette for a need that only exists outdoors mid-trip; a TM-local token override is two orders cheaper.
**Changes if:** light mode is wanted post-v5, which needs a declared design freeze and a re-baseline budget of its own.

### D-166 · (v5 plan lock, approved 2026-07-16; recorded S176) · AI concierge: build it — Gemini free tier primary, Groq fallback, keys Worker-only
**Decision:** the AI concierge is in for v5: chat-only (`/chat` SSE via the one free Cloudflare Worker), Gemini free tier primary with a Groq fallback, and all keys Worker-side secrets, never in the client bundle or `out/`. Concierge means chat; AI itinerary *generation* stays out of scope (the section 0 fence). Provider, model and quota specifics were subsequently locked by the S197 spike as D-174, which implements this decision rather than amending it. Cut-order position: the second thing dropped under pressure (section 0).
**Why:** it is the one genuinely-wanted backend feature, free and card-free, and the proving ground for the first Worker.
**Changes if:** S197/S198's go/no-go conditions fail (see D-174's change triggers), or it is de-scoped at the re-confirm checkpoint.

### D-167 · (v5 plan lock, approved 2026-07-16; recorded S176) · Web Push morning briefing: build if runway allows, after the concierge proves the Worker; raw VAPID preferred
**Decision:** the morning-briefing push is a runway-permitting build (S201/S202), sequenced strictly after the concierge Worker exists and after FU-18's deployed rules (a hard precondition for `pushSubs`). Raw Web Push + VAPID is preferred over FCM to protect the single-generated-SW invariant, finalized by the S197 spike as D-175. Dormant-unless-configured (the D-038/D-055 pattern).
**Why:** push is the lowest-priority backend feature (the first thing cut, section 0) but cheap once the Worker exists.
**Changes if:** runway runs out (cut it cleanly, nothing else depends on it), or D-175's change triggers fire.

### D-168 · Superseded by D-205 · Auth: full auth rejected, FU-18 anon-allowlist now, small Google sign-in slice before departure

### D-169 · (v5 plan lock, approved 2026-07-16; recorded S176) · Flight-status APIs: rejected, deep-links instead
**Decision:** no live flight-status integration. Evidence-checked: no card-free API exists (FlightAware, AeroDataBox, aviationstack and OpenSky are all disqualified on card, quota or coverage). The app deep-links to the airline and FR24 instead (S188's next-flight card; transit deep-links ride the same pattern).
**Why:** the free-tools-only standing rule is absolute, and a deep-link delivers ~90% of the value for 0% of the risk.
**Changes if:** a genuinely card-free flight API with real coverage appears (re-verify, don't assume).

### D-170 · (V5-DEVPLAN section 1, fact 1; recorded S176) · `gen-sw.mjs` `ROUTE_HTML` is hand-maintained — every new route has to be added explicitly
**Decision:** `scripts/gen-sw.mjs`'s `ROUTE_HTML` list is hand-maintained by design (the walk() only auto-discovers `_next/static`, icons and the manifest; the S153 `/journal` omission bug is the precedent). Any slice adding a route (`/travel` in S184, any future page) has to add its `index.html` to `ROUTE_HTML` in the same slice, and `e2e/pwa.spec.ts` is the tripwire that must assert the new precache count. Recorded so this is never re-litigated or "discovered" again.
**Why:** a missed entry silently breaks offline for that route, the exact failure class the app's offline guarantee cannot absorb.
**Changes if:** the SW generator ever gains reliable route auto-discovery (then this becomes a check rather than a hand-edit).

### D-171 · (v5 plan lock, approved 2026-07-16; blueprinted S176) · View Transitions: manual `document.startViewTransition()` progressive enhancement; React canary forbidden; route fade suppressed via `html[data-vt-active]`
**Decision:** View Transitions ship as a manual progressive enhancement: a `useViewTransition()` helper (S179) wrapping `document.startViewTransition()`. React canary and `experimental.viewTransition` are forbidden (versions are frozen through Dec 9, fact 9). Reconciliation with `app/template.tsx` (fact 2, no double-animation): the helper sets `html[data-vt-active]` before starting the transition and removes it on `finished`, plus a ~1500ms safety timeout, and one CSS rule `html[data-vt-active] .animate-route-fade { animation: none; }` suppresses the route fade for the bracketed window. `template.tsx` itself is untouched. Ladder: reduced motion gets a plain `router.push` and no transition of either kind (the D-007/D-056 hard guarantee, plus a belt-and-braces `::view-transition-*` reduced-motion CSS kill); an unsupported browser gets a plain push and the existing route fade (exactly current behavior); a supported browser lets VT drive with the fade suppressed. Style-only VT CSS goes under `@supports (view-transition-name: none)`. Full contract: blueprint section 5.
**Why:** the attribute handshake is the only mechanism that deterministically brackets template's per-navigation remount without coupling template to the helper; manual VT keeps the frozen React version and degrades to today's behavior everywhere it isn't supported.
**Changes if:** Next/React ship a stable first-class VT integration post-trip (migrate deliberately), or the pathname-commit promise pattern proves flaky in S179's real run (fallback: suppress VT, keep the fade).

### D-172 · LOCKED (v5 plan lock, approved 2026-07-16; blueprinted S176) · Trip Packs storage: grandfather, never migrate; legacy keys byte-identical, `trip:{id}:{slot}` for future packs, one `keyFor()` in the gateway
**Decision:** the app becomes multi-trip-capable by namespacing, never migrating. (1) The current trip (default pack `nepal-japan-2026`) keeps every legacy storage key byte-identical, forever: grandfathered. (2) Future packs use `trip:{tripId}:{slot}` (slot = the `STORAGE_KEYS` registry name), minted by one `keyFor(slot)` in `core/storage/gateway.ts` and nowhere else (D-097). The grandfather test is id-equality (`activeTripId === DEFAULT_TRIP_ID`), not key absence. (3) `activeTripId` lives at a pack-independent localStorage key (`tripPlannerActiveTrip`); switching means writing it plus a full reload, with no live re-hydration. (4) The switcher UI stays hidden until a second pack exists (S194). (5) The live Firestore tree is never migrated: new packs are new TRIP_IDs, and sync activates only when the default pack is active, so a non-default pack runs strictly local-only in v5. (6) Per-slot `Store` facts are preserved verbatim (`tripPlannerTodayOverride` stays sessionStorage, D-075); app-scoped slots (identity, guest, clock-sim, tour, chunk-guard) are never namespaced, enforced structurally by `keyFor`'s `TripScopedSlot` type. (7) D-002 is unchanged and reaffirmed: localStorage stays the source of truth, and Trip Packs is key namespacing rather than a persistence change. (8) D-006 is amended, not broken: the trip dates become the default pack's dates, every derived constant stays byte-identical (the untouched FU-10/S82 suites are the parity gate), and the D-005/D-126 annotations are intact. Contracts: blueprint sections 1–4 (`TripConfig`, facade, `keyFor`).
**Why:** migrating a live, synced, multi-device trip's keys mid-year is this project's historically highest-regression-risk move (compare D-139); grandfathering makes "current trip untouched" a structural property instead of a test hope.
**Changes if:** only with explicit sign-off (LOCKED). A post-trip archival migration could be considered after Jan 9, never before.

### D-173 · CLOSED NO-GO by D-197 · PMTiles offline maps: a spike-gated stretch, opt-in OPFS download, never SW-precached

> **The gate this entry set was run and it came back NO-GO (D-197, 2026-07-17): the street-level extract is 180MB–3.7GB against a 100MB hard cap.** The "If GO:" branch below therefore never opened and is not standing guidance. Do not read it as a design to build. **The shipped answer is D-286: the map *engine* is precached, tiles are not**, and the copy says so. This header carries the marker because the body does not: the entry read as live approved scope for three weeks after it was closed.

**Decision:** offline vector maps via PMTiles are a stretch, gated on the S209 extract-sizing spike (go/no-go: the extract must clear the GitHub Pages 100MB cap). If GO: maplibre 5.24 `addProtocol`, an opt-in OPFS download (user-initiated, deletable), and never SW-precached, because D-073's precache stays small and deterministic.
**Why:** offline maps are high-value mid-trek but the size risk is real and external, so a spike answers it with evidence before any build cost.
**Changes if:** S209 measures over-cap (NO-GO, deep-link to offline-capable map apps instead), or a smaller extract source appears. **Fired: S209 measured over-cap. See D-197.**

<!-- v5 Phase-4 backend-activation spike (S197), recorded 2026-07-16. The go/no-go feasibility report is docs/v5-ai-concierge-feasibility.md; these two entries lock what it decided. D-164–D-173 are reserved for the S176 blueprint, so these start at D-174. -->

### D-174 · (proposed S197, recorded 2026-07-16) · AI concierge providers: Gemini free tier (gemini-2.5-flash-lite) primary, then a Groq fallback ladder (llama-3.3-70b → llama-3.1-8b), keys Worker-only; verdict GO
**Decision:** the concierge runs Gemini free tier primary with a Groq fallback, all free-tier and no-card (paid or card-required is out of v5, `docs/V5-DEVPLAN.md` section 0). Primary is gemini-2.5-flash-lite: its higher RPD than flash makes it the right chat default, and flash is a Worker-side model-string escalation if lite quality proves weak, not a re-decision. On a Gemini 429/5xx/timeout, fall through to Groq llama-3.3-70b-versatile (30 RPM / 1,000 RPD / 12,000 TPM / 100,000 TPD, confirmed against official docs), then to llama-3.1-8b-instant (30 RPM / 14,400 RPD / 6,000 TPM / 500,000 TPD, confirmed) as the deep well if the 70b daily cap is hit. Gemini's exact quotas are dynamic per-project and only shown live in AI Studio's dashboard (`aistudio.google.com/rate-limit`); the public-aggregator estimate is ~15 RPM / ~1,000 RPD for flash-lite, recorded as an estimate, with the live number to be pasted in before S198 (`<PASTE LIVE RPD HERE>` in the feasibility doc). Headroom check: a generous 4-person / 32-day party at ~30 msgs/day/person is 120 turns/day, about 12% of Gemini-lite's estimated 1,000 RPD on its own, with a ~15,400 RPD Groq fallback behind it. That is comfortable by an order of magnitude even at flash's lower ~250 RPD. All keys are Worker-only secrets, never in the client bundle or the static `out/` export (the D-004 boundary). The journal (gateway key 12) is never in concierge context (the D-152 privacy posture), enforced in S199's context builder. Verdict: GO, conditional on the no-card check passing at key-creation; if either provider demands a card, that provider flips to no-go.
**Why:** two independent free-tier providers with a three-model fallback ladder give an order-of-magnitude quota margin over the real trip party's use, at zero cost and with no card; keeping keys Worker-only is the whole reason this is a server slice (the D-004 exception) rather than a client fetch.
**Changes if:** the live Gemini RPD comes in far below estimate and Groq's fallback proves insufficient (revisit models and limits), a provider requires billing (drop it; never go Blaze or paid, the D-088 posture), or answer quality on flash-lite is weak (escalate the primary to gemini-2.5-flash, a model-string swap rather than a new decision).

### D-175 · (proposed S197, recorded 2026-07-16) · Web Push protocol = raw Web Push + VAPID, not FCM: single-SW collision avoided, Worker-signed, iOS 16.4+ PWA supported; verdict GO
**Decision:** the morning-briefing push uses raw Web Push + VAPID, not Firebase Cloud Messaging. The deciding factor is the single-SW invariant: this app has exactly one hand-maintained service worker doing precaching (`scripts/gen-sw.mjs` output, D-073), and FCM's web SDK expects its own `firebase-messaging-sw.js` at origin scope (or an `importScripts` merge), which risks a scope/registration collision. Raw VAPID is just a `push`/`notificationclick` handler added to the existing SW, which S202 already plans to merge behind a dormant-unless-configured gate, with the Worker signing payloads via a VAPID keypair it holds server-side (private key = Worker secret; public key ships to the client as the `applicationServerKey`, public by design). No FCM SDK, no second SW file, no new client dependency. iOS Safari installed-PWA (16.4+) supports the standard Web Push API + VAPID for A2HS apps, so raw VAPID sacrifices no reach. Verdict: GO, free-tier and no-card (pure web platform plus self-held keys). Client-side push subscription persistence (`pushSubs`) additionally waits on FU-18 (the rules deploy).
**Why:** raw VAPID keeps the one-SW model intact and adds zero client deps, where FCM would force a second SW surface or a merge for no benefit the standard web platform doesn't already provide, including on iOS 16.4+.
**Changes if:** a future need arises that only FCM provides (e.g. cross-platform native push beyond web PWAs), and then it is revisited as a new greenlit server-side decision, never a silent add of a second SW.

> **APPROVED, NEVER BUILT. Recorded 2026-08-10 (S418).** This is a protocol GO for a feature that has no code. The verdict above is sound and was simply never acted on: `scripts/gen-sw.mjs` has no `push` and no `notificationclick` handler, no `applicationServerKey` is ever passed anywhere in the app, and the `pushSubs` slot this entry names does not exist in `core/storage/gateway.ts`'s key registry. Full proof of absence is on D-167, which is the build GO this protocol serves; the disposition (build or retire) is ruled there, and this entry follows it.

### D-176 · (S174, recorded 2026-07-16) · Expenses export schema v1: its own envelope, separate from the itinerary Vault; trust boundary = `sanitizeExpenses`
**Decision:** expenses backup/restore uses its own versioned envelope (`{ schemaVersion: 1, updatedAt, payload: Expense[] }`, file `nepal-japan-expenses.json`, `lib/expense-export.ts`), not an extension of the itinerary Vault schema (D-098 stays itinerary-only). The trust boundary on import is the existing `sanitizeExpenses`, the same lenient/total validator the storage read path uses, and a fail-safe parse quarantines corrupt files (`EXPENSE_QUARANTINE_KEY`, the D-096 pattern). Restore semantics: dormant is a plain overwrite; sync on is the D-156 tombstone-replace in one commit via `mergeItems` (tombstone all live rows, re-add backup rows as fresh-id copies), mirroring `restorePlans`. No migration machinery until a real v2 exists. S217's synced checklist rides this same pattern later.
**Why:** a separate small envelope keeps D-098's Vault contract untouched; reusing the storage path's own sanitizer means import can never admit a shape the app itself wouldn't; tombstone-replace is the only restore that converges under sync instead of resurrecting deleted rows.
**Changes if:** the Expense shape changes (mint schemaVersion 2 plus a real migration), or a combined multi-domain backup file is ever wanted (a new decision, not a silent merge).

### D-177 · (S177, recorded 2026-07-16) · Semantic surface tokens: RGB-channel CSS vars with `<alpha-value>`, navy scale single-sourced in globals.css
**Decision:** the navy palette is single-sourced as RGB-channel CSS custom properties in `globals.css` (`--navy-900: 10 14 39` and so on), with a semantic tier that components actually reference: `surface` (navy-900, page/base field), `surface-raised` (navy-800, lifted panels) and `surface-overlay` (navy-700, top overlay/hairline), wired in `tailwind.config.ts` via `rgb(var(--x) / <alpha-value>)`. The RGB-channel form is used rather than shadcn's `hsl(var())` because navy is the one palette needing opacity suffixes (`bg-surface/60`), which the HSL var form can't take. Raw `navy-*` classnames are banned in `app/` and `components/` tsx (grep-enforced). Two documented exceptions keep hex literals: `lib/map-style.ts`/`trip-map.tsx` `BRAND` (WebGL paint props, since CSS vars can't reach the GL canvas) and `app/layout.tsx` `themeColor` (PWA meta needs a resolved literal). Later slices (S178 Glass 2.0, S189 outdoor toggle) retune tokens, not components. S177 was proven pixel-neutral (24/24 baselines, zero regenerated).
**Why:** one alpha-capable var scale plus a semantic tier is the smallest structure that lets Glass 2.0 and the TM legibility toggle re-skin the app without touching 47 components again.
**Changes if:** a real second theme (light mode, post-v5) arrives, and then the semantic tier gets a second value set with the names unchanged.

### D-178 · (S178, recorded 2026-07-16) · Glass 2.0 freeze: FU-40 fixed at the shared `--accent-foreground` token; `html[data-trip-phase]` ambient contract; visual-tolerance caveat. Section 2 is dead, sections 1 and 3 stand (S366, 2026-08-02). Section 2's entire mechanism is gone, and not only its palette: the `html[data-trip-phase]` overrides are deleted along with the `body::before` aurora they re-coloured, and `route-accent-engine.tsx`, the component that stamped the attribute, no longer exists. There is no trip-phase ambient contract to honour or test. Section 1 (the `--accent-foreground` fix landed at the root token, so every `bg-accent` consumer inherits it) and section 3 (a green visual pass at 0.02 `maxDiffPixelRatio` is not proof of pixel-identity for small regions) are unchanged and still binding; section 3 in particular is a live trap.
**Decision:** (1) FU-40's contrast fix landed at the root token: `--accent-foreground` flipped light→dark (`222 36% 6%`, 6.25:1 on the D-058 accent, which is untouched), rather than being scoped to `ui/command.tsx`. Every `bg-accent` consumer (dropdown, context-menu, menubar, select, toggle, navigation-menu) pairs the same two tokens, so all of them render dark-on-sakura now, and the cmdk axe exclusion is removed so the scan runs at full strength. (2) Ambient trip-phase gradient contract: `html[data-trip-phase]` ∈ `pre|nepal|japan`, stamped once per load by `route-accent-engine.tsx` via read-only `getTodayInTrip()`, which composes with the D-075 `?today=` sim so frozen-clock baselines deterministically get `pre`. Post-trip intentionally falls back to `pre`: there is no fourth palette. Animation is `body::before` background-position keyframes, neutralized under reduced motion by both the global `*::before` block and an explicit `animation:none` entry (D-007/D-056). (3) Caveat for future gates: the visual suite's 0.02 `maxDiffPixelRatio` can swallow a real small-area design change (it absorbed the palette-highlight recolor until `--update-snapshots=all` was forced), so a green visual pass is not proof of pixel-identity for small regions.
**Why:** one token flip is the root-cause fix everywhere the pair is used; a documented attribute contract keeps the ambient system testable; the tolerance caveat is recorded so no future slice trusts a green visual gate as byte-parity evidence.
**Changes if:** a real fourth trip phase is ever wanted (a post-trip palette), or the tolerance caveat bites again (then tighten maxDiffPixelRatio at a declared freeze).

### D-179 · (S180, recorded 2026-07-16) · Scroll-driven CSS pattern: @supports plus a retained-JS fallback; reduced motion never uses the scroll-timeline path (the 0.01ms idiom doesn't neutralize progress timelines)
**Decision:** the app's scroll-driven CSS pattern (established on `scroll-progress.tsx`, extended by S214) is: (1) `animation-timeline: scroll()`/`view()` under `@supports (animation-timeline: scroll())`, feature-detected post-mount (`CSS.supports`, SSR/hydration-safe, since SSR and the first client render emit the JS path); (2) the existing JS implementation is retained verbatim as the non-supporting-browser fallback (render one or the other), rather than replaced with a static stand-in when the surface is a live indicator; (3) reduced motion never renders the scroll-timeline path. The global `animation-duration: 0.01ms !important` idiom does not reliably neutralize a progress-based timeline: a time duration against a scroll timeline re-maps keyframes, engine-dependent, rather than disabling it. So each converted component needs an explicit `useReducedMotion` guard routing to its proven JS/instant behavior, plus a neutral base state (e.g. `transform: scaleX(0)`) so a killed animation can never freeze mid-state. Also recorded: the S180 devplan row's original target (`route-accent-engine.tsx`) was stale, since that component has been route-driven since S62/D-072; it was re-scoped to `scroll-progress.tsx` at dispatch.
**Why:** the one place the D-007/D-056 hard guarantee could silently break is a scroll timeline surviving the global neutralizer, so making the explicit-guard rule a recorded pattern stops S214 from rediscovering it per component.
**Changes if:** browsers standardize reduced-motion semantics for progress timelines (then the global block may suffice), or S214 finds a surface where the JS fallback is genuinely dead weight.

<!-- v5 Phase-4 Worker build (S198), recorded here. Renumbered D-178/D-179 -> D-180/D-181 at the merge: a concurrent commit independently claimed D-178 for S178 (Glass 2.0), and because both were append-only additions on divergent branches, git auto-merged them without a textual conflict and left a genuine number collision. The Glass 2.0 D-178 landed first and keeps the number; these two are renumbered forward. Code and mocked-fetch tests only; not deployed. -->

### D-180 · (S198, proposed) · Cloudflare Worker `POST /chat`: top-level `worker/` dir, `jose` + Google JWKS token verification (no Admin SDK), stateless relay only
**Decision:** the AI concierge relay is a single Cloudflare Worker at `worker/`, top-level, sibling to `nextjs_space/`. This is the D-004 exception, scoped to exactly one route (`POST /chat`) and nothing else: no general-purpose API surface. Firebase ID token verification uses `jose` (`createRemoteJWKSet` against `https://www.googleapis.com/service_accounts/v1/jwk/securetoken@system.gserviceaccount.com`, `jwtVerify` with `issuer: https://securetoken.google.com/{FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID}` and `audience: FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`), not the Firebase Admin SDK, which is Node-only and does not run on Workers' edge runtime. The Worker is a stateless relay: no Firestore access, no persistence, and no logging of message bodies or tokens anywhere. D-152's journal-never-syncs posture extends structurally to "this Worker has no journal-shaped input at all", and more generally no chat content is ever written to any log or store. All provider secrets (`GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GROQ_API_KEY`) are Worker secret bindings (`env.GEMINI_API_KEY`/`env.GROQ_API_KEY`), read only inside `src/providers.ts` to build outbound requests, and never echoed into a response body, header or log (grep-verified). `wrangler.toml` carries only free-tier config: `[vars] ALLOWED_ORIGIN` (a placeholder; the real Pages origin is filled in at deploy) and `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, with no `[[kv_namespaces]]`, no Durable Objects and no paid bindings. Not deployed in this slice, since no Cloudflare account or keys exist yet; it is proven entirely via `tsc --noEmit` plus a mocked-`fetch` vitest suite (JWKS, Gemini and Groq are all faked, and a real RSA keypair with `jose`'s own `SignJWT` proves the signature check is non-vacuous). the worker README carries the exact manual deploy steps (`wrangler login` / `wrangler deploy` / `wrangler secret put …`), the same pattern as the `firestore.rules` deploy runbook (D-044): those commands are run by hand, never from the repo.
**Why:** Workers' edge runtime can't run the Node-only Admin SDK, so JWT-verification-via-JWKS is the standard, correct substitute (used by Auth.js, Clerk and Cloudflare's own docs) rather than a hand-rolled crypto path; keeping the Worker to exactly one route and zero persistence is what keeps the D-004/D-152 boundaries intact as this app's very first server-side exception.
**Changes if:** a second Worker route is ever proposed (a new decision, not a silent extension of this one), or Cloudflare's free tier terms change enough to threaten the no-card guarantee (re-verify, per the standing free-tools-only rule).

### D-181 · (S198, proposed) · `/chat` operational specifics: explicit CORS 403 (not silent omission), 8KB body cap, any non-2xx from Gemini triggers Groq fallback, generic 502 on total failure
**Decision:** four operational choices left to the implementer, recorded so they aren't silently re-decided later. (1) CORS enforcement returns an explicit `403 origin not allowed` rather than only omitting CORS response headers. CORS is a browser-enforced contract and a non-browser client can set any `Origin` header, so the Worker needs its own server-side reject instead of relying on the browser to withhold the response from JS. (2) The request body cap is 8KB (`MAX_BODY_BYTES`), checked both via `Content-Length` (the fast path) and the actual read length (the authoritative path, since `Content-Length` can be absent or wrong). That is generous for a chat turn plus a short history, and small enough to bound abuse of the free-tier quota. (3) Any non-2xx response from Gemini triggers the Groq fallback, a broader net than the 429/5xx the spec literally listed, so it also covers something like a transient 400 from a malformed-but-not-our-fault upstream hiccup; network errors (a thrown `fetch`) fall through identically. (4) The total-failure response is a fixed generic JSON `{ "error": "concierge temporarily unavailable" }` at `502`: no upstream provider error body or key material is ever forwarded, proven by a dedicated test asserting the string never appears in the response.
**Why:** these are the kind of small implementation calls someone has to make to ship a checkable slice, and recording them means a future slice (S199 client wiring, or a Worker revision) doesn't have to reverse-engineer the behavior from the code.
**Changes if:** we want CORS failures to silently drop instead of returning 403 (revisit #1), the 8KB cap proves too small or too large in real use (revisit #2, a cheap Worker-side constant change), or the fallback trigger needs narrowing back to exactly 429/5xx (revisit #3).

### D-182 · (S184, 2026-07-16) · Travel Mode chrome suppression: per-island `isTravelRoute()` null-guard, event-hosts suppressed too, `layout.tsx` untouched
**Decision:** the chrome-free `/travel` route (D-164) is implemented as an `if (isTravelRoute(pathname)) return null` guard placed after all hooks in each of the six chrome-island client components. All six import the single boundary-checked helper `lib/travel-route.ts` (`=== '/travel' || startsWith('/travel/')`), which accepts both trailing-slash forms and never matches a sibling like `/travelogue`, so the match rule cannot drift. `layout.tsx` is not restructured. The two invisible event-hosts (`QuickAddHost`, `ExpenseLogHost`) are suppressed as well (TM-9 zero-chrome-leakage; nothing in Phase 2 dispatches their open events under `/travel`). A later TM slice wanting the global add/expense flow lifts that guard deliberately, per the comment each guard carries. `CommandPalette`/`OfflineBanner`/`ServiceWorkerRegistrar` stay mounted, since they aren't chrome per the six-island definition. Also recorded (this amends the working assumption behind D-170's counting, not the decision): adding a route to `ROUTE_HTML` grows the precache by **two** entries, the route HTML plus its auto-discovered `app/<route>/page-*.js` chunk (S184: 133→135, not 134).
**Why:** one helper beats six string-compares; suppressing the inert hosts is the honest zero-leak default and cheap to reverse; the precache-delta fact stops the next route slice from "fixing" a correct count.
**Changes if:** a TM slice needs the quick-add/expense flow inside `/travel` (lift that host's guard), or the chrome-island set changes.

### D-183 · (S200, 2026-07-16) · Google sign-in: shared `ensureSignedIn` non-clobber fix + opt-in popup sign-in, no rules/sign-out change
**Decision:** Google sign-in (S200, implementing D-168) is built as a shared `ensureSignedIn(auth, signInAnonymously)` helper in the new `lib/firebase-auth.ts`, used by both `itinerary-remote.ts`'s `getRemote()` and `presence.ts`'s `getPresence()`. Previously each called `signInAnonymously` unconditionally, every time, with no check for an existing signed-in user. Since Firebase Auth persists sign-in state across reloads by default, this was a live bug waiting to happen the moment any non-anonymous sign-in existed: a blind `signInAnonymously` call would silently replace a persisted Google session with a fresh anonymous one, defeating the point of a stable uid. The fix: `await auth.authStateReady()` before checking `auth.currentUser` (it safely waits for the SDK's persisted-session restore, since reading `auth.currentUser` synchronously right after `getAuth(app)` can race and see a stale `null`), and only mint a fresh anonymous user when none exists, of either kind. Google sign-in itself is `signInWithPopup` rather than redirect: simpler for a single user-gesture click, no `getRedirectResult` handling needed.
