It lives in a new opt-in Settings group (`components/settings-panel.tsx`'s `GoogleSignInGroup`, dormant/guest-gated exactly like `hooks/use-presence.ts`'s existing pattern); success reloads the page, matching the S194 Trip Pack switcher precedent (no live cache-invalidation built for the two module-scope handle promises), and failure shows an inline, re-clickable error. There is no `firestore.rules` change: the allowlist's `request.auth.uid in [...]` shape is uid-source-agnostic, and only which uids get collected changes (now via Settings, documented in the runbook, section 8). No sign-out flow, no second provider, no accounts system (D-168 LOCKED scope fence).
**Why:** the non-clobber fix is what makes Google sign-in actually deliver a *stable* uid instead of silently reverting to anonymous on the very next reload, exactly the failure mode D-168 exists to close. Extracting one shared helper, rather than patching the same bug twice, keeps both synced-domain entry points correct together, per D-047's "one firebase app, one uid" invariant.
**Changes if:** a sign-out flow or a second auth provider is ever wanted (both explicitly out of scope here); or if `authStateReady()`'s real-browser behavior ever diverges from its mocked-test behavior. That was verified against the SDK's shipped type surface plus mocked unit tests only, not a live Firebase project, since no `.env.local`/live project access exists in the build sandbox.

### D-184 · (S185, 2026-07-16) · Travel-hero "now" window: `[start, start+duration)`; open-ended fallback = next timed item's start, capped at 120 min
**Decision:** the Now/Next hero card's current-activity ("now") window is `[start, start+durationMinutes)`. When `durationMinutes` is absent, the window ends at the start of the day's next timed item, capped at `DEFAULT_NOW_BLOCK_MIN` (120 min): an open-ended item is "now" until the next thing begins but never longer than 2 hours, after which the card honestly falls back to `upcoming` rather than pinning a stale multi-hour "now". The cap also bounds the day's last item, which has no successor to end it. Overlapping live items resolve to the latest-starting one. The derivation is pure in `lib/travel-hero.ts` (D-016 shape: clock injected, `?today=` drives it for free) and composes the frozen `nextUp()` for the "next" slot. `lib/whats-next.ts` and its suite are byte-untouched.
**Why:** the missing-duration case is the common one for real itinerary items; an explicit bounded rule beats either "now forever" (stale) or "no now without duration" (empty card for most days).
**Changes if:** field use shows 120 min is the wrong cap (single-constant change), or a later slice adds explicit end-times to the item editor.

### D-185 · (S185, 2026-07-16) · Travel-hero flip is React-level reduced-motion gated (spring-free branch, not a neutralized animation)
**Decision:** under `prefers-reduced-motion` the hero card renders a spring-free plain branch (`data-flip-animated="false"`) giving an instant state swap, rather than relying on the global animation neutralizer to zero out the framer spring. Same rationale family as D-179's scroll-timeline rule: an explicit guard where the global idiom can't be trusted. The time-driven progress bar stays width-correct in both states, since its width is data, not motion. E2E proof follows the `motion.spec.ts` route-fade pattern (assert the React branch plus opacity, not framer internals); the recalc spec uses Playwright `page.clock` with `timezoneId: 'Asia/Kathmandu'`, since `?today=`'s noon-freeze can't express two instants in one page load.
**Why:** D-007/D-056 are hard guarantees. A spring on a state flip is exactly the class of motion the global config governs only if the component routes through `m.*`, and the explicit branch makes the guarantee deterministic and testable.
**Changes if:** the app's LazyMotion/MotionConfig setup changes shape.

### D-186 · (S186, 2026-07-16) · Shared `TripAgenda`: one list, `variant: 'today' | 'travel'` discriminated union; consumer owns the mutation
**Decision:** the agenda list is one shared component `components/trip-agenda.tsx` with a discriminated-union prop shape. `{variant:'today'} & Common` renders byte-equivalent to the pre-S186 `today-panel.tsx` markup (frozen visual baseline held: 24/24, 0 regenerated); `{variant:'travel'; ctx: NextUpContext} & Common` renders 48pt (`min-h-[48px]`) rows with per-row phase emphasis. `ctx` is required only for `travel`, so the `today` branch stays clock-free and presentation-only. Done-tracking is consumer-supplied `onToggle` into the existing `updateItem(date, id, {done})` (D-018), so Today and Travel Mode toggles are one mutation and reflect on each other (E2E-proven cross-route). The today row renderer (`TodayAgendaItem`) was moved verbatim and deliberately not merged with the travel row: byte-equivalence beats DRY here. A third consumer passes the same props; never fork the list.
**Why:** the extraction's whole risk was today-panel drift under a zero re-baseline budget. Verbatim-move plus variant-branch is the shape that makes 0-drift provable rather than hoped.
**Changes if:** the today-panel design intentionally changes (then the two row renderers can merge under a declared re-baseline).

### D-187 · (S186, 2026-07-16) · `deriveRowPhases` is the per-row view of the travel-hero machine — same module, same injected clock, never a re-derivation
**Decision:** TM agenda row phases (`now`/`upcoming`/`past`/`done`/`untimed`) come from the pure, additive `deriveRowPhases(items, ctx)` in `lib/travel-hero.ts`, which reuses that module's own `currentActivity`/`timedItems` internals. A row's "now" is therefore definitionally the same "now" the hero card shows (D-016/D-184: one injected clock, one 120-min cap, no fork). Output is order-aligned with `items`.
**Why:** two independent phase derivations would eventually disagree on a boundary minute. The card saying "now" while the row says "past" is exactly the class of bug a shared pure module exists to prevent.
**Changes if:** D-184's window rule changes (both surfaces follow automatically, which is the point).

### D-188 · (S187, 2026-07-16) · `resolveTravelDate`: `?date=` picks the day, `?today=` drives the clock; previewed days reinterpret "now" at the previewed day's place offset; no post-trip forced default
**Decision:** Travel Mode date resolution is the pure `resolveTravelDate()` (`lib/travel-date.ts`), which never reads `?today=` itself. It consumes the already-resolved `getTodayInTrip()` (D-075 stays a raw parse in `trip-now.ts`, untouched). With no `?date=`: on-trip (real or simulated) resolves to today, following the sim clock live; pre-trip resolves to Day 1 plus "Trip starts in N days" (via the existing `computeCountdown`); post-trip resolves to `date: null`, the pre-S187 off-trip UI, with no forced default (deliberately unspecified in the plan; revisit only on request). With both params set, `?date=` wins for which day renders and `?today=` still drives the hour. The hero/agenda cards now reinterpret the injected instant at the **previewed day's** country/place offset (`getCountryForDate`) rather than today's. That fix is what makes a Dec 18 (NPT) vs Dec 19 (JST) preview resolve to the correct leg, day number, and phases. It was a latent seam bug in S185/S186, where header and offset always derived from today's country; the result is byte-identical when `date` is absent or today, proven by the untouched S185/S186 specs. Selection writes via `router.replace` plus rebuilt `URLSearchParams` preserving other params (the S147/D-161 `?focus=` idiom). Also in this slice: `day-strip.tsx` weekday label `text-white/40`→`/60`, a real WCAG AA failure (~3.5:1) previously invisible to axe because `/plan` renders the strip inside `lg:hidden`, while Travel Mode exposes it at every width. Visual pack re-verified 24/24 against unchanged baselines (the label sits outside captured regions).
**Why:** one pure resolver keeps the two-param composition (D-164's "compose, never merge") testable in isolation, and the place-offset reinterpretation is what "preview another day" means across a timezone hop.
**Changes if:** a post-trip default day is wanted, or the trip window (D-006) changes.

### D-189 · (S188, 2026-07-16) · Frankfurter is the Essentials display-rate source — free/keyless, but confirmed live to not carry NPR; the NPR fetch is short-circuited
**Decision:** the Travel Mode Essentials currency line uses `api.frankfurter.dev` (free, keyless, no card, the v5-plan-approved choice under the free-tools-only standing rule) for a display-only live USD→leg-currency rate via `lib/currency-rate.ts`. It is deliberately separate from `core/budget/model.ts`'s manual user-overridable rates: D-088's "zero rate APIs" line governs the budget and is untouched. Live-probed 2026-07-16: JPY is served (200), NPR returns HTTP 404 (Frankfurter mirrors ECB reference currencies, and the Nepalese Rupee isn't one). A non-2xx `fetch` prints an un-suppressible browser console error, so `fetchCurrencyRate` never issues the NPR request (bounded `UNSUPPORTED_CURRENCIES` short-circuit); the Nepal leg shows the cached last-good value (stale) or an honest "unavailable" state. The cache is a module-owned localStorage key (`nepal_japan_currency_rate_cache`, `weatherCache`-shaped), a deliberate, flagged exception to D-097's one-registry convention per the slice's no-gateway-key-change constraint.
**Why:** a known-always-failing network call would print a console error on every Nepal-leg `/travel` load and break the console-clean gate. Skipping it reaches the same honest outcome with zero noise.
**Changes if:** Frankfurter adds NPR, or a different free/keyless source carrying NPR is adopted for that leg.

### D-190 · (S188, 2026-07-16) · `gen-sw.mjs` gains its first cross-origin exception: stale-while-revalidate for `api.frankfurter.dev` only
**Decision:** the SW fetch handler intercepts `GET` requests to `api.frankfurter.dev` with a stale-while-revalidate strategy (own cache `trip-frankfurter-v1`) before the general cross-origin passthrough. Every other host (Firebase/gstatic/fonts) still hits the untouched passthrough, so D-073/D-038 protection is intact. Note that `gen-sw.mjs` had no prior runtime-caching precedent: Open-Meteo is cached at the app level (`weatherCache`) only, and this is the first and only SW-level cross-origin route, hand-added and commented per D-170. It is a network-layer nicety on top of `lib/currency-rate.ts`'s own localStorage cache, which alone carries the D-038/D-055 dormant-safe guarantee.
**Why:** the brief asked for SW s-w-r explicitly, and scoping it to exactly one hostname keeps the Firebase-protecting passthrough auditable.
**Changes if:** never widened without deliberate re-review. Any future cross-origin SW exception must follow this same narrow-host pattern.

### D-191 · (S188, 2026-07-16) · Screen Wake Lock on `/travel`: feature-detected, visibility-scoped, zero-dependency; subtle hint only while held
**Decision:** `lib/use-wake-lock.ts` requests `navigator.wakeLock` while `/travel` is mounted and `document.visibilityState === 'visible'`, re-acquires on visibilitychange, and releases on hide/unmount. It is a feature-detected no-op elsewhere (iOS Safari 18.4+ supported). UI: a one-line "Screen stays awake while Travel Mode is open" hint (`travel-wake-lock-hint`), rendered only when the API is supported and a lock is currently held, absent everywhere else, with no layout reservation.
**Why:** day-of transit use is exactly when a sleeping screen hurts, and the hint makes the battery-relevant behavior honest without a settings surface nobody asked for.
**Changes if:** we want it silent, a toggle, or app-wide.

### D-192 · (S189, 2026-07-16) · TM outdoor high-legibility: `html[data-tm-legibility]` contract — token retune + root type bump + `[class*='text-white/']` catch-all
**Decision:** the Travel Mode outdoor toggle (D-165's deliberate light-mode substitute) stamps `html[data-tm-legibility="high"]`, mounted and removed only inside the `/travel` client tree (`travel-legibility-toggle.tsx`: removed on toggle-off, on route leave, and unconditionally on unmount; leak E2E-proven absent on every other route). `globals.css` uses that attribute to (a) retune the D-177 semantic surface tokens darker (same navy hue family, `--surface: 4 6 16` etc.) plus `--foreground`/`--border`/`--muted-foreground`, (b) bump root `font-size` to 112.5% (rem is root-relative, so the type-scale lever must live on `html`, not a container, which is safe because the attribute only exists on the chrome-free route), and (c) raise every `text-white/*` utility's computed color to `rgb(255 255 255 / 0.92)` via a `[class*='text-white/']` catch-all with `!important`. That last one is needed because TM's cards render body copy as raw `text-white/NN` opacity utilities rather than `text-foreground`, so the D-177 token layer alone would move zero rendered pixels of TM text. It is a monotonic raise only (0.92 exceeds every alpha TM uses): worst case `text-white/45` on `.glass-card` goes from ≈4.4:1 normal to ≈14.4:1 high. Persisted as gateway **key 18** (`travelLegibility: 'nepal_japan_travel_legibility'`, `String(boolean)`, D-097 registry) so a PWA relaunch restores the state. The off-state is byte-identical, since the block only applies when stamped (visual pack 24/24 vs unchanged baselines).
**Why:** D-177 assumed component text consumes semantic tokens and TM's actual markup doesn't. The catch-all changes what the same classNames compute to, keeping "retune tokens, not components" in spirit while actually being visible.
**Changes if:** TM components migrate body copy to semantic text tokens (then narrow or retire the catch-all), or the high state's values want tuning after real-sunlight field use.

### D-194 · (S190, 2026-07-17) · Travel Mode enter/exit: one entry hook, push-enter / replace-exit history model, 3-state flag (key 19), session return slot (key 20), honest arrival heuristic
**Decision:** all four entry surfaces (the persistent nav-chrome button on every page/phase/width per the D-164 directive; the Home hero CTA; the on-trip in-trip card; the on-trip arrival toast) route through one hook, `useEnterTravelMode()`. It records `travelReturn` (**key 20**, sessionStorage, origin `pathname+search`), arms `travelMode` (**key 19**, localStorage, 3-state presence string `absent → 'seen' → 'active'`), then **pushes** `/travel/`, so browser Back alone leaves cleanly to the origin. The exit X (≥44px, hard DoD) downgrades the flag to `'seen'`, clears the return slot, and **replaces** to the remembered route (or `/` on a cold start). Replace drops `/travel` from history, so Back after exit can never loop: no history trap, E2E-proven both directions. PWA relaunch re-enter is a boot-once null-render island (`travel-mode-relaunch.tsx`, mounted in the provider tree beside FirstRunTour, `layout.tsx` untouched) that replaces to `/travel` when the flag is `'active'` and the user isn't a guest. Back-without-exit deliberately leaves the flag `'active'`, so a later relaunch re-enters: that is D-164's stickiness, and the field test will judge the feel. The arrival toast fires exactly once per device, on "on-trip (`getTodayInTrip()`, D-075-aware) and flag-absent and not guest and not on `/travel`"; dismiss writes `'seen'` and never clobbers `'active'`; no geolocation, no network. For guests, entry surfaces lead to the existing S184 guest wall and the flag is never armed. Entry never touches `?today=`/`?date=` (D-075/D-188). **Implementation note:** the key-19/20 accessors live in `core/storage/travel-mode-store.ts`, not `gateway.ts`. They compose gateway primitives over `STORAGE_KEYS` literals (D-097 holds: raw storage and key literals stay in the gateway) and are split out purely to keep `gateway.ts`'s app-wide First Load chunk byte-stable. **Accepted cost:** shared First Load 104→~105 kB (~0.6 kB, emergent webpack re-chunking from the app-wide toast/relaunch islands; it was isolated as not attributable to one removable import, and on main the final build reports shared 104 kB / routes 106–107). The two home-hero mobile/tablet baselines were deliberately re-baselined for the new hero CTA; they are the only baselines that changed, and desktop regenerated byte-identical.
**Why:** one entry hook makes the flag/return bookkeeping unforkable, and push-in/replace-out is the minimal history model that satisfies both "Back leaves cleanly" and "exit can't loop".
**Changes if:** the entry/exit UX is redirected after the Nov 9 field test (especially the Back-without-exit stickiness), or a second chrome-level entry point (BottomTabBar) is requested.

### D-193 · (S229, Session B, 2026-07-16) · Sync-status affordance: single app-wide `lastAckAt`, not per-domain; outbox slot persists once any ack has occurred
**Decision:** `OutboxSlot` (gateway key 15, S141/D-150) gains an additive `lastAckAt?: string`: a single, app-wide ISO timestamp of the most recent successful `ack()` across every sync domain, not a per-domain timestamp. One "synced" signal is what the badge needs, and a traveler doesn't need to know which domain acked last. `saveSlot()`'s behavior changes: previously a fully-clean outbox (`dirty` empty) removed the gateway key outright, and now the key persists holding `{dirty:{}, lastAckAt}` once any ack has ever landed, so the resting "Synced Xm ago" UI state survives a reload. Old slots with no `lastAckAt` field remain valid, read as "no ack yet", never throw, and there is no `version` bump. `saveSlot()` also dispatches a new same-tab `SYNC_OUTBOX_CHANGED_EVENT` (D-026 idiom) on every write, enqueue or ack, read by the new `hooks/use-sync-status.ts` plus a cross-tab `storage` listener. The new `outboxSnapshot()` export reuses the outbox's own `enabled()` gate (D-038/D-055/D-120), so a dormant or guest build always reads `{dirty:{}, lastAckAt:null}` and `components/sync-status-badge.tsx` (mounted once in `app/layout.tsx`, sibling of `OfflineBanner`) renders nothing on those builds with no separate gate duplicated in the UI layer. Pending count sums `Object.values(dirty).flat().length` over every domain key currently present, never a hardcoded 3-domain sum, so a future 4th `SyncDomain` needs zero edits here (proven by a dedicated test seeding a synthetic 4th key). No new gateway key, no new dependency.
**Why:** completes the offline-push outbox's UX story (S141 made sync survive a reload; this makes its state visible) using the exact reactive-store pattern already proven for favorites and online status, without touching the push/flush/merge algebra itself.
**Changes if:** a 4th real `SyncDomain` is added (already tolerated, no change needed), or we want per-domain sync status surfaced instead of one app-wide signal (that would need a shape change on `lastAckAt`).

### D-196 · (S221, Session B, 2026-07-17) · Manifest shortcuts: 3 fixed entries, "Trip Budget" substituted for the spec’s nonexistent "Add Expense" target
**Decision:** `scripts/gen-sw.mjs`'s `buildManifest()` (D-170, hand-maintained) gains a `shortcuts` array with 3 entries, each an existing, already-working route via the same `withBase()` idiom the `icons` array already uses: "Today's Itinerary" → `/plan/`, "Trip Budget" → `/plan/#budget-panel-title`, "Countdown" → `/#dashboard`. The budget entry is an in-page anchor to the budget panel's heading, because there is no separate add-expense route or query-param deep link, so we used the real feature and its existing heading id rather than inventing a target. Icons are omitted on shortcut entries (optional per the manifest spec, kept minimal). `e2e/pwa.spec.ts` is extended to assert the shape and drive a real navigation to each target confirming an `<h1>` renders.
**Why:** every shortcut target must resolve to a real route (no speculative UI), and "Trip Budget" is the honest name for what actually exists at that anchor.
**Changes if:** a dedicated add-expense entry point is ever built, or we want different or more shortcuts (the manifest supports up to ~4 on most platforms).

### D-197 · (S209, Session B, 2026-07-17) · S210 offline PMTiles maps: NO-GO for v5, dropped from the Phase-5 candidate pool
**Decision:** the S209 feasibility spike (`docs/pmtiles-feasibility.md`) found that a self-hosted PMTiles vector basemap covering this trip's geography fits the GitHub Pages 100MB-per-file hard cap only at an overview zoom (z ≤ 12), which is district/region level with no street detail. The street-level zoom (z14–15) that would make offline maps genuinely useful runs 180MB–3.7GB for the dense Tokyo–Kyoto–Osaka (Tokaido) corridor, well over the cap git enforces on push. MapLibre 5.24 plus `pmtiles` npm v4 (promise-based `addProtocol`) is technically compatible; compatibility was never the blocker. **S210 is dropped from the v5 Phase-5 candidate pool.** Sizing was computed via reproducible Web-Mercator tile-count arithmetic across a bracketed average-tile-size range, since the sandbox had no outbound network to run a live extract. The doc's section 5 has the exact free, no-signup commands to produce a live measured number if this is ever revisited.
**Why:** capping to z12 to fit ships a district overview that adds little over the already-working, free, keyless online CARTO raster basemap (D-003/D-079's "polished mock"). In exchange it costs a new runtime dependency, a hand-authored vector style plus glyph/sprite assets, and a 50–100MB staleness-prone binary that roughly doubles the repo's `.git` size on every commit. Offline maps were never a stated project goal.
**Changes if:** a concrete offline-map requirement is stated and it accepts (a) overview-only zoom (z≤12), (b) a live-measured <100MB confirmation, and (c) the binary handled via Git LFS or a build-time fetch, never committed to history.

### D-198 · (S207, Session B, 2026-07-17) · Currency-converter command is typeahead-parse inside the palette, not a new popover; reuses S188's `currency-rate.ts` as-is
**Decision:** the ⌘K command-palette command for currency conversion is a typeahead-parse ("100 usd to jpy") rendered as a `forceMount`ed dynamic `CommandGroup`, the same machinery S147's "In your plan" group established (memoized parse → async effect → forceMount render), not a new dialog or popover UI surface. The new pure `lib/currency-convert.ts` (`parseConversionQuery`/`convertCurrency`) is a thin wrapper that imports and calls S188's `fetchCurrencyRate` from `lib/currency-rate.ts` unmodified: no second Frankfurter client, no new cache (grep-proof re-confirmed, `frankfurter` appears only in `currency-rate.ts`, its own spec, and the new `currency-command.spec.ts`). USD is never fetched (implicit rate 1). NPR goes through the existing D-189 `UNSUPPORTED_CURRENCIES` short-circuit unmodified, so the palette shows an honest "NPR rate unavailable — no cached rate yet" (or a cached-with-as-of-date value if one exists from prior Essentials-block use) rather than a doomed fetch or a raw error.
**Why:** reusing the existing dynamic-group and shared-cache machinery is the smaller diff against the palette's established static/dynamic-group shape, and it keeps exactly one Frankfurter integration point in the codebase (D-189's grep-proof invariant holds).
**Changes if:** we want a persistent "last used currency pair" (explicitly scoped out of this slice as creep) or a dedicated standalone converter page instead of a palette command.

### D-199 · (S228, Session B, 2026-07-17) · Compressed export/import: gzip via native Compression Streams, magic-byte auto-detect (not filename), D-098 contract unchanged
**Decision:** both export/import surfaces, the itinerary Vault (`core/vault/export-import.ts`/`backup-restore.tsx`, D-098 LOCKED) and S174's expenses backup-restore (`settings-panel.tsx`), now transport their JSON envelope gzip-compressed via a shared `core/vault/compression.ts` (`compressToBlob`/`decompressBlobOrText`/`supportsCompression`, native `CompressionStream`/`DecompressionStream`, zero new dependency). Export filenames gain a `.gz` suffix when the browser supports it: a visible, intended behavior change, and evergreen browsers all support it. Import detects compressed-vs-plain by reading the actual gzip magic bytes (`0x1f 0x8b`), never by filename or extension, proven by both a unit test and an e2e spec using deliberately misleading filenames in both directions. This makes old, pre-S228 plain-JSON export files still importable, proven end-to-end rather than only unit-asserted. D-098's export/import schema and contract are unchanged: only the wire encoding changed, and the decompressed string is byte-identical to today's plain export.
**Why:** magic-byte detection is the only auto-detect mechanism that actually holds up. A user can rename a file, and filename-based detection would silently mis-decode it.
**Changes if:** the S217 candidate ships its own checklist export (mentioned as a maybe in the original candidate description) — it should reuse this same shared helper rather than fork it.

### D-195 · (S191, 2026-07-17) · Device-project policy: real-hardware viewports via chromium-engine emulation, testMatch-scoped to the TM acceptance net only
**Decision:** the `iphone-15-pro` (393×852) and `iphone-15-pro-max` (430×932) Playwright projects emulate the iPhone 15 Pro / Pro Max device descriptors (DPR 3, `isMobile`, `hasTouch`, iOS-Safari UA) on chromium, not real WebKit. WebKit is uninstalled in this sandbox, its download is an unverifiable step, and WebKit-on-Windows against the single-threaded `serve-out` server is exactly the documented parallel-flake class, so determinism on this machine beats engine purity (brief-blessed rule). Viewport is pinned to the device's full screen points, the standalone-PWA display Travel Mode targets (the V5-DEVPLAN TM-8 numbers), not the descriptor's Safari-chrome-reduced web viewport (659/739). Each device project is `testMatch`-scoped to `e2e/tm-acceptance.spec.ts` alone, and the default `chromium` project `testIgnore`s that same file, so the default net's spec count stays byte-stable (proven: `--list --project=chromium` identical with and without S191 on the same tree). Future device-scoped acceptance nets follow the same pattern: one scoped spec file plus a matching testMatch/testIgnore pair.
**Why:** the acceptance evidence must be deterministic on the machine that produces it. An engine that isn't installed and can't be verified here would make Phase 2's DoD rest on an unverified step.
**Changes if:** real WebKit coverage is ever required (say, an iOS-Safari-specific defect is suspected). Then install and burn in WebKit deliberately as its own slice; don't fold it into a green net.

### D-200 · (S226, Session B, 2026-07-17) · Self-hosted subsetted fonts: DoD already met by default `next/font/google` behavior, except font preload links (architectural, out of scope)
**Decision:** verification-only slice, zero net code change (`app/layout.tsx` confirmed byte-identical before and after; an experimental preload-option edit was tested then reverted). Confirmed via a real production build plus inspection of the built `out/` tree: fonts are self-hosted (zero `fonts.googleapis.com`/`fonts.gstatic.com` requests, served from `_next/static/media/*.woff2`), subsetted (`latin`), `font-display:swap` on all 15 emitted `@font-face` rules, and CLS-mitigated via `next/font`'s automatic fallback-font `size-adjust`/`ascent-override`/`descent-override` metric-matching. One gap is confirmed not closeable within this slice's scope: no `<link rel="preload" as="font">` is ever emitted, because `output:'export'` (the LOCKED static-export architecture, no Node server) disables `next/font`'s per-request preload-link injection regardless of font-loader options. That was proven by testing `display:'swap', preload:true` explicitly and rebuilding with zero output change. Manually hardcoding a preload link would require reading build-time content-hashed filenames at author time: real new engineering, not a config fix, and explicitly fenced out of this brief. Practical impact is judged small, since the render-blocking font stylesheet already loads first in `<head>`, so the browser's preload scanner discovers the `.woff2` files early regardless.
**Why:** the spec’s own constraint (minimal-diff-only, no restructure) correctly stopped at a real architectural boundary rather than forcing a fix that doesn't fit the slice.
**Changes if:** we want the preload gap closed anyway. That would need a small new follow-up slice (a build-time script reading `next-font-manifest.json` or the emitted CSS to inject explicit preload links into `layout.tsx`'s JSX), not a reopening of this one.

### D-201 · (S206, Session B, 2026-07-17) · Packing checklist: fixed 28-item template (no add/remove), gateway key 21, framework-free core mirrors `core/journal/model.ts`
**Decision:** a new `/packing` route ships a country-scoped packing checklist: 28 fixed items (10 universal, 9 Nepal-leg, 9 Japan-leg), seeded from a built-in `DEFAULT_TEMPLATE` on first load. There is deliberately no add/remove UI. The domain's only mutation is `toggleItem` (check/uncheck), which is a different shape from the journal/expenses/favorites domains, which start empty and grow. Persisted via gateway **key 21** (`packing`, `STORAGE_KEYS.packing` → `nepal_japan_packing`), framework-free pure core (`core/packing/model.ts`, mirroring `core/journal/model.ts`'s total-function/no-throw contract), wired through the existing `createReactiveStore` factory (D-148) via `hooks/use-packing.ts`, with no hand-rolled hydrate/listen/commit skeleton. It is reachable via a new command-palette entry (`components/command-palette.tsx`, `More` group) only; `navbar.tsx`/`nav-items.ts` wiring is explicitly deferred, the same historical pattern as `/journal`/`/safety`/`/recap`'s original launch (FU-33 precedent), to avoid touching nav surfaces while Session A's Phase-2 navbar work is active. `scripts/gen-sw.mjs`'s `ROUTE_HTML` gains one line for the new route (precache 144→145); no other `gen-sw.mjs` logic is touched.
**Why:** a fixed template is the actual value proposition here — a ready-made packing list, not a blank list to fill in. It is distinct in shape from the S217 candidate's critical-docs checklist, which is a separate page by design (see the S217-candidate scope-note in `docs/V5-DEVPLAN.md` section 7 on why proliferating near-identical checklist pages is avoided; packing and critical-docs are genuinely different item sets).
**Changes if:** user-added custom packing items are wanted (that would need an add/remove UI plus a schema change, a real scope expansion), or `/packing` is wired into the primary nav (a later rider once Phase-2 navbar churn settles).

### D-202 · (S192, 2026-07-17) · Travel Mode offline scope: current-day interactions are offline-durable; cross-day `?date=` navigation requires the network (Next static-export RSC refetch)
**Decision:** with the SW active, `/travel` is fully functional offline for the *loaded* day (hero, agenda, done-toggle into vault, hero-expand) and survives a cold offline reload with the persisted edit intact (TM-11, proven on both iPhone projects). Switching to a *different* trip day is not offline-capable: a day-strip pick's `router.replace` makes Next's app-router refetch the route RSC payload (`/travel/index.txt`), and offline that fetch fails, so Next hard-navigates to the SW nav-fallback (the app-root shell). Accepted as an architectural boundary of D-131 (static export) plus D-073 (SW), not a Travel Mode defect. TM-11's spec asserts exactly the guarantees that hold and does not claim offline day-switching.
**Why:** a fix (precache per-date RSC payloads, or move `?date=` off the router to a client-only signal) crosses the D-188/D-164 date-resolution contract and exceeds the in-slice fix budget. The work correctly stopped and reported per the spec; documented in an in-test note and `docs/tm-field-test.md`.
**Changes if:** offline day-switching becomes a real field need. Then a deliberate slice precaches per-date RSC payloads or reroutes `?date=` client-side (both touch D-188/D-164, blueprint first).

### D-203 · (S192 follow-up, 2026-07-18) · TM `?date=` selection is a same-document `history.replaceState`, not `router.replace` — offline day-switching now works (supersedes D-202's accepted limitation and D-188's mechanism sentence only)
**Decision:** `travel-date-picker.tsx`'s day-strip selection writes the `?date=` param via native `window.history.replaceState` (Next ≥14.2 syncs it into `useSearchParams`) instead of `router.replace`. Root cause the TM-11 net exposed: in the static export, `router.replace` for a same-page search-param change fetches the route's RSC payload (`/travel/index.txt?…`), which is not precached; offline the failed fetch made Next hard-navigate to the `.txt` URL, and the SW answered with the nav-fallback Home shell, killing offline day-picking. `history.replaceState` is the network-free equivalent: same URL shape, `?today=` and other params preserved by the existing `withDateParam`, nothing fetched. D-188's resolution semantics, param preservation, and Dec 9–Jan 9 bounds are untouched; this is mechanism only. D-202's "cross-day nav requires the network" clause is superseded. Its durable-current-day and offline-reload guarantees stand, and TM-11 now additionally asserts offline cross-day picking (unseeded day → empty state → back → agenda plus persisted edit intact).
**Provenance:** the original S192 work survived a session clear and independently found and fixed this (~8 lines); the re-dispatched attempt had honestly stop-reported the same defect (D-202). We harvested the fix, grafted the offline date-pick assertions into the landed TM-11, discarded the duplicate spec/baseline work, and re-verified: tsc clean · build 13 routes `/travel` 106 kB sw 145 · TM net 62/62 ×2 · chromium travel specs green (travel-essentials flake green isolated, documented signature) · Vitest 1016/1016.
**Changes if:** the app leaves static export, or a slice deliberately precaches per-date RSC payloads in `gen-sw.mjs`.

### D-204 · (S224, 2026-07-18) · Map-linked itinerary editing: day anchors are a local-only derived-view mechanism — no schema change, no routing API
**Decision:** the map (mock, D-003/D-079) becomes an input to planning. Assigning a map pin to a trip day adds it via the existing `addItem` CRUD (the one synced write, D-088 one-commit-per-action; an idempotent re-anchor writes nothing) and records the pin as that day's anchor. The day's stops then re-order nearest-first by client-side haversine (`lib/day-anchor.ts`: pure `haversineKm()` plus `orderByProximity()`, coord-less items sink stably to the end, antimeridian-correct). There is no routing or geocoding API (free-tools-only LOCKED). The reorder is a derived view, never persisted into the itinerary array: no new `ItineraryItem` field, and the `mergeItems` sync contract (D-149) is untouched. The anchor lives in new gateway **key 22 `dayAnchors`** (`Record<dateISO, markerId>`, local-only, non-synced, self-healing on stale ids). Affordances: a popup day-select plus Anchor button (the keyboard/touch a11y floor, D-100) and a desktop HTML5 pointer-drag handle (convenience, manually verified, since HTML5 DnD over the MapLibre canvas is untestably flaky under Playwright and the tested select is its functional equivalent). Dormant and sync-off behavior is unchanged (D-038); axe `/map` serious/critical = 0. D-157/D-158 map behaviors are intact (a mid-slice popup-height regression against FU-34 favorites was found and fixed: a compact single-row assign block plus popup `max-height:70vh`).
**Why:** the derived-view design answers the spec’s D-149 decision point with zero sync-contract risk, and the local anchor is the blessed no-schema path.
**Changes if:** anchors ever need to sync across travelers (that would need a schema/domain decision, blueprint first) or a real routing provider is greenlit (a D-003 change, owner-gated).

**AMENDED IN PART BY D-281 (informed ruling, 2026-08-05):** the nearest-first haversine re-order is retired. Every surface, the map included, now sorts by time (`sortItemsByTime`), and `orderByProximity` is deleted. The call was made with the cost stated explicitly: it costs the day-anchor feature its walking-route purpose. Everything else in this entry still stands — the `addItem` write, the local-only self-healing anchor (key 22), no schema change, no routing API. The anchor is now the day's base point (map focus plus the per-row distance label), not a sort key. See D-281.

### D-205 · SIGN-OFF (2026-07-18) · FU-18 is RETIRED — auth model replaced by capability-token multi-tenant trips; supersedes D-168, amends D-172
**Decision:** the FU-18 anon-uid-allowlist plan (the runbook doc, D-168) is rejected outright, not "tighten it" but replace the model. The new direction, confirmed on two forks:
1. **The token is the trip's secret Firestore path id** (a capability-link model, chosen over a separate-shareable-ID plus edit-token split). Creating a trip mints one high-entropy token, and that token becomes the `trips/{token}/...` path segment. Rules collapse to `allow get, write: if true` scoped under a known `tripId`, with one non-negotiable: `list` and collection-group queries at the top-level `trips` collection must stay denied. The model's entire security rests on tokens being unguessable and unenumerable, never on rule logic beyond that. Whoever holds the token or link has full read and write on that trip only, and no one can discover a trip without it. No stored hash and no `get()` cross-doc lookup is needed, because the token never appears inside document data at all.
2. **The existing live Nepal×Japan trip converts too** — one auth model, not two. The default pack becomes just another trip secured by a minted token instead of the anon-uid/Google-uid allowlist.
**Consequences (to be blueprinted at S231, not decided here):** anyone on the internet may now create a trip (mint a token, write the initial trip doc) and share its token to add collaborators. That is the intended, accepted security posture, not a gap. `firestore.rules`' allowlist block is deleted, so no more collecting friends' uids. This supersedes D-168 in full: the full-auth-rejected verdict stands, and this is still not an accounts system, but the anon-allowlist-now/Google-sign-in-before-departure plan it locked in is retired, and S200's Google sign-in slice (D-183) becomes dead code to be stripped or left inert per the blueprint's call. This also amends D-172 item (5): sync is no longer gated to `activeTripId === DEFAULT_TRIP_ID`, and remote sync must work for any trip pack once it holds a valid token. LOCKED item (3) (`activeTripId`/switch-by-reload) and the grandfather-storage-keys rule are otherwise unchanged and still bind. The FU-18 runbook doc and the FU-18 backlog row are retired, replaced by whatever the blueprint plus a new sequenced slice produce.
**Why:** Firestore Security Rules cannot verify an arbitrary client-presented secret on a *read* except by folding the secret into the document path itself, since no custom-claims backend is affordable under the standing free-Spark-only rule (D-088). The capability-link shape is the only honest zero-backend design that gates both reads and writes by "having the token", which is exactly what was asked for: anyone with the token can modify anything on their trip.
**Changes if:** only with explicit owner sign-off (LOCKED, the same bar D-172 set).

### D-206 · (S215, 2026-07-18) · 3D card tilt: shared gyro stream + section-level iOS opt-in; reduced-motion = fully absent
**Decision:** the recommendation-card 3D tilt (`hooks/use-card-tilt.ts`) uses one module-global `deviceorientation` listener feeding all subscribed cards (N cards ≠ N listeners, and one permission grant covers all, proven by unit test), with springs via framer `useSpring` on the existing LazyMotion-strict platform. iOS motion permission (`DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission()`, gesture-required) is satisfied by one unobtrusive opt-in pill rendered inside `RecommendationSection`: client-only, rendered only on permission-gated browsers pre-grant, never on desktop or Android, never under reduced motion; denied or absent is a silent no-op, never re-prompted. This resolves the spec’s "cards only, no other surface" versus "needs a gesture affordance" tension, since the pill is part of this feature's own surface. Reduced motion (D-007/D-056b): the hook attaches no listeners and returns no style, an explicit guard rather than MotionConfig auto-neutralization. Gyro calibration constants (`GYRO_NEUTRAL_BETA`/`GYRO_SENSITIVITY`) are declared real-device tuning knobs in an in-code comment; the gyro path is implemented and permission-tested but not device-verified. Build deltas declared and attributed: `/nepal` and `/japan` First Load 106→107 kB (+~1 kB hook), sw.js precache 145→146 (+1 code-split chunk, verified against a fresh main-tree baseline build).
**Why:** shared listener plus module-global permission is the minimal shape that scales to a card grid, and the in-section opt-in is the least-nagging legal path to the iOS sensor.
**Changes if:** tilt extends beyond the `RecommendationCard` family (a new decision, since the cards-only fence is deliberate), or real-device testing retunes the calibration constants.

### D-207 · (S218, 2026-07-18) · Celebration/haptic events: first-observation-seeded edge detection; skeletons confirmed complete
**Decision:** completion celebrations (countdown-hits-zero on Home, last-packing-item-checked) fire only on a live `false→true` transition observed while mounted. `lib/celebration.ts::crossedIntoComplete(prev: boolean|null, next)` returns `next && prev === false`, so the first observation (prev `null`) seeds the baseline and never fires, and call sites additionally gate the seeding read on hydration/mount. Pre-hydration renders must not seed a false baseline: a mid-trip page load or an already-complete stored list must not celebrate, proven by an E2E regression. Haptics are one shared `lib/haptics.ts::haptic()`: `navigator.vibrate` feature-detected, reduced-motion-gated via the same outside-React `matchMedia` pattern as `lib/fly-chip.ts`. iOS's lack of `vibrate` is accepted as progressive enhancement, with no workaround. The celebration burst is `m.*`/AnimatePresence, `aria-hidden`, with a D-056(b) explicit `useReducedMotion()` guard so nothing renders under reduced motion. Skeleton audit outcome: every page-section lazy island already had `SectionSkeleton` (S67/S73/S116) and photo thumbnails their own pulse tile (S160), so S218's skeleton item closed as a verified no-op. Nested toggle-gated panes (`PlanDayMap`, `TravelEssentialsCard`) deliberately keep their small custom fallbacks.
**Why:** the tri-state baseline is the minimal shape that distinguishes "arrived while watching" from "arrived before I loaded the page". Review caught the every-page-load-celebrates defect on the first pass.
**Changes if:** a future completion event needs to celebrate on load by design (that would want a separate, explicitly-named path, not a relaxation of this one).

### D-208 · (S220, 2026-07-18) · Share-target inbox: GET capability on the static export; key 23 local-only v1 lifecycle
**Decision:** the PWA registers as an OS share target via a `share_target` block hand-added to `scripts/gen-sw.mjs::buildManifest()` (D-170): GET method (the only shape `output:'export'` can serve, since there is no server to POST to), `action: /share/` (the trailing slash matches static-route resolution), params `title/text/url`. The `/share` route is both receiver and triage inbox in one ssr:false island. On hydrated mount it parses `window.location.search` raw (trip-now precedent), persists the item, and strips the query via `history.replaceState` so a reload never re-adds, with a session-scoped title+text+url dedupe as the second guard. Data: gateway **key 23 `shareInbox`** (D-097 append, never-throw), self-contained `core/share/model.ts` (Zod parse-don't-validate), store via the D-148 `createReactiveStore` factory. The v1 lifecycle: items land unassigned (manual bounded Dec 9–Jan 9 day triage), there is no auto-prune (manual delete only), it is local-only with no sync (sync is a possible follow-up, not scope), and it caps at 100 drop-oldest so the key stays small. Reachability is command-palette-only; navbar wiring is deferred (S206 precedent). Guest stance: `/share` sits behind the S113E guest wall like every non-Home route, and a share captured while walled persists and surfaces after sign-in. **Precache note refining D-182:** an ssr:false dynamic-island route adds +3 precache entries (route html, page chunk, island chunk), not +2 — observed 146→149. Rider `e348a78`: the dedupe-key separators must be written as unicode escapes, never literal NUL bytes, since raw NULs make git treat the source as binary, which is grep-invisible and a mirror-scrub hazard.
**Why:** GET share_target with a receiver that strips then dedupes is the only honest static-export shape, and the capped local-only lifecycle keeps a brand-new persisted domain out of the sync/rules surface while that surface is mid-rewrite (S231).
**Changes if:** the inbox ever syncs (a new domain decision on the S174 pattern, post-S231), or a share payload type beyond title/text/url (files, say) is wanted — files require a POST/service-worker handler the static export cannot serve.

### D-209 · (2026-07-18) · S231 blueprint section 10 open questions resolved; any item may be overturned
**Context:** the direction was "finish up everything for v5" (2026-07-18). The S231 blueprint left four open calls in its section 10, and we resolve them now rather than block the token lane. Each is reversible, and sign-off can overturn any of them.
**Decision:**
1. **Create-a-trip scope = a fresh data instance over the fixed Nepal×Japan content template** (dates, legs and recommendations via `getTripConfig()`'s existing unknown-id fallback). It is not a custom trip-authoring system: nothing in D-205 asks for one, and a trip builder would be its own blueprint.
2. **Auth removal = full strip** (the blueprint's section 9 recommendation): delete `lib/firebase-auth.ts` entirely, Google and anonymous both; simplify `getRemote()`/`getPresence()` to initializeApp plus getFirestore; make the presence doc id a locally-generated persisted `crypto.randomUUID()` device id in a new app-scoped gateway slot. No security-theater auth call is left in the hot path, and the new rules never read `request.auth`.
3. **Trip Token becomes a free-text nickname everywhere, including the grandfathered default pack** (the blueprint's section 8 recommendation): `resolveToken` accepts any non-empty trimmed string, the `TRAVELERS` roster is retired, per-traveler accent becomes a deterministic name-hash into the existing palette, and the `createdBy`/`updatedBy` attribution pipeline is unchanged. This is a real, small behavior change for the 3 friends: they type any nickname instead of a roster name. Flagged.
4. **Old-tree cleanup:** the migration runbook explicitly instructs deleting the `trips/nepal-japan-2026/**` docs via the console after verifying the new path. The literal id is public, and leaving stale data at a guessable path is needless residual risk.
**Why:** all four follow the blueprint's own recommendations. The alternatives buy smaller diffs at the cost of permanent branches or dead auth weight.
**Changes if:** any item is overturned (each is independently revertible before S213 ships).

### D-211 · (S222, survey, 2026-07-18) · Geolocation auto destination-switch: no target surface exists; slice PARKED
**Decision:** S222 as scoped is a no-op. The app has no single manual "active destination panel, Nepal vs Japan" with shared state. Destination context is realized as separate static routes (`/nepal/`, `/japan/`; `lib/nav-items.ts`), guide/essentials/photography components take a fixed `country` prop, the map filters by category rather than country, and timeline country is date-derived (D-184/D-188). Auto-switching would require either an app-wide destination-context provider or geolocation-triggered route navigation, both new subsystems needing a contract decided first. S222 is parked out of v5; it was already a flagged first-cut candidate. If ever pursued: an unobtrusive opt-in `permissions.query`-gated pill that links to the matching country route and never auto-navigates, on the S190/D-206 affordance precedent, after a blueprint.
**Why:** the devplan premise predates the shipped route-per-country IA (D-070/D-071). This is an honest stop per the spec, section 3: no code written, no baseline touched.
**Changes if:** the suggest-pill variant is asked for post-v5 (blueprint first).

### D-210 · (S232, 2026-07-18) · Capability-token mechanics landed — dynamic `getTripId()`, itinerary Vault trip-scoped, Firebase Auth fully stripped
**Decision:** implements the load-bearing half of the S231 blueprint. (1) `firestore.rules` matches the blueprint's section 4 (top-level `trips` `list` denied; `trips/{tripId}/{document=**}` `allow get, list, write: if true`, no `request.auth`), deployed (D-044). (2) `lib/firebase-config.ts` replaces the fixed `TRIP_ID` const with `getTripId()`: the default pack uses `NEXT_PUBLIC_TRIP_ID` (a separately-minted secret, while the public `nepal-japan-2026` slug stays only the local id, D-172 grandfathered), and a non-default pack's own id is the token. `isRemoteConfigured()` drops the default-pack clause (D-205 amends D-172 #5, so sync now works for any pack). (3) The itinerary Vault is trip-scoped: `itinerary`/`itineraryCorrupt` moved into `STORAGE_KEYS`/`TripScopedSlot`, and `lib/itinerary-storage.ts` builds its VaultConfig per call via `keyFor()`, closing the local-data-bleed gap the blueprint found (the default pack is byte-identical; non-default packs namespace to `trip:{id}:*`). (4) Full auth strip (D-209 item 2): `lib/firebase-auth.ts` deleted, `getRemote()`/`getPresence()` are initializeApp plus getFirestore, the presence doc id is a persisted `crypto.randomUUID()` device id (gateway **key 24 `deviceId`**, app-scoped), and `GoogleSignInGroup` is removed from Settings. sw.js precache 149→147 (deleted lazy auth chunks). **Known follow-up for S233 (blueprint slice 5):** `use-itinerary`'s cross-tab `storage` listener still keys on the default-pack literal, so non-default packs lack cross-tab (not same-tab) reactivity until the create/join slice wires it.
**Why:** the token as path secret is the whole security model (D-205); an un-scoped Vault would bleed the most important domain across trips; auth is vestigial once rules never read `request.auth`.
**Changes if:** a D-209 item is overturned, or server-side auth is ever greenlit (it is not).

### D-212 · (S233, 2026-07-18) · Capability-token UX landed — free-text nickname sign-in, Settings Trip group, `?trip=` handshake, migration runbook
**Decision:** completes the user-facing half of S231 (blueprint slices 4–6). (1) `lib/token-auth.ts`: `resolveToken` accepts any non-empty trimmed name (D-209 item 3, roster retired from sign-in); per-name accent is a deterministic hash into the existing 6-hex brand palette; the `Traveler`/`signIn`/`signOut`/`getActiveTraveler` plus `setUserName` pipeline is unchanged (zero attribution changes); `token-gate.tsx` drops the unreachable roster-mismatch error and renames token copy to nickname copy. (2) `TRAVELERS` survives as the default expense-split roster only (`expense-dialog`/`settle-up-summary`/`budget-panel` need a fixed member list); dynamic split membership is a possible future slice, not something silently absorbed here. (3) Settings gains a "Trip" group: Create (mint `crypto.randomUUID()` → `setActiveTripId` → reload), Join by key, and the current Trip Key = `getTripId()` shown as a secret with copy plus a `?trip=` share-link. (4) `trip-join-handshake.tsx` (an ssr:false chrome island) compares `?trip=` against `getTripId()`, the remote token the link encodes, which is the correct "already here" test on the grandfathered default pack and a refinement of the blueprint's section 3.2 `getActiveTripId()` wording; Join is a D-172 switch plus reload, and Cancel strips the param via `history.replaceState`. (5) D-210's cross-tab gap is closed for itinerary: `createReactiveStore`'s `storageKeys` may be a function evaluated at event time (`() => [keyFor('itinerary')]`), while expenses/budget/journal still pass literals, so the same gap remains there (flagged, minor: cross-tab only, same-tab reactivity is fine). The runbook `docs/trip-key-migration.md` replaces the deleted FU-18 runbook. The presence-bar accent for arbitrary nicknames falls back to gold (cosmetic, flagged).
**Why:** smallest UI that makes D-205 real, avoiding the TokenGate structural risk per the blueprint's section 3.3.
**Changes if:** a D-209 item is overturned, or expense-split membership goes dynamic (its own slice).

### D-213 · (S217, 2026-07-18) · Docs-checklist domain: single-doc row-merge; `docs` is a first-class SyncDomain
**Decision:** the critical-documents plus day-zero-readiness checklist lives at `/checklist`, not `/docs`, which avoids repo-docs confusion. It uses gateway **key 25 `docsChecklist`** (trip-scoped), 18 fixed id-keyed rows (10 critical docs, 8 day-zero), check/uncheck plus a per-item note (commit-on-blur), and palette-only discovery (S206 precedent). Sync is a single doc `trips/{tripId}/docs/checklist` whose payload is a `DocItem[]` merged with `mergeItems`: budget's single-doc chunking (D-151 quota) crossed with expenses' row-merge algebra (D-149). Whole-doc LWW was rejected because two clients' offline toggles must both survive. There are no tombstones, since there is no remove path. Stamping is gated on `isRemoteConfigured()` (D-038 byte-identity, unit-proven). `docsCompletion(state)` is the pure selector S219 consumes. **Merge riders:** `'docs'` was added to the `SyncDomain` union (a one-line additive change replacing the fence-honoring cast, which is type-honest and follows the expenses/budget precedent), and S217's guest-wall spec locator was updated to S233's nickname copy (cross-lane test drift, test-only). Precache 147→152 (+5): route html plus 4 chunks, because the first synced island route carries its gated remote-sync chunks. That is a legitimate extension of D-208's +3 rule for local-only island routes.
**Why:** a fixed-template row-merge is the smallest shape that converges, and the union member is how every prior domain joined.
**Changes if:** the checklist gains add/remove custom items (needs tombstones plus GC per D-153) or a navbar entry (FU-33 sweep).

### D-215 · (S219, 2026-07-18) · Trip Wrapped is an always-available composed summary, not post-trip-gated like the day story
**Decision:** `core/recap/wrapped.ts::deriveWrapped(inputs, now)` is pure and total (null/undefined domains degrade) with an injected clock. It composes six existing read-only domains (itinerary; expenses via `expensesToSpent` plus max-category per leg; journal; photos; packing via `packingProgress`; docs via `docsCompletion`) into headline stats, mounted as `components/wrapped-story.tsx` below `trip-story-recap.tsx` on `/recap` via its own ssr:false island. Unlike the day-by-day story, which is post-trip-locked (S156), Wrapped renders at any phase with honest status copy (`pre`, `mid` "so far", `post`), because packing/docs readiness and running counts are legitimately useful pre-trip. This is recorded so future slices don't assume all `/recap` sub-surfaces share one gate. Share is `navigator.share`, feature-detected, with a clipboard-plus-toast fallback, text only (D-159: photo blobs are never shared). The celebration burst is reused, post-trip and reduced-motion gated. Zero writes, dependencies or keys; `trip-story-recap.tsx` is byte-untouched; `/recap` First Load is unchanged at 106 kB; sw.js 152→153 (+1 wrapped island chunk, `gen-sw.mjs` untouched). `/recap` has no visual baselines (verified), so no drift is possible.
**Why:** pure composition over six proven selectors is the smallest capstone, and the always-available call maximizes the feature's window of usefulness.
**Changes if:** a shareable image card is wanted (a client-canvas slice), or Wrapped gains per-day drill-ins.

### D-214 · (S234, 2026-07-18) · Multi-trip isolation verified for remote+itinerary; S234-F1 local-key bleed found on every other trip-scoped domain — remediation = S235
**Decision:** S234's net (4 E2E tests run twice, deterministic unit probes, and a 38-island hydration audit, `docs/multi-trip-audit.md`) proved two things: remote Firestore paths are correctly pack-isolated, since every `*-remote.ts` composes `getTripId()` per call, and the itinerary Vault is locally isolated, shown by a byte-identical default-pack dump after a real Create → edit → Join-back round trip. **S234-F1 (structural, not fixed in S234):** only itinerary routes through `keyFor()`. Expenses, budget, journal, favorites, photos, packing, docsChecklist, dayAnchors, shareInbox, weatherCache and syncOutbox persist via gateway accessors using the default literal keys, so a non-default pack (reachable via S233's UI) reads and silently overwrites the default pack's local data for those domains. Root cause: the S231 blueprint's section 6 conflated TripScopedSlot union-eligibility with actually calling `keyFor`. **S234-F2:** no UI affordance returns to the grandfathered default pack in a production build, because the displayed Trip Key is the remote secret, not the local id, so pasting it lands on a third empty pack. **Remediation is S235:** route ~11 accessors and `has()` seed-checks through `keyFor`, convert the 6 reactive-store `storageKeys` literals to the function form (absorbing D-212's cross-tab sub-gap), and add a "Switch to my main trip" affordance. No island-level defects were found. Until S235 lands, creating or joining a second trip and editing non-itinerary domains is unsafe, and v5 does not ship without S235.
**Why:** the net did exactly its job — verification over trust. The bleed was invisible pre-S233 because no second pack could exist.
**Changes if:** S235 lands (the Part B probe inverts to assert the fixed behavior).

### D-218 · (S235, 2026-07-19) · S234-F1/F2 remediated — all trip-scoped domains route through `keyFor`; "Switch to my main trip" affordance
**Decision:** closes D-214 and clears the ship-blocker. Every `TripScopedSlot` accessor now composes its on-disk key via `keyFor(slot)`: 10 gateway store objects and their `has()` seed-checks, `core/sync/outbox`'s syncOutbox load/save/remove, and `core/vault/export-import`'s import-quarantine (a bleed path the S234 audit missed, caught by sweeping every `STORAGE_KEYS` consumer). The 7 reactive-store `storageKeys` configs adopt the D-212 function form, and the 2 hand-rolled cross-tab listeners (favorites, sync-status) match on `keyFor`. `packing`, `dayAnchors` and `shareInbox` were added to `TripScopedSlot`; they were previously omitted, union-ineligible despite being trip data. The default pack is provably byte-identical (a table-driven unit proof over the whole slot union plus two-domain E2E byte-identity round trips). **Photos:** the metadata index is trip-scoped, while the IndexedDB blob store deliberately stays shared, since blobs are keyed by opaque id and reachable only via the per-trip index, so there is no cross-pack path. Orphan GC is a future concern, not an isolation one. **F2:** the Settings Trip group shows a "You're on a shared trip" banner plus **Switch to my main trip** (writes `setActiveTripId(DEFAULT_TRIP_ID)` then reloads) only on non-default packs. No new dependency, key or schema; routes and sw unchanged (15/153).
**Why:** the root fix is the single choke point (`keyFor`): one guard in the shared layer instead of per-caller patches.
**Changes if:** per-trip blob-store scoping or orphan-blob GC is ever wanted (its own slice).

### D-216 · (S203/S204/S205, 2026-07-19) · Phase-of-day grouping never reorders the calendar's manual order; countdown-ring formula; Home bento island
**Decision:** (1) `lib/phase-of-day.ts::groupItemsByPhase` extends D-142 (LOCKED): the calendar day-detail's timed items keep their exact stored/manual order, and only untimed items move to one trailing "Anytime" run. Phase headers (Morning/Afternoon/Evening) mark boundaries in that order rather than a re-sort, so `sort-clash.spec.ts`'s stored-order guarantee holds byte-for-byte. The glance-pill row composes the existing spend (S103) and weather (S216) pills with new items-count and first-start pills. (2) The Home countdown ring's fraction is `1 − min(totalDays,365)/365`, clamped, and forced to 1 on `isPast`: a pure derivation over frozen `computeCountdown()` output, with D-016 math untouched. The ring transition is suppressed under reduced motion (it snaps per tick). Declared re-baseline: exactly `home-hero-{mobile,tablet,desktop}` ×3, for the ring's new pixels; all other 21 baselines show 0 drift. (3) `components/home-bento.tsx` is a below-fold `LazyVisible` ssr:false island (D-116) composing existing hooks only (next-up, budget rollup, weather cache, packing %, docs %, map link, Travel Mode entry; D-018 read-only). Home First Load held at 107 kB, sw.js 153→154 (+1 bento chunk).
**Why:** the stored-order reading is the only one compatible with LOCKED D-142; the rolling-365 window is an honest, testable ring semantic; bento composes instead of inventing state.
**Changes if:** D-142 is ever re-litigated (owner-gated), or bento tiles gain interactions beyond links.

### D-217 · (S211/S212, 2026-07-19) · Reduced-motion + axe completeness nets are permanent gates; framer scroll-linked opacity is a ViewTimeline hazard under reduce
**Decision:** (1) `e2e/motion-reduced-audit.spec.ts` (15 tests) is the permanent route-complete reduced-motion gate. Every route under reduced-motion emulation must poll to an empty `document.getAnimations()` running set (>50ms filter; a percent-duration means a scroll or view timeline, which is an automatic violation). The poll tolerates the established short one-shot opacity-only fades (framer `reducedMotion="user"` semantics, the D-100 idiom), so infinite loops, scroll timelines and stuck springs can never pass. (2) This extends D-179 beyond CSS: framer hardware-accelerates scroll-linked opacity MotionValues into WAAPI ViewTimeline animations, so D-056b's collapse-ranges idiom is not sufficient for scroll-linked opacity. The MotionValue must not be bound at all under reduce (hero glow fixed, 8-line diff, pixels identical); transform parallax stays on the collapse idiom, since it is per-frame rAF with no timeline entry. (3) `e2e/a11y-full-audit.spec.ts` (15 tests) completes axe coverage: all 15 routes, 6 TM designed states and 4 key dialogs, serious/critical-gated, measured 0 at all levels including moderate and minor. The one sanctioned exclusion app-wide remains the opaque MapLibre canvas, and any new exclude requires a recorded decision. Audit docs: `docs/motion-audit.md` and `docs/a11y-audit.md`. Zero structural findings; budgets byte-stable.
**Why:** the timeline-level check is the only route-generic proof that "nothing animates", and the ViewTimeline hazard was invisible to every computed-style spec.
**Changes if:** framer changes its WAAPI acceleration strategy, or reduced-motion semantics for progress timelines standardize (the D-179 revisit clause).

### D-219 · (v5 cutover, 2026-07-19) · S232's rules made trip tokens enumerable — rules_version 2 recursive wildcards match zero segments; fixed with a split match
**Decision:** live verification during the cutover proved `getDocs(collection(db,'trips'))` succeeded under the deployed S232 rules, meaning the whole capability model was open. Under `rules_version = '2'` the recursive wildcard `{document=**}` matches zero or more segments, so `match /trips/{tripId}/{document=**} { allow get, list, write: if true }` also matched `/trips/{tripId}` itself and granted `list` on the trips collection. The explicit `match /trips { allow list: if false }` was decorative: a `false` allow grants nothing but does not veto another matching allow, since Firestore rules are an OR of allows with no deny primitive. **Fix (published live 2026-07-19 2:57 AM, then committed):** a split match — `match /trips/{tripId} { allow get, create, update, delete: if true; match /{sub}/{document=**} { allow get, list, write: if true; } }`. No rule grants `list` at `/trips/{tripId}`, so enumeration is denied while subcollection queries (days, budget, expenses, presence — all the real call sites) keep working. Re-verified live from the deployed site: top-level list is `permission-denied`, and known-trip get plus days(32)/budget(1) lists are green. Standing rule for any future rules edit: adversarially live-verify the enumeration-denial property with a real `getDocs` after every publish. The emulator-less "reads as denied" review missed this.
**Why:** the v2 zero-segment wildcard semantics are a documented but obscure footgun, and only a real adversarial query against the live DB exposed it.
**Changes if:** Firestore ever adds a true deny primitive, or a trips-directory feature is wanted (which would need its own non-secret index collection instead).

### D-221 · (S236, 2026-07-19) · Worker `/chat` auth swaps Firebase-ID-token verification for trip-token possession — matches D-205's zero-backend trust model
**Decision:** `worker/src/verify-token.ts`'s JWT/JWKS check verifies a Firebase ID token, but D-209 item 2 stripped Firebase Auth from the client entirely (`lib/firebase-auth.ts` was deleted in S232), so the client can no longer produce one. The check is dead code against a token type nothing issues anymore. Replace it with the same trust model D-205 already established for Firestore: possession of the trip token is the authorization. The client sends its active trip id (`getActiveTripId()`, `core/storage/gateway.ts`) as a new `X-Trip-Token` header, and the Worker requires it present, non-empty, and under a generous length cap. That cap is an abuse and shape guard only, since there is no server-side registry of valid tokens to check it against, exactly as with Firestore rules (D-088 free-Spark-only forecloses a custom-claims backend). Delete `verify-token.ts`, its JWKS singleton, and the `jose` dependency's only use in `index.ts`, plus the matching test helpers (`worker/test/helpers.ts` JWKS kit). The `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` env var is removed from `wrangler.toml` and `Env`, since nothing checks it. The origin allow-list (403) and the free-tier request cap remain the Worker's only real abuse defenses, unchanged.
**Why:** cryptographic verification of a value with no issuer and no backend registry is theater. It would only prove the client can echo a string, the same as the plain presence check, at the cost of a JWKS fetch dependency and a project-id coupling to a Firebase Auth system that no longer exists. It matches D-205's own rationale verbatim ("having the token" is the whole model) instead of inventing a second, incompatible auth scheme for one route.
**Changes if:** a real per-trip quota or abuse problem emerges (that would need either a Firestore existence-check read, a new D-152 exception, or a paid rate-limiting layer, both out of scope now).
**Decision:** the devplan's S193 (a one-shot adoption of the 9 non-vault `nepal_japan_*` slots) predates the D-205 redirect. D-172 (LOCKED) plus S235/D-218's landed `keyFor` make the legacy literals the default pack's on-disk keys permanently, so no adoption code exists or should. S193 is re-scoped to its surviving acceptance intent, the devplan's section 4 "Migration hard guarantee": a real localStorage dump, captured 2026-07-19 from the owner's live browser via Chrome (8 keys including a 52,717-byte schemaVersion-5 itinerary of 32 days), landed at `e2e/fixtures/live-v5-vault/live-dump.json` (private repo only, since `e2e/` is mirror-sync-excluded), and `e2e/live-vault-acceptance.spec.ts` proves that the gate passes on the dump's identity, real data renders, all 8 keys are byte-identical after boot plus two reloads (with a 1s grace so debounced rewrites can't dodge), `updatedAt` is not restamped on a read-only boot, the quarantine key is never created (D-096), and there are zero console errors. Ran ×3 green, then re-run independently green. **Known cosmetic:** the real capture lacks `nepal_japan_first_run_tour_seen`, so that browser sees the first-run tour once post-cutover. That is expected first-run behavior, not a defect, and the spec walks the real skip path.
**Why:** the grandfather made migration code not just unnecessary but wrong. The byte-identity proof on real bytes was the only part of S193 with surviving value.
**Changes if:** a future schema bump makes a clean boot legitimately rewrite a slot (the spec's stop-and-report clause fires).

### D-223 · (S266, 2026-07-23) · Expense-split roster is trip-derived on custom trips; fixed TRAVELERS on the default pack
**Decision:** the expense-split and settlement roster is the fixed `TRAVELERS` list (Powan/Sushil/Uttam) only on the default Nepal×Japan pack, where it stays pixel-identical. On a custom, wizard-created trip the roster is derived at read time from that trip's own expense history: the distinct, first-seen-order union of the active traveler (self, listed first so a zero-expense trip can create its first split) plus every `paidBy`, `split[]` member and `createdBy`, with case-insensitive dedupe and first-seen casing winning. A guest on a custom trip degrades to an empty roster, with no invented UI. There is a single helper `rosterForActiveTrip(expenses)` in `lib/token-auth.ts`, and accents come via `rosterAccent` (a fixed brand tint for a default TRAVELER, an `accentForName` hash otherwise, no new colours). No Firestore, schema or rules change; settlement math is unchanged, since it already takes a `string[]`.
**Why:** the hardcoded 3-friend roster leaked into every custom trip's split UI. Deriving from expense history needs no new storage and converges with what sync already carries.
**Changes if:** custom-trip budgeting grows a real roster-management UI, or the flagged sibling leak (the expense dialog's hardcoded nepal/japan leg toggle) is paid down and pressures the same seam.

### D-222 · (S269, 2026-07-23) · Trip removal = local forget + Sync-Code tombstone; never deletes remote data
**Decision:** forgetting a trip drops it from this browser's known-trips list (key 26) and writes a tombstone `{id, removedAt}` to gateway key 29 `tripPlannerRemovedTrips` (raw transport; shape and policy live in `core/trips/registry.ts` per D-097). `mergeTripLists` folds both sides' tombstones LWW by `removedAt` and drops a tombstoned id from the additive union, unless the surviving entry's recency — the later of `updatedAt` and `joinedAt` — beats `removedAt`. A re-join or a post-forget rename therefore revives the trip and discards the stale tombstone. The recency signal is deliberately not `updatedAt` alone: `upsertKnownTrip` stamps a fresh `joinedAt` but no `updatedAt` on join, so the strict reading would make a re-join lose to its own tombstone. The profile doc `trips/{code}/profile/tripList` becomes `{version:1, trips, removed?}`, which is additive; pre-S269 clients ignore `removed` and may re-add until updated (accepted). The default pack is never removable or tombstoned in either direction. Forgetting never deletes the trip's Firestore data: anyone with the key can still open it, and pasting the key re-joins, which is exactly what the hub's confirm copy says. `removeKnownTrip` refuses `DEFAULT_TRIP_ID` and switches the pointer to default when forgetting the active trip (D-172 reload by caller). No Firestore rules change.
**Why:** closes the S255 "no delete path" ceiling, the only behavioral debt in the 2026-07-23 paydown run. Without it, any local removal resurrects via the LWW additive union.
**Changes if:** tombstone growth ever matters (there is no safe purge without a device-set, so unbounded growth is accepted), or a future profile-doc version bump lets old clients be retired.

### D-224 · (S274, 2026-07-24) · Travel-Mode trip-DAY derives from the destination leg offset on the real clock; device-local only for the `?today=` override and custom trips
**Decision:** the "which trip-day / today's city" header (`getTodayInTrip` → `dayInTripFor`) derives from the destination wall-clock, not the device calendar day (P4, audit section 5b). It splits by clock source, preserving the D-099 purity boundary and the D-140 override philosophy:
- **Real clock (no override):** derive the day at the current leg's fixed `utcOffsetMin`. `utcDayAtOffset(now, offsetMin)` is the UTC calendar parts of `now.getTime() + offsetMin*60000` (B-01-safe: UTC getters only, never `new Date(string)`). The leg offset is chosen by a two-pass seed: compute a provisional day at the earliest leg's offset (Nepal +345), look it up via `legForDate`, then use that leg's offset. Seeding with the earliest leg means the day never advances to the next leg before the origin leg's own local midnight. On the night of Dec 18 a traveler physically in Kathmandu stays on Day 10 until Kathmandu midnight (18:15 UTC), not Tokyo midnight (15:00 UTC).
- **`?today=` override active:** unchanged, the device-local calendar day of the local-noon override Date. The override declares the destination day by fiat (D-140's rule), and offset-shifting it would slip a day with the test machine's TZ. The frozen S82 `?today=` boundary net exercises only this unchanged branch.
- **Custom trips (single `'main'` leg, `utcOffsetMin: 0`, no geography):** device-local. The discriminator `activeTrip.legs.some(l => l.utcOffsetMin !== 0)` is false, so there is no known geography and we pass `offsetMin = null`.
Signature: `dayInTripFor(now: Date, offsetMin?: number | null)`. `null` or omitted means device-local, byte-identical to the frozen behavior, so the existing pure unit suite and all direct callers are untouched; a number selects the offset branch. `offsetMin` is injected by the `lib/trip-now.ts` adapter, which keeps the impure clock and leg read in the adapter and the mapping pure and `page.clock`-testable.
**Rejected, device-local (the status quo):** correct only when the phone's TZ equals the destination. A home-time phone (airplane mode, or no auto-TZ) shows the wrong day for up to ~10¾ h/day in Kathmandu on the most safety-critical screen, while item times were already place-accurate. **Rejected, a full TZ library:** unnecessary. Both destinations are fixed-offset with no DST (+5:45 / +9:00), already encoded as per-leg `utcOffsetMin`.
**Why:** the offset path is fully TZ-deterministic — it keys off the UTC instant plus a fixed constant and ignores device TZ — so it fixes the home-time-phone bug outright and stays trivially `page.clock`-testable. There is zero risk to the frozen S82 matrix, since that net runs only through the untouched override branch.
**Changes if:** a pack travels westward (a later leg with a smaller offset), where the single earliest-leg seed can under-shoot and the two-pass must become a short fixpoint loop; or a leg spans a same-day TZ change (out of scope per D-137).

### D-225 · (S275, 2026-07-24) · Optional per-item `tzOffsetMin` overrides the day-country offset for Travel-Mode instant math
**Decision:** an itinerary item may carry an optional `tzOffsetMin?: number` (added to `ItineraryItem` in `lib/trip-data.ts` and to `contentItineraryItemSchema` in `core/content/schema.ts`, additive-optional like `sourceId`/`lat`/`lng`). When present it overrides the day's `offsetForCountry` value in the UTC-instant computation only, via `effectiveOffsetMin(item, dayOffsetMin)` (`core/dates/item-time.ts`, barrel-exported), consumed by `lib/travel-hero.ts` (`currentActivity`, `deriveRowPhases`) and `lib/whats-next.ts` (`nextUp`). When absent the day offset is unchanged, so every existing item is byte-identical. It is used to correct the Dec-19 Guangzhou layover items `j1-1`/`j1-2` (China UTC+8 = +480) that live on a `country: 'japan'` (JST +540) day, whose instant math was 60 min off (P5, audit section 5b). The displayed time and the TZ badge are untouched (D-137 LOCKED: the badge is per-day, never per-item), so `time` still renders verbatim (05:55/08:50) with the day's JST badge, and only now/next/progress/past-detection changed for these two items. This is a per-item place offset, distinct from D-224's trip-day decision about which calendar day it is. The lenient Vault read schema is `.passthrough()` so a value survives round-trip, and no UI write-path sets the field (seed content only).
**Why:** the offset resolves once per day into `NextUpContext.placeOffsetMin` and is applied uniformly, so no content-only edit could make both the displayed wall-clock and the computed instant correct for an item physically in a different zone than its day's country. A minimal optional override was the only correct fix (the S275 stop-gate, path 2).
**Changes if:** a future trip has many mixed-zone items (which would justify a real per-item timezone model rather than a single-offset override), or a UI write-path ever needs to set it.

**AMENDMENT (S390, 2026-08-06) — the badge is suppressed, never re-derived, when the item carries a place override.** The original text above (*"The DISPLAYED time and the TZ badge are untouched"*) described S275's scope, not a ruling, and is left byte-intact per the D-204 precedent. It shipped a measurable defect: `describeItemTime` (`lib/item-time-display.ts`) labelled every timed item with the day-country badge, so the 9 seeded `tzOffsetMin` items rendered a zone they are not in. `j22-5` (Detroit layover, `tzOffsetMin: -300`) on `2027-01-09` rendered "3:35 PM JST", 14 hours wrong, reachable through 8 consumers. `describeItemTime` now returns `badge: null` when `effectiveOffsetMin(item, dayOffset) !== dayOffset`. It asks the existing resolver rather than testing the field directly, so the badge cannot drift out of agreement with the instant math. D-137 (LOCKED) is untouched and unviolated: it governs how a badge is *derived* from the day's country, and this path derives nothing — it reuses the `badge: null` state that already existed for legacy free-text (*"free text carries no asserted zone"*). Sort, `whats-next` and `travel-hero` are unchanged; they already read `effectiveOffsetMin`, which is why every test was green. **Ceiling, stated rather than hidden:** an unbadged time among badged siblings still reads as the day's zone by context. This is harm reduction, not the fix, and it trades a wrong-but-checkable label for a wrong-but-uncheckable one. The row stays open. **Authority:** D-137's own `Changes if` (*"a trip ever has a same-day timezone change… forces a per-item place override — a new decision"*) had already fired, and D-225 is that successor; D-225 carries no LOCKED tag (verified against D-137/D-138/D-139/D-140, which do). **Changes if:** a ruling lands on showing the real per-item zone (EST/IST/CST), the open-questions doc Q1, which supersedes this amendment rather than extending it.

### D-282 · (S389, 2026-08-06) · An inverse must address the identity the store returns, and a call-recorder fake may not be its sole guard

**Decision:** `ItineraryStore.moveItem` returns the id the item actually landed on (`string | undefined`). Anything constructing an inverse — undo, redo, a reorder that re-lists ids — must address that returned id, never the id it passed in. Under sync (D-248) the target is a `freshCopyOf` mint, so the original id no longer resolves.
