The test half is part of the rule, not a suggestion: an operation whose store mints, tombstones or re-keys may not be guarded solely by a call-recorder fake, because such a fake asserts routing and can never observe behaviour. Its guard must assert resulting state, on the branch where the defect exists.

**Why:** `applyOp`'s `moveItem` inverse dispatched by the original id, so under sync the undo toast appeared and did nothing — live in production since before v5.7.0. An in-code comment described the defect correctly and sat three lines above the broken line, and it still shipped, because `concierge-ops.test.ts`'s guarding test asserted `store.calls[1]` against a `fakeStore()` that holds no state: it would pass identically on broken and fixed code. The dormant-mode test passed on the old code too, because the branch the old test modelled was the branch that worked.

**Changes if:** the sync model stops re-keying on move (then the returned id is always the input, and the rule is inert but harmless).

### D-283 · (S390, 2026-08-06) · A rename records the previous name; identity matching is by current-or-prior name, and stored stamps are never rewritten

**Decision:** renaming the local traveller appends the old name to a prior-names list (`STORAGE_KEYS.priorNames`), and author matching (`itemMatchesAuthor` / `filterItemsByAuthor` / `distinctAuthors`) resolves *"my edits"* against current or prior names, collapsing priors into the current name for display. Stored `createdBy`/`updatedBy`/`doneBy` stamps are not rewritten. `itemMatchesAuthor` additionally honours `doneBy`, so a traveller who ticked items but authored none is no longer filtered to empty (this closes the D-230 gap).

**Why:** stamps are per-device, sync-replicated data. Rewriting them is a mass mutation across every synced device and is the owner's call, not ours. **Explicit non-decision:** no heuristic may infer that a stored name "is" the current user. `DEFAULT_TRAVELER_NAME` was rejected as exactly that heuristic wearing a constant's clothes, since every signup-door user was `Traveler` first, so seeding from it would fold a fellow traveller's items into the owner's identity on a shared trip.

**Ceiling, stated:** this prevents future splits. It cannot repair a rename that already happened, because no record of the old name exists. The owner's existing `Traveler` → `Powan` split persists until the open-questions doc Q3 is answered (REWRITE or LEAVE).

**Changes if:** the ruling is REWRITE, which adds a one-time migration and supersedes the non-rewrite half.

### D-226 · (S271, 2026-07-24) · SW install is now atomic (rejects on any precache miss) + `activate` GCs by cache allowlist
**Decision:** the hand-rolled SW (`scripts/gen-sw.mjs::buildServiceWorker()`) hardens two behaviors under D-073, which stays LOCKED with no skipWaiting change. (1) **Atomic install:** the `install` handler now throws on any non-OK same-origin precache fetch, rejecting the whole `waitUntil`. This deliberately reverses the old "individual puts so one stray 404 can't brick install" best-effort comment. Because the old precache cache is deleted only in `activate`, a rejected install never activates and the last-good cache stays fully intact, so a flaky-Wi-Fi or torn deploy can no longer half-populate the precache and leave `/travel` permanently serving the Home shell offline (P3, audit item 5b). (2) **Nav backfill:** a same-origin navigate that misses the cache but fetches OK (`type:'basic'`) is `cache.put` into PRECACHE, so a route missed at install is repaired on the next online visit. (3) **Allowlist GC:** `activate` now deletes any cache key not in `{PRECACHE, IMAGES_CACHE, FRANKFURTER_CACHE}` (was prefix-only `trip-precache-`), so a bumped runtime-cache version can't linger. Precache count unchanged (164); only handler logic changed. R5 five-point invariant audited clean.
**Why:** offline reliability for the trip. The old best-effort install could silently ship a broken offline shell; atomic activation makes a torn update fail safe onto the previous good build.
**Testing note (hand-rolled SW):** the torn-update invariant is proven by decomposition in `e2e/pwa-torn-update.spec.ts`: the surviving-cache half runs on the real registered worker, and the atomic-reject and nav-backfill halves drive the shipped `out/sw.js` source in a controlled harness. A live A→B build swap remains non-reproducible in the single-immutable-`out/` harness, consistent with the pre-existing `pwa.spec.ts:275` skip.
**Changes if:** a runtime cache is renamed or added. It has to be added to the `activate` allowlist or it will be GC'd on the next activation.

### D-227 · (S273, 2026-07-24, proposed) · Full-trip backup lifeboat widens D-098 to all local user domains + a photo bundle; per-domain best-effort, never-destroy
**Supersedes only D-098's "v1 scope = itinerary-only" line.** D-098's LOCKED never-destroy discipline and its same-schema trust boundary are kept verbatim and extended per-domain. This does not change **D-159** (photos zero-egress); it exercises D-159's own pre-authorized "explicit user-initiated export of a separate photo bundle, never the default export, never sync" clause. All three D-159 conditions are honored: a distinctly-labeled "back up everything incl. photos" action producing a distinct file (`nepal-japan-trip-backup.json.gz`, not the default itinerary export, since the label is what prevents silent bytes-creep); explicit and user-initiated; and zero network egress (no Firestore, no sync, no Worker, so the file stays in the user's hands). Flagged as privacy-adjacent: proceed unless vetoed.
**Scope:** the active trip's user-authored local domains, namely `itinerary, journal, photos (meta + blob bytes), expenses, budget, docsChecklist, packing, favorites, dayAnchors, shareInbox`. Excluded: `weatherCache` (regenerable), `syncOutbox` (transient sync machinery), `itineraryCorrupt` (quarantine). Synced domains are included because sync is opt-in (it needs a Sync Code) and off by default for a solo traveler.
**Format:** one gzip file via the existing S228 pipeline, no new dep. Envelope `{ format:'nepal-japan-trip-backup', version:1, exportedAt, tripId, tripName?, domains:{…}, photos:{ meta, blobs:{[id]:base64 data URL} } }` → `compressToBlob` (gzip, plain-JSON fallback). `itinerary` nests its own versioned and migratable Vault envelope; other domains store raw on-disk JSON, validated on import by each domain's existing total sanitizer. Photo JPEGs are stored inline as a base64 data URL per id (self-describing MIME, decoded via chunked `atob`), a superset of bare base64. Known ceiling: monolithic `JSON.stringify` peaks at roughly 4–5× the photo payload (a ~40 MB payload means ~160–200 MB transient), which is fine for a one-off. The upgrade path is streaming blobs or a per-count cap, only if real usage OOMs. Don't pre-build it.
**Import fail-safe (extends D-098):** Phase A does no writes. Decompress, parse, recognize `format`; anything unrecognized or non-JSON is quarantined via `itineraryCorrupt`, returns `{ok:false}`, and writes zero domains. A legacy itinerary-only export routes to the existing itinerary path for back-compat. Each present domain is parsed through its own sanitizer into memory, and malformed domains are dropped rather than aborting the import. Phase B commits: each parsed domain overwrites the active trip via its existing accessor; `itinerary` reuses the dual `restorePlans` (sync) / `importItinerary` (dormant) path; photo blobs go through `putWithId` (id-preserving, so meta↔blob links survive and re-import is idempotent), then the meta index; then reload to re-hydrate (D-172). Never-destroy holds: a fully-corrupt file writes nothing, and a partially-corrupt one writes only the valid domains. A photo blob that fails to write (quota) leaves the D-160 placeholder tile plus "N photos not restored", which is non-destructive. **Synced-domain ceiling:** `itinerary` propagates via `restorePlans`, while `expenses`/`budget`/`docs` restore as a local overwrite governed by the next server snapshot. That is acceptable and already hedged by sync; the hard guarantee is the purely-local domains (journal, photos, favorites, dayAnchors, packing, shareInbox), which are the actual P2 loss surface.
**Riders (C25):** the integrity check is skipped (gzip CRC32 plus the per-domain sanitizers already cover it). Auto-snapshot is deferred: a real hedge has to leave the sandbox, which means a user-gesture download, and a same-origin auto-copy dies in the same eviction. That needs the FSA API rather than anything near-free, so it is a separate later slice only if we want it. S273 is a manual one-tap.
**Constraints kept:** client-side only (D-004), no Firestore or rules change, no new dependency, no photo network egress (D-159 intact).
**Changes if:** measured photo sets OOM real devices (stream or cap, don't pre-build); a synced non-itinerary domain needs a sync-propagating restore (extend the D-176 tombstone-replace pattern); or D-159's photo-egress scope is reopened (this decision stays within the separate-bundle clause).

### D-228 · (S278, 2026-07-24, proposed) · HLC clock-skew clamp bounds the device running clock only (`hlcReceive`), never the stored merge stamp; convergence preserved, bad-peer-wins-own-edits accepted as the trusted-clock ceiling
**Decision:** add `MAX_SKEW_MS = 24h` and clamp the remote contribution inside `hlcReceive` alone: `pt = max(physicalNow, localPt, min(remote.pt, physicalNow + MAX_SKEW_MS))`. The stored merge-stamp path (`compareHlc`/`mergeItems`/`mergeBudget`/`resolvePair`, blueprint sections 2.4 and 3.5) and the local mint (`hlcSendOrLocal`) are unchanged. Wire format is unchanged (`{pt,ct,actor}`) and there is no Firestore or rules change. The `ct` block is left as-is: a capped remote makes `pt !== remote.pt`, which correctly routes `ct` to the local/reset branch (we did not adopt the peer's clock), and an uncapped remote is byte-identical to today.
**Why convergence-safe:** convergence is decided by `compareHlc` over the raw stored `hlc`, which has no `physicalNow` term, so every device computes the identical winner regardless of wall clock. The clamp lives only in `hlcReceive`, whose output is the device running clock that mints future new events; it is never a fixed cross-device comparison target. Clamping a value that is never a shared comparison target cannot change any stored winner. No update is lost: a peer within MAX_SKEW is absorbed unchanged, only a peer more than 24h out is defanged, and concurrent edits to a far-future item still win via the `ct` ratchet. Monotonicity holds, since `pt` still includes `localPt` and `hlcSendOrLocal` is unchanged.
**Wired-reality note:** `hlcReceive` is currently unwired. Production merge runs only `compareHlc` on stored stamps and no device-global clock exists. So P8's "device poisoned forever, every future stamp" overstates it: the live effect of a far-future peer is bounded per-item `pt` stickiness, which monotonicity requires, and good-device edits to those items still win via `ct`. This clamp is defense in depth. It closes the latent trap for a future wiring of the running-clock primitive and corrects `hlcReceive`'s test contract from "absorb unconditionally" to "absorb up to MAX_SKEW."
**Accepted ceilings (these need a trusted clock and are not fixed here):** (1) a device self-stamping in the far future wins its own item merges on every device, convergently and without losing good-device updates; removing this needs a server-sourced `pt` (Firestore `serverTimestamp`). (2) `hlcSendOrLocal` is not self-clamped, because a long legitimate offline gap is indistinguishable from a fast clock. (3) A minor one: a far-future-`pt` tombstone never reaches the 30-day GC horizon (D-153), which is conservative-safe and never loses data.
**Changes if:** a trusted or server `pt` source is wired (that would skew-bound the stored stamp and retire ceiling 1), or a real device is ever observed legitimately more than 24h ahead (re-tune MAX_SKEW).

### D-229 · (S284/S285, 2026-07-24) · Place-link import ships client-side in manual mode; My Places is a local-only domain reusing the itinerary `recommendation` sourceType — **the local-only half is retired by the issue #17 addendum at the end of this entry** (pointer added 2026-08-14; the ruling text below is untouched and still reads as it was made, but a heading that says "local-only" with no forward reference gives a scanner the wrong answer)
**Decision:** the "import a Google Maps place" feature (plan the planning notes) ships its entire client side (slices S-b=S284, S-c=S285) without the gated Worker `/resolve` endpoint. `lib/place-resolve.ts` never throws and degrades to `null` on an unconfigured origin, a non-200, a timeout or bad JSON, so every entry point funnels into one always-manual `ImportPlaceSheet` (D-021 portal contract, D-028 always-confirm parity) and resolution merely pre-fills it. `MyPlace` is a new framework-free total domain (`core/places/model.ts`) persisted at **gateway key 31** (`nepal_japan_my_places`, TripScopedSlot, local-only). The plan text said "key 30", but S272 took 30 for `installHintDismissed`, so the next free slot is 31. Cross-device sync of places is the deferred S-d, only if we want it.
**Why sourceType stays `recommendation`:** a plan item created from an imported place uses `sourceType: 'recommendation'` plus `sourceId: 'myplace-<id>'`. The vault enum (`core/vault/schema.ts`: `['recommendation','photo','map','featured']`) is a synced shape, so adding a `'myplace'` value would fail Zod on not-yet-updated peers. `findPlacements` matches on `sourceId` only, so the Added badge and dedupe still work and nothing is lost. The vault enum is untouched.
**Impl notes (S285):** `ImportPlaceSheet` gained one minimal optional `onImported?(): void` callback, fired after save and before `onClose`, so the `/share` inbox row action can remove its row only on a real save. `onClose` fires on both save and cancel and can't distinguish them. `command-palette.tsx` now keys its `CommandItem` list by `section.label` (it was `route+hash`, no longer unique now that two entries point at `/share/`). There is no image field on cards, because og:image hotlinking is fragile and ToS-risky, so card art is a vibe-gradient. No new dependency, no Firestore or rules change.
**Ceilings / deferred:** resolver pre-fill is dormant on deploy builds (`NEXT_PUBLIC_CONCIERGE_URL` unset) until Worker v1.2.0 `/resolve` is deployed (S-a, a greenlit backend slice plus `wrangler deploy`). The e2e asserts pre-fill only when wired, so it stays green on either build. Places sync (S-d) is deferred.

**Addendum 2026-08-14 (issue #17): the places-sync deferral above is CLOSED for My Places, and stays open for Favorites.** Key 31 now mirrors to a single doc `trips/{tripId}/places/list` in the `{version, items:[…]}` container, merged per-row with `mergePlaces` (`core/places/merge.ts`) over the shared `mergeItems` algebra — the docs-checklist shape (D-217: one singleton doc, D-151 quota; row-merge, D-149 algebra) with the itinerary's tombstone discipline added. The wiring gap was literally one missing `sync` key on `createReactiveStore` in `hooks/use-my-places.ts`; everything below is why it could not be *only* that.

**Scope: My Places only. Favorites (key 14, the "Saved" chip on `/map` and `/guides`) stays per-device, deliberately.** A favorite is a bare `string[]` of ids — there are no rows to merge on, so none of the three available routes is acceptable at this size: add-only set union means un-saving never propagates (the chip lies on the other phone); whole-doc LWW means one device's saves are silently lost; and reshaping to id-keyed rows is a back-compat migration of a live, deployed key that D-157 pins as a shared flat store. My Places was tractable precisely because a `MyPlace` was already an id-keyed row with a timestamp. No code or comment in this change implies the Saved chip is covered; the key-31 gateway comment says so explicitly.

**The four things a bare `mergeItems` drop-in gets wrong here, and what each cost:**
1. **Deletes resurrect.** `removePlace` physically filters the row out, and under a row merge a physical absence is indistinguishable from "this device has not seen it yet" — so the peer's copy re-enters on the next snapshot. The docs precedent does not cover this (a fixed template has no remove path). Fixed with the itinerary's tombstone discipline: under sync a delete flips `deleted:true` and advances `rev`/`hlc` via `nextSyncStamp`, and `useMyPlaces` filters tombstones out of the exposed list. `gcTombstoneRows` drops them past the shared 30-day horizon.
2. **Every row would carry the same HLC.** `mergeItems` seeds a missing `hlc` from `updatedAt`; a `MyPlace` has `addedAt`. Every un-stamped row therefore resolved to `{pt:0,ct:0,actor:''}` and any same-id collision fell through to the JSON content fingerprint — deterministic but meaningless, and a *newer* pre-#17 import lost to an *older* stamped peer row. Fixed by seeding `updatedAt` from `addedAt` at the merge boundary only. The seed is transient on purpose: it is re-derived deterministically on every merge, so persisting it would add bytes and a second source of truth. `addedAt` keeps its display-ordering meaning and no field was added to the stored row for this.
3. **The cap and the sort order fought.** `mergeItems` returns live rows HLC-**ascending**; `MyPlace[]` is a newest-first invariant and `sanitizePlaces` capped with `.slice(0, PLACES_CAP)` — i.e. it kept the *first* 200, so feeding merged output straight to `saveMyPlaces` would have discarded the **newest** places. Measured on the naive version: of 150 recent imports merged against 150 old ones, only 50 recent survived. Fixed by re-sorting live rows newest-first (`addedAt` desc, `id` asc — a total order over stored fields, so still convergent) before the cap.
4. **Tombstones would have eaten the 200-place budget.** Fixed with `capPlaces`: live rows and tombstones are capped independently, so a delete can never evict a real place and the slot is still bounded (2×cap). Under the cap on both counts the array is returned verbatim, which is what keeps the local-only path byte-identical.

**Doc shape is load-bearing for the rules.** `firestore.rules`'s catch-all `match /{sub}/{document=**}` already grants a member create/update under `boundedWrite()`, and `boundedWrite()` bounds `len('items') <= 5000`. Reusing the `items` field name means **no firestore.rules change ships with this** and the container arrives pre-bounded. A differently-named container field would be unbounded by those rules — do not rename it without adding it to `boundedWrite()`.

**Spark quota (D-088 LOCKED, free tier only; cf. D-151).** One singleton doc, one `onSnapshot`, and one transactional read→merge→set per changed commit — the cheapest shape available, identical to the checklist's. Places is the lowest-traffic synced domain in the app: importing a Google link is a deliberate, occasional act, and `PLACES_CAP` bounds the doc at 200 live rows plus at most 200 tombstones (~2 kB/row worst case, so ≲0.8 MiB, inside the 1 MiB doc limit and far inside `boundedWrite`'s 5000). A heavy day of 30 imports across 3 devices is ~30 writes and ~90 reads against Spark's 20k/50k — under 0.2%, well inside D-151's ≥19× margin, which the expenses/budget math already dominates. Nothing here needs Blaze, now or at 10× this volume.

**Scope is per-TRIP, and the default sample pack stays local-only.** A `MyPlace.legId` names a leg of the active trip, so the gate is `isTripRemoteConfigured()`, not `isRemoteConfigured()`. The default pack's remote id is retired (`getTripId()` returns `''`), so on the sample trip nothing is stamped, a delete stays physical, the outbox is never written and no stream opens — silently, with no invalid empty-path compose. That path is unit-pinned, not assumed. Sign-out teardown is unchanged: key 31 was already in `TRIP_SCOPED_SLOTS`.

**Changes if:** (a) a place ever gains an EDIT path (rename/re-note) — today the only mutations are add and delete, so `addedAt` doubles as the legacy order key; a real edit needs its own `updatedAt` on the stored row rather than the transient seed. (b) A trip routinely runs against `PLACES_CAP` — then the newest-first eviction becomes user-visible data loss and the cap needs raising or the doc needs chunking by leg (D-151's escape hatch). (c) Someone finds a back-compat-safe row shape for Favorites — then the paragraph above is what has to be answered first, not the wiring. (d) A tombstone burst ever hits its own 200 cap, which drops the oldest tombstones and can in principle resurrect a row a peer has been offline longer than; the 30-day GC horizon already bounds this and the real fix is server-side compaction, which Spark does not offer.

**One ceiling that is NOT a places property and is worth stating here anyway, because this issue's scenario is exactly where it bites: the HLC's `actor` is a traveller DISPLAY NAME, not a device id** (`hooks/use-my-places.ts`, identical to `hooks/use-docs.ts:70` and every other synced domain). The merge order is `pt → ct → actor`, so two phones signed in as the SAME person — "saved places should follow you between phones", i.e. the literal case this entry closes — tie on all three and fall through to `contentFingerprint`. That is still commutative, idempotent and convergent, so it is not a data-loss path and it is not a reason to hold the slice; but the final tie-break carries no signal in the one scenario the feature exists for, and a reader should not mistake `actor` for a device discriminator. Fixing it means giving every domain a per-device id, which is a sync-algebra change (D-149), not a places change.

### D-230 · (S316, 2026-07-25, proposed) · Completion attribution = two additive immutable fields `doneBy?`/`doneAt?`, stamped on the done→true transition and cleared on true→false; not `updatedBy` (which every edit overwrites); rides the D-106 per-item HLC merge
**Decision:** `ItineraryItem` (in `lib/trip-data.ts`, declared in `itineraryItemSchema` in `core/vault/schema.ts`) gains **two optional additive fields**, following the same house pattern as `done`/`lat`/`lng`/`endDate` (D-095 lenient-read) and the same identity model as D-041:
```ts
// added to ItineraryItem — additive, nothing existing touched (mirrors the done/updatedBy entries):
doneBy?: string;   // display name of the traveler who checked this item done — SAME identity kind + source as updatedBy (D-041/D-043): the getUserName() display nickname, a plain string
doneAt?: string;   // ISO-8601 timestamp of that completion — SAME format + source as updatedAt (D-041): new Date().toISOString()
```
Both are declared `z.string().optional()` in `core/vault/schema.ts` beside `done`. There is no migration and no version bump: `CURRENT_ITINERARY_VERSION` stays 5. An item without them is trivially "no completion attribution", exactly as `done` absent means not done. `.passthrough()` already tolerated them on read; declaring them makes the surface explicit and typed.

**Why not `updatedBy` (the crux):** `updatedBy`/`updatedAt` (D-041) are re-stamped by `stampUpdated` on **every** `updateItem` content edit. Editing a completed item's title, time or notes would reassign "who completed it" to the last editor and reset the time, so `updatedBy` cannot durably carry completion attribution. `doneBy`/`doneAt` are written only on the done transition and are otherwise immutable across edits, so they durably answer "who checked this off" regardless of later edits. Per-item granularity is deliberate (the footer is per-card); a full audit trail stays out of scope, and D-041's deferred clause covers completion too.

**Stamp / clear semantics (transition-gated):**
- `done` falsy → true: stamp `doneBy = getUserName()`, `doneAt = now ISO`.
- `done` true → falsy: clear both (delete the keys).
- any edit that does not change `done`: `doneBy`/`doneAt` are left untouched. This is the whole point versus `updatedBy`.
- **Name-gate mirrors `stampUpdated` (D-041/D-018/D-038):** with no display name set, stamping is a no-op and both stay absent; clearing always runs, since deleting an absent key is a no-op. So the dormant portfolio build (no name) is byte-identical: a done toggle changes only `done`, exactly as today. No `isRemoteConfigured()` gate is needed, because the name-gate already delivers D-038 byte-identity.

**Impl seam (unambiguous for whoever builds it):** the whole-row toggle routes through `updateItem(date, id, { done })` (S98). The hook's `updateItem` stamper closure in `hooks/use-itinerary.ts` already has `patch` in scope. Add a small `stampDone(item, patch, getUserName)` helper in `lib/attribution.ts` beside `stampCreated`/`stampUpdated`, called in the stamper after `stampUpdated` and before `stampSyncUpdated`, so `done`, `doneBy`/`doneAt`, `updatedBy` and `rev`/`hlc` all land on one merged item in one commit. Gate on the patch: `'done' in patch && patch.done === true` stamps, `=== false` clears, anything else leaves them untouched. The toggle is the only writer of `done` and always sends `{ done: !current }`, so `patch.done` is the real transition and patch-gating equals transition-gating today. Known ceiling: if a future writer ever sets `done` idempotently (non-flipping), switch the gate from `patch.done` to a `prev.done → next.done` comparison, which needs prev, read inside `core/itinerary.updateItem`.

**LWW (D-106), safe with no new reconciliation:** `doneBy`/`doneAt` are plain per-item content and ride the same per-`id` whole-item, higher-HLC-wins merge (`core/sync/merge-day.ts`) as `done`. Because they are written in the same `updateItem` commit that flips `done` (which bumps `rev` and advances `hlc` via `stampSyncUpdated` under sync), the `(done, doneBy, doneAt)` triple is one atomic per-item write: the higher-HLC writer wins the whole triple together, so `done` and its attribution can never desync across peers. Concurrent completions mean the higher-HLC completer wins `done`, `doneBy` and `doneAt` consistently, and an uncheck is a normal higher-HLC edit that wins and removes both fields (whole-item resolution rather than field-level, so there is no stale-`doneBy` resurrection). No Firestore, rules or wire change. `freshCopyOf` carries `doneBy`/`doneAt` alongside `done` unchanged, so a duplicate or move of a done item keeps its completer, consistent with `freshCopyOf` already carrying `done`.

**Footer resolution (no lookup):** `doneBy` stores the display-name string directly, like `updatedBy` (D-043 surfacing), so the card footer renders `✓ Completed · {item.doneBy}` verbatim with no uid→name map. `doneAt` renders via the existing relative-time/`formatDate` helper (D-043), giving "✓ Completed · Powan · 2h ago". The footer shows only when `done === true`; with no name set, `doneBy` is absent and the footer shows "✓ Completed" nameless (dormant/local-only parity, D-043 dormant-when-sync-off).

**Forward-compat / mixed-fleet (D-107 ceiling, additive per D-224/D-227):** old clients that don't know the fields preserve them on read via `.passthrough()` and never set them. Two irreducible mixed-fleet imperfections remain, both bounded and non-destructive, and both resolved by the D-107 coordinated reload: (1) an old client unchecking `done` sets `done:false` but leaves `doneBy`/`doneAt` stale, which is harmless because the footer only renders when `done === true`; (2) an old client re-checking an item shows the previous completer, since it can't stamp, until it reloads the new build. A new-client-only fleet is clean. Don't engineer old-client compat (D-107).
**Why:** completion attribution needs a durable "who checked it off" that survives later edits, and that is impossible on `updatedBy`. Two additive immutable transition-stamped fields deliver it with zero migration, D-038 byte-identity in the dormant build, D-106 convergence for free (they ride the existing per-item HLC merge atomically with `done`), and a zero-lookup footer.
**Changes if:** a full audit or history trail is wanted (D-041's deferred clause); a UI needs to edit or backdate completion attribution; or `done` gains a non-toggle idempotent writer (switch the patch-gate to a prev→next transition gate).

### D-231 · (S320, 2026-07-25, proposed) · Amends LOCKED D-071: mobile IA is 5 tabs (Today · Plan · Map · Guides · More); Nepal/Japan consolidate behind a `/guides/` landing, the 9 hamburger-stranded destinations + sign-out re-home to a `/more/` route
**Amends D-071 (LOCKED), authorized by the approved plan** (the research notes, item **A2**: "Tab bar → 5: Today · Plan · Map · Guides · More"). D-071's own **"Changes if"** clause names a "tab-bar redesign" trigger, and this fires it. Only D-071's tab count/set clause changes. Everything else in D-071 stays LOCKED and intact: the **z-ladder** (scrim 30 < presence/panels 40 < navbar/tab-bar/dialogs 50 < gate 70), **safe-area** `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` plus `viewportFit:'cover'`, the **≥44px** target floor, the `hsl(var(--accent-scroll))` active tint with `aria-current="page"` (D-072), the **quick-add FAB** placement and offset, and the **desktop top navbar** as a surface. D-070's five-page route tree is intact: the `/nepal/` and `/japan/` pages are not deleted, they only stop being tabs. This follows S319 (`a6fce3f`), which deleted the mobile hamburger and stranded 9 destinations (Journal, Safety, Recap, Packing, Trips, Settings, Documents/`/checklist/`, Shared Links/`/share/`, Flights) plus the sign-out row. This entry re-homes exactly those.

**(1) The 5 mobile tabs + routes.** `Today → /` · `Plan → /plan/` · `Map → /map/` · **`Guides → /guides/`** · **`More → /more/`**. Both new pages are `output:'export'` static routes and have to be added to `scripts/gen-sw.mjs` `ROUTE_HTML` in the impl slice (D-170), with `e2e/pwa.spec.ts`'s precache-count assertion updated. `isRouteActive` drives `aria-current` per tab; `/nepal/`, `/japan/` and the companion routes are drill-downs that own no tab.

**(2) "Guides" = a real `/guides/` landing page** (a chooser), not a sheet and not a direct country route. It fronts Nepal and Japan as cards plus the photography, nightlife and essentials entry points. **Why:** tab = screen keeps the model uniform, the route is deep-linkable and precacheable, and one slot replaces two former tabs. Direct-to-country was rejected because which country is arbitrary and it breaks on custom trips; a sheet was rejected for the reasons in (3).

**(3) "More" = a dedicated `/more/` route** (an inset-grouped list page), not a bottom sheet. A route wins over a sheet on **back-button** behavior (browser and Android back return to the prior page for free), **deep-linkability and PWA precache**, the **D-021 modal contract** (a route needs no focus-trap, Esc, focus-return or scroll-lock), and the **z-ladder** (a route touches nothing). D-164 is honored automatically, since the tab bar already returns `null` under `/travel`. **Inset groups (keyed by href, absent items skipped, empty groups hidden), in order:** **Plan & prep** — Flights · Packing · Documents · Safety · Shared Links; **Memories** — Journal · Recap; **Account** — Trips · Settings · **Sign out**. Sign-out is a button calling `signOut()` (token-auth, fires `identity:changed`, mirroring `navbar.handleSignOut`), and it is the mobile home for sign-out (desktop keeps it in the `TravelerChip`). There is no Search/⌘K row on mobile (A1: palette desktop-only).

**(4) Custom-trip tab set = Today · Plan · Map · Journal · More.** `Guides` is `defaultTripOnly` and is dropped on a custom trip; the vacated seat is refilled by the one promoted `customPrimary` companion, Journal. Packing and Trips lose `customPrimary` and fall to the More page. Custom-trip More renders `navItems(custom) − primary` = Safety · Recap · Packing · Documents · Shared Links · Trips · Settings plus sign-out (no Nepal, Japan or Flights, which are `defaultTripOnly`).

**(5) `nav-items.ts` delta: additive, one source, both navs, no drift (FU-4/D-081).** The three helper formulas are unchanged; only the flag data changes what they yield.
- `Home` → relabel **`Today`** (`href:'/'`, primary).
- **Remove** `Nepal` and `Japan` from `NAV_ITEMS` (the `/guides/` page owns those links; routes and pages remain).
- **Add** `{ label:'Guides', href:'/guides/', icon: BookMarked, defaultTripOnly:true }` (primary by default).
- `Flights` → add `primary:false` (keep `defaultTripOnly:true`).
- **Add** `{ label:'Documents', href:'/checklist/', icon: FileCheck2, primary:false }` and `{ label:'Shared Links', href:'/share/', icon: Inbox, primary:false }` (both were command-palette-only).
- Keep `customPrimary:true` on `Journal`; **remove** `customPrimary` from `Packing` and `Trips`.
- Result: `PRIMARY_NAV_ITEMS` (`primary !== false`) → **Today · Plan · Map · Guides** (4). The custom branch → base (Today · Plan · Map) plus promoted (Journal) = **4**. Consumed by both the desktop top row and the mobile tab bar.
- **Mobile tab bar** appends one synthetic `{ label:'More', href:'/more/', icon: Menu }` after the 4 primaries, giving 5 tabs. It is not a `NAV_ITEMS` entry, being a mobile-only affordance; the desktop equivalent is the existing S256 "More" dropdown. Neither surface duplicates the catalog, so D-081 holds.

**Desktop is brought along (Option A, chosen over the spec’s "recommend desktop stays as-is"; flagged and reversible):** a single shared primary projection is what satisfies the no-drift mandate, and a mobile-only Guides/More is incoherent, since desktop would keep the Nepal/Japan tabs mobile drops. So the desktop top row consolidates 6 → 4 as well: Nepal and Japan via the Guides link, and Flights, Documents and Shared Links in the existing desktop "More" dropdown, which already sources `navItems − primary`. No desktop reachability is lost. **Fallback if desktop should be literally frozen at 6:** keep Nepal, Japan and Flights `primary` and add a second `mobileTabsForActiveTrip()` helper in this module (still one source) projecting only the 5 mobile tabs. That is worse mental-model architecture, but it is clean and reversible.
**D-172 / D-164 unaffected:** the tab bar still computes `primaryItemsForActiveTrip()` once (D-172 memo) and returns `null` under `/travel` (D-164); the synthetic More tab is appended after the memoized primaries.
**Why:** five tabs or fewer is the Apple-HIG mobile ceiling and the reviewed simplicity feature (plan A2). Consolidating Nepal and Japan behind one Guides page and the long tail behind one More page is "fewer options" applied to the IA, delivered as pure route-pages, with no new overlay, no z-ladder pressure and no modal contract, and with one shared nav source so the two navs can't drift.
**Changes if:** a 6th tab is ever wanted (that re-opens the HIG ceiling and needs sign-off); Guides or More want a different surface; or the desktop-frozen fallback is elected. The rest of D-071 stays LOCKED.

### D-232 · (S330, 2026-07-25) · `getCityForDate` is trip-scoped: the default-pack per-day city map applies only to the default trip; custom trips resolve via `legForDate(activeTrip,date).fallbackCity`
**Decision:** the default-pack per-day city map (`TRIP_CITIES`, derived from the default content root per D-136) applies only to the default trip. Non-default (custom) trips resolve every date via `legForDate(activeTrip, date).fallbackCity`, their own leg city, never the default map. `getCityForDate` gates the `TRIP_CITIES` lookup on `isDefaultTrip()` (`core/trips/index.ts:48`): default gets the map hit then the leg fallback, exactly as before, and non-default skips the map and returns the leg fallback. This fixes the bug where a custom trip overlapping the default window (Dec 9 – Jan 9) showed Kathmandu, Osaka and Tokyo. The active trip and `isDefaultTrip()` are captured at module load, which is correct because a trip switch is a pointer write plus a full page reload (D-172), so a fresh module graph re-captures the new active trip.
**Preserved (LOCKED):** the default trip is byte-identical to pre-S330 (the S82 frozen boundary matrix and the derivation-identity test `lib/__tests__/trip-cities.test.ts` are green and unchanged); D-006 (`getCityForDate` is still the one resolver, no second one added); D-099 (no `lib/` import, since `isDefaultTrip` is a core→core import); D-136 (`deriveTripCities`/`TRIP_CITIES` intact). A new regression test `lib/__tests__/trip-cities-scoped.test.ts` proves a custom trip over the default window resolves its own leg city.
**Why:** a custom trip's per-day cities were silently wrong, leaking default-pack cities. Gating on the already-existing `isDefaultTrip()` fixes it with zero new infra and default byte-identity.
**Changes if:** custom trips gain authored per-day city data (then derive per-active-trip like the default pack), or trip-switch stops reloading (D-172), in which case resolve the gate at call time.

### D-233 · (S326, 2026-07-25, proposed) · Flight-card phase/countdown derives from the existing trip-clock, not booking labels; layover verdict and departDate are authored additive fields, operating within D-034/D-169/D-016 with no amendment
**Decision (3 rulings for the Flighty gold-standard card, S326):**
1. **Phase strip and proximity-scaled countdown** derive from the app's existing, D-034-independent trip-clock subsystem: `getNow()` (honors `?today=`, D-075), `computeCountdown(target, now)` (D-016), and `TRIP_DATES`/`dayInTripFor` (`core/dates`), targeting a per-journey date-only anchor. Add one additive authored field `Journey.departDate: 'YYYY-MM-DD'` (a member of `TRIP_DATES`) to `lib/booking-data.ts`. Phases run at day granularity: `upcoming` → `today`/`departing` (now's trip-day equals `departDate`) → `completed`; proximity scaling is `computeCountdown`'s month/week/day decomposition. The card never reads `departLabel`, `arriveLabel`, `duration` or `totalDuration` for timing, and adds no `Date` or parse to the verbatim-label render path.
2. **The layover Relaxed/Normal/Tight verdict** is an additive authored field `Layover.verdict?: 'relaxed' | 'normal' | 'tight'`; the presenter maps it to a label and color via a static record (D-020). There is no parsing of `Layover.duration`. An absent verdict shows duration only.
3. **Empty Terminal/Gate/Seat/Confirmation chips** are pure presentation (labelled-empty, non-reflowing). Gate and Confirmation aren't in the data and render as "not yet assigned", never fabricated. No locked-decision pressure.
**Why D-034-clean (letter and intent):** `departDate` is date-only, with no time, no timezone and no `Date` object in `booking-data.ts`, so it cannot recompute or "correct" the trans-Pacific/date-line time, duration or total labels D-034 protects. There is no arithmetic in a bare date to diverge, and it is authored and colocated with the label (the D-036 content-agreement precedent). `verdict` is authored human judgment, more correct than a naive minutes threshold that can't see immigration or terminal-change tightness, and it is not an invented fact. D-034's "no Date in the presenter" governs the booking-label render path, which stays Date-free; the countdown consumes the separate `core/dates` subsystem the hero already uses. **Rejected:** a per-leg ISO datetime with a time of day, which would duplicate the time the label owns and re-import the timezone math D-034 banished. **D-169-clean:** the countdown is driven by the local clock, never a live feed. **D-016 not forked:** `TRIP_START` (the hero target, D-006) is untouched, and per-journey targets are inherent to per-journey countdowns; the unit assertion `OUTBOUND_JOURNEY.departDate === TRIP_DATES[0]` pins the outbound card to the hero.
**Implementation seam:** phase and countdown read `getNow()`, `computeCountdown`, `TRIP_DATES` and `Journey.departDate`; layover reads `Layover.verdict`. Never parse or Date-math any booking label. Add exactly two additive authored fields to `lib/booking-data.ts` (`Journey.departDate`, `Layover.verdict`) and leave all verbatim label rendering unchanged.
**Residual risk (accepted):** the countdown zeroes at local midnight of `departDate` rather than at gate time. That is coarser than "boarding in 2h", but it is consistent with the hero (`TRIP_START` is midnight) and fully D-034/D-169-clean.
**Changes if:** gate-minute precision is wanted (author a per-journey departure instant plus offset, which reopens D-034 and needs sign-off), or `booking-data.ts` gains real per-flight datetimes, at which point it stops being pure verbatim data (D-034's "Changes if").

### D-234 · (S327–S329 / Lane D "AI agent v2", proposed 2026-07-26) · The concierge agent contract: a `{reply, ops[]}` envelope the Worker emits (shape) and the client validates and executes (state); proposals only, id-addressed, no bulk-destructive verbs
**Context.** Lane D upgrades the concierge from a chat-only relay to an agent that can add, remove and update plans, which is what we asked for; it supersedes S287. D1/S327 (`8594c9f`) tags each digest item with its stable ` #<id>`. This decision fixes the single contract that D2/S328 (Worker) and D3/S329 (client) both build against. The Worker owns the **shape** of what the model proposes; the client owns **state-validity and execution**.
**Decision.**
**1) Op vocabulary: four single-item, id-addressed verbs. No bulk, no clear, no copy.** Ops map 1:1 to `useItinerary()` CRUD and address items by the stable `ItineraryItem.id` from the D1 digest, never by index: `addItem`(date,title,category [+startMinutes,durationMinutes,notes,location]) · `updateItem`(itemId,date,≥1 patch field) · `removeItem`(itemId,date) · `moveItem`(itemId,fromDate,toDate≠fromDate). Deliberately excluded: `copyDay`, all bulk ops, and `clearDay`/`clearAll`. Single-item `removeItem` behind an explicit confirm is the destructive ceiling, matching Wanderlog shipping no agent delete. Time is authored as `startMinutes` (int 0–1439), not the legacy `time` string. The agent never supplies an id for a new item; the client mints via `generateItemId()` at execution.
**2) Response envelope + strict JSON Schema.** One response per message (request-count parity, no agent loop, no server state). `{ reply: string, ops: Op[] }`, where `reply` is human chat text and `ops` is 0 or more proposed ops (empty means pure chat). `Op` is a single flat object with a `type` enum plus a nullable-superset of fields, not a oneOf or discriminated union, because (a) Groq `strict:true` needs every property in `required` with `additionalProperties:false`, and the nullable-superset is the strict pattern, and (b) it stays portable to Gemini `responseSchema`, which is weak on anyOf. The `category` enum is the exact D-012 `ItineraryCategory` 10-set (plus null). The schema constrains shape only; per-type required fields and all state checks are the client's job.
**Provider-ladder requirement (load-bearing):** the ladder is Gemini→Groq→Groq (D-174), Gemini first. The envelope must be produced by whichever leg answers, so S328 applies the schema to both legs: Gemini via `generationConfig.responseMimeType:'application/json'` plus `responseSchema` (Gemini dialect: `nullable:true`, `propertyOrdering`), Groq via `response_format:{type:'json_schema',json_schema:{…strict:true}}`. The flat, union-free Op schema is what lets one logical schema render to both dialects. Fallback if applying it to both is impractical: a Groq-first plan-mode ladder. Never ship a plan-mode where Gemini returns prose with no ops.
**3) Validation contract (`validateOps`, client-only, run against the live itinerary before a chip shows):** (1) `ops` is a 0–N array (empty is valid); (2) `type` is one of the four verbs, else drop; (3) per-type required fields present and typed, else drop; (4) every date is in `TRIP_DATES` (`core/dates`), else drop; (5) `category` is in D-012, else drop; (6) **target existence:** for update, remove and move, `itemId` must exist among live items (tombstones excluded, mirroring `visiblePlans`) on the stated date or fromDate, else drop, as a stale-id guard; (7) `startMinutes` in [0,1439] and `durationMinutes` > 0 when non-null, else drop; (8) `updateItem` carries at least one non-null patch, else drop. **Rule: reject and drop invalid ops silently, never surface a raw validation error.** One bad op must not nuke a good reply, so render `reply` plus every surviving op. Nothing is logged (D-152).
**4) Execution / undo: proposals only.** Ops are proposals and nothing mutates on render. Each valid op becomes a chip ("Add Ramen to Dec 20 · 7:00pm"); on explicit user confirm it executes through `useItinerary()` (addItem mints the id; updateItem patch is the non-null content fields; removeItem; moveItem). Each applied op yields undo via the existing undo-toast, capturing pre-state at apply. Routing writes through `useItinerary()` rather than the storage port the digest reads gains attribution (D-041/D-230) and rev/hlc sync stamping (D-106) for free. This keeps the concierge read-only until confirmed: the model only reads (the digest), its output writes only on explicit user action through standard CRUD, and the model never autonomously mutates. Honors D-152.
**5) The D2↔D3 seam.** The Worker (S328) emits one JSON `{reply, ops}` as `application/json`, with ops shape-constrained on both ladder legs. It guarantees valid JSON, that `ops` is an array, and that each op matches the property schema. It does not validate against itinerary state, adds no server state, logs no ops (D-152), and makes the same single upstream call; the request shape is unchanged (`{message, history, context}` plus `x-trip-token`). The client (S329) owns `validateOps` (section 3), chip rendering, and confirm-gated execution via `useItinerary()` with undo (section 4). **Boundary invariant: the Worker owns shape; the client owns state-validity and execution.**
**6) Streaming.** The structured turn returns `application/json` as one object, not SSE. Constrained JSON can't cleanly interleave streamed prose with a terminal ops block, ops must be complete to validate, and the reply is one or two sentences. S329 does `await res.json()`. The concierge SSE path (`lib/concierge-sse.ts`) becomes unused for this route: leave it in place and flag it for later removal, don't delete it in this slice. *Ceiling: to restore the streaming feel, re-wrap SSE Worker-side, emitting `reply` as `delta.content` events (the existing `extractDeltaText` handles them) followed by a terminal `data:{"ops":[…]}` event.*
**Why.** One flat, union-free schema is the only shape satisfying Groq `strict:true` and Gemini `responseSchema` from a single source, so the envelope survives whichever ladder leg answers. Splitting shape (Worker) from state-validity (client) puts each check where the data to run it lives: the Worker has no itinerary, the client has no secrets. Proposals through `useItinerary()` keep the model read-only until confirmed while inheriting attribution and sync. Four id-addressed verbs with a single-item delete ceiling bound the blast radius to what a user can already do by hand.
**Implementation seam — S328 (Worker):** in `worker/src/providers.ts`, switch plan-mode to non-streaming JSON on both legs (Gemini `:generateContent` plus `responseSchema`; Groq `response_format` json_schema `strict:true`), return `{reply, ops}` as `application/json` (retiring `passthroughSSE` for this route), and extend `SYSTEM_PROMPT` to instruct ops-by-`#id` for edit, remove and move, and `ops:[]` for pure chat. No server state, no op logging, one upstream call, same request shape. S328 also raises `CONTEXT_TRUNCATE_LENGTH` to at least 6517 (~6600–7000, per the S327 measurement) along with the coupled client `DIGEST_CAP`; compatible, tracked separately.
**Implementation seam — S329 (client):** add `validateOps` (section 3, run against `useItinerary().plans`), swap `use-concierge-chat.ts`'s SSE read for `res.json()` → `{reply, ops}`, render surviving ops as proposal chips, and on confirm execute via `useItinerary()` (section 4) with the existing undo-toast. Mint new-item ids client-side and never trust an agent id for `addItem`.
**Changes if:** Gemini `responseSchema` can't hold the flat Op schema in practice (then a Groq-first plan-mode ladder); a real multi-item or "copy my whole day" ask appears (then add `copyDay` or bulk behind the same propose-and-confirm gate); the streaming reply is wanted back (the section 6 SSE-rewrap ceiling); or a hard "concierge never writes at all" line is drawn, in which case this decision reverts, though the write path is exactly the add/remove/update ask, so it stands.

### D-235 · (S323 / Lane A "A5 token pass", proposed 2026-07-26 in the planning notes; **LOCKED 2026-07-26 via the A5 checkpoint**) · One gold chrome accent + country tints demoted to content-wayfinding; 17px body with a full overflow re-check; amends/retires D-058, D-072, D-178. **Section 1's hue is superseded by D-269: the chrome accent is cyan (S353), not gold. The one-accent rule and sections 2–3 stand.**
**Context.** The 2026-07-25 "iOS-like simplicity" directive drives A5, the token-layer visual pass and the last Lane-A slice. The plan's "one accent" target collided with three LOCKED accent decisions: D-058 (sakura interactive-fill), D-072 (route-driven accent) and D-178/FU-40 (palette). Shown the blueprint at the A5 checkpoint, we chose the middle path and the full 17px change. This entry records that sign-off so S323 executes without re-litigating the locked entries it changes.
**Decision — LOCKED (sign-off 2026-07-26):**
**1) One gold *chrome* accent.** All interactive chrome (buttons, focus rings, links, active states, the command-palette/cmdk composite) uses a single gold accent, everywhere, route-independent. This retires the accent-behavior clauses of **D-058** (sakura as interactive-fill), **D-072** (accent switches by route: himalaya on Nepal, sakura on Japan) and **D-178/FU-40** (the two-accent palette composite). Those three entries are amended: their accent *rules* no longer govern chrome. **[Superseded in part — S353, 2026-07-30. Authority: locked wave scope call #3, "Full visual overhaul, chosen over the researched 'evolve, your tokens are already good' recommendation." The word *gold* in this clause is dead: the single chrome accent is now cyan `189 90% 60%` (`--primary`/`--accent`/`--ring`/`--accent-scroll`), and gold is a warning colour only (`globals.css:98`). Everything else in this clause is unchanged and still binding: one accent, all interactive chrome, everywhere, route-independent, and the retirement of D-058/D-072/D-178's accent rules. See D-269. Don't "restore" gold chrome on the strength of this entry's LOCKED status.]**
**2) Country tints survive as content wayfinding only.** himalaya (Nepal) and sakura (Japan) are kept, but demoted from interactive chrome to content and wayfinding color: page-section tints, guide-card identity, map-leg coloring. The two-country visual identity is preserved; only the *interactive* accent unifies to gold. **[Hue dead, see D-269. The "unifies to one accent" half of this sentence is live and binding; the word *gold* in it is superseded by cyan `189 90% 60%`. Section 2's country-tint rule itself is unaffected and remains load-bearing.]** (This is why D-058/D-072/D-178 are amended rather than deleted: the tints they defined live on in a content-only role.)
**3) Body 17px + mandatory overflow re-check.** The base body rises to 17px for iOS-native density. Because the whole rem scale derives from the base, this shifts spacing and sizing app-wide and risks **D-022** layout overflow at mobile breakpoints. S323 has to re-run the full D-022 overflow matrix at every breakpoint and declare every moved baseline. This is the biggest baseline mover of the wave.
**Mechanical scope carried into S323 (not sign-off-gated; blueprint direction, minimalist deletions):** 6 glass tiers → 1 (de-glass content surfaces at the token layer; the 5 content-only tiers gut with roughly zero component edits, since the nav layer hand-rolls its own `backdrop-blur`, and `.glass-card-dark` is the one shared tier, which splits into the FU-41 Sheet primitive); 3 fonts → 1 (a tailwind alias, dropping 2 downloads, with `tabular-nums` for numerals); 6 shadow tiers → 2 (glow kept as a separate effect); ~13–15 type sizes → ~6; and a new `--hairline` separator token. **FU-41:** hand-roll one dark `Sheet` primitive (props `open/onClose/onExitComplete/labelledBy/side`, keeping the LOCKED **D-021** focus contract) and refit all three hand-rolled sheets (place-detail, import-place, FilterSheet). **[Corrected at S323 execution 2026-07-26:** the blueprint's premise that `components/ui/sheet.tsx` (radix) is *dead* was factually wrong. `components/concierge-chat.tsx` actively imports it (`Sheet/SheetTrigger/SheetContent/…`, radix `onOpenChange` API), so it was not deleted, because deleting it breaks the build. The new hand-rolled primitive lives at `components/ui/sheet-dark.tsx`, and radix `ui/sheet.tsx` is preserved for its one live consumer. Fully retiring radix means migrating concierge-chat to the dark primitive, which is **FU-43, a separate slice** (different surface, different API). Recorded so the "delete the dead radix" clause is not treated as unmet.]**
**Constraints (S323 stays in-bounds):** install nothing (Konsta is a markup reference only); token-layer and shared-primitive only, so it must not re-open S317's /travel layout or any A1–A4 IA; de-glassed content surfaces must stay at or above AA contrast, and prove it; re-run the sheet-open axe; respect reduced-motion. Declare the full re-baseline set in the report, since the standing re-baseline watch points hardest here.
**Why.** Unifying interactive chrome to one accent is the iOS-simplicity win, while keeping himalaya and sakura as content color keeps the Nepal-versus-Japan identity that makes the app *this* trip's app. 17px is the density we asked for, accepted with its overflow cost gated by the D-022 re-run. The de-glass is an Apple-HIG correction (glass belongs on nav and overlay, not content) done cheaply at the token layer.
**Changes if:** the de-glassed content surfaces can't hold AA (then keep a faint tint and re-check); 17px forces unfixable overflow on a real surface (then fall back to 16px and re-run the matrix); or country accent chrome is wanted back later (that reverts D-072's route-switch, but the choice was a unified gold **[hue dead, see D-269; read "unified accent", not "gold"]**).
**AA text-floor (S323 finding, deliberate, do not revert):** de-glassing content surfaces removed the gradient background-*image* that had been making axe return "incomplete" and silently skip contrast on roughly app-wide text. Solid surfaces are evaluable, which exposed that low-alpha white text (`text-white/25..60`) composites to grey and cannot reach 4.5:1 on any dark navy fill. No surface darkening fixes it, so the text was floored to `rgb(255 255 255 / 0.62)` in `globals.css @layer utilities` (hover:text-white still wins by specificity, TM legibility `!important` still overrides, and border and variant tokens are untouched). Net effect: tertiary and secondary text is uniformly brighter, which is more iOS-like. This is the "can't hold AA" clause landing text-side rather than tint-side. These must not be lowered back, because the app was silently failing AA before S323.

**Clause status at 2026-08-01** _(verified in `nextjs_space/app/globals.css`, so a reader gets the whole verdict without leaving this entry):_ **Section 1's one-accent rule is live** (`globals.css:29-32`, S353 kept it deliberately). **Section 1's hue "gold" is dead, now cyan** (`:23/:33/:44/:98/:123`, see **D-269**). **Section 1's retirement of D-058/D-072/D-178 is live and now stronger** (`route-accent-engine.tsx` was deleted entirely at S354, `globals.css:179-182`). **Section 2's country tints as content wayfinding are live and load-bearing** (`:99-100` cite this entry by name; `.glass-nepal`/`.glass-japan` at `:300-312`). **But section 2's own trailing clause, "only the interactive accent unifies to gold", carries the dead hue: read it as "unifies to one accent." See D-269.** **Section 3's body 17px is live** (`:250-256`, `font-size: 106.25%`). **Mechanical scope: 6 glass → 1 is live** (`:279-281`); **6 shadow → 2 is live but re-valued** (`:147-151`, the resting tier is now a hairline ring); **3 fonts → 1 is amended by D-245** (`tailwind.config.ts:47` now reads `var(--font-display)`). **The AA text-floor paragraph above is live; see the correction on D-246, which asserts it was deleted.**

## D-236 · Superseded by D-241 · Guest = full-roam sandbox on the showcase content

## D-237 · Superseded by D-239 · Front door v2 = three-path wall + post-login routing

## D-238 · Guest conversion = "Keep this trip" prefix-scan adoption; capability secrets and trip-mutating actions require an identified traveler
_(S337, proposed 2026-07-27)_
**Decision:** (1) **Conversion (S340):** a persistent quiet CTA while `isGuest` opens one dialog (nickname required, trip name defaulted), then synchronously runs `id = crypto.randomUUID()` → `adoptSandbox(id)` → `signIn(nickname)` → `clearGuest()` → `joinTrip(id, tripName)` → full reload to `/`. `adoptSandbox` is a new gateway helper (D-097, raw storage stays in `gateway.ts`) that prefix-enumerates every localStorage key beginning `trip:guest-sandbox:`, copies each to `trip:{id}:{suffix}`, then deletes the sandbox keys. The key list is the scan, not a hardcoded enumeration; today that is at most the 14 `TripScopedSlot`s, of which only edited slots exist on disk, and a future slot can never be forgotten. Photo blobs (IndexedDB, id-keyed, app-scoped) need no copy, because the copied metadata resolves them. It is lossless: the sandbox becomes a normal capability-token trip (its id is its Trip Key) and the default pack is untouched. A companion `wipeSandbox()` clears the namespace on fresh guest opt-in. (2) **Secret confinement (S339), a new invariant full-roam makes necessary:** the Trip Key display (`getTripId()` in Settings' Trip group), the `?trip=` copy-link builders (Settings and trips-hub), the Sync Code group, and every trip-mutating registry action (create, join, switch, rename, forget) require an identified traveler. A guest sees a sign-in or "Keep this trip" affordance instead, gated via the existing `useActiveTraveler()`. Without this, full-roam would hand every anonymous visitor the default pack's live-trip write capability. Full-roam means routes and showcase content, not full features: sync, push, presence, concierge (Worker quota) and travel mode (D-164) stay identity-gated.
**Why:** the prefix scan is the only conversion spec that stays complete as slots are added, and the secret gate preserves D-205's entire security model (unguessable, unenumerable tokens) under the widened guest surface.
**Changes if:** conversion ever needs to merge into an existing trip (a new decision, since adoption currently only mints), or guests should be able to share the demo itself (a demo-specific, non-secret link, and a separate design).

---

## D-234 section 3 — Amended (S342, 2026-07-27): op targets resolve globally by `itemId`; the op's date is a hint
Target existence resolves globally by `itemId`, not by `(date, itemId)`. Item ids are globally unique (`generateItemId()` uuid, and the D-018 merge invariant keeps a given id in exactly one `DayPlan.items[]`), so the day an item sits on is a fact the client already owns and does not need the model to restate. For `updateItem`, `removeItem` and `moveItem`, `validateOps` resolves the first live (non-tombstoned) item with that id across all dates; the op's `date`/`fromDate` is a hint only, and a mismatch no longer drops the op. `applyOp` and `describeOp` act on the resolved date. Rule 4 is correspondingly narrowed to the dates that address a *new* day: `addItem.date` and `moveItem.toDate` must still be in `TRIP_DATES`. Tombstone exclusion, the `toDate ≠ current day` guard (evaluated against the resolved day), and rules 1, 2, 3, 5, 7 and 8 are unchanged. **Rationale:** requiring the model to restate a known date was a pure liability, silently dropping otherwise-perfect ops on any non-ISO or wrong-year echo. **Companion:** drops are no longer invisible, and surface as one muted "N suggested change(s) didn't match the current plan." line, with no rule names, no op detail and nothing logged (D-152 holds). **Worker note:** `worker/src/providers.ts` hardcodes the trip window (`2026-12-09`/`2027-01-09`) as two named consts, since the worker cannot import `core/dates`. That is a manual edit if the trip year ever moves.

**Addendum (issue #13, 2026-08-14): a dropped op now says WHY. Section 3's "silently" and the S342 companion's "no op detail" are RETIRED; three invariants around them survive and are what this addendum is really recording.** Written inside D-234 rather than as a new entry, the shape D-316 used for issue #19 and for D-262's reason: the person reading about the silent-drop contract is reading *this* entry, and a clause retired somewhere else is a dangling pointer.

**What is retired, exactly.** The muted line still counts the drops and is still one line — it is evolved, not doubled — but it now ends `: <reason>; <reason>.` So section 3's word *silently* is retired, and so is the S342 companion's *"no op detail"*, because a cause **is** op detail at the coarsest grain. What replaces them: a drop is answered with a **fixed, human sentence chosen from a closed set**, deduped by cause, and nothing else about the op is shown. Reported symptom: a suggestion vanishing is indistinguishable from the assistant ignoring you, which is exactly the "the concierge can't modify my plans" report S342 half-fixed by making the *count* visible.

**The three invariants that survive, and why each is still load-bearing.** (1) **One bad op never nukes a good reply** — `validateOps` still filters rather than throwing, surviving ops still render their chips, and the reason line sits beside them. (2) **No raw validation error reaches a person** — no rule number, no field name, no machine text, no JSON, no value lifted out of the op. This is now STRUCTURAL rather than a promise: `lib/concierge-ops.ts` exports `dropReason(raw, plans) → DropCode | undefined`, a code union with no prose in it at all, and the copy is a `switch` in `components/concierge-chat.tsx` (the reason-code → copy shape `components/photo-attach.tsx::reasonMessage` already set). Lib has no sentence to leak. (3) **Nothing is logged anywhere on this path (D-152)** — still pinned by the console spy in `lib/__tests__/concierge-ops.test.ts`, which now also calls `dropReason`.

**One predicate, not two.** `dropReason` holds the rule logic and `isValidOp` is its boolean wrapper (`dropReason(...) === undefined`), so the stated reason and the decision to drop cannot disagree — the same discipline D-316 Part 2 used for the clash predicate. The three ANDed `addItem` checks were split so date, title and category are distinguishable. The reason is derived at RENDER time from `(rawOp, plans)` and never held in state: `useItinerary().plans` has a fresh identity every render, so an effect keyed on it is the infinite loop D-316's addendum records. The line stays a plain `<p>` inside the panel's existing `aria-live="polite"` log — **not** `role="alert"`: a drop is part of the reply, where the D-316 clash refusal is a blocked user action answering a button press.

**This is not licence to move the #19 clash check into `validateOps`.** A dropped op is GONE; the clash refusal must keep its chip on screen and confirmable, and `validateOps` re-runs every render where the refusal answers a press. D-316's ruling stands unchanged.

**Second half of issue #13 — the client never renders a non-2xx response body (`hooks/use-concierge-chat.ts`).** Two paths leaked machine text to travellers: a dead or unreachable provider while the device is online (Worker deleted, DNS/TLS failure, CORS rejection) rejected with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` and that literal string reached the error row through `err.message`; and the non-2xx branch overwrote its own status sentence with the body's `error` field. Both now use our own copy, keyed on status class (auth 401/403 → sign in again; 413 → shorten the message; everything else → try again in a moment), in the established voice: name what happened, give the next action, never show machine text. **The evidence for choosing replacement over pass-through:** the Worker authors every `error` string it sends (`worker/src/index.ts::jsonError`, plus `providers.ts`' 502) and never passes an upstream provider body through — but the status code does not separate its readable copy from its machine text (401 is `missing trip token`, which D-239 also bans as UI copy; 400 is `context must be a string`; the one genuinely traveller-ready sentence is on the 502), and the client cannot tell our Worker's body from a proxy's, a CDN error page's, or a future Worker version's. With no clean split, nothing from the wire is rendered. Cost, recorded honestly: the Worker's own good 502 sentence is discarded along with the rest, and pinned assertions in `lib/__tests__/use-concierge-chat.test.ts` and `e2e/concierge.spec.ts` that asserted the body verbatim were deliberately inverted to assert it is absent. A second cost in the same bucket: `403` carries both `this trip isn't on your account` and D-181's `origin not allowed`, so a CORS/deploy misconfiguration now tells the traveller to sign in again — advice that cannot help. Distinguishing them needs the pass-through this entry rejects, so it waits on the same trigger below. **Changes if:** the Worker ever marks its own copy as traveller-safe on the wire (for example `{error, safe:true}`), at which point pass-through becomes checkable rather than assumed.

---

## D-239 · Two-token account model: User Token (account) ≠ Trip Token (per-trip); supersedes D-237's door shape, amends D-238 section 1, D-205 naming amended
_(S337B, proposed 2026-07-28; the 2026-07-27/28 two-token directive is the sign-off)_
**Decision:** two capabilities, never mixed (blueprint the planning notes). **User Token** is the promoted Sync Code: the same on-disk key `tripPlannerSyncCode` (key 28, zero migration, so every device that already minted a Sync Code is automatically an account), the same `crypto.randomUUID()` mint, the same remote doc `trips/{userToken}/profile/tripList` (mergeTripLists LWW union plus tombstones, zero Firestore-rules change). Only the UI and docs name changes. It grants the account (the cross-device trip list), is minted at account creation, and is never shareable: copy exists only for "your other device", and the copy warns never to share it. **Trip Token** is the trip id, unchanged (the old "Trip Key"). It grants one trip, is minted at trip creation, and sharing a trip is sharing its Trip Token. **State model:** authenticated means a traveler is present (nickname slots, with every D-055/D-057/D-120 identity gate untouched); account-complete means a traveler and `getSyncCode()!==null`; nothing but the upgrade affordance gates on completeness, for grandfathering compat. **Front door v3** (`token-gate.tsx`, wall always-on per D-054, firebase-free in all builds, with no push or lookup at the door; `subscribeTripList`'s absent-first-snapshot seed and present-snapshot merge do the remote work after reload): (a) **"Log in"** is "Enter your token" (the user token) plus nickname → `setSyncCode` → `signIn` → full reload (D-172, and this sequencing retires the mount-once-subscribe hazard) landing on `/trips/`; the form may offer the device's stored key-28 token (D-053 soft security), which prevents orphan accounts after an unsaved-token sign-out. (b) **"Create an account"** is nickname → mint → `setSyncCode` plus `signIn` → a show-once screen (prominent token, copy, "save this, only way back in", explicit confirm) → reload landing on `/trips/`; the door no longer creates trips, since account creation and trip creation are separate acts. (c) **"Explore the demo"** is unchanged (D-236). D-237's nickname-only door path is deleted. **/trips (login-gated per D-238 section 2, which the login rule strengthens):** select a trip, create one (minting its Trip Token; per-trip share is the token plus a `?trip=` link), or add by Trip Token (the old join). The `?trip=` handshake is unchanged when identified; unidentified or guest holds the pending token, completes door path (a) or (b), then calls `joinTrip(pending)` before the reload, landing on `/`, since the join is the selection. **Conversion (D-238 section 1 amended):** "Keep this trip" now means create an account and adopt: mint userToken → `setSyncCode` → `adoptSandbox(newId)` → `signIn` → `clearGuest` → `joinTrip` → show-once → reload `/`; adoptSandbox, wipeSandbox and the prefix-scan are unchanged. **Sign-out:** `signOut()` is unchanged and clears the identity pair only; key 28, the registry and trip data stay on disk (D-053-consistent and lockout-safe), and a returning signed-out user sees the wall. **Grandfathered friends (D-209 item 3):** no lockout, since sessions keep working (authenticated means a traveler). A one-time "Finish your account" affordance (`traveler && !getSyncCode()`, on /trips and Settings) mints their User Token plus show-once; minting touches only key 28, so identity slots, knownTrips, the pointer and all trip-scoped bytes are untouched by construction. **The never-mix guard is labels and flow, not validation (accepted residual risk):** both tokens are UUIDs and the door has to stay firebase-free, since dormant builds can't look up anyway. A Trip Token pasted at login yields a working-but-empty account, recoverable losslessly (sign out, log in right; local data isn't keyed by user token). A synced-build stray seed of `trips/{tripId}/profile/tripList` is accepted: reserved subcollection, self-inflicted, recoverable. Carried over intact from D-237: wall always-on (D-054), guest-route mode deleted, the D-021 a11y contract, and `joinTrip`/`upsertKnownTrip` refusing `GUEST_SANDBOX_ID`.
**Why:** the Sync Code already is the account credential in all but name, being a high-entropy capability owning the cross-device trip list, so promoting it is a rename plus a door rather than a new subsystem. Keeping key 28 makes every existing synced device an account for free, and the seed and merge already living in `subscribeTripList` let the door stay firebase-free in every build.
**Changes if:** token-format discrimination is wanted (prefix future mints `user-`/`trip-` and grandfather bare UUIDs); real users hit the paste-confusion residual; account-level identity ever syncs (nickname-per-account would need a profile field, which is a new decision); or a real backend arrives (D-205's zero-backend premise).

**D-205 naming — amended (S337B, the directive is the sign-off):** qualified token names are now the rule. The account credential is the **"User Token"** and the per-trip capability the **"Trip Token"**, always qualified; unqualified "token" stays banned in UI copy. **"Trip Key" and "Sync Code" are retired names**, and every copy site updates. The identity remains a plain "name" or "nickname", never a token. Code-level identifiers (`Traveler.token` holding the nickname, `getSyncCode`/`setSyncCode`) are internal misnomers: keep them with comments, no rename churn.

**D-237 — superseded by D-239** in its door and credential shape (Trip Key as the door credential, the three-path door, the nickname-only path). Its carried-over parts (wall always-on D-054, guest-route mode deleted, the D-021 contract) are restated inside D-239.
**D-238 section 1 — amended by D-239** (conversion now mints a User Token plus show-once). D-238 section 2 survives verbatim; its "Sync Code group" phrase now denotes the "User Token group".

**Login is token-only — amended (2026-07-30, direct directive = sign-off):** door path (a) "Log in" now asks for the User Token only. The name field is removed from login and is collected only on path (b) "Create an account". The display name is still required by the identity model (`signIn` writes it to the identity slot `getActiveTraveler` reads, and it is what dismisses the wall and attributes edits), so on login it is reused from this device's saved name if present, and otherwise defaults to **"Traveler"**. New: **Settings → Identity** carries a rename field (re-running `signIn` with the new name) so the default isn't permanent; this is the app's only standalone display-name editor. Everything else in D-239 (the create path, show-once, the `?trip=` hold, sign-out, guest, the never-mix guard) is unchanged.

---

## D-240 · impl-note · `signOut()` is reload-free; any app-root subscription must teardown on `identity:changed`
_(S346, proposed 2026-07-30)_
**Decision:** `signOut()` (`lib/token-auth.ts`) clears the identity pair and fires `IDENTITY_CHANGED_EVENT` without a reload, and all three callers (navbar, settings-panel, more-list) invoke it directly. So any live subscription or heartbeat opened from an app-root mount (for example `itinerary-provider.tsx`) that gates on identity (D-055) has to re-evaluate reactively on `IDENTITY_CHANGED_EVENT`. A mount-once subscription (`[]`, no listener) outlives the session and keeps syncing for a signed-out user. The four domain sync effects and the presence heartbeat already do this; S346 fixed the sync-code trip-list effect, which did not, and which was dormant behind a Firebase gate and therefore latent. Pattern: `activate()` (re-checking all gates) on mount plus `teardown()→activate()` on the event, with an import-invalidation token so a late lazy `import()` can't re-subscribe after teardown.
**Why:** it captures the bug class, a mount-once identity-gated subscription, so a future such subscription isn't written the same broken way.
**Changes if:** `signOut()` is ever made to reload, in which case mount-once teardown is automatic and the reactive listener is redundant, though harmless.

---

# Wave (S347–S361) — D-241 … D-250

_Recorded 2026-07-30 at wave kickoff, ahead of implementation, so everyone working the wave carries them. Source: the plan authored after a 7-lane research wave. **Four of these are locked scope calls, each chosen against a researched counter-recommendation. Don't re-argue them.**_

## D-241 · LOCKED · There is no guest mode; supersedes D-236, retires the D-127/D-128 lineage
_(S351, a direct scope call 2026-07-30 is the sign-off)_
**Decision:** a logged-out visitor sees a marketing landing page and an auth form. Nothing else. Every sandbox mechanism is deleted: `sessionGate.setGuest/clearGuest/isGuest`, `wipeSandbox`, `adoptSandbox`, the `keyFor` guest branch, `GUEST_SANDBOX_ID`, `STORAGE_KEYS.guest`, `components/guest-convert.tsx`, and the two guest e2e specs. That removes roughly 400 LOC and 2 specs. `isDefaultTrip()` semantics and its 9 call sites are unchanged.
**Why:** D-236's sandbox was a *render* gate that never controlled what shipped, the same category error D-242 fixes. Deleting it is a smaller, more honest surface than maintaining a two-identity storage namespace. The counter-argument ("this kills the try-before-signup funnel") was on the table and we chose deletion anyway; on a private site for a friend group the funnel has no value.
**Changes if:** the app ever becomes publicly marketed and needs a try-before-signup path, in which case it wants a real demo trip, not a storage-namespace fork.

## D-242 · LOCKED (impl note) · The content pack is public by construction
_(S347, recorded 2026-07-30)_
**Decision:** `core/content/*` and `lib/booking-data.ts` compile into eagerly-loaded chunks on a public origin and must contain no PII: no real flight numbers, seat assignments, booking refs, personal addresses, or the party's real names. Realistic *fictional* equivalents only. Real booking detail belongs in a user-created itinerary item in localStorage. This is a standing content rule rather than a one-time scrub, and every future content edit is bound by it. **Ruling on the seam (S347):** prose in `core/content/` takes generic descriptors and all seat strings are deleted outright; `lib/booking-data.ts` keeps its *structure* and takes fictional same-shape values so the flights page and the FlightRadar24 tracker code path stay exercised; the hotel drops to area level, with no street and no postcode. The tracker URL encodes the flight number (`'Meridian Air 4471'` → `.../flights/ma4471`), so the deep link is the PII in URL form and must be regenerated from fiction, not merely hidden.
**Why:** verified empirically. `grep "Meridian Air 4471" out/_next/static/chunks/8167-*.js` matched, and that chunk is in the eager `<script>` set of `out/index.html`. `TokenGate` decides what to *render*, never what to *download*, so no client-side gate can ever fix this. Real flight numbers plus seat rows plus a street address next to exact travel dates is a machine-readable 32-day empty-house notice. What the fiction removes is the *actionable* part (which airport when, which seat row, which bed); the dates themselves are deliberately kept.
**Changes if:** never for the public build. If a private, authenticated build is ever added, real detail may live there, but not in `core/content/`.
**Residual, stated honestly:** the data has already been public. A scrub does not un-publish it.

## D-243 · LOCKED (impl note) · `GET /resolve` follows redirects and parses the final URL only
_(S348, recorded 2026-07-30)_
**Decision:** the Worker's `/resolve` endpoint follows redirects and parses `res.url` only; the response body is never read. The Google host allow-list is duplicated server-side and never trusted from the client (anchored regex `^(www\.|maps\.)?google\.(com|co\.[a-z]{2}|[a-z]{2,3})$` plus the literal set `{share.google, goo.gl, maps.app.goo.gl}`, `https:` only). Coordinates parse in precedence order: `!3d/!4d` from `data=`, then `@lat,lng`, then `?q|query|destination=lat,lng`. No Google API key, ever.
**Why:** the Places API requires billing, which free-tools-only forbids. The share-link flow (`maps.app.goo.gl/…` → `google.com/maps/place/<Name>/@lat,lng,z/data=!3d<lat>!4d<lng>`) puts everything needed in the redirect target. Not reading the body eliminates response-size, consent-interstitial and HTML-parsing risk at once, and pins outbound fetches to Google hosts, so there is no SSRF surface.
**Changes if:** Google changes the share-redirect format. Three independent patterns in precedence order are the hedge, and a parse miss degrades to `{ok:true, finalUrl}` with no coords, which the client already handles as a name-only prefill.

**Amended at implementation (S348, 2026-07-30, live-verified): the ruling stands, but its rationale was partly wrong.** The premise above, that the redirect target puts *everything needed* in the final URL, does not hold for the dominant share form. A real `share.google/<id>` link resolves live to `https://www.google.com/search?…&kgmid=/m/028h3l&q=Arashiyama`: no `/maps/place/` segment, no `!3d/!4d`, no `@lat,lng`, no coordinates anywhere. Only the desktop address-bar `/maps/place/…` form carries a pin. Consequences, all accepted:
- **`share.google` links yield a name only.** The name parser therefore takes a second shape, `?q=`/`?query=`, after `/maps/place/`, rejected if it looks like a coordinate pair. Without it the dominant share form pre-fills nothing and the feature ships dead. (The older in-repo plan the planning notes:50` did anticipate the `google.com/search?...` target; the wave plan did not.)
- **Coordinates for that form are unobtainable without a paid Places API**, which free-tools-only forbids. Name-only prefill is the honest ceiling and not a bug, and the user completes the pin manually via S357's "Drop a pin".
